# JIT and DynASM work on OC-LuaJIT's LuaJIT

> **What this covers.** How to change what our LuaJIT fork's parser, interpreter and JIT compute, and how to prove the change: the pipeline as our code meets it, the IR and the fold engine, loop optimization, snapshots and exits, the x64 backend, DynASM in `vm_x64.dasc`, the build, recipes, invariants, pitfalls, verification and tools. Round 3 (Lua 5.3.6's 64-bit bitwise operators, commit `2dd5f05`) is the worked example throughout.
>
> **The pin.** LuaJIT 2.1 at upstream `1ee778a4` (`1ee778a4e37122d8ca7d5733c590a47dafd6b15c`), x64 only, GC64, no FFI, no DUALNUM, with our twelve changes ([section 1](#1-scope-and-the-pin)). Written 2026-10-17, at `2dd5f05`; brought up to the CRC32 follow-up's table-index fold on 2026-10-20 ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)), and to the select fix the same day (P54, [11.5b](#115b-the-sel-part-t_select)), and to FIX-NUMCONV on 2026-10-21 ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound), [11.5c](#115c-the-nc-part-t_numconv-ncir)), and on 2026-10-22 to its follow-up: negctl's 36th arm, NUMCONVNOOP ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)), and upstream's fix of #1535 with the pin-move plan (P54, [8.2a](#82a-bumping-the-pin)), and on 2026-10-23 to the proof that shifts of an exact operand, and `&` of two, are exact ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)) and to the int/i64 CSE twins ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400), S5, S6), and the same day to their fix, `patch-bitop-fold.sh`'s hunk 12, a type test in `lj_opt_cse` (P63, [11.5d](#115d-the-cse-part-t_csefwd-cseir-t_cserev), [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)). Since 2026-10-20 the pin is one file, `native/luajit/upstream.commit`; build-native.sh refuses a checkout whose HEAD is any other commit, and the LuaJIT source it compiles is the tree the pin's 40 digits name, byte for byte up to a SHA-1 collision before our twelve changes: it exports that tree with `git archive` and checks it object by object (the commit object hashes to the pin, every tree object to its name, every exported file to its blob id), never the checkout's working tree; a git filter or attribute or a forged object that would change the export is a BUILD FAIL, and replace refs are ignored. It compiles with its own flags, and drops the environment's make, gcc and ld variables that its list names (`CFLAGS`, `CPATH`, `MAKEFLAGS`, `MAKEOVERRIDES` and the rest of [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)'s list; a name that is not a shell identifier it cannot drop, [open question 15](#open-questions)). Every directory it is given is taken at its physical path before its guards check it and its tools use it, and on Windows a path with a name ending in a dot or a space is refused ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)). The toolchain is not pinned. The jar build refuses a native whose blob fingerprint names anything but the pin ([1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is)).
>
> **Citations.** LuaJIT internals are cited by **function name plus the pin**. A line number is only a hint, written `~:N`: "`simplify_conv_i64_num` (lj_opt_fold.c ~:1140)" means line 1140 of the pristine tree `prototype/watchdog/luajit/src` at `1ee778a4`. The pristine tree is CRLF, and the line numbers are the same with or without the CRs. Lines in the patched build copy are marked "build copy". >
> **Marks.** Every claim carries one: **[M]** measured (the run, probe or log is named), **[S]** read in source, **[I]** inferred. A claim with two marks rests on both.
>
> **Upkeep.** Every round that touches the JIT, the interpreter, the parser or the fold engine updates this guide in the same commit, and that round's review checks the guide against the code. When a function moves, update its hint; when a claim stops being true, fix it or mark it.
>
> **Next task.** The CRC32 follow-up's first round shipped the table-index fold and left roadmap row 209 open ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)); the select fix (roadmap row 213, 2026-10-20) bounded select's index in `rec_varg`, so compiled `select(k, ...)` gives the interpreter's results and errors (P54, [11.5b](#115b-the-sel-part-t_select)), and the divergence, reported upstream as LuaJIT issue #1535, was fixed there by `a4c0645` (2026-10-21); FIX-NUMCONV (roadmap row 211 (5), 2026-10-21) removes the result guard of an identity operation on a double, whose value is its operand's own conversion and so always exactly a double ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)); and the CSE-twin fix (roadmap row 271, 2026-10-23) keeps `lj_opt_cse` from commoning an int64 instruction with one of another type, so a 32-bit instruction on the same operands no longer serves a 5.3 operator ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400), P63). Next, in order, by the user's decisions of 2026-10-23, the CSE-twin fix done: **the shift and `&` exactness rule's measure-first round** (roadmap row 211 (6), [3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)); then (1) **row 209's loop-carried widths**, a design pass ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)), which would change `lj_opt_fold.c` or `oclj_bitop_fold.c`, so read [1](#1-scope-and-the-pin), [3](#3-ir-and-the-fold-engine), [4](#4-loop-optimization-snapshots-exits-and-side-traces), [8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script), [8.4](#84-adding-a-fold-rule-safely), [8.4a](#84a-emitting-instructions-or-a-guard-from-a-fold-rule), [9](#9-invariants-and-soundness-arguments) and [10](#10-pitfalls), then [11](#11-verification-discipline); and (2) **the pin move to upstream's v2.1 tip**, a round of its own ([8.2a](#82a-bumping-the-pin); roadmap row 270). The target is `a4c0645`, upstream's v2.1 tip at the 2026-10-21 fetch, 23 commits past `1ee778a4` [M: `rev-list --count` in the scratch bare clone `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/upstream.git`]; re-fetch when the round starts. The pin moves whole, not by cherry-picks, by the main session's decision of 2026-10-21 under the user's delegation: the pin is one upstream commit checked object by object, and every cherry-pick would be a permanent patch-script hunk. `a4c0645` is upstream's fix of #1535 (P54), so the round adopts it: it drops hunks 4 and 5 of `patch-bitop-record.sh`, retires negctl's SELGUARD (upstream has no path C), re-anchors or retires SELRECK (upstream's record-time abort above `LUAI_MAXSTACK` subsumes ours above 2^27), rewrites t_select's path-C coverage gates, which would read PROVED NOTHING, and adds t_select's stitched and side-trace cases ([11.5b](#115b-the-sel-part-t_select)). It takes upstream's two v3.0 syntax backports past the pin unpatched, `0218af7` (empty statements anywhere) and `c41d727` (a line-number directive, `# <n> ["name"]` at a line start), after grepping the OS trees the census boots for a first line that starts with `#` but not `#!` and for any line that starts with `#` and a digit, spaces or tabs allowed between them as the lexer allows them (`# 100 "x.lua"`, the directive's own form, is one) (one hit and the directive is re-decided; disabling it is one hunk), and documents every v3.0 backport the build accepts in README's Lua dialect section (roadmap row 270). What the move meets [M: the census of 2026-10-22, the repo's patch scripts run as build-native.sh runs them on `git archive` exports of both trees, no build; scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/docs-up/`]: on `a4c0645`'s tree eleven of the twelve scripts apply, and `patch-bitop-record.sh` refuses at hunk 5, whose text upstream's fix rewrote, the only one of the round-3 scripts' 28 hunks not found exactly once (the census predates hunk 12, the 29th, which applies on `a4c0645`'s `lj_opt_fold.c` too: 76 lines added, 9 removed, of 2730 [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/runs/fc-ffpatch-1`]); negctl's SELGUARD and SELRECK lose their anchors with it, and its 18 other arms on LuaJIT files keep theirs ([8.2a](#82a-bumping-the-pin) step 4).

## Agent checklist

One page, in the order the work goes. Each item links to the section that explains it.

**Before you start**

1. Read [1](#1-scope-and-the-pin) (what is ours), [2](#2-the-pipeline) (where your change enters) and [10](#10-pitfalls) (what has already gone wrong). For a fold or recorder change, add [3](#3-ir-and-the-fold-engine), [4](#4-loop-optimization-snapshots-exits-and-side-traces) and [9](#9-invariants-and-soundness-arguments); for an interpreter change, [6](#6-dynasm-in-practice).
2. Know the pin: `1ee778a4` (`native/luajit/upstream.commit`, the one place it is written), x64, GC64, `LJ_HASFFI 0`, no DUALNUM. Grep any LuaJIT function you rely on for `LJ_HASFFI`, `LJ_DUALNUM` and `LJ_GC64` first: without FFI some of it is stubbed (P1). build-native.sh refuses a LuaJIT checkout whose HEAD is another commit and compiles the pin's committed tree, checked object by object against the pin's 40 digits, with our twelve changes and its own flags (it drops the environment's `CFLAGS`, `CPATH`, `MAKEFLAGS` and the rest of its list, and takes every directory it is given at its physical path, [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)), so an edit in the checkout is reported and not built, anything that would change the export (a git filter or attribute, a forged object) stops the build, and replace refs are ignored; `OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED=1` builds the working tree instead, for experiments whose native never ships, and wants its own `OCLJ_BUILD`. To move the pin, follow [8.2a](#82a-bumping-the-pin).
3. Work in a fresh scratch copy of the build tree, never in `build/` ([7.4](#74-running-make-by-hand)); for the build gates and negctl, run build-native.sh with `OCLJ_BUILD` pointing at scratch ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)). Never clear or reuse a results directory.
4. Write every script with a file tool. Patch scripts contain no backslashes and no tabs, and no command text you hand the shell tool contains a backslash either (P8). Repo files are LF; in Python use `newline=""`; count CRs by bytes (P7). Never run `python -` or `python -I -`: it waits on stdin and hangs; run a script file (P53).

**While changing code**

5. Put each change in a patch script run on the build copy (extend the file's existing script if it has one), using round 3's hunk engine ([8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script)). Put new code in an `oclj_*` file that the patched file `#include`s.
6. Put rules shared by the parser, the slow path and the recorder in one header, as `oclj_bitop.h` is, so the three cannot disagree ([9.9](#99-one-source-of-truth-for-every-c-path)).
7. **Interpreter:** store the result slot last; keep the registers each slow-path label needs, or reload them ([6.5](#65-what-a-handler-may-clobber), [6.7](#67-round-3s-checks-instruction-by-instruction)).
8. **Recorder:** check the runtime operands first and abort (`lj_trace_err`) on anything you will not compile; emit each guard right after the value it guards; bound a value before any 32-bit arithmetic a guard does on it, and compute record-time constants so they cannot overflow in C ([8.5](#85-adding-a-recorder-path), [8.6](#86-adding-a-guard), S1, P54).
9. **Fold engine:** extend the function that owns a key; keep `LJFOLD` lines in `lj_opt_fold.c`; return existing refs; use `PHIBARRIER` before building on an operand's operand; across a PHI, return only a ref of the folded instruction's own type ([8.4](#84-adding-a-fold-rule-safely), S2-S5); to emit IR from a rule, [8.4a](#84a-emitting-instructions-or-a-guard-from-a-fold-rule), and build what you emit on the side only on loop-body refs (P55); keep a fact of exactness out of a range bound, in a predicate and a rule line of its own (P60, P62, [3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)); on x64 an int ref may stand in for an int64 operand (`simplify_conv_sext`'s LEFTFOLD), so every matcher that compares refs must also compare widths: since 2026-10-23 `lj_opt_cse` does, for I64 against the rest (hunk 12, P63, [3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)); when a rule joins others, grep this guide for every sentence that names the rules it joins ("only the width rule", "both rules") and update each (FIX-NUMCONV's review found 3.9's stale).
10. **Backend:** check which CONV modes the backend can emit (only `int.num` may be guarded) before you design IR ([5](#5-the-backend)).
11. If the bytecode's meaning changes, bump `BCDUMP_VERSION` (0x81 is next) and the fingerprint token ([8.1](#81-adding-or-changing-an-operator-end-to-end)).

**Building**

12. Delete what make cannot see: the objects that `#include` your `oclj_*` file; for a `.dasc` edit, `host/buildvm_arch.h host/buildvm.o lj_vm.o lj_bc.o` ([7.4](#74-running-make-by-hand), P4, P14).
13. Pass `Q=` (empty), never `Q=.`; on Windows the binary is `mingw32-make` (P5, P45). Pass build-native.sh's `XCFLAGS`. A Linux .so that may be packaged is built in AlmaLinux 8 with `OCLJ_GLIBC_MAX=2.28`; a Linux build on anything newer gets its own `OCLJ_BUILD`, never `build/native` ([7.7](#77-the-linux-path), P49).
14. Compare object md5s, not `luajit.exe` (P13). Only the objects your change reaches may differ. Compare like with like: a build without `OCLJ_VARIANT` is the dropin, whose `lj52shim.o` and `jnlua.o` differ from `build/native`'s additive ones, so all 80 objects compare only with `OCLJ_VARIANT=additive` ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)).
15. For a new file or script: add it to build-native.sh (re-copy, patch, marker assertions, freshness gates, a `luajit.exe` gate, [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) and to negctl-bitop.sh's lists ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)). A new static function in an `oclj_*` file must also be called from build-native.sh's `bitop_wgate.c`, or `-Wunused-function` fails the warning gate ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)).

**Testing**

16. The interpreter is the oracle: JIT on against `-joff`, whole outputs, both directions, CR stripped ([11.1](#111-the-interpreter-is-the-oracle)).
17. Check the interpreter against PUC 5.3.6 through a twin generated from the same source ([11.2](#112-the-puc-twin)).
18. Run the generator and the floor suites under every option set (`-Ohotloop=1`, `-Omaxside=0`, `-Ohotloop=1 -Ohotexit=1`, `-O-loop`, and others) ([11.3](#113-option-sets), [11.4](#114-the-generator)).
19. Require coverage: `jit on`, and traces in at least a quarter of the loops. A run that compiled nothing proved nothing (P27). Traces are not enough for a fold: count the rule's own IR signature, keyed on its operands as well as its opcode and mode, because two rules can share one (P56, [11.5a](#115a-the-idx-part-t_idx-t_cnt33-t_idx2-phiidx)). A rule that removes an instruction leaves an absence: count it only where its precondition is present, and refuse the option sets where no rule runs (P61, [11.5c](#115c-the-nc-part-t_numconv-ncir)). An IR reader that exits 0 with the JIT off must print a line that only a JIT run prints, and its runner must require that line (ncir's summary).
20. Test chained idioms and boundary values the lexer can actually spell, not single operators (P16, P47, S7). For a speed change, also time the loops where its speculation fails, with values that are legal under U1-B ([11.12](#1112-measuring-speed)).

**Proving**

21. Run every new check against a case that must fail it before you trust a pass ([11.8](#118-fail-first)).
22. Give every fix site a one-line sabotage arm in a negctl script, caught at a named check ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)).
23. When a reading surprises you, suspect the instrument first ([11.9](#119-instrument-first)).
24. Prove a crash fix only by a black-box census A/B plus build assertions; never write a test that forces a crash ([11.10](#1110-crash-fixes)).
25. Runs longer than a minute belong to the main session; never edit a script a running chain executes ([11.11](#1111-long-runs-and-results-directories), P17).

**Docs and upkeep**

26. Read IR with `-jdump=+rsT`, remembering its blind spots: KINT64 constants and the exact mark (P3, P30, [8.7](#87-reading-a--jdump)).
27. Archive evidence under `bench/runs/<date>-<name>/` with md5s, not binaries (P20). Mark every claim [M], [S] or [I].
28. Update this guide, the roadmap row and the patch list in [1](#1-scope-and-the-pin) in the same commit as the code.

## 1. Scope and the pin

### 1.1 What the fork is

- **Upstream.** LuaJIT 2.1 at `1ee778a4`, cloned by `make -C prototype/watchdog luajit-src` into `prototype/watchdog/luajit`, which is not tracked in this repo (build-native.sh PREREQUISITES) [S]. That checkout is the **pristine tree**: tracked sources only, CRLF on Windows (`core.autocrlf=true`). It also holds ignored build products from 2026-09-01, which are harmless because nothing builds there [M]. A pinned build does not copy it: it exports the pin's tree from its `.git` and checks the export against the pin ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) [S][M].
- **The pin is one file**, `native/luajit/upstream.commit`: the full 40-digit hash, then an LF or nothing, and nothing else (since 2026-10-20) [S]. Every reader reads it [S]:
  - `prototype/watchdog/Makefile` checks the pristine tree out at it. Its `luajit/.stamp` depends on the file, so a bumped pin is checked out by the next `make -C prototype/watchdog luajit-src`, with a fetch only when the commit is not there yet [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin/impl/mk-stamp-3`, a copy of the layout]. `prototype/watchdog/build.bat`, the older Windows path, reads the file's first line [M: `pin/impl/bat-reader-2`], and quotes the file's path in its four messages: unquoted, a repository path holding `)` made cmd fail every run, a good pin included (rc 255), and one holding `&` cut the message and ran a stray command [M: `pin2/impl/v8/bat-runs.txt`, 40 runs].
  - `serializer/Makefile` stamps all 40 digits into its test host's fingerprint, as before [M: `pin/impl/ser-banner-2`, the banner equal to HEAD's].
  - build-native.sh's preflight refuses a file that is not exactly 40 lowercase hex digits and an optional LF. A pinned build (the default) then needs git, refuses a checkout whose HEAD is not the pin's 40 digits or whose `.git` lacks the pin's commit, and starts from exactly the tree the pin's 40 digits name. **What is verified**, link by link, with every git call made with replace refs off and no line-ending conversion: the pin's commit object hashes to the pin; the root tree is the one that object names on its `tree` line (and `rev-parse <pin>^{tree}` agrees); every tree object under it hashes to its name; and step 1's export, written by `git archive` of that tree into a fresh directory, holds exactly the files the tree lists, each of whose bytes hashes to the blob id the tree gives it. So the export, from which the build copy is made before the twelve patch scripts run, is the pin's committed tree byte for byte, up to a SHA-1 collision, whatever git's configuration, attributes or object store hold; anything that would make it otherwise is a BUILD FAIL ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) [S][M]. Not compared: the exported files' modes and timestamps [S]. The checkout's working tree is never compiled; a tracked file that differs there is named in a WARNING and not built [S][M]. **What is compiled** is that tree with our twelve changes ([1.2](#12-our-twelve-changes-to-luajit)), with build-native.sh's flags: before anything reads them it unsets, whatever the case of the name, the variables of its list, those that make, gcc and ld were measured, or read in their manuals and LuaJIT's Makefiles, to take from the environment and change what is compiled or linked, and prints the names it dropped (THE BUILD ENVIRONMENT, [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) [S][M]. The list is what was found, not every name make reads: round 4's missed `MAKEOVERRIDES`, which mingw32-make 4.4.1 takes (its review, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/verify/runs/env2-new/`), and a name that is not a shell identifier cannot be unset at all ([open question 15](#open-questions)) [M][I]. **The toolchain is not pinned**: the release DLL is built with WinLibs' MinGW-w64 gcc 16.2.0 (binutils 2.47, GNU make 4.4.1) and the release .so with AlmaLinux 8's gcc 8.5.0 (binutils 2.30, GNU make 4.2.1), as the libraries' own `GCC: (...)` strings say [M: `build/native/dist`'s pair, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/impl/exp/release-gcc-strings.txt`, and `gcc --version` on both hosts, `exp/win-host.txt`, `exp/alma/probe.txt`, 2026-10-20]; another compiler builds and gives other objects [I].
- **The threat model of these checks** (since round 4 of the pin work, 2026-10-20): they guard against accidents and plausible configurations, namely a wrong or stale checkout or build copy, local edits, git configuration and attributes, the environment variables of THE BUILD ENVIRONMENT's list that a developer may have set, and directories named through symlinks, junctions, `..` or another letter case. Since round 5 every directory the script is given is taken at its physical path before its guards check it and its tools use it, and on Windows a path with a name ending in a dot or a space is refused ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) [S][M]. Within that model the build removes and overwrites only its own products: it removes only the three directories it remakes in `$OCLJ_BUILD` and files of its own fixed names, and writes files of its own fixed names only, in `$OCLJ_BUILD`, in `$OCLJ_OUT` (the library) and, for a pinned additive build, in `$OCLJ_BUILD/dist` (its `rm` and write targets, [S]); and its guards refuse a checkout inside a directory it remakes, an `$OCLJ_BUILD` inside the checkout or inside a repository (`OCLJ_REPO`'s, or the one the script lives in) outside its `build/`, and an `$OCLJ_OUT` in `$OCLJ_BUILD/dist` or in either repository's `build/native/dist`. So it removes or overwrites no file of the checkout or of a repository, and only a pinned additive build replaces the library in a `dist` [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin5/final/final-check2.sh`, run into `pin5/final/run-impl/`: its U2, U2L, U3, T1, T2, T4, G3 and H2 lines, on Windows, and its ALMA lines in AlmaLinux 8]. Round 4 made this claim and its review measured four ways past it on Windows, three of them through the spelling Windows' own programs read ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)). Not covered: a file of one of the build's names that is not the build's, in an `$OCLJ_BUILD` outside the repositories, is overwritten (`OCLJ_BUILD` is documented as a scratch directory); and what [open question 15](#open-questions) lists, writes through a symlink planted in `$OCLJ_BUILD` among them. Deliberate tampering by someone who can edit build-native.sh itself is out of scope, and a review reports such findings as low [S].
  - gradle's `verifyModAssets` refuses a native whose fingerprint's commit field is not the pin's first 8 (an unpinned build stamps `unpinned`) ([7.7](#77-the-linux-path)) [S][M].
- **`OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED=1`** builds the checkout's working tree instead, whatever its commit and edits, for an experiment: it warns at the check and again after the `OK` line, stamps the literal `unpinned` as the fingerprint's commit, and does not collect the native into `$OCLJ_BUILD/dist`. gradle refuses such a native, and its blobs and a pinned native's do not restore on each other (`eris_lj.c` compares the whole fingerprint) [S][M: `claude-tmp/oclj/pin2/impl/v2/new-step1.log`, `pin2/impl/v5/`]. It needs no git. An empty value means unset; any other value is refused [M].
- **Before 2026-10-20 nothing compared the checkout with the pin.** The hash was written out in three build files, and build-native.sh stamped whatever commit the checkout was at: HEAD's script built a complete additive DLL from upstream `8c23d6aa` (the pin's parent, which lacks one fold rule in `lj_opt_mem.c`), stamped `8c23d6aa|LuaJIT 2.1.ROLLING|...`, and HEAD's gradle accepted it [M: `pin/impl/win-1/head-parent`, `pin/impl/gruns-head-1`]. The pin round's first fix compared only commit names, and two paths still compiled another commit's code under the stamp `1ee778a4`, which gradle accepted: a build copy left by an unpinned build of `8c23d6aa` (the copy was made once and kept, P44), and a checkout at the pin with the parent's `lj_opt_mem.c` left uncommitted. Both built the parent's `lj_opt_mem.o` (`05d34fc1`; the pin's is `42bd49f9`) [M: the pin round's review, `pin/verify/holes/`; again with HEAD's script, `pin2/impl/v2/head-step2.log`, `pin2/impl/v3/head-dirty.log`], and both build the pin's object now [M: `pin2/impl/v2/new-step2.log`, `pin2/impl/v3/new-dirty.log`]. The build-copy round's review then shipped the parent's `lj_opt_mem.o` under `1ee778a4` through the export itself, with gradle accepting it: a replace ref of the pin by its parent (git archive, ls-tree and cat-file all honour one), a smudge filter set through `.git/info/attributes` and the repo's config, or through the environment alone (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`), which `git archive` applied while the WARNING named the working tree's correct file as the one not built, and a forged loose blob or subtree object [M: `pin2/verify/runs/w-replace.log`, `pin2/verify/gruns/replace.log`, `pin2/verify/runs/w-filter.log`, `pin2/verify/exp/probe-envcfg.log`, `probe-forgeblob.log`, `probe-forgesub.log`]. With the checks above, the replace ref builds the pin (`42bd49f9`, a WARNING naming `lj_opt_mem.c`), and the filter, the environment's filter, the forged blob and the forged subtree are each a BUILD FAIL naming the file or the tree, on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8 [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin3/impl/fc4e-w2/`, `fc4e-alma-w2/`]. To move the pin, follow [8.2a](#82a-bumping-the-pin).
- **Our LuaJIT is a fork.** Since 2026-10-13 it may take Lua 5.3 additions (the decision is recorded in roadmap row 146; rounds 2 and 3 applied it) [S].
- **The configuration** is `native/luajit/oclj_features.h`, one header that the build copy's `lj_arch.h` includes [S]:
  - `LUAJIT_ENABLE_LUA52COMPAT` (OC's platform is Lua 5.2 source);
  - `LUAJIT_ENABLE_CHECKHOOK` (a compiled loop checks the hook mask, so the watchdog can stop it; see [docs/watchdog.md](watchdog.md));
  - `LUAJIT_DISABLE_FFI` (with FFI the lexer mints cdata from `1LL`);
  - an `#error` for every other option that changes a structure, the bytecode, the unwinder or what a gate measures, including `LUAJIT_NUMMODE` (so no DUALNUM) and `LUAJIT_NO_UNWIND`;
  - feature words for what we added: `OCLJ_LIB_STRPACK 1`, `OCLJ_OPS_BIT64 1`.
- **The preprocessor's verdict** (`gcc -E -dM lj_arch.h` on the build copy) [M]: `LJ_HASFFI 0`, `LJ_DUALNUM 0`, `LJ_GC64 1`, `LJ_FR2 1`, `LJ_HASJIT 1`, `LJ_52 1`, and on Windows `LJ_ABI_WIN 1` and `LJ_UNWIND_EXT 1`. What every build checks [S]: the `-dM` gate (build-native.sh ~:1586) asserts `LJ_52`, `LJ_HASFFI`, `LJ_HASJIT` and `LJ_GC64` (and the OCLJ_* words); FR2 is held by stage 4's `fr2` token, which luajit.exe's dump flags must agree with; DUALNUM by `oclj_features.h`'s `#error` on `LUAJIT_NUMMODE` (the word's `dn0` is the serializer's compile-time view, not compared with luajit.exe); `LJ_ABI_WIN` and `LJ_UNWIND_EXT` are not gated.
- **Platforms.** x64 only: build-native.sh refuses any other machine, because the fast path is patched in `vm_x64.dasc` only [S]. Windows (MinGW-w64, PE/COFF, `buildvm -m peobj`) and Linux (ELF, `buildvm -m elfasm`) are both built and gated. The Linux library that ships is built in AlmaLinux 8, on glibc 2.28, because a .so can need symbol versions as new as the glibc it was linked against, and the release may need nothing newer than 2.28 ([7.7](#77-the-linux-path)) [S]. macOS and aarch64 are parked as unsupported (roadmap) [S].
- **What reads LuaJIT internals besides LuaJIT.** `serializer/eris_lj.c`, `native/lj52shim.c`, and the C tests `penalty_test.c`, `race_test.c` and `wd_test.c` compile against the build copy's headers (`oclj_features.h` header) [S]. A change to a LuaJIT structure reaches them. build-native.sh stage 1b compares the archive's `GG_State` size (6376) with the one our headers describe [M] (logs).

### 1.2 Our twelve changes to LuaJIT

Each change is a script that patches one file of the build copy. build-native.sh re-copies the pristine file (from the pin's export, [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) first, asserts the change is absent, runs the script, and asserts the script's marker is present the right number of times before make [S] ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)).

| # | build-copy file | script (`native/luajit/`) | what it changes | commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `lj_func.c` | `patch-penalty-scrub.sh` | `lj_func_freeproto` scrubs a dying prototype's loop heads from the trace-abort penalty cache, which is keyed by bytecode address | `83aeabd` |
| 2 | `lj_err.c` | `patch-fastfunc-errmem-top.sh` | `lj_err_mem` and `lj_err_err` never push their message below the current frame | `a4994e6` |
| 3 | `lj_ffrecord.c` | `patch-strfmt-xint-noffi.sh` | `recff_format` keeps `%x %X %o %u`, and `%d` with a flag or width, compiled without FFI | `5d782d7` |
| 4 | `lj_arch.h` | `patch-features-include.sh` | includes `oclj_features.h` right after `lua.h` | `5d782d7` |
| 5 | `lib_string.c` | `patch-strpack-register.sh` | registers `string.pack`, `string.packsize`, `string.unpack`; includes `oclj_strpack.c` | `c9ae4f9` |
| 6 | `lj_parse.c` | `patch-bitop-parse.sh` | `foldbitop` and the unary `~` fold use 5.3.6's rules (`oclj_bitop.h`) | `2dd5f05` |
| 7 | `vm_x64.dasc` | `patch-bitop-vm.sh` | `BC_BNOT` and `ins_bitop` compute on int64 and return only exact results; new label `->vmeta_bitop_pc` | `2dd5f05` |
| 8 | `lj_meta.c` | `patch-bitop-meta.sh` | `lj_meta_bitop`'s no-FFI tail calls `oclj_meta_bitop`; includes `oclj_bitop_meta.c` | `2dd5f05` |
| 9 | `lj_record.c` | `patch-bitop-record.sh` | the seven bit cases of `lj_record_ins` become one call of `oclj_rec_bitop`; includes `oclj_bitop.h` (after `lj_prng.h`) and `oclj_bitop_rec.c` (after the `emitir` macros); since 2026-10-20 (hunks 4 and 5, the select fix) `rec_varg` bounds select's index: `OCLJ_SELECT_KMAX` (2^27) defined above it, a record-time abort for a k above it, and a guard `1 <= k <= 2^27` before the 32-bit shift on the path beyond the varargs (P54) | `2dd5f05`; hunks 4-5 the select fix's |
| 10 | `lj_opt_fold.c` | `patch-bitop-fold.sh` | seven int64 folds compiled without FFI; `narrow_convert` leaves int64 alone; the exact-mark and width rules and fix1 in `simplify_conv_i64_num`, and since 2026-10-21 FIX-NUMCONV's rule there (hunk 7, a line of its own after the width rule, calling `oclj_isnumconv`); the table-index fold in `simplify_conv_int_num` (hunk 11, 2026-10-20), directly above which it includes `oclj_bitop_fold.c`; and since 2026-10-23 a type test in `lj_opt_cse`, so an int64 instruction never commons with one of another type (hunk 12, the CSE-twin fix, P63) | `2dd5f05`; hunk 11 the CRC32 follow-up's; hunk 7's third rule FIX-NUMCONV's; hunk 12 the CSE-twin fix's |
| 11 | `lj_bcdump.h` | `patch-bitop-bcdump.sh` | `BCDUMP_VERSION` becomes 0x80 | `2dd5f05` |
| 12 | `lib_jit.c` | `patch-bitop-jitlib.sh` | `jit.util.funcbc` and `tracesnap` return unsigned numbers, so LuaJIT's own `jit/*.lua` decode exactly under 64-bit `>>` | `2dd5f05` |

[S] native/build-native.sh header and each script's header; commits from `git log --diff-filter=A`.

### 1.3 Our files in the build copy

build-native.sh copies these next to LuaJIT's sources, compares each with `cmp`, and refuses a pristine tree that already holds one [S].

| file | included by | what it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `oclj_features.h` | `lj_arch.h` (change 4), so every LuaJIT object, buildvm, the Makefile's DynASM flag probe, and our C | the feature set and its `#error`s |
| `oclj_num53.h` | `oclj_strpack.c`, `oclj_bitop.h` | Lua 5.3.6's readers: `luaO_str2num` for strings, `luaV_tointeger` mode 0 for floats (`oclj_flt2int`) |
| `oclj_strpack.c` | `lib_string.c` (change 5) | 5.3.6's pack/unpack, ported |
| `oclj_bitop.h` | `lj_parse.c`, `lj_record.c` and `lj_opt_fold.c` (each by its patch script), and `oclj_bitop_meta.c` | the operator rules with no Lua state: `oclj_bitop64`, `oclj_bit2num`, the shift rules, `OCLJ_IRCONV_EXACT` |
| `oclj_bitop_meta.c` | `lj_meta.c`, directly above `lj_meta_bitop` | the slow path: `oclj_meta_bitop`, its three errors |
| `oclj_bitop_rec.c` | `lj_record.c`, after the `emitir` macros | the recorder: `oclj_rec_bitop` |
| `oclj_bitop_fold.c` | `lj_opt_fold.c`, directly above `simplify_conv_int_num` (since 2026-10-20; above `simplify_conv_i64_num` before) | `oclj_bitwidth`, the bound both of its callers read: the table-index fold's 32 and the width rule's 54; and since 2026-10-21 `oclj_isnumconv`, FIX-NUMCONV's exactness predicate, which `simplify_conv_i64_num` reads ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)) (no LJFOLD lines: P14) |

### 1.4 Out of scope

- Sandbox and security analysis: not here.
- The C API shim, the serializer's protocol and the watchdog's design: `native/lj52shim.c`, [serializer/README.md](../serializer/README.md), [docs/watchdog.md](watchdog.md).
- The bitwise metamethods and `//` (roadmap row 210) are a future round. The CRC32 follow-up (row 209) shipped its first change, the table-index fold, and stays open; this guide records what it did and what remains ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)).

## 2. The pipeline

One operator, from source text to machine code and back again, with round 3's bitwise operators as the running example. Our code is cited by path and function name. Every [M] in this section comes from a single-process run of the built `build/native/luajit-windows-x86_64/src/luajit.exe`, run from that directory so that `jit.*` loads. For the probes behind `guide1`, `side1` and `side2`, compiled code, `-joff` and PUC 5.3.6 gave the same output; for `exit1` and `abort2`, compiled code and `-joff` did (PUC computes their integers exactly and raises nothing) [M]. `guide1.lua` is printed in [8.7](#87-reading-a--jdump) and `crcprobe.lua` in [8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains); the other four are kept here, line for line, so a review can re-run them and the cited line numbers hold.

`side1.lua`, a branch that switches at i = 500 ([4.6](#46-closing-a-trace-and-linking-it), [4.8](#48-side-traces)); prints 2000:

```lua
local s = 0
for i = 1, 1000 do
  local x = i * 3
  if i > 500 then x = x & 0xFF else x = x | 1 end
  s = s ~ x
end
print(s)
```

`side2.lua` ([4.6](#46-closing-a-trace-and-linking-it), [4.8](#48-side-traces)); prints 1002000:

```lua
-- The shift count is a variable from a table: 3 for i <= 500, 64 after.
local cnt = {}
for i = 1, 1000 do cnt[i] = i > 500 and 64 or 3 end
local s = 0
for i = 1, 1000 do
  s = s + (i << cnt[i])
end
print(s)
```

`exit1.lua` ([4.7](#47-exits)):

```lua
-- x is positive until i = 80; at i = 81, x = -1 and x >> 1 has 63 significant bits.
local s = 0
local ok, err = pcall(function()
  for i = 1, 100 do
    local x = 80 - i
    s = s + (x >> 1)
  end
end)
print(s, ok, err)
```

`abort2.lua` ([2.6](#26-the-recorder)):

```lua
-- The result is inexact in the iteration being recorded: the recorder aborts,
-- then the interpreter raises.
local s = 0
print(pcall(function()
  for i = 1, 100 do
    local x = 1 - i
    s = s + (x >> 1)
  end
end))
```

### 2.1 The map

| stage | LuaJIT entry (1ee778a4) | takes | gives | round 3's hook |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| lex | `lj_lex.c` `lex_scan` (~:297; the operator cases ~:365-379) | characters | `TK_shl` `TK_shr` `TK_sar`, `'&' '\|' '~'`; numerals as doubles | none [S] |
| parse | `lj_parse.c` `expr_binop` (~:2340) → `bcemit_binop` (~:950) → `foldbitop` (~:818) or `bcemit_arith` (~:838); `expr_unop` (~:2319) → `bcemit_unop` (~:996) | `ExpDesc` operands | a folded constant, or `BC_BAND+(opr-OPR_BAND)` | `patch-bitop-parse.sh` [S] |
| dump/load | `lj_bcwrite.c` `bcwrite_header` (~:388), `lj_bcread.c` `bcread_header` (~:394) | a prototype | version byte, flags | `patch-bitop-bcdump.sh`: `BCDUMP_VERSION` 0x80 [S] |
| interpret | `vm_x64.dasc` `BC_BNOT` (~:3522), `ins_bitop` (~:3544) | the instruction word | a slot value, or a jump to `->vmeta_*` | `patch-bitop-vm.sh` [S] |
| slow path | `lj_meta.c` `lj_meta_bitop` (~:243) | `L, ra, rb, rc, op` | a result, or an error | `patch-bitop-meta.sh` → `oclj_meta_bitop` [S] |
| hot count | `vm_x64.dasc` `hotloop` macro (~:332) → `->vm_hotloop` (~:2323) → `lj_trace.c` `lj_trace_hot` (~:781) | a back-edge | recording begins | none (`patch-penalty-scrub.sh` touches the penalty cache) [S] |
| record | `lj_record.c` `lj_record_ins` (~:2227) | one bytecode plus the runtime operand values | IR through `emitir` | `patch-bitop-record.sh` → `oclj_rec_bitop`; and `rec_varg`'s bound on select's index (P54) [S] |
| fold/CSE | `lj_opt_fold.c` `lj_opt_fold` (~:2524), `lj_opt_cse` (~:2601) | one instruction (`fins`) | a ref | `patch-bitop-fold.sh` ([3](#3-ir-and-the-fold-engine); `lj_opt_cse`'s type test, hunk 12, [3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)) [S] |
| loop | `lj_opt_loop.c` `loop_unroll` (~:265) | the pre-roll | `LOOP`, a copy of the body, PHIs | none [S] |
| assemble | `lj_asm.c` `lj_asm_trace` (~:2471); `lj_asm_x86.h` `asm_conv` (~:857), `asm_guardcc` (~:75) | IR and snapshots | x64 code and exit stubs | none [S] |
| exit | `->vm_exit_handler` (~:2449) → `lj_trace.c` `lj_trace_exit` (~:886) → `lj_snap.c` `lj_snap_restore` (~:940) → `->vm_exit_interp` (~:2499) | the machine state | the interpreter state at the snapshot's pc | none [S] |
| side trace | `lj_trace.c` `trace_hotside` (~:799) → `lj_record.c` `lj_record_setup` (~:2863) → `lj_snap.c` `lj_snap_replay` (~:508) | the parent's snapshot | a new trace | none [S] |

### 2.2 Lexer and parser

- **Lexing.** `lex_scan` returns `TK_shl` for `<<`, `TK_shr` for `>>` and `TK_sar` for `~>>`. `&`, `|` and `~` are one-character tokens, and `~=` is `TK_ne` [S]. The lexer turns a numeral into a double before any operator sees it. A hex literal with more than 53 significant bits is therefore already rounded (U9): `0x00ff00ff00ff00ff & 0xff` is 0 here, and `0x7fffffffffffffff | 0` raises `number has no integer representation`, because that literal is 2^63 [M] (P47).
- **Precedence.** `token2binop` (~:2269) and `priority[]` (~:2302) rank BOR 4 < BXOR 5 < BAND 6 < the shifts 7 < CONCAT 9 < ADD 10 < unary 12. These are Lua 5.3's ranks [S].
- **Emission.** `expr_binop` parses the left side through `expr_unop`. While the next operator's left priority beats the limit, it calls `bcemit_binop_left`, parses the right side at the operator's right priority, and calls `bcemit_binop` [S].
  - For a bit operator, `bcemit_binop_left` leaves a numeric-constant left operand undischarged (`if (!expr_isnumk_nojump(e)) expr_toanyreg(fs, e)`), so the operator can still fold [S].
  - `bcemit_binop` sends OPR_BAND..OPR_BSAR to `foldbitop`. If that fails, it sets `PROTO_BITOP` (~:956) and calls `bcemit_arith`, which puts both operands in registers and emits `BC_BAND+(opr-OPR_BAND)` [S].
  - The unary path is `expr_unop` → `bcemit_unop`. Its `~` fold is at ~:1050-1054 and its `PROTO_BITOP` at ~:1056 [S].
- **Round 3's change** (`patch-bitop-parse.sh`). `foldbitop` and the `~` fold read each constant with `oclj_flt2int` (5.3.6's `luaV_tointeger`, mode 0), compute with `oclj_bitop64` and accept the result only through `oclj_bit2num`. They store it with `setnumV` where upstream used `setintV`. `expr_bitV` (~:89), BitOp's 32-bit reader, is gone. `oclj_bitop64` is also what the C slow path and the recorder call, so the three cannot disagree [S].
- **A second route to the same emitter: compound assignment.** The pin's backported compound assignments `&= |= ~= <<= >>= ~>>=` (doc/extensions.html ~:425) go through `parse_compound` (lj_parse.c ~:2410). It puts the variable in a register and calls `bcemit_binop`, so they emit the same `BC_BAND..BC_BSAR` (never folded, since one operand is a register) and set `PROTO_BITOP`; round 3's semantics apply unchanged [S]. As a statement, `x ~= y` is xor-assignment (`OPR_NE` becomes `OPR_BXOR`). `&&` and `||` lex as the logical `TK_and_` and `TK_or_` (lj_lex.c ~:393-398), never as two bit operators [S]. Measured: `local x = 1; x <<= 40; local y = 5; y ~= 3; local z = 6; z &= 3; print(x, y, z)` prints `1099511627776 6 2`, `-bl` lists `BSHL 0 0 1` and `BXOR 1 1 2`, and `local x = -1; x >>= 1` raises the 53-bit error [M]. PUC 5.3 has none of these forms, so neither the twin nor the generator nor the fuzzer covers them (no file in `test/native/bitop/` uses one); a round that changes the parser tests them by hand (Open questions).
- **When the parser declines to fold** [S]:
  1. an operand is not a numeric constant without pending jumps (`expr_isnumk_nojump`), for example a local;
  2. an operand has no integer representation (`1.5 | 0`);
  3. the result has more than 53 significant bits (`-1 >> 1` is 2^63 - 1).

  Cases 2 and 3 leave the operator to run time, which raises, the way 5.3.6's `lcode.c` `validop` leaves invalid operands to run time (`lcode.c:960-971`) [S]. `luajit -bl` shows all three [M]:

```
local r1 = 1 << 40   ->  0002  KNUM     2   0      ; 1099511627776
local r2 = ~0        ->  0003  KSHORT   3  -1
local r3 = 1.5 | 0   ->  0004  KSHORT   4   0
                         0005  KNUM     5   1      ; 1.5
                         0006  BOR      4   5   4
local r4 = -1 >> 1   ->  0007  KSHORT   5   1
                         0008  KSHORT   6  -1
                         0009  BSHR     5   6   5
local r5 = a & b     ->  0010  BAND     6   0   1
local r6 = ~a        ->  0011  BNOT     7   0
local r7 = a ~>> 3   ->  0012  KSHORT   8   3
                         0013  BSAR     8   0   8
```

- **`PROTO_BITOP` collides with the closure counter** (an upstream bug, roadmap row 212, P32). `PROTO_BITOP` is 0x80 (`lj_obj.h` ~:404), inside the 3-bit closure counter at 0x20..0xe0 (~:409-411), so `flags >= PROTO_CLC_POLY` (`lj_record.c` ~:802, and `rec_upvalue` ~:1795) is true in every function that uses a bit operator [S]. In such a function an immutable upvalue is never constified: a loop reading upvalue `K = 7` compiles `ADD … +7`, and the same loop in a function that also contains `n & 1` compiles `UREFO` plus `ULOAD` [M] (an open upvalue; round 3's design work saw `UREFC` for a closed one, roadmap row 212). Speed only; not fixed in our fork [S].

### 2.3 Bytecode and the dump version

- **Formats.** `lj_bc.h` `BCDEF` (~:189-196) defines `BNOT` as dst/___/var (AD: A = destination, D = source slot) and `BAND`..`BSAR` as dst/var/var (ABC). The metamethod column is `___` [S].
- **No constant forms.** Unlike `ADDVN` and `ADDNV`, both operands of a bit opcode are registers, so a literal operand is first put in a register by `KSHORT` or `KNUM` (`0008 KSHORT 6 -1; 0009 BSHR 5 6 5` above) [S][M].
- **ORDER OPR / ORDER BIT.** `OPR_BAND..OPR_BSAR` and `BC_BAND..BC_BSAR` run in the same order. The parser relies on that (`opr-OPR_BAND+BC_BAND`), and so does `oclj_bitop64`, which switches on `BC_*` numbers [S]. The IR order differs: `IR_BSWAP` sits between `IR_BNOT` and `IR_BAND` (`lj_ir.h` ~:56-66), so `oclj_rec_bitop` maps bytecode to IR by arithmetic only for BAND..BXOR and names the IR op of BNOT and of each shift explicitly [S] (P42).
- **Same bytecode, new meaning: the dump version.** `bcwrite_header` writes `BCDUMP_VERSION` (~:397) and sets `BCDUMP_F_BITOP` only when `pt->flags` has `PROTO_BITOP` (~:401). `bcread_header` refuses any other version (~:400) [S]. Measured [M]:

| case | header bytes |
|---|---|
| dump of a function containing `a & 1` | `27 76 74 128` and flags 0x18 (FR2 \| BITOP) |
| dump of a function containing `1 << 3` | flags 0x08: the expression folded away, no bit opcode is left, so `BCDUMP_F_BITOP` cannot carry the version |
| the version byte set back to 2 | `load` fails with `cannot load incompatible bytecode` |

  The blob fingerprint gains `bit64` from `oclj_features.h`'s `OCLJ_OPS_BIT64`, and `serializer/eris_lj.c` refuses to compile if that word is on while `BCDUMP_VERSION` is below 0x80 [S].

### 2.4 The interpreter

- **Decode.** `ins_NEXT` loads the instruction word, sets RA = A and OP = the opcode, leaves RC (= RD) holding the upper 16 bits, and jumps through `[DISPATCH+OP*8]` (`vm_x64.dasc` ~:212) [S]. `ins_bitop` swaps registers: "RB = dst, RC = src1, RA = src2". The register table and the macros are in [6](#6-dynasm-in-practice) [S].
- **The fast path.** `patch-bitop-vm.sh` changes only the non-DUALNUM half of `BC_BNOT` and of `ins_bitop` [S]:

| step | instructions | goes to C when |
|---|---|---|
| type | `checknumtp [BASE+R*8]` | an operand is not a number |
| operand → int64 | `cvttsd2si`, `cvtsi2sd`, `ucomisd`, `jne`, `jp` | the double is not integral; or it is outside [-2^63, 2^63), where `cvttsd2si` gives INT64_MIN, whose round trip differs; or it is NaN (unordered, so PF is set) |
| shift count | `cmp RA, 63`, `ja` (unsigned) | the count is < 0 or > 63; 5.3.6's `luaV_shiftl` runs in C |
| operation | 64-bit `not`/`and`/`or`/`xor`/`shl`/`shr`/`sar`, count in `cl` | — |
| result | `cvtsi2sd`, `cvttsd2si` back, `cmp`, `jne` | the result has more than 53 significant bits, or rounded to 2^63 |
| store | `movsd [BASE+dst*8]`, `ins_next` | — |

  [S] The instruction-level listing and which registers survive each exit are in [6.7](#67-round-3s-checks-instruction-by-instruction).
- **Into C.** `->vmeta_bitop` (~:1105) calls `lj_meta_bitop(L, ra, rb, rc, op)`: on Win64 the first four arguments go in rcx, rdx, r8 and r9 and `op` in the stack slot `ARG5`; on SysV all five go in registers. It stores `L->base` and `SAVE_PC` (which gives the error its line), calls, reloads BASE and continues at `->cont_nop` [S]. For `BNOT`, `->vmeta_bnot` passes the operand as both `rb` and `rc` [S].
- **The slow path.** `lj_meta_bitop`'s no-FFI tail calls `oclj_meta_bitop` (`native/luajit/oclj_bitop_meta.c`). `ob_toint` converts each operand, reading a string through 5.3.6's readers in `oclj_num53.h`. The result is `oclj_bitop64` passed through `oclj_bit2num`; if either step fails, one of three errors is raised [S]:

| case | raiser | message [M] |
|---|---|---|
| result has more than 53 bits | `ob_err_inexact` | `result of '>>' has more than 53 significant bits (mask each operand to 32 bits first)` |
| numbers or numeric strings, one with no integer value | `ob_err_noint` | `number (local 'x') has no integer representation` |
| any other type | `ob_err_type` | `attempt to perform bitwise operation on local 't' (a table value)` |

  All three raise through `ob_errv`, a copy of `lj_err.c` `err_msgv` (~:931): it restores `L->base` from `jit_base` if set, sets `L->top = curr_topL`, then calls `lj_strfmt_pushvf`, `lj_debug_addloc` and `lj_err_run` (~:887). It is a copy so that `lj_err.c` (already patched by change 2) and `lj_errmsg.h` stay unchanged [S]. Measured: `"3" & 1` is 1, `1 << 64` is 0, `256 >> -4` is 4096 [M].

### 2.5 Hot counting and trace start

- **The counter.** `BC_FORL`, `BC_ITERL`, `BC_ITERN` and `BC_LOOP` run the `hotloop` macro (vm_x64.dasc ~:4558, ~:4709, ~:4308, ~:4739). It computes `(PC>>1) & HOTCOUNT_PCMASK`, a byte offset that selects one of 64 16-bit counters (`HOTCOUNT_SIZE`, `lj_dispatch.h` ~:75; `hotcount_get` ~:126 indexes `(pc>>2) & 63`), and subtracts `HOTCOUNT_LOOP` = 2. On a borrow (`jb`) it jumps to `->vm_hotloop`. Function headers subtract `HOTCOUNT_CALL` = 1 [S].
- **The start value is fixed.** `lj_dispatch_init_hotcount` (~:86) starts every counter at `hotloop*HOTCOUNT_LOOP - 1` = 111 [S]. `->vm_hotloop` calls `lj_trace_hot`, which resets the counter to `hotloop*2` and, if the JIT is idle, sets `LJ_TRACE_START` and calls `lj_trace_ins` → `trace_state` → `trace_start` → `lj_record_setup` [S].
- **Measured** (a `jit.attach` "trace" counter, one loop, fresh state): recording begins after back-edge 56 by default, in 6 of 6 processes; after 1 with `-Ohotloop=1`; after 10 with `-Ohotloop=10` [M].
- **Why "compiles after N iterations" is only approximate** (P6) [S]:
  - counters are hashed by pc, so loops and functions whose pcs collide share one, and calls decrement it too;
  - an abort sets the counter to a penalty value with random bits (below), and that counter is the shared slot of the aborted loop's pc: `penalty_pc` ends with `hotcount_set(J2GG(J), pc+1, val)` (lj_trace.c ~:413), so another loop whose pc hashes to the slot inherits the penalty, val/2 iterations of it, 36 after a first abort and up to 30,000 (P59) [S];
  - up-recursion sets a random count (`lj_record.c` ~:1896);
  - the PRNG is seeded per state by `lj_prng_seed_secure` (`lj_state.c` ~:257).

  The tests therefore use option sets and coverage gates, never exact counts ([11.3](#113-option-sets)).
- **Penalties and blacklisting.** `trace_abort` (~:585) calls `penalty_pc` (~:392) for an aborted root trace. `penalty_pc` keys a 64-slot cache by bytecode address. A new slot gets `PENALTY_MIN` = 72; a repeat gets twice the old value plus 4 random bits. Above `PENALTY_MAX` = 60000, `blacklist_pc` (~:380) patches `FORL` to `IFORL`, and the loop is never counted again (`lj_jit.h` ~:309-312) [S].
  - A root trace that always aborts is therefore attempted exactly 11 times, since 72·2^10 > 60000 [S]. A loop aborting on a variable shift count of 64 printed 11 `-jv` aborts in each of three runs, then nothing; an FNEW-aborting loop did the same in 6 of 6 processes, its 11th abort coming between iterations 38,215 and 42,958 [M].
  - **`-jv` prints no line for the blacklisting itself**: `blacklist_pc` sends no vmevent [S][M]. A later `-- blacklisted at <file>:<line>` line is a different event: another recording that reached the patched `IFORL` aborted with `LJ_TRERR_BLACKL` (P39).
  - Change 1 makes `lj_func_freeproto` scrub a dying prototype's slots, because the cache is keyed by address and a reused address would inherit them [S].
- **The recorder runs before the interpreter.** While the JIT is not idle, the dispatch table routes every instruction through `lj_dispatch_ins` (~:411), which calls `lj_trace_ins(J, pc-1)` before the instruction executes [S]. So the recorder sees exactly the operand values the interpreter is about to use. That is what lets `oclj_rec_bitop` test whether this iteration's result is inexact [S].

### 2.6 The recorder

**How `lj_record_ins` sees an instruction** (~:2227) [S]:

- If `J->needsnap` is set, it first takes a snapshot (~:2283-2288; [4.3](#43-snapshots)).
- It decodes operands by `bcmode` (~:2312-2346). A `var` operand is copied into `rbv`/`rcv` (the runtime values, `copyTV`), and its TRef comes from `getslot`: `J->base[s]` if set, otherwise `sload` (~:201), which emits `SLOAD` typed by the runtime value, with `IRSLOAD_TYPECHECK` and a guard (`T` in a dump).
- For `BNOT`, mode B is none, so `rb` = 0 and `rc` = D.
- After the switch, a dst-mode op stores its result: `J->base[ra] = rc` (~:2774).

Round 3 replaces the seven cases (~:2511-2546) with one line, `rc = oclj_rec_bitop(J, op, rb, rc, rbv, rcv)`, and includes `oclj_bitop_rec.c` right after the `emitir` macros (~:43-46) [S].

**`oclj_rec_bitop`, step by step** (`native/luajit/oclj_bitop_rec.c`) [S]:

1. **The record-time check.** `oclj_rec_bitval` reads each runtime operand with `oclj_flt2int`, then `oclj_bitop64` computes and `oclj_bit2num` checks. Any failure calls `lj_trace_err(J, LJ_TRERR_BADTYPE)`. A string operand, an operand with no integer value, or an inexact result aborts here, before any IR is emitted.
2. **The operands**, through `oclj_rec_bitarg`:

   | operand TRef | IR emitted | guard |
   |---|---|---|
   | int-typed (`tref_isinteger`, I8..INT: a narrowed value such as the FORL index, or `string.byte`'s `u8 XLOAD`) | `CONV i64.int sext`; on x64 `simplify_conv_sext` (`lj_opt_fold.c` ~:1221) folds it back to the operand itself when SCEV proves the index ≥ 0, and always for a u8/u16 XLOAD (~:1229) | none |
   | constant | `lj_ir_kint64(k)` | none: checked at record time |
   | any other number `x` | `i = CONV i64.num x` (`IRCONV_ANY`); `back = CONV num.i64 i`, **marked**; `EQ.num back, x` | the `EQ`: an exit if `x` is not integral, is out of range, or is NaN |

3. **The shift count.** A constant count is decided now by 5.3.6's rule: `BSHL` or `BSHR` by a `KINT`, or `KINT64 0` when the count's absolute value is 64 or more. `~>>` is handled case by case (`BSAR` by 63 for a count of 64 or more, `BSHL` for a negative count). A variable count must be in [0, 63] at record time, or the trace aborts; it becomes `CONV int.num check` (unless it is already an int), then a guard `ULT cnt, 64`, then the i64 shift by `cnt`.
4. **The operation.** `IR_BNOT`, `IR_BAND+(op-BC_BAND)`, or the named shift, all typed `IRT_I64`.
5. **The result.** `d = CONV num.i64 res`, **marked**. Unless `res` is a constant, the guard follows at once: `back = CONV i64.num d` (`IRCONV_ANY`, **marked**: here the mark means "this is the guard's own check"), then `EQ.i64 back, res`. The function returns `d`, so the slot holds the double and snapshots name the double.

**What FOLD does with that IR** ([3.10](#310-the-two-rules)) [S]: `simplify_conv_i64_num` folds `(i64)(num)y` to `y` by the **exact-mark rule** (the inner CONV is marked and the outer one is not), the **width rule** (`oclj_bitwidth(y)` ≤ 54) or, since 2026-10-21, **FIX-NUMCONV's rule** (y is an operand's own unguarded `CONV i64.num` of a double, `oclj_isnumconv`). When the next operator converts `d`, it therefore gets `res` back: `back` commons with `d` (`cse_conv` ~:1287), and `EQ.num d, d` disappears through `comm_equal` (~:1978), whose comment says converted integers cannot be NaN. The guard's own check is marked, so the exact-mark rule never removes it; two rules do, each by a proof that reads no guard: the width rule when y's range lies within [-2^53, 2^53), and FIX-NUMCONV's when y is exactly a double whatever its range, as the result of an identity operation such as `x | 0` is ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)) [S][M]. The table-index fold (2026-10-20) removes a different check: the guarded `CONV int.num` of a result that is used as an int, a table key or a count ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)) [S]. Measured widths [M]:

| expression | bound | result guard |
|---|---|---|
| `i << 21` (i the FORL index) | 33 + 21 = 54 | none |
| `i << 22` | 55 | kept |
| `x >> 3` | 65 - 3 = 62 | kept |
| `… & 0xFFFF` | 17 | none |

The loop index scores 33, not 32, because `simplify_conv_sext` removed its sign extension and an int value scores 33 in `oclj_bitwidth` [M][S] (P41).

**Record-time aborts.** `lj_trace_err` (~:38) pushes the `TraceError` number and throws `LUA_ERRRUN`. `lj_vm_cpcall` in `lj_trace_ins` catches it, the state becomes `LJ_TRACE_ERR`, and `trace_abort` runs [S]. Under `-Ohotloop=1`, `abort2.lua` (printed in [2](#2-the-pipeline)), whose first recorded iteration (its second) is inexact, printed `[TRACE --- abort2.lua:5 -- bad argument type at abort2.lua:7]`, then the interpreter raised the 53-bit error [M]. A loop that is inexact in iteration 1 raises in the interpreter before any back-edge, so no trace starts and no abort line is printed [M].

- The inexact-result abort is defensive. Without it, the interpreter raises right after the recorder returns, `lj_err_throw` calls `lj_trace_abort` (`lj_err.c` ~:771, `lj_trace.h` ~:45), and the trace is aborted as `LJ_TRERR_RECERR` instead [S].
- `negctl-bitop.sh` measured the same raises, 26 starts and 26 aborts, with the check and without it. That is why INEXACT-RECABORT is not one of its arms [M] (its header; [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)).

**The recorder's other change: select's index** (the select fix, 2026-10-20; P54) [S]. `rec_varg` (lj_record.c ~:1989-2089) records `y = select(k, ...)` in a vararg function's own frame on one of three paths, chosen by the k it records: A, a constant k (a guard of the frame-size word `fr` against `frofs + 8*k`); B, a k within the varargs (their count from `fr`, `rec_idx_abc`'s unsigned bound on k-1, the load); C, a k beyond them (a guard `fr < 8*k + frofs`, no load, nil). Hunks 4 and 5 of `patch-bitop-record.sh` abort the recording for a k above `OCLJ_SELECT_KMAX` (2^27), as upstream already did for k < 0, and put `ULT k-1, 2^27` before path C's 32-bit shift (build copy, lj_record.c ~:2000, ~:2057, ~:2080). Upstream fixed the same divergence after our pin, in `a4c0645`, which has no path C; the pin move adopts it and drops these hunks (P54, roadmap row 270) [S].

## 3. IR and the fold engine

The SSA IR that traces are recorded into, and the fold/CSE engine that every instruction passes through. IR and machine-code excerpts marked [M] come from the round-3 build (`luajit.exe` md5 `df3664c5`, at `2dd5f05`), run with `-jdump`; each names the Lua loop that produced it. In the build copy, the patched files are shifted against the pristine line hints.

### 3.1 The instruction: `IRIns`

```
     16      16     8   8   8   8
 +-------+-------+---+---+---+---+
 |  op1  |  op2  | t | o | r | s |   r/s (register, spill slot) overlap prev,
 +-------+-------+---+---+---+---+   the CSE chain link, until the backend runs
 |  TValue: 2nd slot of KNUM, KINT64 (and GC64 KGC/KPTR/KKPTR) |
```

[S] lj_ir.h `IRIns`, ~:556.

- **`o` is an `IROp` from `IRDEF` (~:14), and the order matters.** Comparison pairs flip with `^1`; `LT^4` is `ULT`. The bit ops `BNOT..BROR` are contiguous ("ORDER BIT"), and so are the loads and stores. Static asserts at ~:163-176 check this [S].
- **`lj_ir_mode[o]` gives each opcode a kind**: `N` (pure, can be CSE'd), `R` (reference), `L` (load), `S` (store), `A` (allocation); on top of that, `C` means commutative and `W` a weak guard [S].
- **`ir_sideeff` (~:600) decides what DCE must keep**: a store, or any op with a non-weak guard. That is why DCE keeps every guard: `EQ` is not weak. The overflow guards of `ADDOV` and friends are weak, so DCE may drop them [S].

### 3.2 Types and flag bits

[S] `IRTDEF` (~:320) numbers the types; these are the ones round 3 touches:

| type | # | role |
|---|---|---|
| `IRT_NUM` | 14 | every Lua number held in a slot (no DUALNUM) |
| `IRT_U8` | 16 | `string.byte`'s `u8 XLOAD` (lj_ffrecord.c ~:911, ~:944). After `simplify_conv_sext`'s u8/u16 LEFTFOLD it is the int64 op's operand itself, and `oclj_bitwidth` scores it 64 ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)) [S][M] |
| `IRT_INT` | 19 | narrowed values: the loop index, `#t` |
| `IRT_U32` | 20 | |
| `IRT_I64` | 21 | round 3's operator values |

The numbers follow from the `IRTDEF` order and match the measured CONV op2 values below [S][M].

- `irt_isinteger` (~:389) means I8..INT. U32 and I64 do not count as "integer" to it, and that decides `loop_unroll`'s type check ([4.4](#44-type-instability-typeins-retry-blacklist)) [S].
- `irt_is64` (~:409) is true for NUM, I64, U64 and P64, and for the GC reference types under GC64 [S].

The high bits of the type byte are flags (~:344):

| flag | bit | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `IRT_MARK` | 0x20 | Scratch mark for DCE and `loop_emit_phi`'s passes. It is not round 3's exact mark, which is bit 0x400 of a CONV's op2 [S]. |
| `IRT_ISPHI` | 0x40 | The instruction is a PHI operand. Only lj_opt_loop.c sets it (and SPLIT, on 32-bit targets only) [S, grep `irt_setphi`], so `PHIBARRIER` matters only inside `loop_unroll` [I]. It also raises the register priority of PHI values (lj_target.h `REGCOST_PHI_WEIGHT`) [S]. |
| `IRT_GUARD` | 0x80 | The instruction is a guard: if it fails, the trace leaves through the snapshot in effect. `IRTG(o,t)` and `IRTGI(o)` (~:361) set it [S]. |

### 3.3 References, constants, literals, TRefs

[S] lj_ir.h ~:455-520.

- **Refs.** `REF_BIAS` is 0x8000. Instructions grow upwards from `REF_FIRST` (0x8001); constants grow downwards from `REF_NIL` (0x7fff), `REF_FALSE` and `REF_TRUE`. `J->cur.nk` is the lowest constant, and `irref_isk(ref)` is `ref < REF_BIAS`. A dump prints `ref - REF_BIAS`, so `0001` is `REF_FIRST`, and prints constants inline.
- **Constants are interned**: the same ref always means the same value (the comment at `LJFOLD(EQ KINT KINT)`, lj_opt_fold.c ~:948). `KINT` keeps its int32 in `ir->i`; `KNUM` and `KINT64` keep their value in the second slot (`ir_k64`, ~:594).
- **Literal operands are raw numbers below `REF_BIAS`**: a CONV's mode, an SLOAD's slot and flags, an FLOAD's field (~:473). This is why CSE's "higher of the two operands" and DCE's `>= REF_BIAS` test work without looking up the opcode's mode.
- **A `TRef` (~:495) is `ref | type << 24`**, plus the flags `TREF_FRAME`, `TREF_CONT` and `TREF_KEYINDEX`. The recorder passes TRefs around. `tref_isinteger` means I8..INT and `tref_isnumber` means NUM..INT; these are what `oclj_rec_bitarg` tests.

The recorder's vocabulary [S]:

| term | what it is | where |
|---|---|---|
| `IRT(o,t)`, `IRTN`, `IRTI`, `IRTG(o,t)`, `IRTGI` | `o<<8 \| t`; G sets the guard bit | lj_ir.h ~:358-362 |
| `emitir(ot,a,b)` | `lj_ir_set` + `lj_opt_fold`: every instruction passes through FOLD and CSE | lj_record.c ~:43 |
| `emitir_raw` | `lj_ir_set` + `lj_ir_emit`, no folding | ~:46 |
| `lj_ir_kint`, `lj_ir_kint64` | interned `KINT` and `KINT64` constants | lj_ir.c ~:200, ~:246 |

### 3.4 The CONV mode word (op2)

| bits | name | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0-4 | `IRCONV_SRCMASK` 0x001f | source type |
| 5-9 | `IRCONV_DSTMASK` 0x03e0 | destination type (also in `ir->t`) |
| 10 | 0x0400 | unused by LuaJIT 2.1; round 3's `OCLJ_IRCONV_EXACT` |
| 11 | `IRCONV_SEXT` 0x0800 | sign-extend int to int |
| 0-11 | `IRCONV_MODEMASK` 0x0fff | what `cse_conv` compares |
| 12-15 | `IRCONV_CONVMASK` 0xf000 | num-to-int check: `TOBIT` 0 (narrowing cache only), `ANY` 1 (= `NONE`, unchecked), `INDEX` 2, `CHECK` 3 |
| 0-9 | (the fold key) | `lj_opt_fold` keys a literal operand on `op2 & 0x3ff` (~:2566) |

[S] lj_ir.h ~:251-266, lj_opt_fold.c ~:2566.

Round 3 emits three kinds of CONV; their raw op2 values, read with `jit.util.traceir` [M]:

| op2 | IR | role |
|---|---|---|
| 0x12ae | `CONV i64.num`, ANY | an operand's conversion (`cvttsd2si r64`) |
| 0x05d5 | `CONV num.i64`, marked | a result `d`, or an operand's back-conversion |
| 0x16ae | `CONV i64.num`, ANY, marked | a result guard's own back-conversion |

The backend supports a guarded CONV only from number to int: `asm_conv` (lj_asm_x86.h ~:902) asserts it [S]. So a checked int64 conversion cannot be written as `IRCONV_CHECK`; round 3 spells it out as a conversion, a conversion back, and an `EQ` [S][I].

### 3.5 Reading a dump

`0016  + num CONV   0015  num.i64` reads: the ref, `>` if a guard, `+` if a PHI operand (`}` if sunk), the type, the opcode, the operands [S].

- **Gaps in the numbering are DCE'd instructions.** NOP and CARG lines are skipped (jit/dump.lua ~:478-495) [S][M].
- **SLOAD flags print as letters**: `P` parent, `F` frame, `T` typecheck, `C` convert, `R` read-only, `I` inherit, `K` key index (`IRSLOAD_KEYINDEX`; jit/dump.lua ~:271-277). `C` means the interpreter's double becomes an int: the narrowed loop index (lj_record.c ~:378) [S].
- **`i64.num none` is the unchecked conversion.** The CONV formatter (jit/dump.lua ~:282-292) prints `none` for `IRCONV_ANY`, which is `cvttsd2si` with no check, not "no conversion" [S][M] (P38).
- **Two blind spots** [M]: a KINT64 constant prints as the trace number (P3), and the exact mark is never printed (P30). Read raw op2 values with the `traceir` snippet in [12.6](#126-reading-the-exact-mark).

A full annotated trace is in [8.7](#87-reading-a--jdump).

### 3.6 How an instruction is folded

[S] Every emitter goes through `emitir`: `lj_ir_set` (fills `J->fold.ins`), then `lj_opt_fold` (~:2524). That covers the recorder, the narrowing code and `loop_unroll`.

- **Inside a rule, `fins` is that instruction.** `fleft` and `fright` are copies of its operand instructions, including the second slot of a 64-bit constant, made at the `retry:` label (~:2553-2567).
- **Writing `fleft` changes nothing in the IR.** To change the instruction, write `fins` and return `RETRYFOLD`, which folds the changed instruction again.
- **The key** is `o << 17 | left->o << 10 | right->o`. A literal op2 contributes `op2 & 0x3ff`; an absent or literal op1 contributes 0.

The key is looked up four times, most specific first; `any` is 0x7f on the left and 0x3ff on the right (~:2569-2581):

```
ins left right  ->  ins any right  ->  ins left any  ->  ins any any  ->  lj_opt_cse
```

A rule that returns `NEXTFOLD` passes the instruction on to the next lookup. Consequences [S]:

- **Each key has exactly one function.** buildvm refuses a second `LJFOLD` line with the same key ("duplicate fold definition", buildvm_fold.c `foldrule` ~:143). "Rule order" means this order of specificity. To add behaviour for an existing key, edit the function that owns it. That is why round 3 put its rules inside `simplify_conv_i64_num` and added no second `LJFOLD(CONV CONV IRCONV_I64_NUM)`.
- **A constant operand matches by its opcode.** `LJFOLD(BAND any KINT64)` fires only when op2 is a 64-bit constant.
- **A CONV is matched on its two types only.** buildvm parses `IRCONV_I64_NUM` as `(I64 << 5) + NUM` (`nexttoken`, ~:114). SEXT, the check mode and the exact mark are not part of the key, so every CONV rule also sees marked conversions.
- **Commutative ops are normalized, lower ref on the right**, which moves constants to the right (`comm_swap` ~:1965, `comm_equal` ~:1978, `comm_dup` ~:2014, `comm_bxor` ~:2031). Rules are written as `op any K`.
- **`-O-fold` sends every N-mode instruction straight to `lj_opt_cse`** (~:2529-2537). No rule runs, round 3's included: traces stay correct but slower. Measured on `guide1.lua` ([8.7](#87-reading-a--jdump)) with `-jdump=iT`: 8 `num EQ` guards under `-O-fold` against 3, and the same result, 16565 [M].

### 3.7 From LJFOLD lines to lj_folddef.h

[S] The Makefile rule `lj_folddef.h: $(BUILDVM_T) lj_opt_fold.c` (~:709) runs `buildvm -m folddef -o lj_folddef.h lj_opt_fold.c`.

- **What `emit_fold` reads** (buildvm_fold.c ~:169): only the lines of that one file that begin with `LJFOLD` at column 0. `LJFOLD(op left right)` adds a key, which binds to the next `LJFOLDF(name)` (a local `fold_name`) or `LJFOLDX(fn)` (an outside function such as `lj_opt_cse` or `lj_opt_fwd_aload`).
- **What `makehash` builds**: a semi-perfect hash table, probed twice; each entry is the 24-bit key with the function's index in the top byte.
- **buildvm never follows an `#include`.** An `LJFOLD` line in an included file is silently missing from the table (P14). Round 3 therefore keeps its rule bodies in `lj_opt_fold.c` and puts only helpers in `oclj_bitop_fold.c` (`oclj_bitwidth`, and since 2026-10-21 FIX-NUMCONV's `oclj_isnumconv`); `patch-bitop-fold.sh` refuses any output whose LJFOLD lines differ from upstream's.
- **`Makefile.dep` does not know the include either.** After editing `oclj_bitop_fold.c`, delete `lj_opt_fold.o` (P4). make regenerates `lj_folddef.h` whenever `lj_opt_fold.c` changes, but its content changes only when an LJFOLD line does [S][I]. All four width-rule sabotage arms rebuild exactly `lj_opt_fold.o` (logs/suite-native-2/negctl-bitop.log) [M].

### 3.8 What a rule returns, and upstream's requirements

[S] lj_iropt.h ~:106-121, and the comment at lj_opt_fold.c ~:33-79.

| return | effect |
|---|---|
| `NEXTFOLD` | no fold; try the next, less specific key, and finally CSE |
| `RETRYFOLD` | `fins` was changed in place; fold it again from the start |
| `INTFOLD(k)` / `KINTFOLD` | the KINT `k` |
| `INT64FOLD(k)` | `lj_ir_kint64(J, k)`, a KINT64 ref |
| `LEFTFOLD` / `RIGHTFOLD` | the operand ref itself; nothing is emitted |
| `CSEFOLD` / `EMITFOLD` | straight to `lj_opt_cse`, or emit without CSE |
| `DROPFOLD` | a guard that always holds is dropped (the result is `REF_DROP`) |
| `FAILFOLD` | a guard that always fails: the trace aborts (`LJ_TRERR_GFAIL`) |
| `CONDFOLD(c)` | `DROPFOLD` if `c` holds, else `FAILFOLD` |
| any other ref | that instruction, existing or newly emitted |

The header (~:89-130) states three requirements [S]:

1. **Preserve the destination type.** Use `INTFOLD` for a KINT result and `lj_ir_knum` for a KNUM; never a helper whose result can be either.
2. **Do not create new instructions that reference operands across PHIs.** A `RETRYFOLD` with `fins->op1 = fleft->op1` is invalid when `fleft` is a PHI, because `fleft->op1` points past the PHI frontier into the pre-roll. `PHIBARRIER(fleft)` (~:157, `if (irt_isphi(fleft->t)) return NEXTFOLD`) prevents this. Returning an *existing* ref, even `fleft->op1`, is allowed: "a new PHI will added, if needed" (sic, lj_opt_fold.c ~:115).
3. **Keep the rule set monotonic.** Rules should reduce strength; a rule that does not, such as commutativity, must work in one direction only, or folding cycles.

Upstream itself breaks or qualifies these in three places that round 3 runs into [S]:

- **`simplify_conv_int_num` and `simplify_tobit_conv` fold across PHIs on purpose** ("Fold even across PHI to avoid expensive num->int conversions in loop", ~:1131 and ~:1193). That is upstream's first test in `simplify_conv_int_num`, which returns an existing ref; the table-index fold added below it builds a new instruction and does not cross ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)).
- **On x64, `simplify_conv_sext` returns an int-typed ref for a `CONV i64.int`**: a `LEFTFOLD` at ~:1241 ("All 32 bit ops do an implicit zero-extension"), taken when `J->scev` proves the loop index non-negative, and for u8/u16 XLOADs. It breaks requirement 1 on purpose, and it is the root cause of fix1 ([4.5](#45-fix1-the-worked-case)) and of the CSE twins, which hunk 12 ends (P63, [3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)): an int instruction and one of our int64 instructions could then share opcode and operands.
- **"A new PHI will be added" is `loop_unroll`'s `ref < invar` branch,** and that PHI takes the returned ref's type. So returning an existing ref across a PHI is safe only if its type equals the folded instruction's type (S4).

### 3.9 CSE, and why the exact mark is bit 0x400

**CSE** [S]:

- **`lj_opt_cse` (~:2601)** walks `J->chain[o]` from the newest instruction downwards, through `prev`, and stops at the higher of the two operand refs. Upstream's matches on `op12`, both operands with literals included, and returns the existing instruction with that instruction's own type; it does not compare the type. **Since 2026-10-23 ours also requires `irt_isi64(IR(ref)->t) == irt_isi64(fins->t)`** (`patch-bitop-fold.sh` hunk 12; build copy, lj_opt_fold.c :2674-2680, the comment and the match), so an I64 instruction never matches one of another type, NUM and P64 as well as the narrower integers, and two instructions of which neither is I64 match as upstream's do [S]. The test is on the type alone: `irt_isi64` masks `IRT_TYPE` (lj_ir.h ~:365, ~:384), so the guard and PHI bits never block a merge, and guards still common (below) [S]. A refused candidate does not end the walk: it goes on through `prev`, where an older instruction of the right type can still match, and when none does `fins` is emitted as an `-O-cse` run would emit it, so the test changes no value, only which instructions a trace computes at a collision site [S]. Compiled, it masks each type with 0x1f and compares it with 0x15 (`IRT_I64`): `lj_opt_cse` grew from 67 to 83 instructions, the only function of `lj_opt_fold.o` that changed [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/impl-code/objdiff/`]. Timed against `build/native` on `bench_bit`, `bench_bit2` (crc-table among its rows), `ident` and `bench_numconv`, 8 rounds alternating the order, every result was equal and every row's median ratio 1.000 [M: a smoke reading, `csetwin/round/integrate/timing/run-1/summary.txt`; the final check's, [11.8](#118-fail-first)].
- **Why: the CSE twins** (found and fixed 2026-10-23, roadmap row 271, P63). Before hunk 12 an int instruction and an i64 one with the same opcode and operands were one instruction to `lj_opt_cse`: once `simplify_conv_sext` lets our i64 op take an int ref directly (the int loop index, or a u8/u16 load, [3.8](#38-what-a-rule-returns-and-upstreams-requirements)), a 32-bit instruction on the same refs, such as LuaJIT's `bit` library's, served the 5.3 operator, which then computed wrong values: BNOT always, BSHL when x * 2^k >= 2^32, a variable count of 32 or more (`bench/runs/2026-10-23-shiftexact/`, `cse/` and the argument's section 8) [M for BNOT and BSHL; for every shape of t_csefwd, [11.5d](#115d-the-cse-part-t_csefwd-cseir-t_cserev)]. In the reverse order, our op first, the 32-bit producer got our I64-typed instruction back (P63).
- **Every merge that can pair our ops goes through `lj_opt_cse`** [S: the fix round's design 2.2, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/design.md`, re-read by its code verifier]: fold exhaustion, the `-O-fold` path and `CSEFOLD`; the `LJFOLDX(lj_opt_cse)` keys; `fload_tab_ah`, `fwd_sload`, `barrier_tab` and `cse_carg`; the nested `lj_opt_fold` of `simplify_shiftk_andk`; and `loop_unroll`'s re-emission of every copied instruction (lj_opt_loop.c ~:330). The other matchers cannot pair them: `lj_opt_cselim` is reached only from `fold_base` and FLOAD forwarding, `cse_conv` compares a mode word that holds both types, `cse_uref` and `cse_urefo` handle their own opcodes, narrowing's cache never sees our ops (hunk 8, [3.13](#313-narrowing-and-why-round-3-goes-around-it)), and `lj_opt_mem.c`'s own type-blind search, `reassoc_trycse`, is reached only with FFI (open question 23). So the test covers both orders, record time, the nested fold, the loop copy, side traces and `-O-fold`, whoever emits the 32-bit side, upstream's code or ours, now or later [S][I]. Without FFI upstream emits no I64 instruction that shares opcode and operands with one of another type (string.buffer's IGC- and INTP-typed BAND, BXOR and SUB, `rec_varg`'s SUB on BASE, which goes to `lj_opt_cselim`, CONVs, HIOP) [S: the design's 2.3; the code verifier's grep], and a census of the refused matches read 830 in the cse part as it stood before `copy` and `side` and 0 in every other suite it ran ([11.10](#1110-crash-fixes)'s list), so outside collision sites no CSE decision changed in those suites [M].
- **In game, before the fix** [S][I]. The game opens no `bit` library [S], and no in-game wrong value is known [I]: the pair families the game can form, `select` beyond its varargs (G1, `BSHL k, 3` against our `k << 3`), a library argument narrowed from `x + x` (G2) and an inner `for j = x, n, x` recorded in the same trace (G3, its FORL's `ADD x, x`), each `ADD x, x` against our `x << 1`, give equal values in the forward order, the 32-bit instruction first [S][I: the design's 1.4-1.5], as t_csefwd's `select`, `byte2`, `sub2` and `forin` measure [M]. In the reverse order G1 and G2 hand our I64 instruction only to int ADDs held in C locals of their recorders, never to a slot (t_cserev's header argues it from the build copy; on `build/native` its four reverse cases merge with every value right and no I64 instruction in a snapshot [M]). G3 reversed, `x << 1` before such an inner loop whose FORL the root trace records, puts the I64 into the inner loop's index slots, which a snapshot restores with no I64 case: a crash candidate at record time, under narrow trace-formation conditions [S][I: the design's 1.5, scratch]. A pair can also form only in `loop_unroll`'s copy, from operands that differ at record time and coincide in the copy (a `string.byte` value, or a slot carried across iterations): `loop_unroll` type-checks only a match with a pre-roll ref (`ref < invar`, lj_opt_loop.c ~:334, raising TYPEINS at ~:352), and nothing type-checks a match between two body instructions, so G3's reverse had a second route, through the copy [S: the build copy's lj_opt_loop.c :330-352; the round's code verifier]. That route is [I] only, never written or run (the user's decision D2 of 2026-10-23). Hunk 12 covers it, because every copied instruction goes back through `lj_opt_cse`; the forward analogue is measured, t_csefwd's `copy` (on `build/native` 336, 393 and 393 of its 400 values wrong over three calls, 0 on the fixed build), and so is a pair that forms only in a side trace, t_csefwd's `side` (285, 295 and 295 of 600) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/runs/cse-base-6/`, `cse-fix-3/`]. Every jar with round 3's operators built before the fix carries the G3 candidate; how often OC programs would meet it is unmeasured, and OpenOS and machine.lua use no 5.3 operator [I: the design's census].
- **CONV has its own CSE rule, `cse_conv`** (`LJFOLD(CONV any any)`, ~:1287). It matches when op1 is the same, `op2 & IRCONV_MODEMASK` is the same, and the existing instruction's guard is at least as strong ("Commoning with stronger checks is ok"). If nothing matches it returns `EMITFOLD`, so generic CSE never sees a CONV. The check mode (CONVMASK) is not part of a CONV's identity; the guard bit decides instead.
- **Guards are CSE'd too.** `EQ` reaches `lj_opt_cse` through `comm_equal` and `comm_swap`. In the loop copy, this is how an invariant guard is hoisted: its copy CSEs to the pre-roll's guard (the lj_opt_loop.c header).

**Why bit 0x400** [S] unless marked:

- **It is inside `IRCONV_MODEMASK`.** So `cse_conv`, and the narrowing CSE (`narrow_conv_backprop` ~:308, `narrow_bpc_get` ~:221), never merge a marked conversion with an unmarked one. The direction that matters [I]: an unmarked conversion CSE'd into a marked one would carry a promise that no guard backs.
- **It is outside the key's `0x3ff`.** So every CONV rule still matches a marked conversion. For example, `kfold_conv_kint64_num_i64` (~:862) still turns a constant result's `d` into a KNUM, which is why the recorder emits no guard for a constant result.
- **No upstream code tests it.** Every test of op2 in the pinned source is a mask (SRC, DST, SEXT, CONVMASK) or an equality with `IRCONV_NUM_INT`. The equalities are at lj_snap.c ~:585/654/738/798, lj_opt_sink.c ~:55, lj_asm.c ~:986, and lj_asm_x86.h ~:1993 (`asm_x87load`, a 32-bit path, found by the implementation review and missing from `oclj_bitop.h`'s list). A `CONV num.i64` is never equal to `IRCONV_NUM_INT`, and `asm_conv` ignores the bit.
- **It is the only free bit in `MODEMASK` outside `0x3ff`.** The other one, 0x800, is SEXT.
- **The check bits lie outside `MODEMASK`, and `IRCONV_NONE` (0x1000) among them** [S: proto-folda's and verify-folda's reading, 2026-10-20]. So `cse_conv` would common a `CONV int.i64` with `IRCONV_NONE` set, for which `asm_conv` emits nothing and the register keeps its upper half, with the table-index fold's mode-exact one, for which it emits `mov r32, r32` ([5.1](#51-our-ir-in-x64)): an S3 case. FFI code emits the first (lj_ffrecord.c ~:1232 and ~:1418, both under `LJ_HASFFI`), and so does string.buffer's recorder outside FFI: `recff_sbufx_len` (lj_ffrecord.c ~:1192-1197, under `LJ_HASBUFFER`, which our build keeps, oclj_features.h ~:119-120) emits `CONV int.intp` with `IRCONV_NONE`, and `IRT_INTP` is `IRT_I64` on x64 (lj_ir.h ~:340); several buffer methods call it, `#buf` (`recff_buffer_method___len`, ~:1381-1387) among them [S: the CRC32 round's docs verifier, re-read by its fixer]. Its operand is always a pointer difference, `SUB.intp`, which `oclj_bitwidth` scores 64 (it has no SUB case, oclj_bitop_fold.c ~:116), so the fold never builds a `CONV int.i64` of the same operand and the two cannot common [S][I]. A round that turns FFI on, or teaches `oclj_bitwidth` a SUB, must look at it again (open question 16). The round's design first said "only FFI code emits the first", from a reading of the FFI paths alone: before a claim that only X emits Y, grep the pristine src for every emitter of the op2 and note each hit's `#if` [S].

**What the mark means** depends on the conversion [S]:
