The objects are equal all the same: on Windows the 77 LuaJIT objects and the 3 of ours equal `build/native`'s, and in AlmaLinux 8 both variants' .so are the release's byte for byte (additive `6c888050`, dropin `6c0715d1`) [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-w1/`, `fc4e-alma-w1/`]. What does carry CRs after make on Windows is what MinGW tools write in text mode: buildvm's generated headers (`lj_bcdef.h` 227 CRs, `lj_ffdef.h` 193, `lj_folddef.h` 1172, `lj_libdef.h` 381, `lj_recdef.h` 254, `host/buildvm_arch.h` 7127) and `jit/vmdef.lua` (354); in AlmaLinux 8 no generated text file does [M: the same census files]. Both line endings build.
- **On this machine's Git Bash, awk (GNU Awk 5.4.1) and grep drop a line-end CR on input** [M]: `printf 'a\r\nb\r\nc\n'` gives awk lengths `1 1 1`; an awk `index($0, CR)` count gives 0 for that file and 0 for a log with 95 CRLF lines; `grep -c $'\r'` gives 0 and `grep -c -U $'\r'` gives 95; awk sees the CRs only with `-v BINMODE=1`.
- **Count CRs by bytes**: `python -c 'import sys;print(open(sys.argv[1],"rb").read().count(13))' f`, `grep -c -U`, or `od -c` (P7).
- **One consequence**: the hunk engine's `ncr()` check ("the output carries CR characters") is awk-based and cannot fail on Git Bash for line-end CRs; on Linux it can. The outputs are LF by byte count anyway [M]. This is an open gap (Open questions).
- **Windows `luajit.exe` and `lua.exe` write CRLF to stdout**: strip CR before comparing. Every gate pipes through `tr -d '\r'` or compares a stripped copy [S] (P18).

### 7.4 Running make by hand

Always in a fresh scratch copy, never in `build/`:

```sh
S=<fresh scratch dir>                                   # never under build/, never reused
mkdir -p "$S/luajit-windows-x86_64" && cp -rp build/native/luajit-windows-x86_64/. "$S/luajit-windows-x86_64/"   # -p keeps mtimes
cp -rp build/native/obj-windows-x86_64 "$S/"            # run-bitop.sh part 3 links these
W=$S/luajit-windows-x86_64/src
( cd "$W" && md5sum *.o host/*.o ) | tr -d '*' > "$S/base-obj.md5"     # 77 lines
# change ONE file (LF, written with a file tool, not a heredoc), then delete what it reaches (table below)
mingw32-make -C "$W" BUILDMODE=static XCFLAGS= "Q=" -j4 > "$S/build.log" 2>&1      # Windows
make         -C "$W" BUILDMODE=static XCFLAGS=-fPIC Q= -j4 > "$S/build.log" 2>&1   # Linux
( cd "$W" && md5sum *.o host/*.o ) | tr -d '*' | diff "$S/base-obj.md5" -          # only the reached objects may differ
```

The layout mirrors `$OCLJ_BUILD`, so the suites run against the scratch build unchanged: they take `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit-<platform>/src/luajit.exe`, and run-bitop.sh also `$OCLJ_BUILD/obj-<platform>` (run-bitjit.sh ~:276, run-bitop.sh ~:108-123) [S]. For example `OCLJ_BUILD=$S OCLJ_LUA53=<PUC 5.3.6> OCLJ_BITJIT_OUT=$S/bitjit-1 sh test/native/run-bitjit.sh`, and likewise run-bitfuzz.sh (`OCLJ_BITFUZZ_OUT`) and run-bitop.sh (`OCLJ_BITOP_OUT`). Name `OCLJ_LUA53` in every such call: its default is relative to `OCLJ_REPO`, and a scratch repository made with `git archive` has no `bench/vendor`. Since 2026-10-20 run-bitjit.sh then says PROVED NOTHING (exit 2) unless `OCLJ_LUA53=none` declines PUC on purpose; before, it skipped every PUC comparison and still passed [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/impl/logs/pucff-1.log`; verify-folda F11]. negctl-bitop.sh accepts the same layout, but it refuses a copy that is not what the repo's files and patch scripts make of the pristine files, which it takes from `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit-export-<platform>` when that exists and from `OCLJ_LUAJIT` otherwise (a scratch layout without the export uses the checkout, which must then be clean) ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages) item 1), so it proves a change only once the change is in `native/luajit/` and the copy was rebuilt by build-native.sh ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) [S].

- **On Linux, copy a build tree inside the distro** (`cp -a` there), not from Git Bash: Git Bash's copy of a Linux tree fails on `host/buildvm`, which it takes for `buildvm.exe` [M: verify-folda, 2026-10-20].
- **`BUILDMODE=static`** builds `libluajit.a` and a statically linked `luajit.exe`. Without it, Windows builds a DLL (`-DLUA_BUILD_AS_DLL`, Makefile ~:568-572). Under static mode `TARGET_DYNCC` is `@:`, so the `_dyn.o` compiles are no-ops [S].
- **Nothing from the environment.** build-native.sh drops the make, gcc and ld variables of its list before it runs make (THE BUILD ENVIRONMENT, [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)); a make run by hand does not, and a `CFLAGS`, `TARGET_CFLAGS`, `MAKEFLAGS` or `CPATH` (in any case on Windows) set in your shell reaches every compile line [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/impl/exp/mkn-win/summary.txt`, `exp/caseenv.log`]. Run it in a shell where none of 7.6's list is set.
- **`XCFLAGS` must be build-native.sh's** (`LJ_FLAGS="$PICFLAG"`: empty on Windows, `-fPIC` elsewhere, :445-448), or every recompiled object differs from the shipped build [S]. Rebuilding with the right flags gives byte-identical objects [M] (negctl canon).
- **`Q=`, empty.** The Makefile's default `Q= @` (:625) hides command lines, and build-native.sh passes `Q=` so its log gates have compile lines to match (:1460) [S]. A literal `Q=.` (a report's full stop copied into the command) prefixes every command with a dot: `make -n` prints `.host/minilua.exe ../dynasm/dynasm.lua ...` and `.gcc ...` [M] (P5).
- **`make` on Windows.** build-native.sh and the negctls call `make`, but WinLibs MinGW-w64 ships only `mingw32-make`, and in a plain Git Bash `command -v make` finds nothing [M]. This machine's launchers prepend `/c/Users/astro/claude-tmp/oclj/bin`, which holds a two-line `make` (`exec mingw32-make "$@"`); `bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/scripts/make-shim` is its archived copy [M] (P45).
- **Cost.** A rebuild takes 1-3 s, a vm_x64.dasc one included; a C object plus the relink about 1 s [M] (negctl-bitop.sh header; impl-C3.json). build-native.sh's whole LuaJIT stage, `make clean` included, takes +17 s on Windows and +6 s in WSL [M] (logs/builds-2, logs/linux).

**What to delete before make**, so make cannot miss a change. This is negctl-bitop.sh's map (`objs_of`, `dirty`), plus `lib_string.o` for `oclj_strpack.c` from negctl-pack.sh [S]:

| you changed | delete |
|---|---|
| vm_x64.dasc | `host/buildvm_arch.h host/buildvm.o lj_vm.o lj_bc.o` |
| lj_parse.c / lj_meta.c / lj_record.c / lj_opt_fold.c / lib_jit.c | its `.o` (make would see the mtime, but delete anyway) |
| oclj_bitop_meta.c | `lj_meta.o` |
| oclj_bitop_rec.c | `lj_record.o` |
| oclj_bitop_fold.c | `lj_opt_fold.o` |
| oclj_bitop.h | `lj_parse.o lj_meta.o lj_record.o lj_opt_fold.o` |
| oclj_num53.h | those four and `lib_string.o` |
| oclj_strpack.c | `lib_string.o` |
| lj_bcdump.h | `lib_string.o lj_bcread.o lj_bcwrite.o lj_lib.o lj_load.o` (only lj_bcread.o and lj_bcwrite.o changed bytes in negctl's BCDUMP arm [M]) |
| oclj_features.h, lj_arch.h | everything: `make clean` |

### 7.5 The patch-script engine

Round 3's seven scripts share one engine; use it for any new change ([8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script)).

- **The engine block is byte-identical in all seven**: 99 lines, md5 `077d1dbebe05` [M], unchanged by the table-index fold's hunk 11 [M: the implementer's check, 2026-10-20], by the select fix's hunks 4 and 5 [M: the block from `# --- THE HUNK ENGINE` to `# --- end of the hunk engine` in each of the seven, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/docs/scripts/engine.py`, 2026-10-20] and by FIX-NUMCONV's change to hunk 7 [M: the same reading, `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/docs/scripts/engine.py`, 2026-10-21], and by the CSE-twin fix's hunk 12 [M: the round's smoke check ENGINE, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/smoke/s1`, 2026-10-23]. The scripts contain no backslash, no tab and no CR [M] (`patch-bitop-record.sh` again after the select fix, `sel/docs/scripts/bytecount.py`; `patch-bitop-fold.sh` again after hunk 12, the same smoke run's PATCH-NO-TAB-BS); in the hunk texts, `@T@` stands for a tab.
- **Hunks.** Each hunk is a pair of files written by the script: `o<h>` holds upstream's text, `n<h>` ours. A text matches only as a run of consecutive whole lines, compared exactly after the trailing CR is stripped [S].
- **Modes** [S]: `count` (per hunk, how often upstream's text and ours occur, and whether upstream's text survives inside ours, which an insertion does); `apply` (each upstream text must occur exactly once at its point); `revert` (the inverse, in reverse order).
- **After `apply`, the output must pass all of these** [S]:
  - the marker exactly `NMARK` times, and every patched text exactly once;
  - per-script content checks (the vm script checks that `->vmeta_bitop_pc` is defined once and named on 7 lines and that there are 3 OC-LuaJIT lines; the fold script that the LJFOLD lines are unchanged and where the include sits, since FIX-NUMCONV that `oclj_isnumconv(` is named on one line, its call inside the rules' block, and since the CSE-twin fix that hunk 12's type test is a line of its own once, directly between `lj_opt_cse`'s `if` and its return, and that `lj_opt_cse` holds no other match or return ([8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) step 0); the record script, since the select fix, that `OCLJ_SELECT_KMAX` is named on exactly 3 lines and that its definition, `rec_varg`, the record-time bound, the guard and `rec_tnew` come in that order);
  - no CR (but see [7.3](#73-line-endings-and-the-git-bash-instrument)), and line count in + NADD - NDEL = out;
  - `diff -d --strip-trailing-cr` counting exactly NADD added and NDEL removed lines (vm 40/15, fold 76/9 since the CSE-twin fix (70/8 with FIX-NUMCONV, 63/8 with the table-index fold, 45/8 in round 3), parse 24/18, record 22/36 since the select fix (7/35 in round 3; hunk 4 adds 11 lines, hunk 5 adds 4 and removes 1), meta 8/26, jitlib 7/3, bcdump 6/1);
  - **reversal**: putting every upstream text back must reproduce the input byte for byte (CR stripped).
- **Output is LF.** An "already patched" input is verified and left alone; that path is for hand use only, because the build always re-copies the pristine file first [S].
- **The earlier five** have the same shape with an older engine: they splice a block with awk, anchor on lines that occur exactly once (CRLF-tolerant), check `diff --strip-trailing-cr` hunk and line counts, and check the removed lines by content [S]. patch-penalty-scrub.sh and patch-fastfunc-errmem-top.sh still contain backslashes (5 and 8 lines) [M]; new scripts follow round 3's no-backslash rule.

### 7.6 build-native.sh, stage by stage

**Preflight: the pin** (THE BUILD ENVIRONMENT, :213-283; THE INPUT DIRECTORIES, :481-571; THE PIN, :572-619; THE DIRECTORIES THIS SCRIPT REMOVES, :620-729; `git_head` and `ljgit`, :869-926; THE CHECKOUT MUST BE AT THE PIN, :927-1022) [S].

- **The build environment** (THE BUILD ENVIRONMENT, :213-283), in both modes, before anything reads it [S]. make takes every environment variable as a make variable, and LuaJIT's src/Makefile uses its flag and path variables without assigning them, so a value in the shell reaches the compile lines as it is: with `CFLAGS='-include <header>'` in the environment, `fc4e465d` put the header on 76 of the 77 LuaJIT compile lines of a pinned build, stamped the DLL `1ee778a4`, collected it into `dist`, and gradle accepted it [M: the export-check round's review, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin3/verify/runs/cflags-fc4e/`, `pin3/verify/gruns/summary.txt`]. Now the script unsets, whatever the case of the name, the variables of this list, the ones make, gcc and ld were measured, or read in their manuals and LuaJIT's Makefiles, to take from the environment and change what is compiled or linked, and prints `ignored from the environment: <the names that were set>` (or `none`), never a value [S]:
  - the conventions `CFLAGS CPPFLAGS CXXFLAGS LDFLAGS LDLIBS LIBS`;
  - every variable the pin's src/Makefile and top Makefile use without assigning it, or assign with `?=` or in some branches only: `HOST_CFLAGS HOST_LDFLAGS HOST_LIBS TARGET_CFLAGS TARGET_FLAGS TARGET_LDFLAGS TARGET_SHLDFLAGS TARGET_LIBS TARGET_ARCH TARGET_SYS CROSS PREFIX MULTILIB LMULTILIB INSTALL_LJLIBD DESTDIR HOST_MSYS HOST_RM MAKE` [S: a census of the two files, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/impl/exp/mkvars-src.txt`, `mkvars-top.txt`]. Set one at a time to `-DOCLJSENT`, 17 of them changed what `make -n` runs for this build, the same 17 on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8 (`CFLAGS CROSS HOST_CFLAGS HOST_LDFLAGS HOST_LIBS INSTALL_LJLIBD LDFLAGS LIBS LMULTILIB MULTILIB PREFIX TARGET_ARCH TARGET_CFLAGS TARGET_FLAGS TARGET_LDFLAGS TARGET_LIBS TARGET_SYS`; `TARGET_ARCH` and `TARGET_SYS` are assigned only in branches x64 does not take, or with `?=`), `HOST_RM` changed what `make clean` runs and `MAKE` what the amalg target runs [M: `pin4/impl/exp/mkn-win/summary.txt`, `exp/alma/mkn/summary.txt`];
  - make's own `MAKEFLAGS MAKEFILES GNUMAKEFLAGS MFLAGS MAKEOVERRIDES`: `MAKEFLAGS='CFLAGS=-DOCLJSENT'`, `GNUMAKEFLAGS` the same, and `MAKEFILES` naming a makefile fragment each put the flag on every compile line; `MFLAGS` changed nothing, and is dropped as a convention [M: `exp/mkn2-win/summary.txt`, `exp/alma/mkn2/summary.txt`]. `MAKEOVERRIDES` joined in round 5: round 4's review measured `MAKEOVERRIDES=CFLAGS=-O0` alone putting `-O0` on 76 LuaJIT compile lines with mingw32-make 4.4.1, 62 of the 77+3 objects differing, the DLL stamped `1ee778a4`, collected into `dist`, logged `ignored from the environment: none`, and accepted by gradle; AlmaLinux 8's make 4.2.1 ignored it, and a parent make does not export it [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/verify/runs/env2-new/`, `alma-mo-new/`, `pin4/verify/exp/mkexport/`]. Now the same build logs `ignored from the environment: MAKEOVERRIDES` and gives W1's objects, no `-O0` line [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/pin5/final/run-impl/`, its `MO-*` lines; on `7c3c2683` they fail, `pin5/ff-7c3c/`];
  - gcc's `CPATH C_INCLUDE_PATH CPLUS_INCLUDE_PATH OBJC_INCLUDE_PATH LIBRARY_PATH GCC_EXEC_PREFIX COMPILER_PATH DEPENDENCIES_OUTPUT SUNPRO_DEPENDENCIES` [S];
  - ld's `LD_RUN_PATH`, which gave a two-line shared object an RPATH in AlmaLinux 8 (a RUNPATH in Ubuntu), and `GNUTARGET`, which changed its section layout in AlmaLinux 8; `LDEMULATION` changed nothing and is kept [M: `exp/alma/probe.txt`, `exp/ubuntu/probe.txt`].

  **Whatever the case of the name**, because MinGW gcc reads its variables through the Windows C runtime, whose `getenv` ignores case: it found a header through `cpath` and `Cpath` as through `CPATH`, and through `cpath` after the shell's `unset CPATH`; mingw32-make's own variables are case-sensitive (`cflags` is not `CFLAGS`) [M: `exp/caseenv.log`]. A name that is not a shell identifier cannot be unset by sh; none of these is one, and make takes such names from the environment too (its own `-*-command-variables-*-` is one; not measured) [S][I]. So the list is what was found to change the build, not every name make, gcc or ld reads: round 4's missed `MAKEOVERRIDES` ([open question 15](#open-questions)). **Kept**: the script's documented inputs (`CC`, `JOBS`, `OCLJ_*` and the rest), `PATH`, and `OS`, `MSYSTEM` and `TERM`, which src/Makefile reads only to tell a cmd.exe host from an MSYS or POSIX one (Makefile ~:157), which Windows and the terminal set on every host, and none of which changed the dry run alone [M: `exp/win-host.txt`, `exp/mkn-win/summary.txt`]. None of the dropped names is set on either host today, in any case, so the canonical builds are unchanged: they log `ignored from the environment: none`, and give the release's objects and .so files (W1 below) [M: `exp/win-host.txt`, `exp/alma/probe.txt`]. A pinned build with `CFLAGS='-include <header>'`, `cpath` naming a directory whose `stdint.h` adds a marker string, and `MAKEFLAGS='TARGET_CFLAGS=-O0'` logs `ignored from the environment: CFLAGS MAKEFLAGS cpath` and gives the canonical objects, with no marker anywhere [M: final-check's `G2-*` lines, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/final/run-impl/`; `pin4/impl/ff2-new/summary.txt`; again on round 5's text, final-check2's `G2-*` lines, `pin5/final/run-impl/`]; `fc4e465d`, given the same environment, built 76 of the 77 LuaJIT objects otherwise, 70 of their compile lines at `-O0`, put both markers into the DLL, stamped it `1ee778a4` and collected it into `dist` [M: `pin4/impl/ff2-fc4e/summary.txt`]. **The toolchain is not pinned** ([1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is)): `CC` (gcc by default) compiles our three translation units and the probes, LuaJIT's Makefile calls `gcc` whatever `CC` says (`CC=-DOCLJSENT` changed nothing), and whatever make, gcc and binutils `PATH` finds do the rest [S][M].

- `native/luajit/upstream.commit` must be exactly 40 lowercase hex digits and an optional LF, checked by byte count, character class and last byte. Anything else (CR LF, a second line, upper case, 8 digits, a space, a NUL, an empty or missing file) is a BUILD FAIL before the compiler is even looked for, and the message shows the bytes with `od -c` [M: `pin/impl/malformed-new`: 14 malformed files refused, and the pin with and without its LF and another 40-digit commit passed; HEAD's script took all 20 cases, three values of `OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED` among them, to the next check, `malformed-head`].
- **The input directories** (THE INPUT DIRECTORIES, :481-571), in both modes, before anything reads them (since round 5) [S]. Every directory the script is given (`OCLJ_REPO`, `OCLJ_LUAJIT`, `OCLJ_SHIM`, `OCLJ_SER`, `OCLJ_JNLUA`, `OCLJ_JNI`, `OCLJ_BUILD`, `OCLJ_OUT`, and its own) becomes the physical path this shell resolves it to: `cd -P` and `pwd -P` for one that exists, and `physpath` for `OCLJ_BUILD` and `OCLJ_OUT`, which need not exist yet (their deepest existing ancestor resolved, the names below it appended, `..` taking the parent). The guards below compare those paths, and every later command, make, gcc, ld and git among them, is handed them; a value that names no directory is left as given, for the check that reads it to refuse it. On Windows, `winname` refuses a path one of whose names ends in a dot or a space, before anything is created [S]. Why: round 4 resolved paths for its guards only, and its review measured that this shell and Windows' own programs read some spellings differently. Git Bash hands a native program `<x>/<junction>/..` as written, and Win32 takes `..` by the spelling (the junction's parent), where `cd -P`, `rm`, `cp` and `mkdir` take it where the junction points; and a name ending in `.` or ` ` is kept by this shell and dropped by Win32 [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/verify/exp/dd1/`, `dot1/`, `dot2/`]. So on `7c3c2683` an unpinned additive build with `OCLJ_OUT=<B>/j/../dist` (j a junction elsewhere) passed the guard, which had read the junction's target, and ld replaced `<B>/dist`'s pinned DLL with one stamped `unpinned`, a regression, since `fc4e465d`'s string comparison had refused that input; `OCLJ_OUT=<B>/dist.` did the same on both texts, also to a scratch repository's `build/native/dist`; and a pinned build with `OCLJ_BUILD=<x>/<junction>/..` ran `make clean` and the LuaJIT build inside a throwaway clone at the lexical `<x>/luajit-windows-x86_64`, deleting an untracked object there and writing 90 build products into it [M: `pin4/verify/runs/u3-new/` (t1, t2), `u3-fc4e/`, `repo-new2/`, `more-new/`]. On round 5's text [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin5/final/final-check2.sh`, run into `pin5/final/run-impl/`]: `OCLJ_OUT=<B>/dist.` is refused, `dist` intact (`T1-trailing-dot`); `OCLJ_OUT=<B>/j/../dist` links into the physical `<E>/else/dist`, `<B>/dist` intact (`T2-junction-dotdot`); the pinned `OCLJ_BUILD=<x>/<junction>/..` builds in the junction's target, objects equal to W1's, the clone's files, its untracked object and its `.git` unchanged (`U2L-*`); and `OCLJ_SHIM=<E>/shim.`, beside a decoy `<E>/shim` whose `lj52shim.c` adds a marker, and `OCLJ_SER=<E>/ser.` are refused before anything is created (`SHIM-dot`, `SER-dot`). Each of those lines fails on `7c3c2683` [M: `pin5/ff-7c3c/`], where the `SHIM-dot` build compiled the decoy [M: `pin5/ff-7c3c/t/shimdot.txt`]. The same holds through a junction: with `OCLJ_SHIM=<E>/x/<junction>/..`, where this shell reads the real shim and Windows' programs a decoy, round 5's text compiles the real one and `7c3c2683` compiled the decoy, its gates having read the real one; and an `OCLJ_REPO` given through a junction is read at its physical path [M: `pin5/scripts/extra.sh`, run into `pin5/extra-impl2/` and `pin5/extra-7c3c2/`, cases x10 and x8].
- **The directories this script removes, and where the library lands** (THE DIRECTORIES THIS SCRIPT REMOVES, :620-729), in both modes, before anything is created, removed or built [S]. Every run removes `luajit-<platform>`, `luajit-export-<platform>` and `layoutneg-<platform>` in `$OCLJ_BUILD`, so the checkout may not be, or lie inside, any of them, and `$OCLJ_BUILD` may not be, or lie inside, the checkout. `OCLJ_OUT` may not be, or lie inside, `$OCLJ_BUILD/dist`, `$OCLJ_REPO/build/native/dist` or, since round 5, the `build/native/dist` of the repository the script lives in. And `$OCLJ_BUILD` may not be, or lie inside, `$OCLJ_REPO` or (since round 5) the repository the script lives in, except under that repository's `build/`: the build writes files of fixed names into `$OCLJ_BUILD` (`strpack.lua`, `bitops.lua`, `jitlib.lua`, `noffi.lua`, `luajit_build.log` and more), and the repository tracks files of such names (`serializer/tests/strpack.lua`, and copies under `bench/runs/`), which an `OCLJ_BUILD` of their directory would overwrite (the export-check round's review [S]; with `OCLJ_BUILD` set to a scratch repository's `serializer/tests`, `fc4e465d` overwrote its `strpack.lua` with the build's probe and wrote 25 files beside it [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/impl/ff2-fc4e/summary.txt`]). The repository the script lives in is guarded too since round 5, because `OCLJ_REPO` can name another: with the script copied into a scratch repository and `OCLJ_REPO` the real one, an `OCLJ_BUILD` in the scratch repository's `serializer/tests` is refused, that repository unchanged, and an unpinned build's `OCLJ_OUT` in its `build/native/dist` is refused, that `dist` intact; on `7c3c2683` the first built into the scratch repository and the second replaced its `dist`'s DLL [M: final-check2's `H2-*` lines, `pin5/final/run-impl/`, `pin5/ff-7c3c/`]. A copy of the script so guards its own parent directory as a repository [S]. **The comparison is by identity** (since round 4): the paths compared are the physical ones of THE INPUT DIRECTORIES; two directories that exist are compared with `test -ef` (one file: the same device and inode); `inside <a> <b>` walks `<a>`'s ancestors, `<a>` first, compares each that exists with `<b>`'s deepest existing ancestor by `test -ef`, and then the names below the two by `pathkey`; and a path that does not exist yet is compared by spelling, through `pathkey`, on every host (`/mnt/c`, where the AlmaLinux 8 release build runs, is NTFS too) [S]. `pathkey` folds A-Z and, since round 5, reads every byte outside printable ASCII as `?`: A-Z folding alone let `OCLJ_BUILD=<E>/Bäu` and `OCLJ_OUT=<E>/BÄU/dist`, neither made yet and one directory on NTFS, through, and the unpinned DLL landed in that `dist` while the log said "dist left as it was" [M: round 4's review, `pin4/verify/runs/u3-new/` t4]. Now that pair is refused with nothing created, on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8 on `/mnt/c` [M: final-check2's `T4-nonascii-both-new` and `ALMA-T4`]. The cost: two names that differ only in bytes outside printable ASCII, as many of them, compare equal while neither exists, so such a path can be refused although it lies elsewhere (`Böu` against `Bäu`); never the other way [S][M: `pin5/fn/`, the "documented cost" case]. Until then a plain `cd` and `pwd -P` gave physical paths and their strings were compared, A-Z folded on Windows only, and the export-check round's review got past both refusals: an `OCLJ_BUILD` written `<dir>/<junction>/..` (a plain `cd` takes `..` by the spelling, and every other call goes where the junction points) and a non-ASCII letter in other case (`Bäu` and `BÄU` are one directory on NTFS; `tr` folds A-Z only). `fc4e465d` then deleted the throwaway checkout at the build copy's real path, and replaced `dist`'s DLL with one stamped `unpinned` while it printed "dist left as it was" [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin3/verify/runs/u2-fc4e/`, `u3-fc4e/`, `pin3/verify/exp/probe-dotdot-win.log`]. `cd -P` and `test -ef` behave alike in Git Bash's bash 5.3, AlmaLinux 8's sh (bash 4.4 in POSIX mode) and Ubuntu's dash: through a symlink or a junction, `<link>/..`, other ASCII case and other non-ASCII case they name one directory, and `-ef` is false for a missing file [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/impl/exp/ef-win.log`, `exp/alma/probe.txt`, `exp/ubuntu/probe.txt`]. `physpath` and `inside`, taken from the script by their markers, pass 17 cases in all three shells, where `fc4e465d`'s pass 8 in Git Bash and 7 in the other two [M: `pin4/impl/exp/fn3-*.log`]; round 5's text passes those and four more with a pair that does not exist yet, 21 in all three shells, where `7c3c2683`'s pass 18 [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin5/fn/`, by `pin5/scripts/fn-test5.sh`]. On round 4's text each of these is refused before anything is created or removed, and again on round 5's [M: final-check2's `U2-*`, `U3-*` and `G3-*` lines, `pin5/final/run-impl/`], which AlmaLinux 8 replays end to end through symlinks and non-ASCII other case on `/mnt/c` (`ALMA-*`): an unpinned `OCLJ_LUAJIT` at the build copy's physical path, through `<junction>/..` and through non-ASCII other case, the throwaway intact; `OCLJ_OUT` physically `$OCLJ_BUILD/dist` through non-ASCII other case (with `dist` present, and with it not made yet, where `fc4e465d` made `dist` and linked a DLL stamped `unpinned` into it while it printed "dist left as it was" [M: `pin4/impl/ff-fc4e/summary.txt`]) and through `<junction>/../dist`, `dist` intact; an `OCLJ_BUILD` inside a scratch `OCLJ_REPO` outside its `build/`, directly, in other ASCII case and through a junction from outside, the scratch repository unchanged; and an `OCLJ_BUILD` under that repository's `build/` passes the guard [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/final/final-check.sh`, run into `pin4/final/run-impl/`, its `U2-*`, `U3-*` and `G3-*` lines]. Measured on `fc4e465d`, each refused with the checkout's or dist's md5 list unchanged: an unpinned build whose `OCLJ_LUAJIT` was a clone at `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit-windows-x86_64` (which `e1bf77df` deleted before failing to copy it, `pin2/verify/runs/w-selfsrc.log`), the same spelled `C:/USERS/ASTRO/...`, a junction to it, a clone at the export's path and one inside `layoutneg-<platform>`; an `OCLJ_BUILD` inside the checkout (not created); and `OCLJ_OUT` set to `dist`, unpinned and pinned, in other case through `LIBDIR/..`, and to a scratch repository's `build/native/dist` (`e1bf77df` replaced dist's DLL while printing "dist left as it was", `pin2/verify/runs/w-unpinned-outdist.log`) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin3/impl/fc4e-w4/`]. Round 4 wrote here that its identity comparison refused everything the string comparison had [I]; its review refuted that on Windows, with `OCLJ_OUT=<B>/j/../dist`, which `fc4e465d` refused and `7c3c2683` let through (the input directories, above) [M]. The guards compare what this shell reads; since round 5 the tools are handed the same physical paths, so `..` and a trailing dot or space can no longer be read two ways, which `T2-junction-dotdot`, `U2L-*`, `T1-trailing-dot` and the `-dot` lines measure [M]; other names Win32 may read otherwise are [open question 15](#open-questions)'s [I].
- **A pinned build** (`OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED` unset or empty, the default) makes every git call through one function, `ljgit` (`git --no-replace-objects --git-dir=<checkout's physical path>/.git -c core.autocrlf=false -c core.eol=lf`), and refuses, in this order: no git on PATH (`BUILD FAIL: no git on PATH`, which names the fix) [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/pin2/impl/v6/win-nogit.log`, and in AlmaLinux 8 `v6/alma-nogit-run.txt`, each with a PATH whose every other tool resolves; the previous script read `.git/HEAD` by hand without git and went on, `v6/win-nogit-pre.log`]; a checkout whose HEAD git cannot read; a HEAD that is not the pin's 40 digits, naming both and the file [M: `pin/impl/win-1/new-parent`, in AlmaLinux 8 `pin/impl/alma-1/parent`; and a `.git/HEAD` forged to `1ee778a4` plus 32 zeros on a clone at `8c23d6aa`, which `git rev-parse HEAD` prints back and the previous 8-digit comparison passed, `pin2/impl/v4/forged8-new.log` against `forged8-pre.log`]; a HEAD that names the pin in a `.git` without that commit (`git cat-file -e <pin>^{commit}`) [M: a clone with every ref to the pin deleted and pruned and HEAD forged to the pin, `v4/nopin-new.log` against `nopin-pre.log`; again `pin3/impl/fc4e-w2/f7-nopin.log`, and the 8-digit forgery `f7-8.log`]; a commit object that does not hash to the pin (link 1); and a commit object whose `tree` line disagrees with `rev-parse <pin>^{tree}` (link 2). git 2.55 itself refuses a forged commit or root tree wherever it parses one ("hash mismatch"), loose or packed, so a forged commit stops at `cat-file -e` and a forged packed root tree at link 2 [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-w2/forgeobj.log`, `forgepack.log`, `forgepacktree.log`; the pack writer is `pin3/impl/scripts/forge-pack.py`], as git 2.43.7 does a loose forged commit [M: `pin2/verify/exp/alma-forge.log`]; link 1 is shown to fail by a scratch copy of the script without the `cat-file -e` refusal, which on the packed forged commit stops at "hashes to '8c23d6aa...', not to its name" [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-w2ff/forgepack.log`]. It then prints `pin     = met: the checkout's HEAD is the pin, whose commit object hashes to it and names the tree 7a0348e8...; step 1 compiles that tree (git archive, checked), not the working tree`, and the fingerprint's commit is the pin's first 8 digits [S][M].
- **`OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED=1`** skips those checks (not the directory ones above). A seven-line warning on stderr, repeated after the `OK` line, names the checkout and its HEAD (`unknown` without git); the fingerprint's commit is the literal `unpinned`; step 1 builds the checkout's working tree; and an additive native is not collected into `dist`. `yes`, `0` and ` 1` are refused in the preflight, and an empty value builds pinned [M: `pin2/impl/preflight/`]. On the pin's parent `8c23d6aa` it built a complete DLL stamped `unpinned|LuaJIT 2.1.ROLLING|412be694|...` with the parent's `lj_opt_mem.o` (`05d34fc1`), and left `dist` as it was (md5 and mtime); gradle refused that DLL and accepted the pinned one from the same `OCLJ_BUILD` [M: `pin2/impl/v2/new-step1.log`, `pin2/impl/v5/`]. Without git it reaches step 1 [M: `pin2/impl/v6/win-nogit-new-unpinned.log`].
- **A canonical build** equals `build/native`'s: on Windows, the additive variant's 77 LuaJIT objects and `obj-windows-x86_64`'s three (`eris_lj.o` md5 `6fcb2dcd`), and the dropin's 77 and the three that the build-copy round's dropin made (`build/native`'s `jnlua.o` and `lj52shim.o` are the additive's, which differ by variant); in AlmaLinux 8 with `OCLJ_GLIBC_MAX=2.28`, both .so files are the release's byte for byte (additive `6c888050`, dropin `6c0715d1`) and the additive's 77 objects and three equal `build/native`'s, although both build copies are now LF ([7.3](#73-line-endings-and-the-git-bash-instrument)) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/pin3/impl/fc4e-w1/`, `fc4e-alma-w1/`, on `fc4e465d`; again on round 4's text, md5 `7c3c2683`, by `claude-tmp/oclj/pin4/final/final-check.sh` (its `W1-*` lines, `pin4/final/run-impl/`), each log saying `ignored from the environment: none`; and on round 5's, md5 `47b9c52e`, by `claude-tmp/oclj/pin5/final/final-check2.sh` (its `W1-*` lines, `pin5/final/run-impl/`, where the additive's `dist` copy is also compared by md5 with its own `libdir-additive` output), although `OCLJ_JNI` now reaches gcc by its physical path (in AlmaLinux 8 the `java-1.8.0-openjdk` symlink resolved)].

**Stage 1: the copy and the twelve changes** (:1032-1462) [S].

- **The build copy is made afresh on every run** (THE BUILD COPY, :1042-1196). `luajit-<platform>` and `luajit-export-<platform>` in `$OCLJ_BUILD` are removed first: they are this script's products, and no runner writes into them [S]. A pinned build then checks that every tree object under the pin's root tree hashes to its name (link 3: `ljgit ls-tree -r -d`, then `cat-file tree | hash-object -t tree` for each, 8 trees), writes `ljgit archive <tree>` to a tar file, unpacks it into `luajit-export-<platform>`, requires as many files there as `ls-tree -r` lists and each file's bytes to hash to its blob id (link 4: `hash-object --no-filters --stdin-paths` in the export), prints `export verified: the commit object hashes to the pin, 8 trees to their names, 232 files to the pin's blob ids`, compares the checkout's working tree with it, and copies it to `luajit-<platform>`. An unpinned build makes `luajit-<platform>` a fresh directory, copies the checkout's contents into it (`cp -R <checkout>/. <copy>/`, never the checkout's path itself), refuses if the copy is a symlink, removes its `.git`, and refuses a copy that holds any symlink. Keeping the copy (until 2026-10-20) saved one copy of an ~8 MB tree, since `make clean` rebuilds every object anyway, and it let another commit's files through (P44) [S][M].
- **The tree, not the commit.** `git archive <commit>` applies the tree's `export-subst` attribute, and LuaJIT's `.gitattributes` sets it on `.relver`, which then holds the commit's date, `1787165859`, where the committed file holds `$Format:%ct$`. With no `../.git`, `src/Makefile` copies `../.relver` into `luajit_relver.txt`, and `host/genversion.lua` puts the number into `LUAJIT_VERSION`: a build from the commit's archive said `LuaJIT 2.1.1787165859`, changed `host/buildvm.o`, `lib_jit.o`, `lj_dispatch.o`, `luajit.o`, `lj52shim.o` and `eris_lj.o`, and changed the blob fingerprint's second field [M: `pin2/impl/win-v1/commitarch-additive.log`, built by a scratch copy of the script that archives the commit; its working-tree check named `.relver`]. A tree carries no commit, so nothing is substituted, and the build says `LuaJIT 2.1.ROLLING` as before [S][M]. The tree archived is the one the pin's commit object names on its `tree` line (`7a0348e8`), by its id [S].
- **The export must be the pin's tree, byte for byte.** An `export-ignore` attribute drops files from `git archive`, so fewer files than `ls-tree -r` lists is a BUILD FAIL, from the tree's own `.gitattributes` (a scratch commit setting `/README export-ignore`, made the pin of a scratch `OCLJ_REPO`) and from `.git/info/attributes` alike: 231 files against 232 [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-w2/eitree.log`, `infoattr.log`]. Any conversion changes a file's bytes, so its hash: a smudge filter set through `.git/info/attributes` and the repo's config, or through the environment alone (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`), and a forged loose blob, are each a BUILD FAIL naming `src/lj_opt_mem.c` (`not the pin's bytes: src/lj_opt_mem.c`), and a forged loose subtree is one at link 3, naming the tree `2d90cb47` [M: `fc4e-w2/filter.log`, `envcfg.log`, `forgeblob.log`, `forgesub.log`; in AlmaLinux 8 the environment's filter, the forged blob and the forged subtree, `fc4e-alma-w2/`]. With `e1bf77df` the filter built the parent's `lj_opt_mem.o` under the stamp `1ee778a4`, and the environment's filter, the forged blob and the forged subtree exported the parent's file, every check passing [M: the build-copy round's review, `pin2/verify/runs/w-filter.log`, `pin2/verify/exp/probe-envcfg.log`, `probe-forgeblob.log`, `probe-forgesub.log`]. A replace ref of the pin by its parent is ignored: the pin builds (`42bd49f9`, and in AlmaLinux 8 the release .so `6c888050`), and the WARNING names `lj_opt_mem.c`, which the replaced checkout holds in the parent's version [M: `fc4e-w2/replace.log`, `fc4e-alma-w2/replace.log`; `e1bf77df` built `05d34fc1` stamped `1ee778a4` and gradle accepted it, `pin2/verify/runs/w-replace.log`, `pin2/verify/gruns/replace.log`]. LuaJIT's tree sets no conversion today, and the canonical build passes every link [M].
- **The working tree only feeds a WARNING.** `diff -rq --strip-trailing-cr <export> <checkout>`: a tracked file that differs, or that is missing from the checkout, is named in a WARNING on stderr (repeated after the `OK` line) and is not built; files only in the checkout (`.git`, the 2026-09-01 build products) and line-ending differences do not count. The parent's `lj_opt_mem.c` left uncommitted in a clone at the pin is named, and the pin's `lj_opt_mem.o` (`42bd49f9`) is built [M: `pin2/impl/v3/new-dirty.log`; HEAD's script built `05d34fc1` from it, `v3/head-dirty.log`]. In AlmaLinux 8 the same clone builds the pin's Linux object (`a96c8af7`, `build/native`'s), and an unpinned build of `8c23d6aa` (stamped `unpinned`, `lj_opt_mem.o` `9d253773`, no `dist`) followed by a pinned one in the same `OCLJ_BUILD` gives the release .so `6c888050` and all 77 objects [M: `pin2/impl/alma-v23/`]. A deleted doc file is named; an untracked file and a README converted to LF are not [M: `v3/classes.log`]. A clone at `8c23d6aa` whose `.git/HEAD` is forged to the pin's full 40 digits builds the pin, with `lj_opt_mem.c` named [M: `v4/forged40-new.log`]. A clone at the pin with `src/lj_record.c` deleted builds the pin, all 77 objects canonical, with `Only in <export>/src: lj_record.c` in the WARNING [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-w4/n1.log`; `e1bf77df` refused it in the preflight, "no LuaJIT source", `pin2/verify/runs/w-norecord.log`]. The pristine checkout reads clean [M]. Again on the final text: the dirty clone builds `42bd49f9` and all 77+3 objects canonical, naming `lj_opt_mem.c`, and in AlmaLinux 8 the release .so [M: `fc4e-w2/dirty.log`, `fc4e-alma-w2/dirty.log`]; an unpinned build of `8c23d6aa` and then a pinned one in one `OCLJ_BUILD` give `05d34fc1` stamped `unpinned` with no `dist` and no export, then `42bd49f9`, all objects canonical, collected into `dist` [M: `fc4e-w2f1/`].
- **The CHECKHOOK option** is checked in the tree that is compiled, `$LJ_SRC/src/lj_record.c` (the export, or the checkout when unpinned), right after the copy (:1197-1206); the preflight only requires the checkout to be a directory. Until 2026-10-20 it was a preflight check of the checkout's working tree [S].
- **The unpinned copy** (since 2026-10-20) never copies the checkout's path itself: GNU cp 8.30's `cp -r <symlink> <copy>` made the copy a symlink, and removing its `.git` then deleted the real checkout's (the build-copy round's review, `pin2/verify/exp/cpsym-alma.log`). With `mkdir <copy>; cp -R <checkout>/. <copy>/`, an unpinned build through a symlink to a throwaway clone left that clone's `.git` unchanged (30 files, md5 list equal) and built a real copy without `.git`, in AlmaLinux 8, and on Windows through a junction and through a native symlink [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-alma-w3/`, `fc4e-w3win/`]. A symlink inside the copy is refused too: `cp -R` keeps it, and the `rm -f src/host/*.o` that `make clean` runs deleted, through a symlinked `src/host`, the files of the directory it named (a probe); a throwaway clone whose `src/host` was a symlink is refused, its target unchanged [M: `fc4e-alma-w3/alma-summary.txt`].
- `lj_gc.h`'s collector-state enum is checked on the build copy as soon as it is made (:1207-1226), so the gate reads the file that is compiled [S].
- On every run, for each of the twelve changed files ([1.2](#12-our-twelve-changes-to-luajit)): assert the pristine source (the export, or the checkout when unpinned) does not carry the change; re-copy the pristine file over the build copy; assert the change is **absent** after the copy; run the patch script; assert the marker is **present the exact number of times** before make. The counts: the scrub line once, the clamp line twice, the `lj_ffrecord.c` marker once, `#include "oclj_features.h"` once and directly after `lua.h`, the strpack marker and its `#include` once each, and each round-3 file its own marker once, no other round-3 marker, and each new `#include` once.
- Our seven files are copied and compared with `cmp`; their presence in the pristine source is refused [S].
- Stage 1 refuses any architecture but x86_64 (:1418-1420) [S].
- Then `make -C $LJ clean`, then `make -C $LJ BUILDMODE=static XCFLAGS="$LJ_FLAGS" Q= -j$JOBS` (:1457-1462) [S].

**Running it outside `build/`**, for agents and for any experiment the gates or negctl must see [S] (build-native.sh EXTERNAL INPUTS and USAGE, ~:112-182):

```sh
OCLJ_BUILD=<fresh scratch dir> OCLJ_JNLUA=<OC-JNLua checkout> OCLJ_JNI=<JDK include dir> \
  PATH=<dir holding make>:$PATH sh native/build-native.sh > <log> 2>&1
```

- Round 3's values for `OCLJ_JNLUA`, `OCLJ_JNI` and `PATH` are in `bench/runs/2026-10-17-bitop/scripts/build-r3.sh`; `OCLJ_VARIANT=additive` builds the other library. Compare like with like: without `OCLJ_VARIANT` the build is the dropin, whose `lj52shim.o` and `jnlua.o` differ from `build/native`'s additive ones (`eris_lj.o` is equal) [M: the CRC32 round's docs verifier and its fixer, md5s of the implementer's dropin `b-impl`], so "only `lj_opt_fold.o` differs" holds for the 77 LuaJIT objects in either variant and for all 80 only with `OCLJ_VARIANT=additive` [M: the CRC32 round's implementer; its final check builds additive and compares all 80, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/impl/fc-smoke-1/summary.txt`, `W-objects`].
- `OCLJ_LUAJIT` (default `prototype/watchdog/luajit`, relative to `OCLJ_REPO`) must be a git checkout whose HEAD is the pin. A scratch repository made with `git archive` has no `prototype/watchdog/luajit`, so a build from one names `OCLJ_LUAJIT` [S][M: design.md gap 13]. For a build at another commit, or of an edit to upstream's files, clone the pristine tree into scratch (`git clone --no-hardlinks prototype/watchdog/luajit <scratch>`, then `git -C <scratch> checkout <commit>` or edit), never touch the pristine tree itself, and set `OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED=1`. Its own `OCLJ_BUILD`, always: an unpinned build replaces `luajit-<platform>`, `obj-<platform>`, `libdir*` and `luajit_build.log` there, which the runners and negctl read. It no longer reaches `dist`, and the next pinned build in that `OCLJ_BUILD` remakes the copy from the pin [M: `pin2/impl/v2/`, `pin3/impl/fc4e-w2f1/`]. Keep the clone outside that `OCLJ_BUILD`'s `luajit-<platform>`, `luajit-export-<platform>` and `layoutneg-<platform>`, and the `OCLJ_BUILD` outside the clone, and outside the repository (and outside the repository a copy of the script sits in) unless it is under that repository's `build/`: the preflight refuses each, by identity, before anything is created or removed. On Windows, spell no directory with a name ending in a dot or a space: it is refused.
- Every run makes the build copy afresh, checks the export and builds everything: 31-36 s on Windows and 13-14 s in AlmaLinux 8 here; a refused checkout or export fails in 1-4 s [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-w1/`, `fc4e-alma-w1/`, `fc4e-w2/`]. The time follows the machine's load: the CRC32 round's Windows builds took 31.5 s on a quiet machine, 37-39 s, and 48 s while another session's tests ran [M: design.md gap 29; the implementer's report]. A chain of builds longer than a minute runs from the main session ([11.11](#1111-long-runs-and-results-directories)).
- On Linux, `OCLJ_GLIBC_MAX=2.28` makes step 6's glibc check a BUILD FAIL above the release floor. Set it on any build whose .so may be packaged; leave it unset for a development build on a newer distribution, which then reports the highest version needed and finishes ([7.7](#77-the-linux-path)) [S]. On Windows it only prints a note [M].
- Point every runner and negctl-bitop.sh at the same `OCLJ_BUILD`. negctl writes its work directory (about 445 MB for round 3's 27 arms, 878 MB for the CRC32 round's 30-arm sweep, [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)) under `$OCLJ_BUILD/negctl-bitop/` (no other knob, ~:916) and needs `obj-<platform>/` (`lj52shim.o`, `eris_lj.o`) as well as the LuaJIT copy (~:368-389). Its setup checks each patched file against its script run on the pristine file, which it takes from the build's export, `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit-export-<platform>/src`, when that exists (the last build was pinned) and from `OCLJ_LUAJIT`'s working tree otherwise, and it prints which; negctl-pack.sh's unregistered arm does the same for `lib_string.c`. Until 2026-10-20 both read `OCLJ_LUAJIT`'s working tree, so one uncommitted comment line in the checkout's `lj_opt_fold.c` failed negctl-bitop's setup with "rebuild with build-native.sh", which no pinned rebuild could cure [M: `pin2/verify/runs/negctl-N2.log`]. On a canonical pinned build, negctl-bitop's setup, canon and the SARCONST arm pass with a clean checkout and with that dirty one, naming the export, in 80-81 s, and negctl-pack's canon and unregistered arm pass (`OCLJ_NEGCTL_ARMS=unregistered`, which runs canon and that arm alone, 7 s); after an unpinned build the setup names `OCLJ_LUAJIT`'s working tree [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-w5/`].

**Freshness gates** (:1465-1563) [S]:

- Each patched object must be newer (`-nt`) than its source and every round-3 file it includes: lj_parse.o, lj_meta.o, lj_record.o, lj_opt_fold.o, lj_bcwrite.o, lj_bcread.o, lib_jit.o, lib_string.o, lj_func.o, lj_err.o, lj_ffrecord.o.
- Each object's compile line must be in the log, anchored as `-c -o X[.]o X[.]c[[:cntrl:]]?$`. A bare file name would also match buildvm's five command lines, which list every LJLIB_C file. The `[[:cntrl:]]?` is there because mingw32-make writes 95 of the log's 185 lines with CRLF [M].
- `host/buildvm_arch.h` must be newer than vm_x64.dasc, and DynASM's command line must be in the log. lj_vm.o must be newer than buildvm_arch.h; on ELF, lj_vm.S must sit between them.
- buildvm_arch.h must echo exactly 3 `//|` lines carrying `OC-LuaJIT`, 2 of them `OC-LuaJIT: Lua 5.3's`.
- `lj_ffdef.h` must list `FFDEF(string_pack|packsize|unpack)` once each.
- **`-f` comes before `-nt`**, because `[ a -nt b ]` is true when b is missing (:1528; P15) [S][M].

**Feature-channel gates** (:1565-1601) [S]:

- No `-D LUAJIT_*` may appear on any compile line except `LUAJIT_TARGET` and `LUAJIT_UNWIND_EXTERNAL`, which are platform facts.
- `gcc -I$LJ -E -dM lj_arch.h` must say `OCLJ_LUAJIT_FEATURES 1`, `LJ_52 1`, `LJ_HASFFI 0`, `LJ_HASJIT 1`, `LJ_GC64 1`, `OCLJ_LIB_STRPACK 1` and `OCLJ_OPS_BIT64 1` (~:1586); `lj_bcdump.h -dM` must say `BCDUMP_VERSION 0x80`. `LJ_DUALNUM`, `LJ_FR2`, `LJ_ABI_WIN` and `LJ_UNWIND_EXT` are not in this list ([1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is)).

**Warning gates** [S]: the LuaJIT compile is `-Wall`, and no warning may name a patched or added file. Our C also compiles under `-Wall -Wextra` in two throwaway translation units, `strpack_wgate.c` and `bitop_wgate.c`, with zero warnings; each calls the entry points, because an uncalled static inline function is never compiled. A new static function that is not inline must be called there too, or `-Wunused-function` fails the gate: rg2's prototype added `oclj_rangeguard53` to `oclj_bitop_fold.c` and needed a one-line change to `bitop_wgate.c` (not shipped) [M: proto-rg2, design.md gap 23]. FIX-NUMCONV's `oclj_isnumconv` is the first helper added to an existing `oclj_*` file since round 3 to ship this way, a `static int` like round 3's `oclj_bitwidth` (which `bitop_wgate.c` has called since round 3) and not `LJ_AINLINE` (the user's decision), and `bitop_wgate.c` calls it (build-native.sh ~:1668) [S]. A planted `-Wsign-compare` inside it, and the call removed (`'oclj_isnumconv' defined but not used [-Wunused-function]`), each fail the build with `BUILD FAIL: round 3's C files must compile warning-clean` [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/impl-code/logs/build-wg-a.log`, `build-wg-b.log`, builds of scratch repository copies].

**luajit.exe gates** (:1681-2063), each a probe with an exact wanted line [S]:

- `table.unpack` exists (LUA52COMPAT); a count hook fires over a hot loop (CHECKHOOK, more than 100 ticks).
- FFI / integer-format: `lit=refused preload=nil loaded=nil s=0/1/0 x=1/0/0 same=true`.
- string.pack: `... hot=14000 jit=0/1/0`.
- **bitops**: `fold=4294967296/-1/0 kx=X>> rt=4294967296/X>>/X>>/9007199254740991/X>>/0/X~/X| err=noint/1/17/type/noint/X>> sar=-2/-1/8 dump=128 bit=1 hot=X>>@151 acc=43800 jit=1/0/0 same=true` [M] (re-run on today's luajit.exe: identical).
- **jit tools**: `jitlib f=1 hot=206208 snapend=4278190080 bc=118 bl=118 blerr=0 bldec=3 jd=1/1/0/0 jdb=12/1 jdim=1/1 jdline=1`, run in `$LJ` with `LUA_PATH='./?.lua'`.
- The JIT gates count loop, stitch and abort traces in the probed function, so they fail with the JIT off.

**Stage 1b** (:2065-2161) [S]: `lua_newstate` with a plain realloc allocator must work (GC64 accepts a foreign allocator); its first allocation must equal `sizeof(GG_State)` as seen through `-I$LJ`, the view the shim and the serializer compile with: 6376 [M]. The **negative arm** recompiles the probe against a header copy with `LUAJIT_DISABLE_FFI` removed and must mismatch: `GG-LAYOUT-MISMATCH lib=6376 tu=6384` on every build [M].

**Stages 2-6** [S]: the shim (`-Wall -Wextra`, zero warnings) and jnlua.c compile; `eris_lj.o` carries the VM-features word, which must agree token by token with luajit.exe's behaviour (today `ffi0.lua52.gc64.fr2.dn0.le.p64.pack1.bit64`, and luajit.exe agrees [M]); the link; then the ABI surface is pinned (PE: exactly 87 `Java_*` + JNI_OnLoad + JNI_OnUnload = 89 names for dropin, 91/93 for additive; ELF: the `Java_*` count). On ELF, step 6 then runs the ELF import gate ([7.7](#77-the-linux-path)), before the library is stamped `OK` and, for additive, collected into `dist`, unless the build is unpinned (COLLECT ADDITIVE ARTIFACTS, :2635-2661) [S].

### 7.7 The Linux path

Same script, with these differences [S]:

- The copy is `luajit-linux-x86_64`, and `PICFLAG=-fPIC` reaches XCFLAGS and our compiles.
- `buildvm -m elfasm` writes lj_vm.S, which gcc assembles; the freshness gate adds lj_vm.S between buildvm_arch.h and lj_vm.o.
- The Makefile's unwind probe adds `-DLUAJIT_UNWIND_EXTERNAL`, which the `-D` allowlist permits.
- DynASM loses `-D WIN`, and gets `-D SHADOW_STACK` where the compiler defaults to CET: Ubuntu 26.04's gcc 15.2 does, AlmaLinux 8's gcc 8.5.0 does not ([6.8](#68-the--d-flags)) [M].
- The binary is `luajit` and the library `libjnlua*52-linux-x86_64.so`.
- **The link** is `-lm $LINK_EXTRA` with `LINK_EXTRA="-static-libgcc -pthread -Wl,--no-undefined"` (build-native.sh :422; the reasons at :383-406) [S]. Before glibc 2.34 the watchdog's six pthread functions live in libpthread; a link without `-pthread` left them undefined with no symbol version and no NEEDED `libpthread.so.0`, so they bound to whatever the host process had loaded [M]. With `-pthread` they are versioned (`pthread_create@GLIBC_2.2.5`); with `--no-undefined`, leaving it out fails the link, naming the six [M]. Not `-ldl`: nothing we link reaches `lib_package`, and the .so imports none of libdl's functions (`dlopen`, `dlsym`, `dlclose`, `dlerror`); its one `dl_` import, `dl_iterate_phdr`, is libc's, and `libdl.so.2` is not NEEDED [M] (`readelf --dyn-syms -W` on both release .so files, 2026-10-20). It comes from libgcc's unwinder, linked in by `-static-libgcc` [I].
- **The ELF import gate** runs in step 6, between `# BEGIN ELF IMPORT GATE` and `# END ELF IMPORT GATE` (:2526-2627), with readelf, whose `--dyn-syms -W` and `-V -W` output reads the same under binutils 2.30 and 2.46 [S][M]. In order: readelf must list some undefined symbol (on a truncated file it lists none and exits 0); a non-weak undefined symbol with no version is a BUILD FAIL whatever the limit, naming each; the version needs (`.gnu.version_r`) give the highest GLIBC_x.y, compared component by component (2.4 < 2.17 < 2.38). With `OCLJ_GLIBC_MAX` set, a need above it, or outside GLIBC_x.y (GLIBC_PRIVATE, GCC_x.y), is a BUILD FAIL listing the needs, their libraries and up to 12 imports, and nothing reaches `dist`. Unset, it reports the verdict against the release floor, `GLIBC_FLOOR=2.28` (:469), and the build finishes [S]. Its awk ran under gawk only, in both distros [M]; other awks are untested [I].
- **The test links** take `-pthread -ldl`: every runner's `TEST_EXTRA`, `negative-control.sh`'s six links and `serializer/Makefile`'s `LIBS`. `luaL_openlibs` pulls LuaJIT's `lib_package`, which calls `dlopen`, and before glibc 2.34 both libraries are outside libc [S][M].

**Where to build.** Each import carries the newest version of its symbol in the glibc of the host that *links* it, so the build host's glibc caps what the .so can need (P49) [S][M]. The supported floor is a policy on top of that: 2.28, which gradle enforces; today's release .so needs only GLIBC_2.17, and systems below 2.28 are untested and unsupported [M][S]:

| host (WSL distro) | glibc, gcc, binutils | the .so needs | use |
|---|---|---|---|
| AlmaLinux 8.10 (`AlmaLinux-8`) | 2.28, 8.5.0, 2.30 | at most GLIBC_2.17; NEEDED `libpthread.so.0` [M] | the release .so, into `build/native`, with `OCLJ_GLIBC_MAX=2.28` and `OCLJ_JNI=/usr/lib/jvm/java-1.8.0-openjdk/include` |
| Ubuntu 26.04.1 (`Ubuntu`) | 2.43, 15.2, 2.46 | GLIBC_2.38 (`fmod`, `__isoc23_strtoul`) [M] | development builds only, each in its own `OCLJ_BUILD`; gradle refuses their .so |

- **Driving it from Git Bash**: `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 wsl.exe -d AlmaLinux-8 -- sh /mnt/c/<script>`, with every command in the script file, and every variable passed inside (`-- env X=1 sh ...`) (P51) [M].
- **The commit in the blob fingerprint** is the pin's first 8 digits for a pinned build, which needs git and compiles the pin's tree, and the literal `unpinned` for an `OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED=1` build (build-native.sh :927-1022; [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) [S]. So a native never stamps `unknown`, and the release .so stamps `1ee778a4`, as the Windows DLL does [M] (the glibc round's `logs/build-alma/glibc.txt`; `pin2/impl/alma-v1/`). AlmaLinux 8 had no git when the glibc round began, and its first builds stamped `unknown`; `git_head` then read `.git/HEAD` by hand when git could not run (until 2026-10-20). It has git 2.43.7 now, and a pinned build without git is refused there as on Windows [M: `pin2/impl/v6/alma-nogit-run.txt`]; `git_head` (:869-926, with `ljgit`) only reports an unpinned checkout's and OC-JNLua's commits now; a pinned build reads its checkout's HEAD through `ljgit`. In AlmaLinux 8 a checkout at `8c23d6aa` is refused as on Windows [M: `pin/impl/alma-1/parent`].
- **Binaries do not cross downwards.** Ubuntu's `luajit` and the Ubuntu-built PUC 5.3.6 both stop in AlmaLinux 8 with ``version `GLIBC_2.38' not found`` among others [M]. In AlmaLinux 8, the gates and the kernel patcher use the `luajit` built there, and `run-bitop.sh` and `run-pack.sh` need a PUC 5.3.6 built there. This machine has one at `/mnt/c/Users/astro/claude-tmp/oclj/puc536-alma/lua` (md5 `12136059`), built from `bench/vendor/lua-5.3.6/src`'s `*.c`, `*.h` and Makefile only, because that directory also holds Windows objects, with `make generic MYCFLAGS="-DLUA_USE_POSIX -DLUA_USE_DLOPEN" MYLIBS="-Wl,-E -ldl"`. Its outputs equal the Ubuntu-built PUC's on 20 reference runs, apart from one error text that names the interpreter's path [M]. Pass it as `OCLJ_LUA53`: the runners' default is the Windows `lua.exe`, which passes `-x` on `/mnt/c` (P52) [S].
- **Packaging.** gradle's `verifyModAssets` reads the same version needs from the file itself and refuses a .so above `glibcFloor` (`build.gradle.kts` :74, the check :282-529). It also reads `.dynsym` and `.gnu.version` (~:406-522) and refuses a non-weak import without a version, so a stale .so linked without `-pthread` cannot ship either; its passing line counts the imports [S][M: `bench/runs/2026-10-20-gradle-imports/`].
- **The pinned commit, at packaging.** Before those, right after the serializer-and-word check, `verifyModAssets` reads every native's fingerprint commit field and refuses one that is not the pin's first 8 digits (THE NATIVE MUST HAVE BEEN BUILT FROM THE PINNED LUAJIT, `build.gradle.kts` :190-245) [S]. Its message names `unpinned` as an `OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED=1` build of a working tree; it refused the unpinned DLL of [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage) and accepted the pinned one [M: `pin2/impl/v5/gruns/`]. It checks a name, not code: that the name stands for the pin's source tree, changed by our twelve patch scripts and compiled with build-native.sh's own flags, is build-native.sh's part (the toolchain is not pinned, [1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is)). The field is the whole run of printable characters, `|` excepted, directly before `|LuaJIT <version>|<8 hex digits>|`, at every place that shape occurs; a native in which it occurs nowhere is refused too, and so is a malformed or missing pin file [S][M]. It accepts the release pair and refuses `1ee778a4` edited in place to `1ee778a5` (in the .so or the DLL, alone or beside a good native), a real DLL built at `8c23d6aa`, the AlmaLinux 8 trial .so (`unknown`), a native whose shape is broken and one whose field has a printable byte glued on; HEAD's gradle accepted every one of those but the trial .so, which its import check refused. With the pin file set to `8c23d6aa`, the `8c23d6aa` natives pass and the release .so is refused [M: `pin/impl/gruns-new-1`, `gruns-head-1`, `gruns-pin-*`]. Of the gradle-imports round's 19 cases, 17 keep their verdicts and messages; `u01-trial` and `u04-alma-new` stamp `unknown`, so the commit check now fires first (u04 was accepted before) [M: `pin/impl/gruns-new-u`].

Verified 2026-10-17 in WSL (Ubuntu 26.04.1, gcc 15.2.0, glibc 2.43): every gate printed the Windows line, and `negctl-bitop.sh` passed all 27 arms in 6 min 20 s [M] (logs/linux/). Verified 2026-10-19 in AlmaLinux 8.10 (gcc 8.5.0, glibc 2.28) with this section's link and gate: both variants pass every gate, every non-weak import is versioned, and the .so needs at most GLIBC_2.17. The native suites, run-pack, run-bitop and the serializer suite pass there, and the JVM harness passed 82/0 on that .so in AlmaLinux 8 and in Ubuntu 26.04 [M] (the glibc round's evidence; the roadmap's glibc row and its Linux-JVM row). Verified again by the round's chain on the release build (`bench/runs/2026-10-19-glibc/logs`): the .so `6c888050` (dropin `6c0715d1`) needs at most GLIBC_2.17; in AlmaLinux 8 `negctl-bitop.sh` and `negctl-pack.sh` pass, and the seven harness arms pass under JDK 8 with the milestones of the Windows chain, line for line [M]. `negative-control.sh`'s first full run there failed 5 of its 56 RESULT lines, from two faults in the test (a Windows-only sabotage and a shell-assignment leak under bash 4.4's POSIX mode); fixed, it passes 57 of 57 in AlmaLinux 8, Ubuntu and Windows [M] (the roadmap's glibc row, open item 2).

## 8. Recipes

### 8.1 Adding or changing an operator end to end

Worked through round 3 (commit `2dd5f05`; evidence in [bench/runs/2026-10-17-bitop/](../bench/runs/2026-10-17-bitop/README.md)). An operator touches every stage of [2](#2-the-pipeline), and each stage has a file, a build gate, a test and a sabotage arm. Do the steps in this order; each later step assumes the earlier ones.

| step | what | round 3's file | held by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Write the semantics down, against PUC 5.3.6, with the user's decisions | `design/design.md` (U1-U10), `design/design-B.md` (decided) | the review workflow |
| 1 | The rules, once, with no Lua state | `native/luajit/oclj_bitop.h`, `oclj_num53.h` | NEGSHIFT, SARSHR, RANGE-HI arms |
| 2 | The parser's constant folding | `patch-bitop-parse.sh` (lj_parse.c) | t_fold; WRAP32-FOLD, NOINT-FOLD, INEXACT-FOLD |
| 3 | The interpreter's fast path | `patch-bitop-vm.sh` (vm_x64.dasc) | t_ops, fuzz V; WRAP32-INTERP, SHIFTMASK, INEXACT-INTERP, INEXACT-BNOT |
| 4 | The interpreter's slow path | `oclj_bitop_meta.c` + `patch-bitop-meta.sh` (lj_meta.c) | t_ops-str, coerce; STRCOERCE, ERROPERAND, INEXACT-SLOW |
| 5 | The recorder | `oclj_bitop_rec.c` + `patch-bitop-record.sh` (lj_record.c) | t_jit2, t_rec, gen; NOINT-JIT, SARCONST, INEXACT-JIT |
| 6 | The fold engine | `patch-bitop-fold.sh` (lj_opt_fold.c) + `oclj_bitop_fold.c` | gen, t_rec, fuzz U, p4v; NARROW, KFOLD64, SELFGUARD, WIDTH55, WSHR, WBAND, WADD0, WSHL0, FIX1 |
| 7 | The bytecode's meaning and the blob fingerprint | `patch-bitop-bcdump.sh` (0x80); `OCLJ_OPS_BIT64` in `oclj_features.h`; the `bit64` token in `serializer/eris_lj.c` | security FF12, the bitops gate's `dump=` field, stage 4; BCDUMP, WORDTOKEN |
| 8 | LuaJIT's own tools | `patch-bitop-jitlib.sh` (lib_jit.c), because `jit/bc.lua` decodes with these operators (P9) | the jit tools gate |
| 9 | The build | build-native.sh: re-copy, patch, marker counts, freshness gates for every include, the echo gate, the `-dM` feature gate, `bitop_wgate.c`, the bitops and jit tools gates | the gates' own fail-firsts |
| 10 | The tests | `test/native/run-bitop.sh` (matrix + provenance + `bitop_test.c`), `run-bitjit.sh` (gen, floor, fix1), `run-bitfuzz.sh` | [11](#11-verification-discipline) |
| 11 | The proof | fail-firsts on round 2's build (`failfirst/`, `failfirst2/`); `negctl-bitop.sh`, 27 arms, on Windows and Linux | [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages), [11.8](#118-fail-first) |
| 12 | The evidence and the docs | `bench/runs/2026-10-17-bitop/` (README, design, logs, md5s); roadmap row 208; this guide | the review |

[S] for the files; [M] for the arms and catchers (negctl-bitop.sh header, logs/suite-native-2/, logs/linux-negctl/).

Lessons from round 3 that apply to the next operator:

- **Decide what a value the double cannot hold does** before writing code. U1-A (round) and U1-B (raise) need different guards, different tests and different arms; the rule went into one header and every C path calls it ([9.9](#99-one-source-of-truth-for-every-c-path)) [S].
- **The parser must decline to fold anything that raises**, so the error happens at run time with a line number, as 5.3.6's `validop` does [S].
- **Check the idioms people write, not single operators.** The design's claim that masked code matches 5.3 held for each operator and failed for the rotate idiom; the harness found it after every unit suite had passed (P16) [M].
- **A tool that uses the operators is a client too.** `jit/bc.lua` broke under 64-bit `>>` and made `-jdump` abort the traces it recorded, which looked like "the JIT does not compile bit operators" (P9) [M].
- **Same bytecode, new meaning means a new dump version**: `BCDUMP_F_BITOP` cannot carry it, because a folded constant leaves no bit opcode ([2.3](#23-bytecode-and-the-dump-version)) [M].

### 8.2 Writing a patch script

0. **Changing a file that a script already patches**, as the CRC32 follow-up did with `lj_opt_fold.c` and the select fix with `lj_record.c`: extend that file's script; never add a second one [S]. The select fix's change is not a bit operator's, and it still went into `patch-bitop-record.sh`, the one script that patches `lj_record.c` ([1.2](#12-our-twelve-changes-to-luajit)); a second script for the file would have needed `BITOP_PATCHES`' loop redesigned, negctl's `PATCHED` list and this guide's 1.2 [S: the select round's implementer].
   - build-native.sh re-copies and patches each file once, and asserts exactly one `OC-LuaJIT (native/luajit/patch-bitop-` marker in it (`BITOP_PATCHES` and its loop, ~:1423-1449). negctl-bitop.sh rebuilds each patched file from the pristine one with that one `patch-bitop-<x>.sh` (~:931-938).
   - **Keep the marker count.** New hunks whose comments say `OC-LuaJIT:` without the script's path leave `NMARK` at 1, so build-native.sh's marker assertion needs no change; the select fix's hunks 4 and 5 do this [S].
   - Add hunks, and update `NH`, `NADD`, `NDEL` and `verify_content`.
   - **Moving text from one hunk to another changes NADD.** The table-index fold put its rule in `simplify_conv_int_num` (pristine ~:1129), above round 3's include, so its hunk 11 took the five-line `#include "oclj_bitop_fold.c"` block out of hunk 7's `n7` and put it above itself: NADD went from 45 to 45 - 5 + 23 = 63, NDEL stayed 8 [S][M: the script's own count, `crc2/docs/pc/patch.log`]. Two changes to one script merge with additive NADDs: the combined prototype with rg2's rule had 45 + 18 + 8 = 71 [M: crc/combine `ps/`]. FIX-NUMCONV added seven lines to n7 (a five-line comment, the call and its return) and changed one word of n7's own comment, which NADD does not see: 63 + 7 = 70 [S][M: the script on the pin's blob, `70 lines added, 8 removed, of 2715`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/docs/pc/patch.log`]. The CSE-twin fix's hunk 12 replaced `lj_opt_cse`'s two-line match (o12) with seven lines (n12): a four-line comment, the `if` ending in `&&` and the type test added, upstream's one-line `if` removed, and the return kept, so 70 + 6 = 76 and 8 + 1 = 9 [S][M: the script on the pin's blob, `76 lines added, 9 removed, of 2720`, and on `a4c0645`'s `lj_opt_fold.c`, `of 2730`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/runs/fc-ffpatch-1/results.txt`]. Its comment opens with `OC-LuaJIT:` without the script's path, so `NMARK` stays 1.
   - For `lj_opt_fold.c`, `verify_content` (patch-bitop-fold.sh ~:518-576) requires `oclj_bitwidth(` on exactly two lines, each rule's call once (`<= 32)` and `<= 54)`), and the order include, `simplify_conv_int_num`, its call, `simplify_conv_i64_num`, its call [S]. Since FIX-NUMCONV (~:533-544, the comment ~:474-488) it also requires `oclj_isnumconv(` on exactly one line, the call on `fleft->op1`, and that call below the width rule's call and above the `  }` that ends the rules' block directly above `simplify_conv_i64_num`'s `PHIBARRIER`, so inside the block, where `fleft` is a `CONV num.i64` and the folded conversion is int64; and the width rule's call and FIX-NUMCONV's each followed directly by their own `      return fleft->op1;` (~:541-544), so neither call sits nested in the other's unbraced `if` [S]. That last check was added by the round's fixer: an n7 that put FIX-NUMCONV's call directly under the width rule's `if`, which makes the next `return` unconditional, passed every other check (the code verifier's mutation m11); with it the script refuses that n7, rc 1, where the script before it took it, rc 0, and evaluated on m11 it fails alone [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/fix/ff-nest/`, `fix/vc-check-1.txt`]. The last link is tighter than the prototype's check, which stopped at `PHIBARRIER`: the prototype's script passes the call moved between that `  }` and `PHIBARRIER` (FF9), which the shipped one refuses (FF3b) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/impl-code/ff-patch-2/results.txt`]. Both checks beyond the prototype's, the call inside the rules' block and each call followed directly by its own return, are kept, by the main session's decision of 2026-10-22; nothing about them is open. With every hunk's text matched, the `oclj_isnumconv(` count fails alone on a stray line; on an edit of n7 the order fails alone on each misplaced call, and the call's text (another operand) and the count (a second call, or none) fail first, the order with them [M: the same file's part B, every check evaluated with a non-exiting fail]. The name is written without `(` in n7's comment, which the count would otherwise count. A count of the form "`oclj_bitwidth(` on N lines" counts comments too, so word comments around such names [S: design.md gap 22]. The script's comment above `verify_content` says which of these checks fail alone and which the others imply (the include count and the two calls' counts, kept for their messages); an implied check proves nothing alone ([11.8](#118-fail-first)).
   - For hunk 12, since the CSE-twin fix (2026-10-23; patch-bitop-fold.sh ~:545-575, the comment ~:489-517), `verify_content` requires the type test `irt_isi64(IR(ref)->t) == irt_isi64(fins->t))`, stripped of leading blanks, as a whole line exactly once; `irt_isi64(IR(ref)->t)` named on that line only; the line between `lj_opt_cse`'s definition line and `lj_opt_cselim`'s; the line above it exactly `      if (IR(ref)->op12 == op12 &&` and the line below it a tab and `return TREF(ref, irt_t(IR(ref)->t));  /* Common subexpression found. */`; and, between the two definition lines, `op12 == op12` on exactly one line and `return` on exactly two, hunk 12's return of the common subexpression and upstream's return of the instruction it emits [S]. So the forward-allowing and reverse-allowing forms (an `||` in the test), a whole-type compare, the test dropped, moved into `lj_opt_cselim`, put after the return or under an `||`, and a second match anywhere in `lj_opt_cse` are each refused, rc 1, with its own message ([11.8](#118-fail-first)) [M]. The two counts were added by the round's fixer after the code verifier's finding: the checks of hunk 12's three lines alone passed an n12 that added a second match beside them, rc 0 with the success line: m1 and m2, a forward- and a reverse-allowing match line above the `if` (78/9), m3, an `else if` after the return (77/9), and m4, a one-line reverse match with the comment cut to three lines, still 76/9, so no line count saw it [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/verify-code/runs/ffpatch-1/`]. The verifier proposed counting the one return text; the fixer counts every `return`, which also refuses m5, a reverse match spelled `op1 == fins->op1 && op2 == fins->op2` with its own return, and added m6, a stray `if (... op12 == op12 && !irt_isi64(fins->t)) break;`: the script before the counts passes m1-m6, rc 0, and the final one refuses each, rc 1, with its own message, and both refuse c5 (CSEFWD's text) and c6 (the reverse-allowing form, an or with `irt_isi64(IR(ref)->t)`) at the type test's whole-line count [M: `csetwin/round/fix/runs/ffpatch-1`, `-2`, `fc-ffpatch-1`; [11.8](#118-fail-first)]. Which fail alone (the script's comment): on mutations of a patched file, evaluated with a non-exiting fail, the `irt_isi64(IR(ref)->t)` count alone on a one-line copy in `lj_opt_cselim`, the order alone on the test moved there with its two neighbours, the neighbours alone on the test after the return and on an `if` ending in `||`, the match count alone on a stray line naming `op12 == op12` (m6's), and the return count alone on m5's; implied and kept for their messages, the whole-line count and the missing check [M: scratch `csetwin/round/impl-code/ff-patch-3/results.txt`; `fix/runs/fc-ffpatch-1/results.txt`]. Not checked: a macro that redefines `irt_isi64` inside n12 [I].
   - For `lj_record.c`, since the select fix, `verify_content` (patch-bitop-record.sh ~:351-385, the select checks ~:365-384) requires `OCLJ_SELECT_KMAX` on exactly three lines (its definition, the record-time bound, the guard) and the order definition, `rec_varg`'s signature, the bound, the guard, `rec_tnew`, so both checks sit inside `rec_varg`. With every hunk's text matched, the count fails alone, and so do two order links, signature before bound (hunk 4 before hunk 5) and guard before `rec_tnew` (a line no hunk holds); the other two links lie inside one hunk each and are implied, as the script's comment says [S][M: the implementer's fail-first, every check evaluated with a non-exiting fail, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/runs/ff-patch-1/summary.txt`].
   - **The pristine file**, for a hand run: `git --git-dir=prototype/watchdog/luajit/.git cat-file blob <pin>:src/<file>`, the committed bytes, LF. A pinned build leaves the same file in `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit-export-<platform>/src`; `build/native` predates the export and has none [M].
   - **Two prototypes of one test file** merged with `git merge-file` leave predictable conflicts in run-bitjit.sh's part lists and negctl-bitop.sh's arm and catcher lists; resolve them by hand and check every mode and arm afterwards [M: crc/combine, `scripts/resolve_bitjit.py`, `resolve_negctl.py`].
   - Then update [1.2](#12-our-twelve-changes-to-luajit)'s row, [7.5](#75-the-patch-script-engine)'s NADD/NDEL list and, for a fold, [3.10](#310-the-two-rules)'s table.
1. **For a new file, copy the smallest round-3 script**, `native/luajit/patch-bitop-bcdump.sh` (231 lines), and rename it. Keep the hunk engine block byte for byte; check that its md5 matches the other seven ([7.5](#75-the-patch-script-engine)).
2. **Set the header variables**: `ME`, `MARK` (a string that appears only in your text, e.g. `OC-LuaJIT (native/luajit/patch-<name>.sh)`), `NMARK` (how often it appears in the output), `NH` (hunks), `NADD` and `NDEL` (what `diff -d` will count).
3. **Write each hunk as `o<h>` (upstream's text, copied exactly from the pristine file) and `n<h>` (yours)**. Each upstream text must occur exactly once in the pristine file; widen it until it does. Write a tab as `@T@`. Use no backslash anywhere in the script (P8): if C code needs one, restructure it, or put that code in an `oclj_*` file copied whole.
4. **Write `verify_content`** for what the hunks cannot say (one define, one label, an include in the right place).
5. **Write the file with a file tool**, LF, and check by bytes that it has no CR (P7).
6. **Run it** (`sh patch-<name>.sh <in> <out>`) on a scratch copy of the pristine file (`git cat-file blob <pin>:src/<file>`, step 0), then on its own output (the already-patched path). Check that the output has 0 CR bytes and the marker count is right. The reversal check (each upstream text put back must reproduce the input) runs inside the script after every apply (patch-bitop-bcdump.sh ~:222-227); there is no separate revert command [S].
7. **Make it fail first**: feed it a file in which the anchor occurs twice, and one where it is missing; both must refuse with exit 1 ([11.8](#118-fail-first)).
8. **Wire it into build-native.sh** like the round-3 seven: assert absent in the pristine tree, re-copy, assert absent, patch, assert the marker count before make, and add freshness gates for every object that includes a new file ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)).

### 8.2a Bumping the pin

Moving to another upstream commit is a round of its own. All twelve of our changes are anchored on upstream text, and the blob fingerprint names the commit, so a bump changes what every blob says [S].

1. **Edit `native/luajit/upstream.commit`** with a file tool: the new full 40-digit hash and an LF, nothing else. It is the only place the pin is written; both Makefiles, `build.bat`, build-native.sh and gradle read it ([1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is)).
2. **Check the pristine tree out at it**: `make -C prototype/watchdog luajit-src`. Its stamp depends on the pin file, and it fetches only when the commit is missing [M: `pin/impl/mk-stamp-3`].
3. **Rebuild both variants**, on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8 with `OCLJ_GLIBC_MAX=2.28` ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage), [7.7](#77-the-linux-path)). Every run exports the new pin's tree and remakes the build copy from it, so after one build no file of the old commit is left in that `OCLJ_BUILD` [S][M: `pin2/impl/v2/`, the same mechanism across an unpinned build]. Build into a fresh `OCLJ_BUILD` anyway, so the old round's objects stay for comparison.
4. **Every patch script must still verify**: each upstream text found exactly once, the reversal check passed, and every marker count right before make ([7.5](#75-the-patch-script-engine)). A refused anchor means upstream changed the text a hunk sits on, and that may be because upstream fixed what the hunk fixes: compare upstream's change with the hunk's purpose before re-deriving anything, and where upstream's change does the hunk's work, drop the hunk and re-anchor or retire the arms and gates built on it. The select hunks are the first case: at `a4c0645`, hunk 5 of `patch-bitop-record.sh` refuses because upstream's own fix of #1535 rewrote its text (P54; roadmap row 270) [M][S]. Hunk 12 of `patch-bitop-fold.sh` (the CSE type test, 2026-10-23) applies at `a4c0645` [M: 76/9 of 2730, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/runs/fc-ffpatch-1/`]; should upstream fix the CSE twin first (P63), the round re-decides it: a type test of upstream's own in `lj_opt_cse` changes hunk 12's anchor, so the script refuses and hunk 12 and CSEFWD retire, and a zero-extension in `simplify_conv_sext` leaves no pair to form, so hunk 12 becomes dead code whose arm nothing can catch, and retires by P34 [S][I: the fix round's design 2.6]. Otherwise re-derive that hunk from the new pristine file ([8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) steps 3-7); never loosen an anchor until it matches. build-native.sh stops at the first refusal, so list every refused anchor before the round builds anything: run the twelve scripts on a `git archive` export of the target in build-native.sh's order, count every round-3 hunk, and count negctl's arm anchors in the patched copies, with the pin's tree as the control, as the census of 2026-10-22 did (scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/docs-up/scripts/`: `census.sh`, `hunkcount.py`, `negarms.py`; on the pin all twelve scripts apply and the patched `lj_record.c` and `lj_opt_fold.c` equal the working tree's build copies, `d490af9b` and `3b11930f`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/fix/b1/`) [M]. A partial clone (`blob:none`, as the scratch bare clone is) fetches any blob it lacks when a command reads it (`git archive`, `git show` or `git log --stat`, say); set `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` for a read meant to leave the clone as it is, and the read then fails instead [M: `git cat-file` on a copy whose remote points nowhere, with and without it, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/fix2/lazy/run-1/`; roadmap row 270].
5. **Run the round's suites** on the new build: every gate's wanted line (stage 1b's `GG_State` size, the `-dM` verdict, the VM-features word), [11](#11-verification-discipline) in full under every option set, `negctl-bitop.sh`, `negctl-pack.sh` and `negative-control.sh`, the serializer's `make test` and `make negctl`, and the JVM harness, on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8. Upstream's lexer and parser changes reach every program a player loads: list them from the commits' diffs, probe what the old and the new build accept, and grep the OS trees the census boots for source whose meaning would change (the 2026-10-21 probe and the checks it set: roadmap row 270).
6. **Blobs move with it.** The fingerprint's first field becomes the new commit, so every blob the old natives wrote is refused by the new ones (`eris_lj.c` compares the whole fingerprint) [S]. Settle with the user what that means for persisted machines (the roadmap's blob-compatibility row) before anything ships.
7. **Docs and packaging.** Re-derive the `~:N` hints this guide gives for LuaJIT files the bump moved. Update every file that states the current pin, which nothing checks: this guide's header pin line, checklist item 2, [1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is) and the glossary's **pristine tree**; `CLAUDE.md`'s first paragraph; `prototype/watchdog/README.md`'s pin line (:63); both NOTICE files, which name the commit LuaJIT is built from, `NOTICE` (:13, :21) and `src/main/resources/NOTICE` (:13, :15), the copy the jar ships; and the roadmap row [S]. When the syntax the build accepts changes, README's Lua dialect section changes with it. Prose that cites the commit it was written against stays (the patch scripts' anchor notes, the research notes, build-native.sh's "1ee778a4 when this was written"). gradle refuses every native built before the bump, so rebuild `dist` before `./gradlew build`.

### 8.3 Changing one line of vm_x64.dasc

This is negctl-bitop.sh's machinery done by hand. Each step is what that script measured in its four vm_x64.dasc arms (WRAP32-INTERP, SHIFTMASK, INEXACT-INTERP, INEXACT-BNOT) on Windows and Linux [M]; the recipe as written was not run end to end.

```sh
# 0. A fresh scratch copy in $OCLJ_BUILD's layout (7.4), mtimes kept; record the canonical objects.
```
