```sh
S=<fresh scratch dir>; W=$S/luajit-windows-x86_64        # never under build/, never reused
mkdir -p "$W" && cp -rp build/native/luajit-windows-x86_64/. "$W/" && cp -rp build/native/obj-windows-x86_64 "$S/" && cd "$W/src"
md5sum *.o host/*.o | tr -d '*' > ../canon.md5          # 77 lines
cp vm_x64.dasc ../vm_x64.dasc.canon                      # LF already: 0 CR bytes in the patched copy

# 1. One line, anchored, exactly once, every other line unchanged. Use negctl's sabotage():
#    awk with whole-line comparison, the old and new text passed in ENVIRON, @T@ for a tab,
#    no backslashes. Tag the new line, e.g. '    |  cmp RA, RA@T@@T@// PROBE-1: ...'.
#    Check it: diff ../vm_x64.dasc.canon vm_x64.dasc shows exactly one < and one >.

# 2. Make make see it, whatever the mtimes say:
rm -f host/buildvm_arch.h host/buildvm.o lj_vm.o lj_bc.o
mingw32-make BUILDMODE=static XCFLAGS= "Q=" -j4 > ../build.log 2>&1

# 3. Did it land?
grep -E -- '-o host/buildvm_arch[.]h vm_x64[.]dasc' ../build.log   # DynASM ran
grep -c -F 'PROBE-1:' host/buildvm_arch.h                         # 1: DynASM read the line
md5sum *.o host/*.o | tr -d '*' | diff ../canon.md5 -             # lj_vm.o and host/buildvm.o differ;
                                                                  # lj_bc.o too iff an offset moved; nothing else
nm -n lj_vm.o | grep -A1 ' lj_BC_BSHL$'                           # then objdump -d -M intel --start-address=...

# 4. Run the behaviour on ./luajit.exe (from src/, so jit/*.lua load): -joff against JIT on, and the catchers
#    (OCLJ_BUILD=$S with a fresh *_OUT, as in 7.4).

# 5. Put it back with a FRESH mtime, rebuild the same way, and require every object to reproduce:
cp ../vm_x64.dasc.canon vm_x64.dasc
rm -f host/buildvm_arch.h host/buildvm.o lj_vm.o lj_bc.o
mingw32-make BUILDMODE=static XCFLAGS= "Q=" -j4 > ../restore.log 2>&1
md5sum *.o host/*.o | tr -d '*' | diff ../canon.md5 -             # must print nothing
```

- If no object changed, the change never reached the code; negctl calls such an arm BROKEN [S]. A pure comment change, whether a `|//` line or a trailing `// ...`, emits no action bytes and leaves lj_vm.o unchanged [I].
- If an object outside the expected set changed, something else changed too (negctl's STRAY) [S].
- Step 5 is negctl's canon and restore check: on Windows and Linux every object reproduced [M] (logs/suite-native-2/negctl-bitop.log, logs/linux-negctl/).
- To ship the change, it goes into a patch script ([8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script)), never into the build copy by hand (P44).

### 8.4 Adding a fold rule safely

1. **Find the key.** If a function already owns it, extend that function; a second `LJFOLD` line with the same key is a build error. Keep `LJFOLD` lines and rule bodies in `lj_opt_fold.c`. Helpers may live in an `#include`d file, but delete `lj_opt_fold.o` after editing one ([3.6](#36-how-an-instruction-is-folded), P4, P14), and call a new static helper from build-native.sh's `bitop_wgate.c`, or the warning gate fails ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage); FIX-NUMCONV's `oclj_isnumconv` is one). The include must sit above the first function that calls the helper (the table-index fold moved it, [8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) step 0).
2. **Return an existing ref, or change `fins` and return `RETRYFOLD`.** If the new instruction uses `fleft->op1` or `fright->op1`, put `PHIBARRIER` on that operand (requirement 2, [3.8](#38-what-a-rule-returns-and-upstreams-requirements)).
3. **Preserve the destination type**, including the case where an int ref stands in for an int64 (x64 `simplify_conv_sext`). Across a PHI, check the type of the returned ref (S4).
4. **Below a PHI-marked operand, read only opcodes, types and constants (S5).** Write down every way a value can enter the trace (S6).
5. **Compare modes under the right mask**: `MODEMASK` for identity, `SRCMASK`/`DSTMASK` for types. Never use `==` on a whole op2 unless upstream does (S3).
6. **Do not let a guard's own justification remove that guard (S2).** And keep each proof in its own place: a fact of exactness is not a range, so give it a predicate and a rule line of its own, never a score in `oclj_bitwidth`, where it would compose (P60, P62, [3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)).
7. **Grep every function you call for `LJ_HASFFI`** (P1).
8. **Prove the rule can fail.** Add a one-line sabotage arm to `negctl-bitop.sh` ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)), caught by a suite that holds the rule's boundary, and see the catcher fail before you trust it ([11.8](#118-fail-first)). Use the generator (`test/native/bitop/gen.lua`) with a boundary template (S7) where the rule's consumers occur in generated loops; otherwise a floor suite built for it. The table-index fold has three, `t_idx.lua`, `t_cnt33.lua` and `t_idx2.lua`, and a generator mode, `idx`, because gen's default index atoms mask with `& 7` and `& 15` and cannot tell a bound of 32 from 33 ([11.4](#114-the-generator), [11.5a](#115a-the-idx-part-t_idx-t_cnt33-t_idx2-phiidx), [8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)). The suite's cases must land where the bound is visible: a truncation shows only where it gives a valid index or count ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains), designing bound tests). A rule that only removes a check that cannot fail changes no value when switched off, so that arm needs a catcher that counts what the rule leaves, as FIX-NUMCONV's NUMCONVOFF is caught by ncir.lua alone, and its value arms need cases that meet the compiled loop past the rule's boundary, as t_numconv's do ([11.5c](#115c-the-nc-part-t_numconv-ncir), P61). A precondition that only some trace layouts exercise needs a catcher that meets many layouts: without `oclj_isnumconv`'s opcode test (NUMCONVNOOP) any unguarded int64 instruction whose op2 reads `IRT_NUM` in its low five bits passes for a converted double [S], so whether a value goes wrong depends on ref layout (the code verifier traced a BOR whose op2 was the constant ref 0x7fee; the arm verifier's IR scan found instructions' refs as well); the generator meets such a layout in every seed, and the nc part's fixed loops only in ncir under `-O1` and `-O-narrow` ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)) [M]. And check a mechanism traced on one loop against many traces before the docs state it: that one trace (a constant's ref) became the documented mechanism until an IR scan of the generator's traces on the shipped and the sabotage build, counting the candidates by opcode, by op2's kind and by whether the result guard stayed, showed it too narrow (scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/verify-arm/scripts/irscan.lua`, `verify-arm/irscan-1/`) [M].
9. **Run the option sets** ([11.3](#113-option-sets)), plus `-O-fold`, `-O-cse` and `-O-loop`, under which the results must still be correct, only slower; and the sets [11.3](#113-option-sets) lists for your kind of change (an index fold, a speculative guard).
10. **Read the IR** with `-jdump=+rsT`. Remember it prints KINT64s as the trace number and hides bit 0x400; use `traceir` when a mark matters ([12.6](#126-reading-the-exact-mark)).
11. **Run the three runners on the build**: run-bitop.sh, run-bitjit.sh with its fix1 part, and run-bitfuzz.sh (fuzz mode U is the fold engine's). Then run the whole negctl-bitop.sh sweep, canon included, from the main session. If the rule can return or build on a pre-roll ref, add a `p4v`-style loop that asserts trace events (a `stop`, 0 TYPEINS, JFORL), because differential tests cannot see TYPEINS (S4). If it must never cross a PHI, or must fire in the loop body, read the IR as `phiidx.lua` does ([12.5](#125-jitutil), [11.5a](#115a-the-idx-part-t_idx-t_cnt33-t_idx2-phiidx)): a crossing that computes the right values is invisible to differential suites and to trace events alike (the PHIIDX and NOLOOPFOLD arms) [M]. For a speed rule, measure ([11.12](#1112-measuring-speed)).

### 8.4a Emitting instructions or a guard from a fold rule

No round-3 rule emits IR: both of its rules return an existing ref, as FIX-NUMCONV's does. The table-index fold rewrites `fins` and returns `RETRYFOLD`, a new instruction but nothing on the side; rg2's range guard, prototyped and not shipped, emitted four instructions on the side, the last of them a guard ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)). These are the facts a rule that emits needs [S]:

- **`fins`, `fleft` and `fright` are `J->fold.ins`, `J->fold.left` and `J->fold.right`**, one slot per `jit_State` (lj_opt_fold.c ~:138-140). A nested `emitir` (`lj_ir_set` + `lj_opt_fold`) overwrites them. Copy every field you need into locals first, as `simplify_shiftk_andk` (~:1689) saves `fleft->ot` before its inner `lj_opt_fold(J)`; then set `fins` again and return `RETRYFOLD`, or return a ref.
- **New instructions land before the folded one**, so a guard emitted there precedes the value's uses (S1).
- **`J->chain[IR_LOOP] != 0` means the rule runs in `loop_unroll`'s copy**: LOOP is emitted before the copy starts (lj_opt_loop.c ~:286), and `loop_undo`'s rollback restores the chain on a retry (`lj_ir_rollback`). The pre-roll and side traces have no LOOP.
- **A guard emitted in the copy exits through the newest substituted snapshot**: `loop_unroll` calls `loop_subst_snap` (~:208) as the copy reaches each recorded snapshot's ref (~:315-316). S8 must hold from that snapshot's pc. A side effect before the guard has its own snapshot after it: rg2's range guard after `math.random` resumed past the call ([4.3](#43-snapshots)) [M].
- **Requirement 2 applies to each new instruction**: none may take `fleft->op1` of a PHI-marked `fleft` without `PHIBARRIER` or a written argument (S4, S5).
- **Instructions a rule emits on the side, and does not return, are invisible to `loop_unroll`'s PHI marking** (P55) [S][M]. Only the ref the rule returns is compared with the pre-roll (`ref < invar`, [4.1](#41-copy-substitution)). A side instruction that CSEs to a pre-roll instruction therefore hands the loop body the pre-roll's value in every iteration, a stale value no PHI carries. verify-rg2's `stale.lua` reads one such guard on a build that allowed rg2 a PHI-marked `fleft` (the RG2-PHI-RELAXED sabotage) and none on rg2 as built, and none on a pre-roll BSAR on the combined build, by default and with `-Ohotloop=1` [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc/verify-rg2/probes/stale.lua`, `runs/dump-stale-relaxed-2.txt`; design.md gap 3 and its evidence list]. Upstream's `cse_carg` (lj_opt_fold.c ~:2499-2505) guards the same hazard for its own instruction: a CSE hit below `J->chain[IR_LOOP]` ("CSE across loop?") is emitted raw instead [S]. So: build side instructions only on loop-body refs, keep `!irt_isphi(fleft->t)` (an unmarked `fleft` may still be an invariant pre-roll instruction, [4.1](#41-copy-substitution)), and write the argument down. Whether a PHI-marked `fleft` whose own operand is PHI-marked may take new instructions is not settled (open question 17).

### 8.5 Adding a recorder path

1. **Find the entry.** A bytecode is a case of `lj_record_ins` (lj_record.c ~:2227); a fast function is a `recff_*` in lj_ffrecord.c, tied to its library function by `buildvm -m recdef` [S]. Put the new code in an `oclj_*_rec.c` that the patched file includes after its `emitir` macros, and reduce the patched case to one call, as round 3 did [S].
2. **Decide at record time from the runtime values** (`rbv`, `rcv`): the recorder runs before the interpreter executes the instruction, so they are exactly this iteration's operands ([2.5](#25-hot-counting-and-trace-start)) [S]. For anything you will not compile, call `lj_trace_err(J, LJ_TRERR_BADTYPE)` (or `NYI`) before emitting IR: the trace aborts and the interpreter runs the instruction [S]. **Bound a runtime value before C arithmetic on it.** A constant the recorder computes from one is C arithmetic, and a signed int overflow there is undefined; our build compiles LuaJIT without `-fwrapv` [M: the `lj_record.c` compile lines in the select round's build logs, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/b2/luajit_build.log`]. `rec_varg`'s `frofs+8*(int32_t)idx` overflows for a constant select index from about 2^28 [S]; gcc wrapped it, and for `select(2^28, ...)` in a one-parameter function the wrapped constant made a guard that always fails (P54) [M]. Abort above a bound you can compile, as the select fix does (`if (idx > OCLJ_SELECT_KMAX) goto nyivarg;`, the same exit as upstream's for k < 0), or compute in int64 [S]. The select round's sweep read one more such expression, `recff_ipairs_aux`'s `numberVint(&rd->argv[1])+1` (lj_ffrecord.c ~:504), left open (roadmap row 214) [S].
3. **Specialize with guards.** What you assumed from a runtime value must be guarded, because the next iteration may differ: round 3's variable shift count becomes `CONV int.num check` plus `ULT cnt, 64` ([8.6](#86-adding-a-guard)) [S]. A guard that computes on the value must bound it first, so that its arithmetic cannot wrap ([8.6](#86-adding-a-guard) step 7).
4. **Emit through `emitir`**, so every instruction folds and CSEs; use `emitir_raw` only to bypass folding on purpose [S]. Make constants with `lj_ir_kint`, `lj_ir_kint64` and `lj_ir_knum` [S].
5. **Return what the slot holds.** Without DUALNUM every Lua number in a slot is `IRT_NUM`, so round 3 returns the double `d`, never the int64: `J->base[ra] = rc` puts it in the slot, and snapshots name it (S6) [S].
6. **After a side effect, set `J->needsnap = 1`**, so the next instruction starts with a snapshot and an exit never re-executes the effect ([4.3](#43-snapshots)) [S].
7. **Test** with `-jv` for the abort reasons, under every option set, against `-joff` and the PUC twin, and add a negctl arm per decision the path makes ([11](#11-verification-discipline)).

### 8.6 Adding a guard

1. **A guard is an instruction with `IRT_GUARD`** (`IRTG(o,t)`, `IRTGI(o)`). When it fails, the trace leaves through the newest snapshot and the interpreter resumes at that snapshot's pc ([4.3](#43-snapshots), [4.7](#47-exits)) [S].
2. **Make sure the interpreter does the right thing from there**: it re-executes from the snapshot's pc and must compute the guarded operation and fail the way 5.3.6 does (S8).
3. **Emit it right after the value it guards** (S1). Between them there must be no snapshot: nothing you call in between may reach `lj_snap_add` (`rec_comp_prep`, `rec_for`, `rec_itern` and `lj_record_ret` do; [4.3](#43-snapshots)) [S].
4. **Use a compare the backend emits**: `EQ`/`NE`/`LT`/`ULT`/... on int, i64 or num; a guarded CONV only from num to int (`asm_conv` ~:902). A number compare exits on NaN through `jpe` as well as the condition's own branch [S].
5. **Expect it to stay.** Guards are side effects to DCE ([3.1](#31-the-instruction-irins)), so a guard costs even when its value is unused. To remove one you need an independent proof, like the width rule, never a fact the guard itself establishes (S2). An invariant guard's copy in the loop body CSEs to the pre-roll's, which hoists it for free [S].
6. **Test the exit**: a loop that passes the guard more than `hotloop` times and then fails it, under `-Omaxside=0` (exit to the interpreter) and `-Ohotloop=1 -Ohotexit=1` (a side trace from the first exit); compare with `-joff` and PUC; see the exit with `-jdump=x`. Then add a negctl arm that removes the guard (round 3's INEXACT-JIT and NOINT-JIT) [S][M].
7. **A guard's own arithmetic must not wrap** (the select fix, P54). Upstream's guard for select's path C, `fr < 8*k + frofs`, computes `8*k + frofs` in 32 bits (`BSHL`, `ADD`), so a k whose 8k wraps passed it, and so did every k from m+1 to 0 in a call with m < 0 varargs, where `fr` is below `frofs` [S][M]. Write such a guard so that it cannot wrap [S]:
   - **Bound the operand first, with one unsigned compare against a constant that makes the later arithmetic fit**: `ULT k-1, 2^27` passes exactly 1 <= k <= 2^27, so it refuses k <= 0 and every large k at once, and after it `8*k + frofs` is at most 2^30 + 1619 for any function the parser makes (`numparams` <= `LJ_MAX_LOCVAR`, 200; the code comment's looser bound, `LJ_MAX_SLOTS`, gives 2^30 + 2019) [S]. A signed `k >= 1` alone was not enough: built as a variant, it still differed from `-joff` in 11 of 15 option sets, through a k that narrowing had wrapped to 2^31 - 1 [M: the select round's `var-gt0`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/runs/bitjit-sel-gt0-2`].
   - **Choose the bound from both sides**: below it must lie every value the path is meant to compile (a frame holds at most LUAI_MAXSTACK, 65500, varargs), and above it nothing the arithmetic cannot hold [S].
   - **List every source of the operand** (S6): in t_select's sources, select's k arrives as a checked conversion of a double or a string, an int loop index (`ADDOV`, which exits on overflow), a bit result through the table-index fold, or a narrowed sum whose outermost ADD or SUB is unchecked and so is wrapped ([3.13](#313-narrowing-and-why-round-3-goes-around-it)); the last is the one a lower bound alone missed [M: t_select]. Other sources exist (an int from a parent snapshot, `#t`, `string.byte`'s result); that none of them arrives wrapped is inferred [I].
   - **Know what a value can be that "cannot" be negative**: a vararg frame's `fr - frofs` is 8 times the count of passed arguments minus the fixed parameters, negative in a call with fewer arguments than parameters; `rec_varg` clamps `nvararg` to 0 on its on-trace path (~:1998) and guards `GE tmp, 0` on path B when there are fixed parameters (~:2054-2055), and path C had no such guard [S].
   - **Emit it on the operand, right before the arithmetic**, with nothing between that adds a snapshot, so it exits through the snapshot the path's own guards use (S1, S8) [S].

### 8.7 Reading a -jdump

```lua
-- guide1.lua
local acc = 0
for i = 1, 300 do
  local x = i * 2654435761         -- a double: MULVN is not narrowed
  acc = (acc ~ (x >> 3)) & 0xFFFF
end
print(acc)                         -- 16565, the same as -joff and PUC 5.3.6
```

The command is `luajit -jdump=+rsT guide1.lua`: `+rs` adds registers and snapshots, and `T` gives plain text, needed because `jit/dump.lua` turns colour on whenever `TERM` contains "color", even through a pipe (P12) [S][M]. Addresses (`0x22f6b370471`, the mcode addresses) change from run to run. The IR [M]:

```
....              SNAP   #0   [ ---- ---- ]
0001 rsi      int SLOAD  #3    CI
0002          u8  XLOAD  [0x22f6b370471]  V
0003          int BAND   0002  +12
0004       >  int EQ     0003  +0
0005 xmm8     num CONV   0001  num.int
0006 xmm8     num MUL    0005  +2654435761
0007 rcx      i64 CONV   0006  i64.num none
0008 xmm5     num CONV   0007  num.i64
0009       >  num EQ     0008  0006
0010 rcx      i64 BSHR   0007  +3
0011 xmm4     num CONV   0010  num.i64
0012 r8       i64 CONV   0011  i64.num none
0013       >  i64 EQ     0012  0010
0014 xmm3  >  num SLOAD  #2    T
0015 rdi      i64 CONV   0014  i64.num none
0016 xmm2     num CONV   0015  num.i64
0017       >  num EQ     0016  0014
0018 rdi      i64 BXOR   0015  0010
0019 xmm1     num CONV   0018  num.i64
0020 rax      i64 CONV   0019  i64.num none
0021       >  i64 EQ     0020  0018
0022 rdi    + i64 BAND   0018  +1
0023 xmm7   + num CONV   0022  num.i64
0024 rsi    + int ADD    0001  +1
....              SNAP   #1   [ ---- ---- 0023 ]
0025       >  int LE     0024  +300
....              SNAP   #2   [ ---- ---- 0023 0024 ---- ---- 0024 ]
0026 ------------ LOOP ------------
0027          u8  XLOAD  [0x22f6b370471]  V
0028          int BAND   0027  +12
0029       >  int EQ     0028  +0
0030 xmm15    num CONV   0024  num.int
0031 xmm15    num MUL    0030  +2654435761
0032 rbp      i64 CONV   0031  i64.num none
0033 xmm6     num CONV   0032  num.i64
0034       >  num EQ     0033  0031
0035 rbp      i64 BSHR   0032  +3
0036 xmm6     num CONV   0035  num.i64
0037 r15      i64 CONV   0036  i64.num none
0038       >  i64 EQ     0037  0035
0039 rdi      i64 BXOR   0035  0022
0040 xmm6     num CONV   0039  num.i64
0041 rbp      i64 CONV   0040  i64.num none
0042       >  i64 EQ     0041  0039
0043 rdi    + i64 BAND   0039  +1
0044 xmm7   + num CONV   0043  num.i64
0045 rsi    + int ADD    0024  +1
....              SNAP   #3   [ ---- ---- 0044 ]
0046       >  int LE     0045  +300
0047 rsi      int PHI    0024  0045
0048 xmm7     num PHI    0023  0044
0049 rdi      i64 PHI    0022  0043
```

`>` marks a guard and `+` a PHI operand. Slot 2 is `acc`, slot 3 the FORL control variable, slot 6 the visible `i`; slots 0-1 are the frame (`J->baseslot` = 1 + FR2) [S].

| ref | what it is, and who emitted it |
|---|---|
| SNAP #0 | `lj_record_setup`'s snapshot; resumes at `MULVN` [M] |
| 0001 | the control variable, narrowed to int by `rec_for_loop`; `C` = `IRSLOAD_CONVERT`, `I` = `IRSLOAD_INHERIT` [S] |
| 0002-0004 | CHECKHOOK, from `lj_record_setup` (~:2953-2973): a volatile load of `hookmask`, AND with `LUA_MASKLINE\|LUA_MASKCOUNT` (12), guard == 0 (P31; [docs/watchdog.md](watchdog.md)). The load is volatile, so `loop_unroll` emits it again at 0027-0029 [S] |
| 0005-0006 | `x` as a double (MULVN is not narrowed) [S] |
| 0007 | `oclj_rec_bitarg`: `i = CONV i64.num x`; "none" is `IRCONV_ANY`, an unchecked `cvttsd2si` (P38) [S][M] |
| 0008 | `back = CONV num.i64 i`, marked exact. The mark is invisible here; `jit.util.traceir` shows op2 = 0x05d5 (P30) [M] |
| 0009 | the operand guard: `x` is integral, in range and not NaN; it exits through #0 [M] |
| 0010 | the shift. The count is constant (a KSHORT-loaded slot), so 5.3.6's rule was decided at record time; `+3` is a `KINT` and prints correctly [S][M] |
| 0011 | `d`, marked (0x05d5): the double the BSHR's destination (bytecode slot 6, IR slot #8) would hold [M] |
| 0012 | the result guard's own check, marked (0x16ae), so the exact-mark rule leaves it alone [M][S] |
| 0013 | the result guard. `BSHR` by 3 bounds at 62 bits > 54, so the width rule kept it [S] |
| 0014-0017 | `acc` enters as a plain double: `sload` with `T`, then its operand guard [S] |
| 0018 | `BXOR 0015 0010`. The second operand is the BSHR's int64 itself: converting `d` (0011) folded to 0010 by the exact-mark rule, its back-conversion commoned with 0011, and `EQ.num 0011, 0011` was dropped [M][S] |
| 0019-0021 | BXOR's `d` and its guard; the bound is max(64, 62) [S] |
| 0022 | `BAND`. `+1` is the KINT64 0xFFFF, printed as the trace number (P3); the machine code says `and rdi, 0xffff` [M] |
| 0023 | BAND's `d`, with no guard: BAND with a non-negative constant bounds at 17 bits, so the guard's check folded to 0022 and `EQ.i64 0022, 0022` folded away [S][M] |
| 0024-0025 | `i + 1` and the loop test; SNAP #1 resumes after the loop [M] |
| SNAP #2 | the loop snapshot (`lj_record_stop`): acc = 0023, the control variable and `i` = 0024; resumes at `MULVN` [M] |
| 0026 | `LOOP`, from `loop_unroll`: everything below is the copy-substituted body [S] |
| 0039 | `BXOR 0035 0022`. `acc`'s SLOAD was forwarded (`fwd_sload`) to 0023, and converting 0023 folded across the PHI to 0022, which fix1 allows because 0022 is int64-typed. `acc`'s operand guard is gone from the loop [M][S] |
| 0043-0044 | BAND and its `d`, again with no guard [M] |
| 0047 | the control variable's PHI [S] |
| 0048 | `acc` as a double. It stays alive because the loop's snapshot names it (SNAP #3 restores slot 2 from 0044) [I] |
| 0049 | `acc` as an int64, what the next iteration's BXOR consumes: what the exact-mark rule buys [M] |

The pre-roll's first operator in machine code is the interpreter's fast path again, with exits in place of `->vmeta_bitop_pc` [M]:

```
test byte [rbx-0xf07], 0xc   ; 0002-0004 CHECKHOOK, fused
jnz ->0
cvttsd2si rcx, xmm8          ; 0007 CONV i64.num
xorps xmm5, xmm5             ; 0008 CONV num.i64 (asm_conv clears the register first)
cvtsi2sd xmm5, rcx
ucomisd xmm5, xmm8           ; 0009 EQ.num: NaN is unordered
jpe ->0
jnz ->0
shr rcx, 0x03                ; 0010 BSHR
xorps xmm4, xmm4             ; 0011 d
cvtsi2sd xmm4, rcx
cvttsd2si r8, xmm4           ; 0012 the guard's check
cmp r8, rcx                  ; 0013 EQ.i64
jnz ->0
...
and rdi, 0xffff              ; 0022 BAND (the IR printed "+1")
```

**Reading caveats** [S][M]:

- **KINT64 constants print as the trace number** (P3). Read the machine code (`and rdi, 0xffff`) or the source instead.
- **The exact mark is not printed** (P30). Use `jit.util.traceir(tr, ref)` and test op2 against 0x400 ([12.6](#126-reading-the-exact-mark)).
- **`i64.num none` is the unchecked conversion** (P38).
- **`KINT` constants print correctly**: the shift count `+3` and the CHECKHOOK `+12` are `KINT`s.
- **A guard's exit resumes at its snapshot's pc**, here `MULVN`, not at the operator (P40). `jit.util.tracesnap(tr, sn, true)` gives each snapshot's pc.
- **A trace's machine-code size varies by a few bytes between processes**: crcprobe's trace 8 on the fold's build read 746 bytes in proto-folda's run and 752 in the combiner's [M: design.md]. Compare sizes within one process, or across many [I].

### 8.8 The CRC32 follow-up: the table-index fold, and what remains

Roadmap row 209, the guide's first real test (2026-10-20). Round 3 left table-driven CRC32 at 7.2 ns per byte, against 2.8-2.9 with round 2's 32-bit operators [M: `bench/runs/2026-10-17-bitop/logs/bench/bench.txt`]. The round prototyped two changes on this build, had each verified adversarially, combined them and timed them; its record is `bench/runs/2026-10-20-crc32/design/design.md` [S]. The user's decisions (2026-10-20) [S]: ship the table-index fold ("fold a"); do not ship the range guard (rg2); the fold never crosses a PHI; FIX-NUMCONV is a later round of its own; row 209 stays open.

**The probe loop** (`crcprobe.lua`, kept here so that [2](#2-the-pipeline) and this section's refs hold; JIT, `-joff` and PUC 5.3.6 all print 3012454781; the loop is trace 8) [M]:

```lua
local T = {}
for i = 0, 255 do
  local c = i
  for _ = 1, 8 do
    if c & 1 == 1 then c = (c >> 1) ~ 0xedb88320 else c = c >> 1 end
  end
  T[i] = c
end
local s = string.rep("The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. ", 64)
local crc = 0xffffffff
local len = #s
for k = 1, 20000 do
  local byte = s:byte((k - 1) % len + 1)
  crc = T[(crc ~ byte) & 0xff] ~ (crc >> 8)
end
print(crc ~ 0xffffffff)
```

**The table-index fold**, `patch-bitop-fold.sh` hunk 11 (build copy, lj_opt_fold.c ~:1140-1167, comments shortened) [S]:

```c
#include "oclj_bitop_fold.c"     /* moved here from above simplify_conv_i64_num */

LJFOLD(CONV CONV IRCONV_INT_NUM)  /* _INT */
LJFOLDF(simplify_conv_int_num)
{
  /* Fold even across PHI to avoid expensive num->int conversions in loop. */
  if ((fleft->op2 & IRCONV_SRCMASK) ==
      ((fins->op2 & IRCONV_DSTMASK) >> IRCONV_DSH))
    return fleft->op1;                                  /* upstream's */
  if ((fleft->op2 & IRCONV_SRCMASK) == IRT_I64 && !irt_isphi(fleft->t) &&
      oclj_bitwidth(J, fleft->op1, 0) <= 32) {          /* the table-index fold */
    fins->ot = IRTI(IR_CONV);
    fins->op1 = fleft->op1;
    fins->op2 = (IRT_INT<<IRCONV_DSH)|IRT_I64;
    return RETRYFOLD;
  }
  return NEXTFOLD;
}
```

- **What it does.** `CONV int.num (CONV num.i64 y)` becomes the unguarded `CONV int.i64 y`, mode exactly `(IRT_INT<<IRCONV_DSH)|IRT_I64` (0x275), when y is an int64 that `oclj_bitwidth` bounds at 32 bits or fewer and `fleft` is not PHI-marked. x64 emits `mov r32, r32` for it ([5.1](#51-our-ir-in-x64)) [S].
- **Why it is sound.** Such a y lies in [-2^31, 2^31): it is exactly a double and exactly an int32, so the num-to-int conversion gives y whatever its check mode, and its check (INDEX, ANY or CHECK) cannot fail; y's low 32 bits are y. The proof is the bound alone, which reads no guard and no exact mark (S2). The consumer's own check still runs on the int: ABC (or ULE for an array extension) for a table key, the string functions' length checks, `ULT 64` for a shift count [S].
- **It speculates nothing.** It adds no guard and removes a check that cannot fail [S].
- **The bound is tight at 32.** At 33, y = -(2^32 - 5) truncates to 5: a different table key, and a shift count that passes `ULT 64` and shifts by 5 where 5.3.6 shifts the other way and gives 0 (round 3's review, `verify:tablefold:0`; `t_cnt33.lua`) [M]. The WIDTHIDX33 arm puts 33 back ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)).
- **The include moved** to directly above `simplify_conv_int_num`, the first of `oclj_bitwidth`'s two callers ([1.3](#13-our-files-in-the-build-copy), [8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) step 0) [S].
- **Never across a PHI**, the user's policy (a) [S]. The new instruction takes `fleft->op1`, which for a PHI-marked `fleft` is a pre-roll ref (requirement 2, [3.8](#38-what-a-rule-returns-and-upstreams-requirements)). It is a conservative policy, not the only sound one [S][M: proto-folda and verify-folda, design.md gap 1]:
  - the PHIIDX sabotage, which lets the fold cross, is value-correct in every suite and in the generator, because `loop_unroll` adds an extra PHI for the CONV hint and so carries the pre-roll y ([4.1](#41-copy-substitution)); only its regular branch adds that PHI, its type-fix branch none, so no argument may lean on it;
  - PHIREUSE, returning the pre-roll's own `CONV int.i64` of y, an existing ref of the folded type, meets requirement 2 and gave phi2's loop in 476 bytes; it needs a written argument and a changed `NEED` in phiidx.lua, and is worth it only if phi2 shows a cost, which the timing chain did not (0.98 of base, below).
- **Where it fires in a loop body.** In phiidx's `ctl` and `crc` loops the body's `CONV int.i64` is the copy of the pre-roll's folded instruction, so the rule does not run there again; it fires in the copy itself only for a key the pre-roll emitted raw, such as `ctl1`'s narrowed `e + 1` ([4.1](#41-copy-substitution)) [S][M: phiidx.lua].

**Its consumers** [S: proto-folda's and verify-folda's reading, design.md gap 5]. The key `LJFOLD(CONV CONV IRCONV_INT_NUM)` ignores the check mode, so the fold reaches every place that narrows a bit result to an int:

| consumer | conversion | from | held by |
|---|---|---|---|
| a table key, `T[e]` and `T[e] = v` | `CONV int.num` INDEX | `rec_idx_key` (lj_record.c ~:1438) → `lj_opt_narrow_index` (lj_opt_narrow.c ~:451) | t_idx, t_idx2, GENMODE idx |
| `rawget(T, e)` | INDEX | `recff_rawget` | t_idx2 |
| a narrowed key, `T[e + 3]`, `T[e - 1]` | CHECK, a leaf that `narrow_conv_emit` emits raw in the pre-roll | narrowing's backpropagation | t_idx, t_idx2, phiidx `ctl1` |
| `string.byte`, `string.sub` | ANY | `lj_opt_narrow_toint` (~:466) | t_idx, t_idx2, GENMODE idx |
| `lj_opt_narrow_toint`'s other users: `string.char`, `string.rep`, `string.find`'s init, `string.format`'s `%c`, `table.concat`'s i and j, `unpack`'s i and j, `tonumber`'s base, `table.new`, `ipairs_aux` | ANY | | t_idx2: char, rep, find, concat, unpack |
| a variable shift count | `CONV int.num` CHECK, then `ULT cnt, 64` | `oclj_bitop_rec.c` ~:141 | t_idx, t_cnt33, t_idx2, GENMODE idx |
| a `for` loop's stop | `fori_conv`'s CHECK, only with an int start (a double start records a number loop and no conversion) | `rec_for` | t_idx, t_idx2 |
| `select(k, ...)` | INDEX | `rec_varg` | t_select's `bx` source, since the select fix (5-31 `CONV int.i64` per option set where the fold runs, [11.5b](#115b-the-sel-part-t_select)); t_idx2 leaves it out, by the user's decision of 2026-10-21 (P54) |
| `recff_sbufx_checkint`, and `ULE asize` for a table with no array part | | | not in the suites |
| `loop_unroll`'s "Fix num->int" | CHECK, always PHI-marked | [4.4](#44-type-instability-typeins-retry-blacklist) | never folds |

**Designing bound tests** [M: proto-folda and verify-folda, design.md gaps 6 and 7]:
- A 33-bit truncation shows only where its low 32 bits make a valid index, argument or count. A negative y with small low bits does: `x ~>> 31` with x = -(2^32 - 5) · 2^31 is -(2^32 - 5), whose low 32 bits are 5 (t_idx's `w33e`, t_cnt33).
- A y in [2^31, 2^32), such as `x & 0xffffffff`, truncates to a negative int32. A table key's ABC and a count's `ULT 64` reject it: a correct exit, and no visible difference. But arithmetic can bring it back into range, and some consumers take a negative int as it is: under WIDTHIDX33, t_idx's `w33a` and t_idx2's `c33` differ through narrowed keys (`T[e + 1]`, `T[e + 3]`: 0xffffffff truncates to -1, and -1 + 1 is 0), a loop stop (`for j = 1, e` runs no iteration) and `table.concat`'s i and j; `c33` in all seven option sets where the fold runs, `w33a` in all but `-O1`. `sub`, `byte` and `find` differed only for the `x ~>> 31` expressions (t_idx's `w33e`, t_idx2's `a33`, `d33`, `e33`), never for `x & 0xffffffff` [M: three runs, the implementer's `crc2/impl/b-impl/negctl-bitop/20261020T151305Z-10987/WIDTHIDX33`, the code verifier's `crc2/verify-code/b-vc/negctl-bitop/20261020T163352Z-796/WIDTHIDX33` and the fixer's `crc2/fix/b-fix/negctl-bitop/20261020T171352Z-1835/WIDTHIDX33`, each option set's output diffed against `-joff` and grouped by expression and consumer, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/fix/scripts/difall.py`].
- A 33-bit truncation of a key that lives in the hash part changes no value: for a key outside the array part, `rec_idx_key` (lj_record.c ~:1438) guards the truncated int only with the inverse bounds check `ULE asize`, which a wrong small int fails (an exit), and then looks the key up in the hash with the original number. So only array-part keys (the ABC path), string positions, counts and loop stops expose it [S][M: the code verifier's report, `crc2/verify-code/suites/carry.lua`: its hash-part key read 0 differences under a bound of 33, its count differed in every option set].
- Write a design claim about which consumer shows a bound together with the per-case differences that show it: diff each option set's output against `-joff` and group the cases by expression and consumer. The round's first claim, that `x & 0xffffffff` shows in `sub`, was a design-time claim its own run did not bear out [M].
- A variable shift count outside [0, 63] at record time aborts the trace ([2.6](#26-the-recorder)), so a count case records with small counts and meets its 33-bit ones later (t_cnt33 cycles each operand through its own list).
- A consumer must really convert. t_idx's first `fori` consumer, `for j = e, e + 1`, had a double start, so `rec_for` recorded a number loop with no conversion at all; with `for j = 1, e` it differs in 21-23 cases per option set under WIDTHIDX33, and t_idx's catch under the default options rose from 28 to 52 (S7) [M: verify-folda F1; crc/combine `b-both/negctl-bitop/20261020T133823Z-362/WIDTHIDX33`].

The release natives were then rebuilt into `build/native` (Windows, and AlmaLinux 8 with `OCLJ_GLIBC_MAX=2.28`): all 80 objects of each equal the final check's builds, and the JVM harness's seven arms on each read what the glibc round's did (82, 69, 80, 76, 53 and 82 checks, and bundleoff's 3 expected failures) [M: `bench/runs/2026-10-20-crc32/logs/final-main/`, `logs/release/`].

**On crcprobe's loop** [M: crc/combine `probes/crc-base.dump`, `probes/crc-folda.dump`; design.md]: trace 8's loop body had 42 instructions, 10 guards and 824 bytes of machine code before, and has 41, 9 and 752 with the fold. The guarded `int CONV <d> int.num index` is gone; in its place `0083 i64 BAND 0079 +8` (the KINT64 0xff, printed as the trace number, P3), `0084 int CONV 0083 int.i64` and `0085 int ABC 0046 0084`. What remains in the body: three result guards, of `crc ~ byte` (bound 64: crc comes from table values and a stack slot, and the byte scores 64, [3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)), `crc >> 8` (57) and the final BXOR (64), which run beside the dependency chain because later operators read each result's int64; and the table value's operand guard (`CONV i64.num`, `CONV num.i64`, `EQ.num`), whose `cvttsd2si` is on the chain [M from the IR; what each costs is I]. Machine-code sizes vary by a few bytes between processes (746 and 752 bytes for the same build), so compare them within one process ([8.7](#87-reading-a--jdump)) [M].

**The numbers** [M]. - So the fold halves table-driven CRC32 on this machine, and speeds loops that use a masked bit result as a key or a count: mix indexes its table with `T[(i & 255) + 1]` and ident with `T[(i & 1023) + 1]`, flag63 shifts by `60 + (i & 3)` [S: the benches][I: that those uses are why]. bench_idx's own masked-key and masked-count rows, idx and cnt, read 1.00; why some masked keys gain and others do not was not examined [I].
- The tools are archived with the round (`scripts/`): the chain, the b0 companion, `summarize.py`, `pairratio.py`, `b0sum.py`, the benches and `ident.lua` ([11.12](#1112-measuring-speed)).

**rg2: prototyped, verified, measured, not shipped** [S][M: design.md, the timing chain]:
- **What it was.** In a loop body only (`J->chain[IR_LOOP] != 0`), when both conversions of a result guard carried the exact mark, `fleft` was not PHI-marked, y was int64-typed and the width rule could not bound y, rg2 replaced the guard's own round trip (`CONV i64.num d`, `EQ.i64`) by `BSAR y, 53; CONV int.i64; ADD 1; ULE 1`, which passes exactly for y in [-2^53, 2^53) (proto-rg2: a rule in hunk 7 and `oclj_rangeguard53`). It speculated: an exact y outside that range left the trace, and the interpreter recomputed it.
- **What it bought on top of the fold** (both/fold, per round): crc-table 0.97 [0.95-1.03], crcprobe 0.97, crctm 0.95, mix 0.79, ident's small rows 0.82-0.91.
- **What it cost where exact results leave [-2^53, 2^53)**, in B-legal loops, which raise nothing under U1-B (`bench_spec.lua`), against base: hi60 6.64x, sh40 7.31x, top8 5.0x, half 3.64x, flag63 3.17x (6.27x against the fold alone); ident's big rows 2.0x (3.09x against the fold alone); and with `-Omaxside=0` (bench_spec at 2e6, one pass, 0.5 ns steps) hi60 11.5 ns against base's 0.5, flag63 12.5 against 1.5. Correctness held in every loop tried: verify-rg2's `adv.lua`, t_rg2, GENMODE=rg2 and its prototype's own suite.
- **Why it did not ship** (the user, 2026-10-20): a few percent on CRC32 against 3-7x on legal loops past 2^53.
- **What it taught, kept in this guide:** instructions a fold rule emits on the side, and stale pre-roll values ([8.4a](#84a-emitting-instructions-or-a-guard-from-a-fold-rule), P55); a side trace that re-enters its root through the pre-roll ([4.8](#48-side-traces)); measuring speculation on B-legal loops ([11.12](#1112-measuring-speed)); an IR signature that two rules share, which fooled a coverage count (P56); and results that are exact whatever their width, which a speculative guard inherits as needless failures ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)).
- **What stays from it:** `t_rg2.lua` in the floor, and `OCLJ_BITJIT_GENMODE=rg2` with negctl-bitop.sh's genrg2 catcher. Both hold the 2^53 boundary on any build ([11.4](#114-the-generator), [11.5](#115-the-floor-t_rec-t_jit2-t_rg2-p4v)).

**What remains of row 209** [M][S][I]:
- **Table-driven CRC32's last quarter**: 3.6-3.7 ns per byte against b0's 2.9. Its remaining guards are crc's three result guards and the table value's operand guard (above). Removing them needs an exactness fact that the width rule does not have: crc's bound is 64, since its value comes from table loads [S][I].
- **Bit-serial CRC32**: 70 ns per byte against b0's 48.5 (1.44 of it); the fold changes nothing there [M]. In `bench_bit.lua`'s loop, `b = (c ~ i) & 0xff`, then eight times `b = (b >> 1) ~ 0xedb88320` or `b = b >> 1`: `b >> 1` scores 65 - 1 = 64 by [3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)'s table, so b's results keep their guards [S]. **The next step is a design pass on loop-carried widths** (the user, 2026-10-20): an inductive bound for a PHI such as b, which the width rule scores 64; if b lies in [0, 2^32) on entry, every step keeps it there [I: an idea, not measured]. Such a bound would also need a shift rule that uses a non-negative operand's width [I]. Read first where this build's bit-serial hot code sits: review1 found it in side traces with no LOOP on the U1-A prototype, where values enter as doubles and no PHI exists [M review1; I for this build].
- **Beside it:** FIX-NUMCONV ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound), roadmap row 211 (5)) shipped on 2026-10-21, a round of its own; it removes identity operations' result guards and leaves crc's, whose values come from table loads: crc-table, crct and crctm read 1.00 of base in its timing chain [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/main/timing-1/summary.txt`]. A candidate: scoring a u8 operand 9 bits and a u16 17 (roadmap row 211 (1)), for which review1 measured crc masked each byte at 11.20-11.30 ns per byte against 8.25-8.30 with `byte & 0xff`, on the U1-A prototype b1 with the fold [M review1]; it does not help the unmasked CRC, where crc's width is unknown anyway [I].

**review1's material**, written against the U1-A prototype b1 on 2026-10-17 (the record: `bench/runs/2026-10-17-bitop/design/review1-results.json`, keys `review:tablefold` and `verify:tablefold:0`; its work directory on this machine: `C:/Users/astro/claude-tmp/oclj/r3/review/`), is in the repo in U1-B form since 2026-10-20: `t_idx.lua` and `t_cnt33.lua`, rebuilt with a PUC twin (`bitop/twin.lua`), which replaced `refidx.lua` [S].

## 9. Invariants and soundness arguments

Each pattern gives a rule, the reason it holds, round 3's instance, and what holds it in place. The arguments are the ones settled by review1 (the width fold; `design/review1-results.json`), the implementation review (`design/review2-results.json`) and `design/design-B.md` §3.4. They are numbered S1-S8 so the rest of the guide can cite them.

### 9.1 S1. Guard immediately after the value

- **Rule.** Emit the guard that establishes a property as the very next instructions after the value. Then every use, which is emitted later, comes after the guard.
- **Why it holds** [S]:
  - A trace is straight-line code, the pre-roll and then the loop body, so "earlier in trace order" means "executed first".
  - The loop copy keeps the order, and a hoisted guard's pre-roll copy comes before the whole loop.
  - A value carried by a PHI passed its guard in the pre-roll or in the previous iteration.
  - Store-to-load forwarding returns the stored ref, whose guard came before the store.
  - No snapshot sits between the value and its guard, because `oclj_rec_bitop` calls nothing that adds one, and the destination slot is written after the operator's IR [S][I]. This is a property of the path, not of the recorder: `lj_record_ins` adds a snapshot at an instruction's start when `J->needsnap` is set (~:2284), but some recorders add one mid-instruction, `rec_comp_prep` before every comparison guard (~:2202) among them ([4.3](#43-snapshots)). A new path must call none of them between a value and its guard.
- **Instance.** `oclj_rec_bitop` emits `d`, then `back`, then `EQ.i64`, and an operand's `back` and `EQ.num` the same way.
- **Held by.** INEXACT-JIT (no result guard at all) is caught by gen with at least 511 cases per seed, and since 2026-10-20 by genrg2 with 603-655 (two runs) [M].

### 9.2 S2. A fact that a guard justifies must not remove that guard

- **Rule.** A fold may use "past the guard, P holds". It must never use that fact to delete the guard that makes P hold. Only an independent proof may delete the guard.
- **Instance: the exact mark.** The guard's own `CONV i64.num d` carries the mark, and the exact-mark rule requires the outer CONV to be unmarked. So only a proof that reads no guard and no mark removes a guard: the width rule's range a result guard; since 2026-10-20 the table-index fold's bound of 32 the guarded `CONV int.num` of a result used as an int ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)); and since 2026-10-21 FIX-NUMCONV's exactness of an operand's own conversion, a property of the instruction `cvttsd2si` and of no guard, the result guard of an identity operation ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)) [S].
- **Held by.** The SELFGUARD arm lets the exact-mark rule fold the guard's own check. Every result guard then disappears, and inexact results flow on as rounded doubles; gen catches it with at least 514 cases per seed ([11.4](#114-the-generator)) [M].

### 9.3 S3. A mark that changes what folds may assume belongs in CSE identity, and outside the fold key

- **Rule.** If a bit on an instruction licenses a fold, CSE must not merge marked and unmarked twins, or a request without the licence can get back an instruction that has it. Keep the bit out of the fold key, so existing rules still apply.
- **Instance.** Bit 0x400 is inside `MODEMASK` and outside `0x3ff` ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)).
- **Instance, for types (2026-10-23): hunk 12.** The same rule holds for a type that changes the value. On x64 an int ref may stand in for an int64 operand (`simplify_conv_sext`'s LEFTFOLD), so an int instruction and one of our int64 instructions can share opcode and operands while their values differ, a 32-bit result zero-extended against the 64-bit one; upstream's `lj_opt_cse` compared operands only, and merged them (the CSE twins, P63). Hunk 12 puts the type's I64-ness into `lj_opt_cse`'s identity, `irt_isi64(IR(ref)->t) == irt_isi64(fins->t)`: the type alone, so the guard and PHI bits stay outside it as the exact mark stays outside the fold key, and guards and invariant hoisting merge as before ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)) [S]. A test of the whole `t` byte would have blocked guard and PHI merges, and with them the hoisting of invariant guards; it was rejected [S][I: the fix round's design 2.1], and `verify_content` refuses it [M: [8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) step 0].
- **How to check a new bit** [S][M]: grep every use of the field; classify each use as a mask (safe) or an equality (which must not involve your bit); check the backend and jit.dump; show the bit with `traceir`. For a type: grep every matcher that compares refs (`op12 ==`, `op1 ==` with `op2 ==`) for whether it compares widths too, as the CSE-twin design did (P63).
- **Held by** (hunk 12). CSEFWD, the forward merge let back in, caught by the cse part's t_csefwd and cseir; the reverse merge by `verify_content` on every build and the test's symmetry, with no arm ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages) item 8, [11.10](#1110-crash-fixes)) [M][S].

### 9.4 S4. Keep types stable across iterations

- **Rule.** A fold that can run in the loop copy and return a pre-roll ref must return a ref of the folded instruction's own type [S]. `loop_unroll` repairs only int↔num and lets I8..INT mismatches through; anything else raises TYPEINS, the loop is retried by recording further, the trace aborts, and after 11 aborts the loop is blacklisted ([4.4](#44-type-instability-typeins-retry-blacklist)).
- **Instance.** fix1. On x64, `simplify_conv_sext` returns an int ref for an i64 conversion, so a fold that crosses a PHI and returns `fleft->op1` must check `irt_isi64(IR(fleft->op1)->t)` ([4.5](#45-fix1-the-worked-case)).
- **Detection.** This is a speed bug, not a wrong value [M review1], so differential tests cannot see it. Assert on trace events instead: `p4v.lua` counts TYPEINS aborts and reads the loop's opcode (JFORL or IFORL), with no timing bound [S]. A fold that crosses a PHI and still computes the right values, as the PHIIDX sabotage does, is invisible to trace events as well; only a read of the IR sees it (`phiidx.lua`, [12.5](#125-jitutil)) [M].

### 9.5 S5. A fold that crosses a PHI may depend only on opcodes, IR types and constants

- **Rule.** A PHI-marked operand's definition is the pre-roll's, so it describes iteration one. Anything a fold reads from it must hold in every iteration.
- **Why it holds** [S][I]: IR types do not change between iterations (S4); constants are the same in every copy; each instruction in the loop copy computes the same opcode on the copies of its operands; folds and CSE preserve values (CSE has preserved them for mixed widths since 2026-10-23, when hunk 12 ended the int/i64 CSE twins, [3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400), P63; before it the premise was false for them, but the bound still covered such an instruction's actual value, since a twin's zero-extended register lay in [0, 2^32), so the conclusion below held for the bound, and the wrong value was the twin's own [I: `bench/runs/2026-10-23-shiftexact/` argument 8.4; the fix round's design 2.5]). By induction over the pre-roll's DAG, a bound computed from opcodes, types and constants covers every iteration's value, which is exactly the set of values the PHI carries.
- **What the code does not rely on**: "a loop copy's bound is at most the pre-roll's" (design.md §3.5); review1 replaced that argument.
- **Requirement 2 still applies.** A *new* instruction built on `fleft->op1` needs `PHIBARRIER`; for that reason the table-index fold excludes a PHI-marked `fleft` ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)). That exclusion is a conservative policy, the user's (a) of 2026-10-20: the PHIIDX sabotage, which crosses, computed the right values through a hint PHI that only one branch of `loop_unroll` adds, and PHIREUSE, which returns an existing ref instead, would meet requirement 2 [S][M: design.md gap 1].
- **Instance.** `oclj_bitwidth` reads `o`, the `irt_*` types and KINT64/KINT values, never a runtime value. `oclj_isnumconv` (FIX-NUMCONV, 2026-10-21) reads y's opcode, its IR type and its CONV mode word, a literal that `loop_unroll` never substitutes; the fact it stands for, that every value of such a y is exactly a double, holds in every iteration by the same induction, with "an exact value" in place of "a bounded range", given the guards emitted before y (S1) [S][I: step 1's `argument.md` section 4.6].
- **Held by.** The gen arms hold only local rule errors (WIDTH55, WSHR, WSHL0, WBAND and WADD0 are all caught [M]). A sabotage that broke iteration invariance would have to read live values, which the project's rules exclude, so that property rests on reading the source [S review1].

### 9.6 S6. List every way a value can enter the trace, and score each one from its type

- **Rule.** A bound or assumption must hold for values from outside the trace, and that list belongs in the rule's comment. For round 3 [S][M]:

| entry | IR | bound |
|---|---|---|
| a double slot, table load or upvalue load | `num SLOAD/ALOAD/HLOAD/ULOAD`, through `CONV i64.num` | 64 |
| a narrowed int (the loop index, `SLOAD ... C`) | int-typed, used directly after the LEFTFOLD | 33 |
| a u8/u16 load (`string.byte`) | `u8 XLOAD`, used directly after `simplify_conv_sext`'s u8/u16 LEFTFOLD | 64 |
| an int through `CONV i64.int` | | 32 with SEXT, 33 zero-extended |
| a parent trace's snapshot (in side traces) | `lj_snap_replay` (lj_snap.c ~:508) emits an `SLOAD PARENT` of the parent ref's type; for bit results, snapshots hold only `d` (num) or ints (measured on the cse part's shapes, and the reverse CSE twin closed since 2026-10-23, below) | 64 / 33 |
| a forwarded store | the stored ref itself: a marked `d` whose guard came before the store | (S1) |
| a PHI | the pre-roll definition | (S5) |

- **Why the list matters.** Round 3's first comments said every outside value enters through a CONV that scores 64. The implementation review found that int-typed values enter directly. The conclusion survived only because the bound of 33 comes from the type alone [S review2]. The select fix met the same lesson in the recorder: of the ways t_select feeds select's k to `rec_varg` (a checked conversion of a double or a string, the loop index's `ADDOV`, a bit result through the table-index fold, a narrowed sum), only the narrowed sum arrived wrapped ([3.13](#313-narrowing-and-why-round-3-goes-around-it)), and only it defeated a guard of k >= 1 ([8.6](#86-adding-a-guard) step 7) [M: t_select].
- **The int64 never crosses a trace boundary**: slots and snapshots hold the double `d`, so a side trace re-converts it behind a fresh guard ([4.8](#48-side-traces)) [S][M]. That snapshots hold only `d` or ints is read from the recorder and seen in every probe's SNAP lines, not proven for every recorder path [I]. Since 2026-10-23 it is measured on the cse part's shapes: cseir.lua's `i64slot` counts the snapshot entries that name a non-constant I64 instruction in every trace of its loops and t_cserev's, and read 0 in every line, on `build/native` (456 case-runs in each of the test implementer's runs on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8, before `copy` and `side` were added, and every line of the fixer's six) and on the fixed build [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/impl-tests/runs/`, `csetwin/round/fix/runs/cse-base-1` to `-6`, `cse-fix-1` to `-3`; [11.5d](#115d-the-cse-part-t_csefwd-cseir-t_cserev)]. The one known way past it was the reverse CSE twin, closed by hunk 12 (P63): an int producer recorded after our I64 instruction on the same operands got it back, and where that producer stores its result in a slot (the bit library's `J->base[0]`, an inner FORL's index slots) a snapshot would carry an I64 that `snap_restoreval` has no case for [S][I: `bench/runs/2026-10-23-shiftexact/`, the argument's 8.6; the fix round's design 1.3]. That half was never run; it is held by `verify_content` on every build, by the test's symmetry, and by t_cserev's two shapes that store nothing in a slot ([11.10](#1110-crash-fixes)).

### 9.7 S7. Test at the boundary, with values the lexer can actually spell

- **Rule.** Hold each threshold with a case one step past it, built from operands that really are what the test assumes.
- **Instances** [M review1]:
  - WIDTH55's boundary is tested this way.
  - t_jit2's `rec-m54` was vacuous: its mask `0x3fffffffffffff` has 54 significant bits, so the lexer reads it as 2^54, and x was 0 in all 400 iterations (P47).
  - Four of t_rec's nine masks did not act as masks either.
  - A claim about which results are exact must be checked on chained idioms: the rotate raises for n in 1..10 (P16).
  - t_idx's first `fori` consumer, `for j = e, e + 1`, was vacuous: its double start made `rec_for` record a number loop, with no conversion to fold; `for j = 1, e` (an int start) caught WIDTHIDX33 in 21-23 cases per option set, and t_idx's catch under the default options rose from 28 to 52 [M: verify-folda F1, the CRC32 follow-up].
- **Shapes.** Write tests for the shape left after other folds: `simplify_shiftk_andk` rewrites `(x & m) << k`, so a BSHL test needs an operand that is not a BAND ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)).

### 9.8 S8. Know what an exit re-executes

- **Rule.** A guard must exit to where the interpreter recomputes the guarded operation and fails the way 5.3.6 does.
- **Instances:**
  - **Same iteration, same message.** Bit-operator guards exit through the newest snapshot, whose pc is at or before the operator; the instructions in between are re-executable ([4.3](#43-snapshots)), and the interpreter raises in the same iteration with the same message. Measured in [4.7](#47-exits), and by review1 on the U1-A prototype: 25 200 bad-operand placements, with PUC = `-joff` = JIT = `-Ohotloop=1` [M].
  - **Exit, and let the interpreter decide.** The select fix's guard exits wherever its path cannot give the interpreter's answer, and the interpreter re-executes the call and raises "index out of range" or returns; t_select reads the same results and errors as `-joff` in 15 option sets, exits and side traces among them [M: [11.5b](#115b-the-sel-part-t_select)]. It exits through the snapshot that upstream's own guards on that path use [S]: in a path-C loop the CONV guard on k (`jnz`/`jpe`), the new guard (`lea`, `cmp` with 0x08000000, `jnb`) and path C's own guard (`jle`) all jump to one exit, exit 4 in the loop and exit 1 in the pre-roll, under one snapshot whose pc lets the interpreter re-execute the load of k, the `VARG` and the call, and raise in the same iteration; the guard cost 18 bytes of machine code in that two-copy trace, 759 to 777 [M: the select round's code verifier, `probes/snap1.lua` under `-jdump=+rsTx` and `+m`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/verify-code/runs/snap-1/`, `runs/mcode-1/`].
  - **Record-time abort.** The recorder aborts when a result is inexact now (`oclj_rec_bitop`), because a trace recorded through it would always leave [S]. The select fix also aborts for a recorded k above 2^27: on path C such a trace would fail its own guard every time, and on path A a constant from about 2^28 overflows in C (P54); a constant from 2^27 + 1 to about 2^28 would have compiled a right nil, and now runs in the interpreter; the user kept both the abort and the bound 2^27 on 2026-10-21 (roadmap row 213) [S]; removing that abort (SELRECK) changes no value either, but unlike INEXACT-RECABORT it compiles traces that `t_select-kmax` counts, so it is an arm ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages) item 9) [M].
  - **No catcher for that abort.** Removing it gives the same raises and changes only the abort's reason (INEXACT-RECABORT; [2.6](#26-the-recorder)) [M].

### 9.9 One source of truth for every C path

- **Rule.** When the parser, the slow path and the recorder each compute an operator in C, they call one function, so they cannot disagree. The fast path in DynASM and the IR cannot call it, so they are tested against it [S].
- **Instance.** `oclj_bitop.h` holds `oclj_bitop64`, `oclj_bit2num` and the shift rules with no Lua state; `foldbitop`, `oclj_meta_bitop` and `oclj_rec_bitop` all call them; `oclj_num53.h`'s readers are shared with `string.pack` [S].
- **Held by.** The arms in the header (NEGSHIFT, SARSHR, RANGE-HI) break all C paths at once and are caught by the matrix and the fuzzer, which compare against PUC [M].

### 9.10 The interpreter's own invariants

- **Store the result last**, after every check that can exit; the slow path then recomputes from untouched operands ([6.5](#65-what-a-handler-may-clobber)) [S].
- **Honour each slow-path label's register contract**, or add a label that reloads (`->vmeta_bitop_pc`) ([6.7](#67-round-3s-checks-instruction-by-instruction)) [S].
- **No push or pop in a handler, and BASE reloaded after every C call** ([6.5](#65-what-a-handler-may-clobber), [6.6](#66-calling-c-from-the-interpreter)) [S][I].
- **Never remove the CHECKHOOK lines** near the head of every trace and after its `LOOP`; the watchdog depends on them (P31, [docs/watchdog.md](watchdog.md)) [S].

## 10. Pitfalls

Each entry gives the symptom, the cause with its evidence, and the fix; where a section already holds the evidence, the entry points there. P1-P18 were known going into this guide (the brief's sixteen, its tenth split three ways); P19-P48 were found in round 3's evidence or by this guide's probes, P49-P52 in the glibc round (2026-10-19), P53-P58 in the CRC32 follow-up (2026-10-20), P59 in the select fix (2026-10-20), which also turned P54 into the record of its fix, P60-P62 in FIX-NUMCONV (2026-10-21), and P63 in the CSE-twin fix (2026-10-23). The numbers are stable: cite them, and append new ones at the end.

| area | pitfalls |
|---|---|
| LuaJIT internals without FFI | P1, P3, P9 |
| IR, folds, loops, exits | P2, P29, P32, P38, P40, P41, P42, P54, P55, P60, P63 |
| reading dumps and tools | P10, P11, P12, P30, P31, P35, P36, P39, P48 |
| the build | P4, P5, P13, P14, P15, P19, P43, P44, P45, P46, P49, P50, P62 |
| text, shells and line endings | P7, P8, P18, P20, P51, P52, P53, P58 |
| tests and measurement | P6, P16, P17, P21, P22, P23, P24, P25, P26, P27, P28, P33, P34, P37, P47, P56, P57, P59, P61 |

**P1. Without FFI, the int64 constant folds are stubbed, and one returns a wrong value.** *Symptom:* `BAND`, `BOR` or `BXOR` of two KINT64 constants folds to its left operand, with no error; most other int64 folds return FAILFOLD, which aborts the trace [S]. *Cause:* `kfold_int64arith` (lj_opt_fold.c ~:368) compiles its bit cases only `#if LJ_HASFFI` (~:376); the operator falls into `default: lj_assertJ(0, ...)`, which a release build compiles to `((void)J)` (lj_jit.h ~:522-525), and returns k1. The seven stubs: `kfold_int64arith` ~:376, `kfold_int64shift` ~:432, `kfold_bnot64` ~:444, `kfold_int64comp` ~:471, `kfold_int64comp0` ~:492, `simplify_andor_k64` ~:1746, `reassoc_intarith_k64` ~:1794 [S]. KFOLD64 (the first stub put back) is caught by fuzz mode U (431-436 mismatches) and t_rec (4-14 lines per seed) [M]. *Fix:* round 3 turns the seven into `#if 1` ([3.10](#310-the-two-rules)). Before relying on a LuaJIT function, grep it for `LJ_HASFFI`, `LJ_DUALNUM` and `LJ_GC64` [I]; `narrow_convert` hid an assumption of the same kind (NARROW) [S].

**P2. TYPEINS: a loop-carried value whose IR type changes is unrolled, aborted, then blacklisted.** *Symptom:* a loop never compiles; `-jv` shows "persistent type instability" aborts, then silence, or later `blacklisted at` lines from other recordings (P39). Or a loop compiles with its body recorded twice [S][M]. *Cause:* `loop_unroll` raises `LJ_TRERR_TYPEINS` for any type change it cannot repair: int against i64, true against false, str against num ([4.4](#44-type-instability-typeins-retry-blacklist)). A build without fix1 read `traces=0 typeins=11 loop=IFORL` for 7 of the 8 p4 loops [M] (`impl-C2.json`). *Fix:* never hand a PHI a ref of a different IR type from the pre-roll's (S4, fix1); test such loops with trace events and the JFORL/IFORL opcode, never with a timing (`p4v.lua`) [S].

**P3. `jit.util.tracek` and `-jdump` print a KINT64 constant as the trace number.** *Symptom:* `i64 BAND 0011 +1` where the mask is 0x3FFFC, `BOR 0015 +1` where the constant is 2^32; in trace 2 the same masks print `+2`; in trace 1 a mask looks exactly like a real constant 1 [M]. *Cause:* `lj_ir_kvalue` (lj_ir.c ~:372) has a KINT64 case only `#if LJ_HASFFI` (~:385-391), so without FFI it writes nothing, and `jit_util_tracek` (lib_jit.c ~:319) returns the slot that still holds its first argument, the trace number [S][M]. Walking every `k` with `tracek` also hits the second slot of each 64-bit constant (GC64) [M][I]. Roadmap row 206 records it; values are unaffected [S]. *Fix:* read an int64 constant from the `m` dump (`and rdi, 0x0003fffc`, `mov rcx, 0x0000000100000000`) or from the source; walk constants from IR operands, as dump.lua does [M]. The real fix, a non-FFI KINT64 case in `lj_ir_kvalue`, is not done [I].

**P4. `Makefile.dep` does not know the `#include`d files, so a build can be stale.** *Symptom:* you edit an `oclj_*` file, run make, nothing is recompiled, and the old code runs [M]. *Cause:* `Makefile.dep` is upstream's `gcc -MM` output and mentions no oclj file; a .dasc whose mtime is older than `buildvm_arch.h` is equally invisible ([7.2](#72-dependency-traps)) [M]. *Fix:* delete the including objects yourself ([7.4](#74-running-make-by-hand)); build-native.sh runs `make clean` and gates each object's freshness against every file it includes [S].

**P5. `Q=.` is not `Q=`.** *Symptom:* commands named `.host/minilua.exe`, `.host/buildvm.exe` and `.gcc`, which do not exist; or, with the default `Q=@`, no command lines in the log for the gates to match [M][S]. *Cause:* `Q` (Makefile ~:625) is pasted in front of every command; `mingw32-make -n BUILDMODE=static Q=.` printed `.host/minilua.exe ../dynasm/dynasm.lua ... vm_x64.dasc` and `.gcc ... host/buildvm.c` [M]. A `Q=` at the end of a sentence in prose reads as `Q=.`, as in one implementer's report [S]. *Fix:* pass `Q=` (empty), and write it in a code span [S].

**P6. Hot counters are shared and penalties are random, so "it compiles after N iterations" is approximate.** *Symptom:* exact counts of iterations, traces or aborts differ between processes or between loops; negctl's JIT catchers' margins vary from run to run [M]. *Cause:* the start value is fixed (recording began at iteration 56 in 6 of 6 processes [M]), but the 64 counters are hashed by pc, and each abort penalty adds 4 random bits from a per-state PRNG; one loop's 11th abort came between iterations 38,215 and 42,958 across 6 processes ([2.5](#25-hot-counting-and-trace-start)) [S][M]. *Fix:* option sets and coverage gates, not exact counts; gate negctl catchers on "nonzero"; find where a trace starts from its `start` event; `jit.flush()` between phases; give each measured loop its own prototype [S][I]. An abort's penalty lands in a shared counter too, and can keep an unrelated loop from compiling (P59) [S][I].

**P7. The pristine tree is CRLF, Windows Python writes CRLF, and Git Bash's awk and grep hide CRs.** *Symptom:* an anchor never matches, a patched file turns CRLF, or a CR count says 0 when there are CRs [M]. *Cause:* the pristine checkout is CRLF (lj_opt_fold.c 2653 CR / 2653 LF); Python 3.13 `open(p, 'w')` wrote CR LF, and `newline=''` wrote LF; Git Bash's awk drops a line-end CR unless `-v BINMODE=1`, and `grep -c $'\r'` counts 0 without `-U` ([7.3](#73-line-endings-and-the-git-bash-instrument)) [M]. *Fix:* anchor patch scripts on CR-stripped text and write LF; in Python use `newline=''` for reading and writing; count CRs by bytes (`open(f,'rb').read().count(13)`, `grep -c -U`, `od -c`) [S][M].

**P8. Heredocs, and any command text, eat backslashes in the agent's shell tool, even when quoted.** *Symptom:* `\b` in a regex becomes a literal backspace, or a doubled backslash a single one, silently [M]. *Cause:* through the shell tool, `cat <<'EOF'` turned a doubled backslash before B into a single one, and `python - <<'PY'` with a doubled backslash between `a` and `b` in a string literal gave `'a\x08'` [M]; one negctl probe's awk edit lost its backslashes and the build failed [M] (`impl-C3.json`). It is not only heredocs: in the CRC32 follow-up a `python -c` whose text held `b'\r'` failed with a SyntaxError, and backslashes in `printf` and `grep` arguments are exposed the same way [M: design.md gap 31]; two others in that round's command text happened to survive, so a pass proves nothing [M: the implementer's report]. That the collapse happens in the tool's transport rather than in bash is inferred [I]. *Fix:* write scripts with a file-writing tool and run the file; write patch scripts with no backslashes and no tabs (`@T@` for a tab); and keep backslashes out of command text: count bytes with a Python file or `od -tx1`, join lines with awk's `printf`, split a word list with a `for` loop, and take a field with awk's `match` and `substr` instead of a sed group [S][M: the implementer's idioms]; match a bracket with `grep -F '['` rather than an escaped one, and make any edit with nested quotes in a `.py` file [S: the code verifier, who broke the rule three times in read-only commands that happened to work].

**P9. `jit.util.funcbc` returned signed words, and `jit/bc.lua` broke under 64-bit `>>`.** *Symptom:* before change 12, `luajit -bl` raised at the first forward jump, FORI or negative KSHORT, and `-jdump` printed "VM handler failed: ./jit/bc.lua:68: result of '>>' has more than 53 significant bits" and aborted every trace it recorded [M]. *Cause:* `jit_util_funcbc` used `setintV` (lib_jit.c ~:212-220) and `jit/bc.lua` decodes with `(ins >> 8) & 0xff`; `jit/dump.lua` reads a snapshot entry's slot as `sn >> 24`, so `tracesnap` had the same problem [S]. Re-signing `funcbc`'s words through `LUA_INIT` reproduces both symptoms on today's build [M]. *Fix:* change 12 returns unsigned numbers; the jit tools gate holds `-bl` and `-jdump` [S]. Any tool that shifts bytecode words must mask them first [I].

**P10. `luajit -b` exits 0 when the listing raises.** *Symptom:* a script that runs `luajit -bl` and checks `$?` passes while stderr holds a Lua error [M]. *Cause:* `dobytecode` (luajit.c ~:391-406) returns -1, and `main` fails only for a status above 0 (~:599); bcsave's own checks (missing input, usage, "FFI library required") call `os.exit(1)` [S][M]. *Fix:* gate on empty stderr and on the number of listed lines, as the jit tools gate's `blerr` and `bl` fields do [S].

**P11. `jit.attach` keeps one handler per event.** *Symptom:* `-jv` or `-jdump` prints nothing once a probe attaches its own `"trace"` handler; `-jv -jdump=tT` prints only dump's lines, and `-jdump=tT -jv` only `-jv`'s [M]. *Cause:* `jit_attach` (lib_jit.c ~:123) stores one handler per event name; attaching A then B to `"trace"` called A 0 times and B twice [S][M]. *Fix:* count events in your own handler and print `-jv`-style lines from it; one `-j` module per run; detach with `jit.attach(f)` [S]. To count events beside a script that attaches its own handler (drv.lua, a suite), wrap `jit.attach` before loading it, so that the script's handler and yours both run: `local attach = jit.attach; jit.attach = function(f, ev) if ev == "trace" and f then return attach(function(...) f(...); mine(...) end, "trace") end; return attach(f, ev) end`, then `dofile` the script (verify-rg2's `stalegen.lua`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc/verify-rg2/probes/`) [S][M]. A probe that attaches its own handler also silences `-jdump`, so read the IR through `jit.util` ([12.5](#125-jitutil)) [M: design.md gap 32]. The idx part's suites attach one through twin.lua's `coverage()`: to read a twin suite's IR with `-jdump`, run it beside a copy of twin.lua whose `coverage()` is a stub [M: the code verifier, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/verify-code/scripts/mknocov.py`; its dump stopped at trace 33 before the stub and was complete after].

**P12. `-jdump=T` only picks plain text, and colour follows `TERM`.** *Symptom:* `-jdump=T` prints the whole default dump, not just trace lines; ANSI codes appear in a log [S][M]. *Cause:* `dumpon` (dump.lua ~:677-690) strips `T`, `A` and `H` from the mode, and an empty rest keeps the default `t b i m`; the colour default comes from `TERM` (ANSI when it contains "color"), and from `COLORTERM` only when `TERM` is unset, whatever the redirection; Git Bash sets `TERM=xterm-256color` [S][M]. *Fix:* `-jdump=tT` for trace lines, `-jdump=+rsT` or similar for logs; always include `T` when grepping or diffing [S].

**P13. `luajit.exe`'s md5 changes on every link.** *Symptom:* two identical builds have different exe md5s. *Cause:* the PE header carries the link time (`objdump -p`: `Time/Date Sat Oct 17 12:29:30 2026`) [M]. *Fix:* compare objects, as `logs/builds-2/natives.md5` and `objects-changed.txt` do [S].

**P14. A `vm_x64.dasc` edit also changes `lj_bc.o`, and `buildvm -m folddef` reads only `lj_opt_fold.c`'s LJFOLD lines.** *Symptom:* an object you did not expect to change does; a fold rule moved into an `#include`d file is silently never registered [M][S]. *Cause:* `lj_bcdef.h` holds the handler offsets, which a 32-bit `and` without REX.W moved [M] (`impl-C3.json`); `buildvm_fold.c` (~:197-220) scans `lj_opt_fold.c` alone for lines that begin `LJFOLD` ([7.2](#72-dependency-traps)) [S]. *Fix:* a `.dasc` change reaches `lj_vm.o`, `lj_bc.o` and `host/buildvm.o`; keep every `LJFOLD` line and rule body in `lj_opt_fold.c` at column 0 and move only helpers out [S].

**P15. bash's `-nt` is true when the second file is missing.** *Symptom:* a freshness gate passes against a file that does not exist. *Cause:* `[ exists -nt missing ]` is true in bash 5.3.15 and in `sh` [M]; build-native.sh ~:1528 says so [S]. *Fix:* `[ -f "$src" ] && [ "$obj" -nt "$src" ]` [S].

**P16. Claims about exactness must be checked on chained idioms and products, not single operators.** *Symptom:* the design said that only `>>` of a negative number is inexact with ordinary operands, and that code masked to 32 bits matches 5.3; both hold for one operator and fail for chains [M] (round 3 README). *Cause:* in 5.3's rotate, `((a >> n) | (a << (32 - n))) & 0xFFFFFFFF`, the `|` joins the pieces into up to 64 - n significant bits before the mask; for a = 0xFFFFFFFF or 0xDEADBEEF it raises for n = 1-10, for a = 0x80000001 never [M] (`design/rotate/`). The harness found it in `p1-sha256` after every unit suite passed [M] (`logs/harness-prefix/`). FNV-1a's double product passes 2^53 before the mask, a wrong value without an error [M] (`fix-results.json`, R3-docs). *Fix:* test real idioms with random words, as the generator and the harness do; write the masked forms (`(a >> n) | ((a << (32 - n)) & 0xFFFFFFFF)`); limit any exactness claim to sums and products below 2^53 [S].

**P17. Never edit a script that a running chain executes.** *Symptom:* a chain dies after a long run with "syntax error near unexpected token", exit 2, and its verdict is lost [M] (round 3 README, deviation 9). *Cause:* `sh` reads a script incrementally; `run-bitfuzz.sh` was edited during a 50-seed campaign, and the shell read the new file at a stale offset [M]. *Fix:* finish or copy the running scripts first, and tell agents which files are in use [S].

**P18. Surprising readings are usually the instrument: CRLF from Windows stdout.** *Symptom:* false differences between outputs identical apart from line endings. *Cause:* `luajit.exe -e "print(1)"` and `lua.exe -e "print(1)"` each write 3 bytes, 1 of them a CR; one ad-hoc diff showed 25 false differences [M] (`impl-C2.json`). *Fix:* `tr -d '\r'` on every Windows output before comparing, as each runner's `run()` does; more generally, [11.9](#119-instrument-first) [S].

**P19. `build/native/luajit_build.log` is the last platform's build.** *Symptom:* the DynASM line lacks `-D WIN` and shows `host/minilua` without `.exe` [M]. *Cause:* the log lives in `$OCLJ_BUILD`, not per platform; on 2026-10-17 it held the WSL build while the Windows `luajit.exe` was older [M]. *Fix:* read the log from the round's archive or a per-platform `OCLJ_BUILD`, and check the platform before drawing a conclusion [I]. Since the glibc round the Linux release build is also made into `build/native`, from AlmaLinux 8, so the log there can be either platform's, and on Linux either distribution's: gcc 8.5.0 and no `-D SHADOW_STACK` in AlmaLinux 8, gcc 15.2 and `-D SHADOW_STACK` in Ubuntu ([6.8](#68-the--d-flags)) [S][M].

**P20. Binaries do not survive `.gitattributes`.** *Symptom:* a committed `libluajit.a` fails `ar t` in a clone. *Cause:* `* text eol=lf` rewrites CRLF byte pairs in binaries [S] (`bench/runs/2026-10-16-noffi/README.md`). *Fix:* archive the md5s of binaries; keep the binaries outside the repo [S].

**P21. The Windows PUC 5.3.6 mis-rounds long hex-float strings.** *Symptom:* `"0x1.0000000000000801p52" | 0` gives 4503599627370496 on the Windows PUC, against 4503599627370497 on the Linux PUC and in round 3 [M] (roadmap row 211 (4)). *Fix:* derive any expectation that depends on such a string from a Linux PUC [S].

**P22. `LUA_INIT` reaches both VMs.** *Symptom:* a JIT-on run that is really interpreted, and passes: `LUA_INIT='if jit then jit.off() end'` made `run-bitfuzz.sh` print BITFUZZ PASS with `hotcases 0/300 ... jit off` [M] (review2). *Fix:* run-bitop.sh, run-bitjit.sh and run-bitfuzz.sh unset `LUA_INIT` and `LUA_INIT_5_3`, and the generator and the fuzzer require `jit on` in the JIT-on run's `#cov` line [S]. negctl-bitop.sh does not unset them itself, and its `gate` and `stage4` catchers run luajit.exe directly: unset both before running it ([12.10](#1210-our-runners-and-their-knobs)) [S].

**P23. `%.17g` hides values beyond 2^53.** *Symptom:* PUC's 9007199254740993 and our 9007199254740992 print alike, so three LEX deviations stayed invisible to the provenance check [M] (review2; `fix-results.json` FF-B, FF-C). *Fix:* print integers with `%d` on PUC; count significant bits with integer shifts, as `xrule.lua` does [S].

**P24. An end-anchored error classifier breaks when a message grows.** *Symptom:* after the hint was appended, the old `bitfuzz.lua` classifier reported 1510 mismatches (`E:INEXACT` read as `E:OTHER`; seed 1, 300 cases) on a correct build [M] (`fix-results.json`, R2-tests). *Fix:* classify by a stable prefix, and let one suite pin the whole text (the matrix, `bitop_test.c` BT3) [S].

**P25. A gate that sees only where a loop raised.** *Symptom:* a JIT that computes wrong in-range values but raises at the right iteration passes [M] (review2). *Cause:* the bitops gate's loop kept its sum in a local, which the raise at i = 151 discarded [S]. *Fix:* carry the running result out of the loop (an upvalue `acc`, 43800, PUC's value) and compare it; KFOLD64 now fails the gate with `acc=399431978520 same=false` [S][M].

**P26. A one-way diff, or a short output, compares equal.** *Symptom:* an empty JIT-on output counted as 0 differences [M] (review2: the old `ndiff`). *Fix:* count differences both ways and require the reference's line count; `bitfuzz-cmp.lua` looks for `#end` before it reads any tuple [S].

**P27. A JIT that compiles nothing passes a differential test.** *Symptom:* 0 differences from a build that records no bit operator and traced 30-42 of 2000 loops [M] (`impl-C2.json`). *Fix:* coverage gates (`OCLJ_BITJIT_HOTMIN`, `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_HOTMIN`, 0.25), `jit on` in every `#cov` line, and PROVED NOTHING (exit 2) when they fail [S].

**P28. Test loops that never compile.** *Symptom:* a loop meant to test a guard runs interpreted (`-jv`: `NYI: bytecode FNEW`), or raises before iteration 56 and never reaches the JIT (7 of 20 old loops raised by iteration 71) [M] (`impl-C2.json`). *Fix:* hoist closures out of loops; keep variants whose results are all exact; check coverage with trace events (`stop` in the loop's function) [S].

**P29. Trace numbers are reused.** *Symptom:* per-trace bookkeeping attributes a later trace to an earlier function [I]. *Cause:* `trace_abort` frees the number after its event (lj_trace.c ~:647), and `trace_findfree` (~:61) hands it out again [S]. *Fix:* set per-trace state on the `start` event (the gate's `mine[tr] = (func == hot)`) [S].

**P30. The exact mark is invisible in `-jdump`.** *Symptom:* a marked and an unmarked CONV print alike, `i64 CONV 0012 i64.num none` [M]. *Cause:* dump.lua's CONV formatter (~:282-292) prints the types, `sext` and the check kind, never bit 0x400 [S]. *Fix:* read `op2 & 0x400` through `jit.util.traceir` ([12.6](#126-reading-the-exact-mark)): `0x05d5` and `0x16ae` are marked, `0x12ae` is not [M].

**P31. Every trace carries three hook-check IR lines.** *Symptom:* an `XLOAD`, `BAND +12` and guarded `EQ +0` that no source line explains [M]. *Cause:* CHECKHOOK emits them at the end of `lj_record_setup` (lj_record.c ~:2953-2973), so they come right after what setup emits first: the loop control's SLOADs (and a range guard for a variable stop) in a root trace, the `PI` SLOADs in a side trace (`guide1`: 0002-0004 after `0001 SLOAD`; `side1`'s trace 2: 0003-0005). Being volatile, they are emitted again right after `LOOP` [M][S]. [docs/watchdog.md](watchdog.md) measures their cost [S]. *Fix:* ignore them when reading IR; never remove them [S].

**P32. `PROTO_BITOP` lies inside the closure counter.** *Symptom:* in a function that uses any bitwise operator, immutable upvalues stay loads (`UREFO` or `UREFC` plus `ULOAD`) instead of becoming constants [M] (roadmap row 212). *Cause:* `PROTO_BITOP` is 0x80, inside the 3-bit counter at 0x20..0xe0 ([2.2](#22-lexer-and-parser)) [S]. *Fix:* account for it when reading IR or timing code; upstream's, speed only, not fixed in our fork (the flags byte has no free bit) [S].

**P33. Repeated calls of one loaded chunk run slower and slower.** *Symptom:* reps that climb (0.559, 0.717, 0.941 s) when one loaded chunk is called five times; a `jit.flush()` before each call removed the climb [M] (`docs/in-game-tests.md` ~:294-303). The account, later calls running through traces compiled for earlier closures, is not measured [I]. *Fix:* load the chunk once per rep, or `jit.flush()` between calls, and compare like with like [M].

**P34.
