A sabotage that changes nothing visible proves nothing.** *Symptom:* an arm "not caught" because it changed no behaviour any test can see, or no code at all: the no-op `1 &&`, or `RANGE-HI`'s obvious `<=`, which does nothing on x86 because `(int64_t)2^63` is INT64_MIN and the round trip refuses it anyway [M][S] (`impl-C3.json`). *Fix:* negctl's object fingerprint marks an arm BROKEN when no reached object changes; drop an arm whose only effect is invisible, such as an abort reason, and record the measurement [S]. A sabotage that changes the code but no value is a different case: it needs a catcher that reads the IR's shape or counts exits. The table-index fold's PHIIDX (the fold across a PHI) and NOLOOPFOLD (no fold in a loop body) are caught by phiidx.lua's IR read alone, and rg2's prototype had four such arms (RG2-SHR, RG2-52, RG2-OFF, RG2-PREROLL), caught only by its own IR-reading suite ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages) item 9) [M: design.md gap 18].

**P35. `-O` order matters, and `jit.opt` settings last for the process.** *Symptom:* `-O-loop -O3` runs with loop optimization on; a test that calls `jit.opt.start` changes every later test in the process [M]. *Cause:* `-O0`..`-O3` replace the whole flag set (`jitopt_level`, lib_jit.c ~:438) [S]. *Fix:* levels first, flags after (`-O3 -O-loop`); one option set per process, as the runners do [S].

**P36. `jit.off(f)` acts on the prototype.** *Symptom:* turning off one closure also turns off its siblings from the same prototype and flushes their traces [S] (`setptmode`, lj_dispatch.c ~:225). *Fix:* write a JIT-off twin as a separate function with the same text (the gate's `cold`) [S].

**P37. Hook tests need COUNT or LINE in the mask.** *Symptom:* a hook that should fire inside a compiled loop never does [S] (docs/watchdog.md, failure-mode table). *Cause:* the CHECKHOOK guard tests `hookmask & (LUA_MASKLINE | LUA_MASKCOUNT)` only [S]. *Fix:* set a COUNT hook in tests of hooks inside compiled code, as `bitop_test.c`'s BT5 does (`LUA_MASKCOUNT`, count 500) [S].

**P38. `i64.num none` in a dump is the unchecked conversion, not "no conversion".** *Symptom:* an operand conversion looks free [M]. *Cause:* `IRCONV_NONE` == `IRCONV_ANY` (lj_ir.h ~:263-266), which dump.lua prints as `none`; it is a bare `cvttsd2si` [S][M]. *Fix:* read `none` as "unchecked"; the check is the round trip and `EQ` that follow ([3.4](#34-the-conv-mode-word-op2)) [S].

**P39. `-jv` prints no line when a loop is blacklisted.** *Symptom:* 11 aborts, then silence; the loop looks idle, not refused [M]. *Cause:* `blacklist_pc` (lj_trace.c ~:380) sends no vmevent [S]. A later `-jv` line `[TRACE --- <file>:<n> -- blacklisted at <file>:<loop line>]` is not the blacklisting. It is `LJ_TRERR_BLACKL`: another recording (an enclosing loop, or the function's entry) aborted on reaching the patched `IFORL` (`lj_record_ins`, `case BC_IFORL`, ~:2721-2726), so it appears only when something else records through the loop. A probe whose loop was blacklisted by a shift count of 64 and then called from a hot outer loop printed 11 `bad argument type` aborts, then 5 such lines (3 from the outer loop, 2 from the function's entry). review1's "x11, then `blacklisted at p4.lua:3`" ([4.4](#44-type-instability-typeins-retry-blacklist)) is such a line [S][M]. *Fix:* read the loop's opcode: `IFORL` means blacklisted (`p4v.lua` `loopop`; [12.4](#124-counting-traces-and-aborts-per-function)) [S].

**P40. A guard resumes at the newest snapshot's pc, often several instructions before the operator.** *Symptom:* an exit seems to land at the wrong instruction, or code before the operator runs again [M]. *Cause:* a bit operator takes no snapshot; its guards exit through the newest one, which may be at the start of the loop body ([4.3](#43-snapshots)) [S][M]. *Fix:* `jit.util.tracesnap(tr, sn, true)` gives each snapshot's pc; a new side effect must set `J->needsnap` so it is never re-executed (S8) [S].

**P41. The width rule scores the x64 FORL index 33, not 32.** *Symptom:* `i << 21` has no result guard but `i << 22` keeps one, a bit earlier than expected [M]. *Cause:* `simplify_conv_sext` removes the index's sign extension, and an `IRT_INT` ref scores 33 ([2.6](#26-the-recorder)) [S]. *Fix:* count 33 for the int loop index and 64 for a `string.byte` operand (a `u8 XLOAD`, which is not IRT_INT; [3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)) when predicting guards; narrower u8/u16 scores are roadmap row 211 (1) [S][M].

**P42. ORDER BIT differs between bytecode and IR.** *Symptom:* a bytecode-to-IR mapping by arithmetic picks the wrong IR op for BNOT or a shift [S]. *Cause:* `IR_BSWAP` sits between `IR_BNOT` and `IR_BAND` (lj_ir.h ~:56-66); `BC_BNOT..BC_BSAR` have no such gap [S]. *Fix:* map by arithmetic only for BAND..BXOR and name the others, as `oclj_rec_bitop` does [S].

**P43. `make clean` deletes `host/buildvm_arch.h`.** *Symptom:* after `make clean`, make regenerates the interpreter from a stale or unpatched .dasc [S]. *Cause:* `buildvm_arch.h` is in ALL_HDRGEN (Makefile :544-548) [S]. *Fix:* re-copy and re-patch the .dasc before `make clean`, as build-native.sh does (:1421-1460) [S].

**P44. A kept build copy let another tree's files through (fixed 2026-10-20).** *Symptom:* a native stamped `1ee778a4` that is not the pin's code: after an `OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED=1` build of `8c23d6aa`, a pinned build in the same `OCLJ_BUILD` compiled the parent's `lj_opt_mem.o` (`05d34fc1`, not `42bd49f9`), and gradle accepted the DLL; a hand edit to an unpatched file in `build/` survived every build the same way [M: the pin round's review, `pin/verify/holes/`; `pin2/impl/v2/head-step2.log`]. *Cause:* build-native.sh copied the tree only when the build copy was missing and re-took only the 12 patched files and our 7 [S]. *Fix, in the script:* every run removes the build copy and makes it afresh, a pinned run from the pin's tree exported with `git archive` and checked against the pin's 40 digits link by link (a replace ref, a filter or attribute, or a forged object let another tree through the first export; the build-copy round's review), never from the checkout's working tree (THE BUILD COPY, :1042-1196; [1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is), [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)); the same sequence now builds `42bd49f9` [M: `pin2/impl/v2/new-step2.log`, `pin3/impl/fc4e-w2f1/`]. *For you:* an edit in `build/` is gone at the next run, so change LuaJIT through a patch script in `native/luajit/`, and try upstream edits in a scratch clone with `OCLJ_LUAJIT_UNPINNED=1` and its own `OCLJ_BUILD` [S].

**P45. build-native.sh calls `make`; WinLibs ships only `mingw32-make`.** *Symptom:* `make: command not found` in a plain Git Bash [M]. *Cause:* the PREREQUISITES footer says "GNU make comes with it", but the binary is `mingw32-make`; this machine's launchers prepend `/c/Users/astro/claude-tmp/oclj/bin`, which holds a `make` that runs `mingw32-make` [M]. *Fix:* copy `bench/runs/2026-10-13-dispose/scripts/make-shim` (`exec mingw32-make "$@"`, the archived copy of that file) as `make` into a directory you put first on PATH, or call `mingw32-make` by hand [M]. On Linux the binary is `make` (GNU Make 4.2.1 in AlmaLinux 8 [M]), which PREREQUISITES 7 names [S].

**P46. The DynASM echo proves a line was read, not assembled.** *Symptom:* a sabotaged .dasc line appears in `buildvm_arch.h`, yet nothing changed [I]. *Cause:* DynASM echoes every `|` line as `//|`, including lines in `.if DUALNUM` branches that are not assembled ([6.9](#69-reading-what-you-assembled)) [M]. *Fix:* pair the echo with object checks: lj_vm.o must change (negctl) or be fresh (build-native.sh) [S].

**P47. The lexer rounds a wide literal before any operator sees it.** *Symptom:* `0x00ff00ff00ff00ff & 0xff` is 0; a mask like `0x3fffffffffffff` (54 bits) is really 2^54, so a test built on it is vacuous [M] (S7). *Cause:* every numeral becomes a double in the lexer (U9) [S]. *Fix:* build masks of at most 53 significant bits, or compute them (`2^53 - 1`), and check that a test's operands are what it assumes [S][M].

**P48. Windows `luajit.exe` cannot open MSYS paths inside `-j` arguments.** *Symptom:* `-jdump=+rsT,/c/Users/...` fails to open its file, while the script argument works [M]. *Cause:* Git Bash converts a path that is a whole argument, not one embedded after a comma [I]. *Fix:* pass Windows-style paths (`cygpath -m`) inside `-j` options [M].

**P49. Ubuntu hides a missing link flag, and the glibc floor.** *Symptom:* a Linux link, suite or library passes on Ubuntu and fails on AlmaLinux 8. In Alma, the native suites shim, security, mem and wd failed to link (`dlopen` undefined), and round 3's .so failed to load there (``version `GLIBC_2.38' not found``, `ldd -r`) [M] (the glibc round). *Cause:* from glibc 2.34, libpthread and libdl are part of libc. Ubuntu 26.04's `libpthread.a` and `libdl.a` are 8-byte empty archives (`ls -l`), so every flag combination links there (27 of 27 in the round's link matrix), and every import takes the newest version of its symbol in Ubuntu's glibc [M]. *Fix:* link and test Linux changes in AlmaLinux 8 as well, and build the release .so there ([7.7](#77-the-linux-path)). Step 6's ELF import gate and gradle's floor check read the artifact, not the host [S].

**P50. A Windows DLL's md5 depends on its output path.** *Symptom:* the same sources, built into two `OCLJ_BUILD`s with identical objects, give DLLs of equal size that differ in 1300 bytes (dropin) and 1948 bytes (additive), the image base among them (`0x1ed2e0000` against `0x34c0b0000` for additive, `objdump -p`) [M] (the glibc round's `win-ab`, `cmp -l`). *Cause:* relinking both builds' objects at one output path gave byte-identical DLLs (`win-relink`) [M]; GNU ld's automatic DLL image base follows the output name [I]. At one path across days, the link's time differs too: the glibc round's DLLs in `build/native` (`3174ef37`, `1e9f4ac4`) differ from round 3's at the same path (`9ae455ce`, `2c37a7a1`) in 8 and 9 bytes, the COFF header's time stamp, the PE checksum and the export directory's time stamp, and nowhere else [M] (2026-10-20, a byte compare). *Fix:* compare objects, as for P13, or relink at one path; compare a DLL's md5 only with one linked at the same path [S]. The Linux .so does not show this: five AlmaLinux 8 builds into five `OCLJ_BUILD`s gave one md5 per variant [M].

**P51. Shell code passed inline to `wsl.exe` is expanded before the distro's `sh` sees it.** *Symptom:* from Git Bash, `wsl.exe -d AlmaLinux-8 -- sh -c 'f=inner; echo "[$f]"'` prints `[]`, and `sh -c 'false; echo "rc=$?"'` prints `rc=0` [M]. In the glibc round this made rc echoes report 0 for builds that had failed, and mangled a `sed -n` script containing a `$` [M] (implementation reports). Separately, without `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` Git Bash rewrites a `/mnt/c/x` argument to `C:/Program Files/Git/mnt/c/x` [M]. *Cause:* wsl.exe hands its command line to a shell inside the distro, which expands `$` before `sh -c` runs [I]. *Fix:* put the commands in a script file under `/mnt/c` and run `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 wsl.exe -d <distro> -- sh /mnt/c/<script>`. Read the script's own summary file, never an rc echoed through wsl.exe [S]. **Variables do not cross either:** `X=1 wsl.exe ...` and an `export`ed X both arrive unset in the distro, and only `wsl.exe -d <distro> -- env X=1 sh /mnt/c/<script>` (or naming X in `WSLENV`) delivers it [M] (2026-10-20, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/glibc/followup/docs-final/lt/env-1`; the review of the glibc round). A harness negative-control arm launched with its switch set on the Git Bash side runs the product and passes (disposeoff reads 82/0 either way), so check the run's `args.txt` [M].

**P52. On `/mnt/c` every file is mode 0777, and WSL interop runs Windows executables.** *Symptom:* in AlmaLinux 8, a Windows `luajit.exe` given as the kernel patcher passed `[ -x ]`, ran, and failed with `cannot open /mnt/c/.../patch-machine-lua.lua`; the harness's message blamed a moved anchor in machine.lua [M] (the glibc round's `ff-patcher-1`). *Cause:* drvfs is mounted without metadata, so `stat` reads 777 for every file, and interop ran the .exe [M]. *Fix:* check what a binary is, not its mode: `smoke-test.sh` refuses a Windows executable on Linux by its first two bytes, `MZ` (:577) [S]. The runners' default `OCLJ_LUA53` is the Windows `lua.exe` (`run-bitop.sh` ~:82), and they fall back to the name without `.exe` only when `-x` fails (~:224), which on `/mnt/c` it never does; pass a Linux PUC explicitly ([7.7](#77-the-linux-path)) [S].

**P53. `python -` hangs in the agent's shell tool.** *Symptom:* a call that runs `python -` or `python -I -`, meant to take a script from a heredoc or a pipe, never returns; four agents of the CRC32 follow-up each lost a call to it, by a stray `-` [M: design.md gap 34]. *Cause:* `-` makes Python read its program from stdin, and the round's agents saw it wait even with `</dev/null` [M: as they reported; not re-run, by the rule below]. *Fix:* write the script to a file with a file tool and run `python <file>`, which also keeps P8's backslashes out of the command text; never run `python -` or `python -I -` (CLAUDE.md's conventions) [S].

**P54. Compiled `select(k, ...)` returned nil where the interpreter raises "index out of range" (upstream's; fixed in our fork 2026-10-20, and upstream 2026-10-21).** *Symptom:* in a vararg function called with 7 varargs, a loop `local v = select(K[i], ...)` compiled with k = 100 (beyond the varargs), then met `K[150] = -2147483628`: `-joff` raised "index out of range", and compiled code gave nil (verify-folda's `probes/selbug.lua` printed `n 30 E 200` against `-joff`'s `E 30 E 200`). The shipped build and the 2026-09-01 upstream binary behaved alike, with no bit operator involved [M: verify-folda, 2026-10-20; the select round's `runs/selbug-1/`]. *Cause* [S: `rec_varg`, lj_record.c ~:1989-2089, read by the main session, the select round's implementer and its sweep; M: t_select on the shipped build, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/runs/diag-base-2/`]: of `rec_varg`'s three paths for `y = select(k, ...)` ([2.6](#26-the-recorder)), path C, a k beyond the varargs, loads nothing and gives nil behind the guard `fr < 8*k + frofs`, computed in 32 bits (~:2061-2069). Three classes of k passed that guard where the interpreter raises: (1) a k whose 8k wraps past the frame (-2^31 + 20, selbug's; -2^29 + 20 and their kin); (2) with no wrap, a k from m+1 to 0 in a call that passed m < 0 varargs, fewer arguments than fixed parameters, where `fr` is below `frofs` (k = 0 and -1); (3) a k that narrowing wrapped, `select(x + c, ...)` with x a double, c a 16-bit constant and x + c outside int32, which reached path C within 2^15 of -2^31 or of 2^31 ([3.13](#313-narrowing-and-why-round-3-goes-around-it)). The interpreter (lib_base.c `select`) raises for k = 0, for a k below minus the vararg count, and for every number outside int32, which `lj_lib_checkint` converts with a C cast that gives -2^31 on this build [S][M: the implementer's `probes/interp1-base-joff.txt`]. verify-folda's reading, the one this entry first gave, named class 1 only. And for a constant k (path A), the record-time `frofs+8*(int32_t)idx` (~:2050) overflows in C from about 2^28 [S]; gcc wrapped it: for `select(2^28, ...)` in a one-parameter function the guard became `LT fr, -2147483621`, which always fails, at a cost in speed, not values (25 trace starts, a root trace and a chain of 24 side traces, in 300 iterations; the probe's other loop, `select(2^29 + 5, ...)`, also wrapped, into a guard `LT fr, +67` that passes with the right nil, one trace) [M: `probes/konst1-base.dump`, `konst1-base.jv`]. *Fix* (`patch-bitop-record.sh` hunks 4 and 5; roadmap row 213): abort the recording for a recorded k above `OCLJ_SELECT_KMAX`, 2^27 (build copy, lj_record.c ~:2057), and guard `ULT k-1, 2^27` before path C's shift (~:2080), so the trace exits unless 1 <= k <= 2^27 and the interpreter decides; `8*k + frofs` then cannot wrap ([8.6](#86-adding-a-guard) step 7). Held by run-bitjit.sh's sel part, t_select against `-joff` under 15 option sets and against PUC, and by the arms SELGUARD and SELRECK ([11.5b](#115b-the-sel-part-t_select), [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)) [S][M]. Upstream's v2.1 tip on 2026-10-20, `dd3b243` (2026-10-18), had the pin's `rec_varg`, byte for byte [M: a compare in the round's bare clone, `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/upstream.git`]. **Reported upstream** as LuaJIT issue #1535 (https://github.com/LuaJIT/LuaJIT/issues/1535), filed by the user on 2026-10-21. **Fixed upstream** the same day by `a4c0645` ("Don't compile select() indexing beyond varargs.", `lj_record.c` only, citing #1535), and #1535 closed as completed [S: the commit in that bare clone; the issue page, read 2026-10-22]. In `a4c0645`'s `rec_varg` the recording aborts for a recorded k above `LUAI_MAXSTACK` (65500), which also keeps path A's `frofs+8*k` small, and for a variable k beyond the varargs (`goto nyivarg` in place of path C's guard and nil), so path C is gone, and with it the compiled `select('#', ...)` in a call with fewer arguments than fixed parameters (`nvararg` < 0), which path C recorded as `LT fr, frofs` and now aborts [S: lj_record.c ~:2042-2063 of `a4c0645`'s tree, the census's export, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/docs-up/census-a4c/tree/src/`]. **Verified** by the main session (2026-10-21) [M]: stock `a4c0645` built GC64 and non-GC64 in WSL Ubuntu (`build-up3.sh`; `a4c.tar` md5 `79c5100b`); on both, the issue's reproducer prints `-joff`'s output with the JIT on, the select round's code verifier's `vsel.lua` differs from `-joff` in 0 of its 209 cases in all 21 option sets, and `konst.lua` records 3 `NYI: bytecode VARG` aborts and no trace; `check4.py` judges the reproducer's output against the issue's, vsel's 21 sets, each build's FR2 byte and every exit code, and prints konst's traces and aborts without judging them; it passes (rc 0), and fails (rc 1) on the outputs of the earlier stock tip `95f931c`, which lacks the fix (up-3's, copied as `ff-check4/`), its fail-first (scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/upreport/`: `up-4/`, `ff-check4/`, `check4.py`, `build-up3.sh`; archive `bench/runs/2026-10-21-numconv/upstream/`; this update re-ran `check4.py` on both, 2026-10-22, with the same verdicts, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/docs-up/check4-*.txt`). On that tree hunk 5's upstream text (`if (idx < 0) goto nyivarg;` and path C's block) is missing, and `patch-bitop-record.sh` refuses there, the only refusal of our twelve scripts [M: the census of 2026-10-22, roadmap row 270]: the first refused anchor that is upstream's fix of what the hunk fixes ([8.2a](#82a-bumping-the-pin) step 4). Our hunks 4 and 5 stay until the pin move, which adopts upstream's change, drops them, retires SELGUARD, re-anchors or retires SELRECK and rewrites t_select's path-C gates ([11.5b](#115b-the-sel-part-t_select); the main session's decision of 2026-10-21, roadmap row 270). *For you:* by the user's decisions of 2026-10-21 (roadmap row 213), SELRECK's record-time abort and `OCLJ_SELECT_KMAX` = 2^27 stay for the current pin; select stays out of t_idx2.lua, where the fold meets an INDEX conversion through `rec_idx_key`'s `CONV int.num`, while t_select's `bx` source feeds select a 32-bit bit result through the fold; t_idx2.lua's header (its "NOT HERE: select" paragraph, to which run-bitjit.sh's header points) says so. twin.lua's mention of select, that its integer argument does not twin with PUC at and past ±2^31, still holds [S].

**P55. An instruction a fold rule emits on the side can read a stale pre-roll value.** *Symptom:* a loop-body guard or value computed from the pre-roll's value in every iteration; `stale.lua` counted one such range guard on RG2-PHI-RELAXED, a sabotage of rg2's prototype that let it build on a PHI-marked `fleft` [M: design.md gap 3]. *Cause:* `loop_unroll` compares with the pre-roll only the ref a rule returns, and PHI-marks only that; an instruction emitted on the side that CSEs to a pre-roll instruction is never marked, so no PHI carries the new value into the body ([8.4a](#84a-emitting-instructions-or-a-guard-from-a-fold-rule)) [S]. *Fix:* build side instructions only on loop-body refs, keep `!irt_isphi(fleft->t)`, and count stale instructions in the IR as `stale.lua` does ([12.5](#125-jitutil)) [S].

**P56. A coverage count keyed on opcode and mode also counts another rule's instructions.** *Symptom:* an instrument says a rule fired on a build that lacks it. On a build with rg2's range guard and no table-index fold, the fold's first instruments read t_idx `convs 179`, t_cnt33 `convs 16`, and phiidx `crc fold=3` and `phicnt fold=1`, so the idx part could not say PROVED NOTHING there [M: crc/combine, `runs/rg2only-oldtwin-*.err`, `runs/rg2only-oldphiidx.txt`]. *Cause:* rg2's `CONV int.i64` of `BSAR y, 53` has the fold's opcode and mode, 0x275 [S]. *Fix:* key a signature count on the operands as well; when only one rule emits the signature, say so where the count is made, as phiidx.lua's header does for `CONV int.i64` today [S].

**P57. A suite's stderr merged into its diffed stdout puts `#cov` lines mid-line.** *Symptom:* a reference line split by a `#cov ...` coverage line, and differences that are not the VM's [M: verify-folda, design.md gap 33]. *Cause:* the two streams interleave wherever the buffered stdout happens to be flushed [I]. *Fix:* keep stdout and stderr in separate files, as every runner's `run()` does, and diff stdout alone [S].

**P58. `pgrep` does not exist in Git Bash.** *Symptom:* a wait loop on `pgrep` ends at once, as if the process had exited [M: the CRC32 follow-up's implementer]. *Cause:* `pgrep: command not found`, exit 127 [M, 2026-10-20]. *Fix:* wait on a sentinel file the job writes, such as the timing chain's `done`, or on the task's own output; `tasklist` lists Windows processes [S].

**P59. A control loop that must compile can miss after an earlier abort.** *Symptom:* a coverage gate that requires named control loops to compile reads PROVED NOTHING on a correct build, once in several runs: the sel part said PROVED NOTHING once on the first t_select, and in eight runs of a copy of it that names each control that missed (`scripts/mkmiss.py`), the control `p2few-K-134217728` compiled nothing, with no trace event at all, in one [M: the select round's `logs/bitjit-sel-b1-1.log`, `runs/miss-1/r5.err`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/`]. *Cause:* `penalty_pc` writes an aborted loop's penalty into the hot counter at that loop's pc+1 (`hotcount_set`, lj_trace.c ~:413), and the 64 counters are shared by pc hash ([2.5](#25-hot-counting-and-trace-start)), so a later loop whose pc hashes to that slot starts from the penalty and may need more iterations than it runs before it is hot [S]; that this is what happened is inferred [I]. *Fix:* run the controls before any case whose recording aborts, as t_select has done since 2026-10-20, or warm a control past the largest penalty; after the reorder the three controls compiled in all 285 JIT-on runs of the round's later logs (19 run directories of 15 option sets each, over the round's builds, its variant and its sabotages, on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8) [M: a count of the `#cov` lines in scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/`] (open question 19).

**P60. A fact of exactness is not a range: it must never enter a bound.** *Symptom:* a bound that is told a value is exact drops guards of values that are not. With a converted double scored 54 by `oclj_bitwidth` (the NUMCONVW sabotage), `x | 1` for x = 2^53 lost its result guard and returned 9007199254740992 where the interpreter raises "result of '|' has more than 53 significant bits": t_numconv differed from `-joff` in 27 cases in each option set that folds, and ncir in 9-10 [M: five selected negctl runs, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/integrate/runs/negctl-nc-1` to `-5`]. *Cause:* a bound composes through every operator above it (`x | 1` scores max(w(x), 2), [3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)'s table); exactness does not: an operand's own conversion of a double is exactly a double whatever its range, and a bit operator on it need not be [S]. *Fix:* hold such a fact in a predicate of its own, read by its own rule on its own line (FIX-NUMCONV's `oclj_isnumconv`), and hold the line with an arm that puts the fact into the bound (NUMCONVW) [S].

**P61. A removal rule's IR signature is an absence.** *Symptom:* an instrument that counts the guards a rule removes reads the rule's count on a build without the rule: under `-O0` and `-O-fold` no fold rule runs, and the base build reads no such guard either; and under `-O-cse` `x & x`, `x | x` and `(x ~ y) ~ y` stop being identities on every build, because their two conversions of x stay distinct, so `comm_dup` and `reassoc_bxor` reach no conversion and the guards stay [M: the FIX-NUMCONV test implementer, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/impl-tests/runs/explore-2`, `ncir-sets-1`]. *Cause:* a removal leaves no instruction of its own; what the IR shows is the guard missing where its precondition is present [S]. *Fix:* key the count on the guard's operands, count it only where its precondition is present and prove the precondition in the same run (t_numconv's `ncin`, ncir's `phiF`), refuse the option sets where no rule runs (ncir exits 2 under `-O0` and `-O-fold`), and hold forms that need CSE to their signature only with CSE on ([11.5c](#115c-the-nc-part-t_numconv-ncir)) [S][M].

**P62. Two proofs merged on one rule line break the checks built on that line.** *Symptom:* verify-rg2's one-line FIX-NUMCONV, written as `oclj_bitwidth(J, fleft->op1, 0) <= 54 || (y a CONV from NUM)` on the width rule's line, was sound on this build [S][I], and would still have failed `patch-bitop-fold.sh`'s `verify_content` ("the width rule's call (54) does not occur exactly once") and negctl's setup, since WIDTH55's anchor, the old line, no longer occurs [S: step 1's `argument.md` section 7, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/argue/`]. *Cause:* `verify_content` counts the text `<= 54)` and finds the width rule's call by it, and an arm anchors on a whole line; a merged condition also leaves no one-line arm that breaks the new proof alone [S]. *Fix:* give a new rule a line of its own, with its own comment, its own `verify_content` check and its own arm's anchor, as FIX-NUMCONV's has (the user's decision, 2026-10-21; [8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) step 0) [S].

**P63. An int ref that stands in for an int64 operand breaks every matcher that compares refs but not widths: the CSE twins (found 2026-10-23, fixed the same day).** *Symptom:* compiled 5.3 operators computed in 32 bits after LuaJIT's `bit` library had run on the same operands in the same loop. Under the JIT, `bit.lshift(i, 3)` then `i << 3` gave 341 wrong values of 401 (456 for 4294967752, at i = 536870969), and `bit.bnot(i)` then `~i` 341 (4294967237 for -59, at i = 58); `-joff`, and the same loops without `bit`, were right [M: `build/native`'s `luajit.exe` `f4038cd6`, `bench/runs/2026-10-23-shiftexact/cse/`]. The fix round's value suite, t_csefwd.lua, reads 13 of its 32 cases wrong on `build/native`, every case whose served value is wrong and none of its 7 right-when-served cases and 12 controls: `lsh3` 342-343 of its first call's 400 results (the first 57-58 right) and all 400 of each later call, `copy` 335-347 of its first call's 400 and 393 of each later one, `side` 285 of its first call's 600 and 295 of each later one; per option set, 13 cases in 6 sets in every run and in a seventh, `-Ohotloop=1 -Ohotexit=1`, in 6 of 7 runs, 12 under `-O-loop` and the two `-Omaxside=0` sets (13 once in 14 readings of those two), 2 under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`, and 0 under `-O0`, `-O-fold` and `-O-cse` [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/runs/cse-base-1` to `-6`, `fix/alma1/base-cse`]. *Cause:* upstream's `lj_opt_cse` matches an instruction on its opcode and operands (op12) and returns the existing one with that instruction's own type; on x64 `simplify_conv_sext` LEFTFOLDs `CONV i64.int SEXT` to the int ref itself (an int loop index whose constant lower bound is >= 0, `ADD idx, k`, a u8/u16 XLOAD such as `string.byte`'s), so our I64 BNOT, BAND, BOR, BXOR, BSHL, BSHR, BSAR and ADD (from `x << 1`) can share op12 with a 32-bit instruction [S]. In the forward order (the 32-bit instruction first) our operator got the 32-bit value, which our 64-bit consumers read zero-extended: wrong for BNOT, for BSHL when x * 2^k >= 2^32, and for a variable count of 32 or more, which a 32-bit shift masks [S][M]. In the reverse order (ours first) a 32-bit recorder got an I64-typed ref, and where it stores that in a slot a snapshot restores it with no I64 case: crash-class, never run on any build [S][I: the fix round's design 1.3, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/design.md`]. *Fix:* `patch-bitop-fold.sh` hunk 12 (the user's decision D1, 2026-10-23, the design's B-i64): a match also requires `irt_isi64(IR(ref)->t) == irt_isi64(fins->t)`, so an I64 instruction never commons with one of another type, in either order ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)); tests by value and IR in the forward order and, by the user's decision D2, in the reverse order only in shapes that put no I64 into a slot ([11.5d](#115d-the-cse-part-t_csefwd-cseir-t_cserev)); one arm, CSEFWD ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)); the reverse half held as [11.10](#1110-crash-fixes) says [S][M]. *Lesson:* where a fold lets a ref of one type stand in for an operand of another, every matcher that compares refs must also compare widths, or the stand-in makes two different values one instruction. Before trusting a stand-in, grep every matcher (`op12 ==`, operand compares, caches keyed on refs) and say why each cannot pair the types: the design did, and found `lj_opt_cse` the only one that could without FFI ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400); open question 23) [S].

## 11. Verification discipline

| rule | what it guards against | where it is enforced |
|---|---|---|
| JIT-on output equals `-joff` output, line for line, under several option sets | a trace that computes what the interpreter does not | `run-bitjit.sh`, `run-bitfuzz.sh`, `run-bitop.sh` part 1 |
| `-joff` equals PUC 5.3.6 with the rule applied per operator (the twin) | an interpreter and a JIT that agree on a wrong value | `run-bitjit.sh` (gen, t_jit2), `run-bitfuzz.sh`, `run-bitop.sh` part 2 |
| expectations come from PUC mechanically, never by hand | a `.expect` line that drifts | `.expect` + `deviations.txt` + `xrule.lua` |
| a differential run proves that the JIT ran | a pass from a JIT that compiled nothing | coverage gates, `jit on` in every `#cov` line |
| every new check is first seen to fail | a gate born unable to fail | `failfirst/`, `failfirst2/`, `negctl-*.sh` |
| every fix site has a one-line sabotage that its catchers catch | tests with no teeth at the fix | `negctl-bitop.sh` (37 arms since the CSE-twin fix, 2026-10-23; 36 with FIX-NUMCONV's follow-up, 35 with FIX-NUMCONV, 32 with the select fix, 30 with the table-index fold, 27 in round 3) |
| a surprising reading is the instrument's until shown otherwise | false findings | controls, `inputs.md5`, the object fingerprint |
| crash fixes are proved by census A/B plus build assertions | — | e.g. `bench/runs/2026-10-05-errmem/`; for a crash-class half no workload reaches, a census of what the fix refuses ([11.10](#1110-crash-fixes)) |
| results directories are fresh, never cleared or reused | destroyed evidence | every runner refuses an existing `*_OUT` |
| chains longer than a minute run from the main session | a chain killed when an agent's turn ends | process rule |
| speed is read within one run, against a same-run baseline | a timing that measures the machine, not the change | `bench-r3.sh` ([11.12](#1112-measuring-speed)) |

The suites live in `test/native/` (`run-bitop.sh`, `run-bitjit.sh`, `run-bitfuzz.sh`, `negctl-bitop.sh`) and `test/native/bitop/` [S].

### 11.1 The interpreter is the oracle

- **Compare whole outputs, both ways.** Every JIT suite compares the whole JIT-on output with the `-joff` output of the same script and inputs, and counts differences in both directions [S] (`run-bitjit.sh` `ndiff`). The first `ndiff` counted only lines missing from the reference, so an empty JIT-on output compared equal [M] (review2; P26).
- **The reference must be complete first**: `-joff` must exit 0 with one line per case: `OCLJ_BITJIT_CASES` lines for the generator (2000 by default), and a literal 400 for t_rec, which does not follow that knob (run-bitjit.sh ~:379, ~:436) [S].
- **Strip CR** from every Windows output before comparing (P18) [S].
- **Print enough to localize a difference.** `drv.lua` prints, per start, `x y z h` as `%.17g` or `ERR@<loop variable>:<kind>`; `t_jit2.lua` prints one result per iteration and the iteration of a raise [S]. Before `t_jit2` printed that iteration, a NARROW sabotage that raised at 258 compiled and at 257 interpreted printed the same line both ways, in 3 runs of 3 [M] (`t_jit2.lua` header).
- **Classify an error by a stable prefix**: `INEXACT(<op>)` from "result of '<op>' has more than 53 significant bits" (`drv.lua` `kind`) [S] (P24).
- **`-joff` agreement is necessary, not sufficient**: the interpreter itself is checked against PUC (the twin, the matrix). The oracle also exposes upstream behaviour: on round 2's build (BitOp's 32-bit operators) the generator's JIT-on runs differed from `-joff` in 42-86 loops per seed and option set, because BitOp leaves an operand's conversion beyond ±2^51 unspecified and LuaJIT's narrowing exploits it (`(i * 2^45 - i) | 0` gave -66 compiled and -33 interpreted at i = 66) [M] (`failfirst/run-bitjit.log`; roadmap row 206).
- **A fresh loop starts recording at its 56th iteration**, so a raise after iteration 60 is probably from compiled code [M][I]; the generator counts those raises separately [S].

### 11.2 The PUC twin

- **The twin is the same program on PUC Lua 5.3.6, with U1-B's rule applied at every operator** through `X(r, '<op>')` [S] (`drv.lua`, `bitfuzz.lua`, `t_jit2.lua`):

```lua
X = function(r, op)
  local f = r + 0.0
  if math.tointeger(f) ~= r then
    error("result of '" .. op .. "' has more than 53 significant bits (mask each operand to 32 bits first)", 2)
  end
  return f
end
```

  An r near `math.maxinteger` gives `r + 0.0` = 2^63, which has no integer, so it fails too [S].
- **The twin's text is generated from the same source as ours, never written twice by hand** [S]:
  - `gen.lua`'s builders return both texts at once: ours `(a op b)` and PUC's `X(a op b, 'op')`. `~>>`, which 5.3 lacks, becomes `SAR(a, k)`, drv.lua's 64-bit arithmetic shift under the same rule. Loop indices start as floats (`for i = 1.0, n`) and constants get `+ 0.0`, so 5.3's integer arithmetic between operators becomes the double arithmetic our VM does.
  - `t_jit2.lua` keeps one text with every result wrapped in `X`, and ours drops each `X`. A mechanical check asserts that each `X` wraps exactly one operator at its own top level, or it prints INSTRUMENT and exits 2; the check was seen to fail ("narrow-add: 1 operators but 0 X's") [M] (`impl-C2.json`).
- **The twin is compared with `-joff`, not with the JIT-on run**: PUC checks the interpreter and `-joff` checks the JIT, a triangle [S][I].
- **A checker must not restate the implementation's test.** `xrule.lua` holds X to 5.3.6's own integers and counts significant bits (lowest set bit to highest) with integer shifts, never through a double [S].
- **Expectations that depend on a long hex-float string come from a Linux PUC** (P21).
- **A library's integer arguments do not twin** [S][M: proto-folda, 2026-10-20]. LuaJIT reads them as int32s (`lj_lib_checkint`, and `lj_opt_narrow_toint` when compiled), PUC 5.3.6 as `lua_Integer`s, so `string.byte`, `sub`, `find`, `char` and `rep`, `table.concat` and `unpack`, and `select` answer differently at and past ±2^31. The table-index fold's suites share one twin, `bitop/twin.lua` (X and SAR as drv.lua's, and the mechanical check), and compare such consumers with `-joff` only: t_idx2.lua's "puc" part prints just the consumers whose meaning PUC shares (tables, shifts, the loop stop). t_select.lua's "puc" part keeps the integral k below 2^31, where LuaJIT's `select` and 5.3.6's agree: LuaJIT truncates a fraction and reads every number outside int32 as -2^31, so it raises, where 5.3.6 raises "number has no integer representation" for a fraction and returns nothing for k >= 2^31 [M: the select round's `probes/interp1-base-joff.txt`, `interp1-puc.txt`].
- **A missing PUC proves nothing** (since 2026-10-20): when `OCLJ_LUA53` names no PUC Lua 5.3.6, run-bitjit.sh skips every PUC comparison and ends PROVED NOTHING (exit 2), unless `OCLJ_LUA53=none` declines PUC on purpose. Before, a run from a scratch repository made with `git archive` (no `bench/vendor`) skipped every twin and passed: verify-folda's F11, and the old runner read PASS, rc 0, where the new one reads PROVED NOTHING, rc 2, with `OCLJ_LUA53` unset and with a bad path, and PASS with `none` and with the PUC [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/impl/logs/pucff-1.log`]. A PUC is a program whose `-v` says Lua 5.3.6 and that runs a 5.3 chunk (`_VERSION` and `math.type`): `luac.exe` prints the same `-v` banner, and the first version took it for PUC [M: the code verifier's F4; `crc2/fix/runs/pucpath-1`, 11.8]. Name `OCLJ_LUA53` in every documented invocation ([7.4](#74-running-make-by-hand), [12.10](#1210-our-runners-and-their-knobs)).

### 11.3 Option sets

Each option set exercises one path, so each suite runs several [S]:

| name | flags | run by | what it exercises [I] |
|---|---|---|---|
| jon | none (`-O3`, hotloop 56) | gen, t_rec, t_jit2, t_rg2, idx, sel, nc, cse | the ordinary path |
| hl1 | `-O3 -Ohotloop=1` | gen, t_rec, t_jit2, t_rg2, idx, sel, nc, cse | recording on the first iteration, specialized to the start values |
| ms0 | `-Omaxside=0` | gen, t_rec, t_jit2, t_rg2, idx, sel, nc, cse | no side traces recorded: a hot exit gets a "fallback to interpreter" stub instead ([4.6](#46-closing-a-trace-and-linking-it)), and a failing guard returns to the interpreter |
| he1 | `-Ohotloop=1 -Ohotexit=1` | gen, t_jit2, t_rg2, idx, sel, nc, cse | a side trace from the first taken exit: parent-snapshot values entering a new trace |
| noloop | `-O3 -O-loop` | gen, idx, sel, nc, cse | no loop optimization, so no pre-roll copy and no PHIs: a difference that disappears here points at a PHI-crossing fold |
| O0, O1 | `-O0`, `-O1` | t_jit2, t_rg2, idx, sel, nc, cse (ncir and cseir under `-O1` only) | no FOLD, CSE or DCE at all; then fold, CSE and DCE without narrowing or loop optimization |
| nofold, nocse | `-O3 -O-fold`, `-O3 -O-cse` | idx, sel, nc, cse (ncir under `-O-cse` only, cseir under neither) | no fold rule at all, so no table-index fold (WIDTHIDX33 differs in 0 cases there) and no FIX-NUMCONV; no CSE |
| hx1, ts1, mx1, hl1ms0 | `-Ohotexit=1`, `-Otryside=1 -Ohotexit=2`, `-Omaxside=1`, `-Ohotloop=1 -Omaxside=0` | sel (since 2026-10-20), nc, cse | the exits and side traces through each of their paths, the sets the CRC32 verifiers used for a speculative guard (below) |
| noabc, nonarrow | `-O3 -O-abc`, `-O3 -O-narrow` | sel (since 2026-10-20), nc, cse | no bounds-check elimination, and no narrowing, so no wrapped index sum ([3.13](#313-narrowing-and-why-round-3-goes-around-it)) |

The levels are flag sets (lj_jit.h `JIT_F_OPT_1..3`, ~:97-102): `-O1` is fold, cse and dce; `-O2` adds narrow and loop; `-O3` adds fwd, dse, abc, sink and fuse [S]. Order matters (P35).

The floor's t_rg2 runs t_jit2's six sets; the idx part runs nine: t_jit2's six, `noloop` (`-O3 -O-loop`), `nofold` (`-O3 -O-fold`) and `nocse` (`-O3 -O-cse`) (run-bitjit.sh `IDXOPTS`, ~:554); the sel part fifteen, `IDXOPTS`' nine and six of the verifiers' sets below, for a guard and for an index (`SELOPTS`, ~:638); the nc part runs t_numconv under `SELOPTS`' fifteen and ncir under the thirteen of them that fold, all but `-O0` and `-O-fold` (`NCIROPTS`, ~:712); and the cse part runs t_csefwd and t_cserev under `SELOPTS`' fifteen and cseir under the twelve with fold and CSE on, all but `-O0`, `-O-fold` and `-O-cse` (`CSEIROPTS`, ~:809) [S]. The CRC32 follow-up's verifiers added sets for two kinds of change, run by hand or through `drv.lua` ([11.4](#114-the-generator)) [M: design.md gap 25]:
- **A speculative guard:** `-Ohotexit=1`, `-Otryside=1 -Ohotexit=2`, `-Omaxside=1` and `-Ohotloop=1 -Omaxside=0`, which put the exits and side traces through each of their paths [I].
- **An index fold:** `-O-abc`, `-O-narrow`, `-O-fuse`, `-O-fwd -O-dse` and `-O-sink`, which change the consumers' bounds checks, the narrowing that makes keys, the fused loads, the forwarding and the sinking around the converted index [I].
- **Counting exits is not counting failures:** once an exit is patched to a side trace or to a "fallback to interpreter" stub, it sends no `texit` event, so an exit count read from `texit` stops growing although the guard keeps failing [M: proto-rg2, verify-rg2].

### 11.4 The generator

`gen.lua` + `drv.lua`: the round-3 width-fold reviewer's adversarial loop generator, in its U1-B form [S].

- **Use.** `luajit gen.lua <seed> <count> <outdir> [phi|idx|rg2]` writes `cases_lj.lua` and `cases_puc.lua` into `<outdir>`, which must exist (gen.lua does not create it; run-bitjit.sh does) [S][M]. Each case is a chunk that returns `run(n, x0, y0, z0)`, a getter for the loop variable, and a converter from that value to the iteration count [S].
- **What a loop contains** [S]: carried values in the slots x, y, z and h, in a table t and in an upvalue u; six loop headers (`1, n`; `-7, n - 8`; `n, 1, -1`; `2^40, ...`; a while loop; a float start in the PUC text); branches (side exits and side traces) and a flipped boolean (type instability, so the loop is unrolled); table stores read back next iteration and an inlined helper; inner loops (a constant trip count is unrolled, a variable one gets its own trace); a swap through multiple assignment; int-typed results (the loop index through a bit operator); `string.byte` values (IRT_U8); double arithmetic on bit results; and an uncompiled C call (`table.sort`) that forces stitching.
- **Boundaries** [S]: shift counts cluster at 9-12, 43-46 and 52-54, masks at 2^52-2^54. 26 boundary templates (gen.lua `BT`, ~:189-221): 20 at exactly 55 bits (must not fold), 4 at 54 (fold), and 2 negative masks with no finite bound.
- **The modes** [S] (`OCLJ_BITJIT_GENMODE`; run-bitjit.sh's default run uses none of them, so each is a campaign):
  - `phi` makes half the statements carry a finitely bounded value across the loop's PHI.
  - `idx` (2026-10-20, the table-index fold) makes a third of the atoms index expressions bounded at exactly 32 bits (fold) or 33 (no fold), used as table keys, `string.byte` arguments and shift counts, or slots read as keys, and a third of the statements set a slot to such an index, so a key crosses the loop's PHI (gen.lua `IDXMODE`, `IDXE`, `idxatom`, ~:144-184).
  - `rg2` (2026-10-20, written for rg2 and kept when rg2 was not shipped) makes a quarter of the atoms and half the statements put results at and past 2^53: exact values in bits 53-63, exact values just outside [-2^53, 2^53), inexact ones just past it, which raise, and values that cross the bound after the loop compiled (`RG2MODE`, `bigatom`, ~:77-118). It holds every result guard's 2^53 boundary on any build, and negctl-bitop.sh runs it as its genrg2 catcher ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)).
- **How drv.lua runs a case** [S]: from **seven starts** of (x, y, z) — {1, 2, 3}, {2^52+1, -3, 7}, {-1, 2^62, -2^63}, {0x7ffffffffffffc00, 12345, -987654321}, {2^53-1, 2^53+2, -2^53}, {0, 0, 0} and {2^52+2^51, 3, 1.5} — for 300 iterations each, in two **closure modes**: `shared` (one closure, so later starts run the first start's traces, side exits and side traces) and `fresh` (the chunk loaded again per start). In shared mode the starts also share the upvalue u, the table t and the case's other state, so once one start differs from `-joff` a later start of the same case can differ only because it inherits that start's changed state: a count of what kind of difference a sabotage makes takes only each case's first differing start in shared mode (the arm verifier's NUMCONVNOOP classification counted 310 such knock-on starts beside 480 first ones, among them the only value turned into a raise [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/verify-arm/classify-2/`]).
- **The comparisons**: `-joff` against the five option sets in both modes, plus the PUC twin in both modes: 12 numbers per seed, all of which must be 0 [S]. A 300-loop run (`OCLJ_LUA53=<PUC 5.3.6> OCLJ_BITJIT_PARTS=gen OCLJ_BITJIT_SEEDS=1 OCLJ_BITJIT_CASES=300`, with a fresh `OCLJ_BITJIT_OUT`) read 0 in all 12 (3.4-3.8 s) [M].
- **The coverage gate.** `drv.lua` writes a `#cov` line to stderr: cases, starts, raises, raises after iteration 60, and the cases in which a trace completed. The JIT-on shared run must say `jit on` and complete a trace in at least `OCLJ_BITJIT_HOTMIN` (0.25) of the loops, or the seed PROVED NOTHING (exit 2) [S]. A copy whose recorder refuses every bit operator showed 0 differences with traces in only 1.5-2.1% of the loops; the build traces 67-70% [M] (`run-bitjit.sh` header). Probe, that 300-loop run: `coverage: 300 loops, 2100 runs, 823 raised, 18 after iteration 60; a trace completed in 214 loops`. The traced count varies between runs (212-218 measured, P6); the rest is fixed by the seed [M]. Traces show that the JIT ran, not that a given rule fired: for a rule, count its own IR signature, keyed on operands as well as opcode and mode, as the idx part counts the table-index fold's `CONV int.i64` ([11.5a](#115a-the-idx-part-t_idx-t_cnt33-t_idx2-phiidx), P56) [S][M].
- **Seeds are deterministic**: the PRNG is integer-exact Park-Miller, so a seed generates the same loops on every VM and run [S][M].
- **Campaign** (each run with `OCLJ_LUA53=<PUC 5.3.6>`, [11.2](#112-the-puc-twin)): `OCLJ_BITJIT_SEED=101 OCLJ_BITJIT_SEEDS=20`, then `OCLJ_BITJIT_SEED=201 OCLJ_BITJIT_SEEDS=20 OCLJ_BITJIT_GENMODE=phi`, about 9.6 MB per seed of 2000 loops; the final build showed 0 differences [S][M] (`logs/campaign-2/`, `phi20.log` starts at seed 201). Since 2026-10-20 a change to an index conversion runs the `idx` mode and a change to a result's guard the `rg2` mode, at least over seeds 1-4 (run-bitjit.sh's header) [S]; on the combined prototype both read 0 in every comparison over seeds 1-4 and 901-902, and `phi` over 201-204 [M: crc/combine `runs/bitjit-both-gen*.log`].
- **What it catches**: every fold-rule sabotage, the missing guards, NARROW and KFOLD64, in every seed. Fewest differing cases per seed over three runs: WBAND 11, WADD0 11, WSHL0 24, WSHR 70, WIDTH55 168, INEXACT-JIT 511, SELFGUARD 516 [M]; one run on FIX-NUMCONV's build read SELFGUARD 514 and genrg2's WIDTH55 153, just below those minima [M: the integrator's reading, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/integrate/runs/negctl-old-A.log`]. In one run each, on sabotage copies of FIX-NUMCONV's prototype, gen caught NUMCONVOP1 in 188-266 cases per seed and comparison in shared mode and NUMCONVW in 141-209, genrg2 NUMCONVOP1 in 111-338 and NUMCONVW in 123-286, and the floor both, t_rec in 63-89 lines per seed and option set, t_jit2 in one loop under he1 and t_rg2 in one under five of its six sets [M: `numconv/impl-tests/runs/genfloor-*-1.log`, `genrg2-*-1.log`]; none of these is listed as their catcher: the two arms keep only the nc part's catchers, as the idx and sel arms keep their own part's, by the main session's decision of 2026-10-22 (open question 21, closed). gen is the listed catcher of NUMCONVNOOP (2026-10-22, `oclj_isnumconv`'s opcode test removed): 18-32 cases a seed, every seed in each of three selected runs (fewest 18, 18 and 19) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/arm/negctl-1` to `-3`], and 17-27 in a fourth, the arm verifier's on its own fresh build, so 17 is the fewest of four runs [M: `numconv/verify-arm/negctl-1/`], and 20-26 in a fifth, the final check's full sweep, so 17 stays the fewest of five [M: `numconv/main/final-1`, the archive's `logs/final-main/negctl-work/NUMCONVNOOP/bitjit.log`]; one earlier run, on the code implementer's scratch build of the same sabotage, read gen 17-27, genrg2 24-28 and t_rec 0-3 a seed [M: `numconv/impl-code/sab-noop/r1/`]. The `rg2` mode, run as negctl's genrg2, caught in every seed of two runs INEXACT-JIT 603-655, SELFGUARD 595-654, KFOLD64 513-586, NOINT-JIT 361-408 and WIDTH55 154-162, and in one run, too thinly to list, WSHR, WSHL0, WBAND, WADD0, NARROW and SARCONST; not FIX1 or WIDTHIDX33. The `idx` mode caught WIDTHIDX33 in only 1-5 cases a seed, and is no catcher [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/impl/logs/negctl-probe-genrg2-A.log`, `-B.log`, `negctl-genrg2-arms-1.log`, `negctl-probe-genidx-A.log`].
- **Extending it** [S]. The builders `P(lj, puc)`, `B(op, a, b)` and `U(a)` return the pair of texts (gen.lua ~:71-73). Operands come from `atom()` (~:181), which takes an `idx` atom or the default `atom0()` (~:121), and boundary shapes from the `BT` list (~:189-221). The default table-index atoms mask with `& 7` and `& 15` (~:129-136, ~:321), widths 4-5, so they cannot tell a bound of 32 from 33; the `idx` mode's index expressions bound at exactly 32 and 33 (`IDXE`, ~:155-167, after review1's `t_idx.lua` cases: `x ~>> 31`, `x & 0xffffffff`, keys near ±2^32).
- **Seeds.** Every added atom or template shifts the PRNG's draws, and the old seeds stop generating round 3's loops, which gen.lua's header promises they do (~:25-29, ~:39-41). Add new shapes behind a new `OCLJ_BITJIT_GENMODE` value [S][I]. **The recipe** (the CRC32 follow-up's) [S][M: design.md gap 14; the implementer's report]:
  1. put each of the mode's draws behind its own flag, short-circuited, `MODE and rnd(n) == 1`, so that a mode that is off draws nothing;
  2. make the modes exclude each other (one `arg[4]`);
  3. **check it byte for byte**: every mode's output against its own reference gen.lua (the default and `phi` against HEAD's, each new mode against its prototype's) over several seeds, plus a control that one mode differs from the default. The combiner's check read 24 of 24 mode-seed pairs identical, the implementer's 36 mode, seed and reference triples [M: crc/combine `gencheck-1/`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/impl/logs/gencheck-1.log`]; its fail-first, with proto-folda's gen.lua as the one under test, read 9 DIFFER and exit 1 [M: `crc2/impl/logs/gencheck-ff1.log`].
- **By hand.** `luajit drv.lua <cases file> <shared|fresh> [N]` (drv.lua's header) runs one case file. Use it for option sets the runner lacks (`GENOPTS` is fixed, run-bitjit.sh ~:357), such as 8.4's `-O-fold` and `-O-cse`, and compare with `-joff` the same way, CR stripped [S].

### 11.5 The floor: t_rec, t_jit2, t_rg2, p4v

Deterministic suites that hold a minimum of coverage whatever the generator draws [I].

| suite | what it is | option sets | what it catches |
|---|---|---|---|
| `t_rec.lua` | loop-carried recurrences `x, y = F(x, y, i), G(x, y, i)`, 400 per seed, 300 iterations, several starts; prints the final x and y and a position-weighted sum of every intermediate x (exact, fmod 2^53); seeds 1-2, and 3-4 in `counts` mode (shift counts negative, 64 or more, and -2^63) | jon, hl1, ms0 | NARROW, KFOLD64, the missing guards, SELFGUARD; not the width rule's off-by-ones, which gen catches [M] |
| `t_jit2.lua` | 27 hand-built loops, one result per iteration, `ERR@<iteration>:<kind>`; the PUC text comes from the one X-wrapped source | jon, O0, O1, hl1, ms0, he1 | NOINT-JIT (guard-exit, in all six sets); NARROW (in some sets only, so gate it as "nonzero in some option set") [M] |
| `t_rg2.lua` (since 2026-10-20) | 15 loops at a result guard's 2^53 boundary: in range at both bounds, exact outside it (bits 53-63), inexact just past it, many crossing it after the loop compiled; written for rg2 and kept, since every result guard must agree with the interpreter there on any build; t_jit2's format and X-wrapped source, and PUC equals `-joff` in 14 loops (all but `sar-edge`) | jon, O0, O1, hl1, ms0, he1 | KFOLD64 [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/impl/logs/negctl-genrg2-arms-1.log`]; no arm names it as a catcher (open question 18) |
| `p4v.lua` (fix1) | eight loops that set a value from the loop index (seven identity bit operations and one non-identity control) and use it in a bit operator next iteration; each must compile: a `stop` in its function, 0 TYPEINS aborts, its FORL patched to JFORL, not IFORL | default | FIX1 (`traces=0 typeins=11 loop=IFORL` for 7 of 8 without it); a JIT that records no bit operator [M] |

Lessons from building the floor [M] (`impl-C2.json`): a loop that raises before `hotloop` never tests the JIT, so keep a variant whose results are all exact; a closure created inside a loop (`FNEW`) is NYI and keeps the loop from compiling (P28); print the iteration of a raise.

### 11.5a The idx part: t_idx, t_cnt33, t_idx2, phiidx

run-bitjit.sh's fourth part (since 2026-10-20, in the default parts), for the table-index fold ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)) [S]. The three differential suites share `bitop/twin.lua`: one text with every operator's result in `X(...)`, ours without the X's, PUC's with U1-B's rule, `~>>` as SAR, and a mechanical check that prints INSTRUMENT and exits 2 if a text is not what it says ([11.2](#112-the-puc-twin)). Each runs under `IDXOPTS`' nine option sets against `-joff`, and with PUC its twin against `-joff`; its default run's `#cov` line must say `jit on`, a trace completed in `OCLJ_BITJIT_HOTMIN` of the cases, and at least one `CONV int.i64` in them (the fold fired), else the part proved nothing.

| suite | what it is | catches [M: three runs, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/impl/logs/negctl-idx-arms-1.log`, `crc2/verify-code/logs/negctl-idx-arms.log` and `crc2/fix/logs/negctl-sel-1.log`] |
|---|---|---|
| `t_idx.lua` | review1's cases rebuilt for U1-B: index expressions at bound 32 (fold) next to 33 (no fold), with operands at and past ±2^31 and ±2^32, through nine consumers (get, set, byte, sub, a narrowed key, a key carried across the PHI, two shift counts, a `for` loop's stop); 684 cases | WIDTHIDX33: 20-51 cases per option set where the fold runs, 0 under `-O0` and `-O-fold`, which have none |
| `t_cnt33.lua` | variable shift counts of 33 bits whose low 32 bits are small, each operand cycled so the loop records with small counts first; 16 cases | WIDTHIDX33: 10 |
| `t_idx2.lua` | verify-folda's `vf_idx.lua` without its select part: table keys (get, set), `rawget`, narrowed keys, `string.byte`, `sub` (twice), `find`, `char`, `rep`, `table.concat`, `unpack`, two shift counts and a loop stop, each plain, in a branch (side traces) and with the key carried to the next iteration; 1440 cases, 720 in its "puc" part | WIDTHIDX33: 92-138 |
| `phiidx.lua` | reads the compiled loops: in each trace's loop body, `fold` counts `CONV int.i64` (0x275), `keep` a `CONV int.num` (0x26e) of a PHI-marked pre-roll `CONV num.i64`, `cross` a fold that crossed a PHI; in the pre-roll, `crossp`. Seven variants each state what they need (`NEED`); `cross` and `crossp` must be 0 in all; each loop must compile (no TYPEINS, JFORL) and its `acc=` equal `-joff`'s; with no fold anywhere it says INSTRUMENT | PHIIDX: 3 of 7 variants (`phi` and `phicnt` cross=1, `phi2` keep=0); NOLOOPFOLD: 1 of 7 (`ctl1` fold=0) |

- **On the implementer's build** [M: `crc2/impl/runs/bitjit-impl-all-1.log`]: t_idx 0 differences in all nine sets and against PUC, a trace in 595 of 684 cases, 1495 `CONV int.i64`; t_cnt33 0, 16 of 16, 13; t_idx2 0, 1301 of 1440, 1736, and its 720-case puc part equal to PUC; phiidx 7 of 7. On the base build, with no fold, t_idx, t_cnt33 and t_idx2 read 0 `CONV int.i64`, phiidx says INSTRUMENT, and the part says PROVED NOTHING, exit 2 [M: `crc2/impl/runs/bitjit-base-idx-1.log`].
- **The instruments count every `CONV int.i64` as the fold's**, because nothing in these suites emits another one; rg2's prototype did, and that cost a round (P56) [S]. They read `op2 % 1024`, so string.buffer's `#buf`, which records a NONE-mode `CONV int.i64` (0x1275, [3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)), would count too; no suite uses string.buffer, and twin.lua's and phiidx.lua's headers say so [S].
- **Lessons** [M: proto-folda, verify-folda]: a consumer must really convert (t_idx's first `fori`, S7); a bound shows only where it lands on a valid index or count, so the cases sit at negative y with small low bits, and at y in [2^31, 2^32) behind narrowed keys, loop stops and concat's bounds ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)); a library's arguments do not twin ([11.2](#112-the-puc-twin)); never merge a suite's stderr into its diffed stdout (P57).

### 11.5b The sel part: t_select

run-bitjit.sh's fifth part (since 2026-10-20, in the default parts; ~:633-705), for the select fix (P54). It is not about the bit operators: run-bitjit.sh is the home of the JIT-against-interpreter suites, and negctl-bitop.sh already rebuilds `lj_record.c` [S]. One suite, `test/native/bitop/t_select.lua` (417 lines, no backslash; it loads `twin.lua`) [S]:

- **The cases** (110). A loop in a vararg function calls `select(k, ...)` once per iteration; its first call records the loop with a record value, 150 times: within the varargs (class B), beyond them (C), `'#'` (H), or a literal constant (K, one loop per literal on each of `v7` and `p2few`: 27 literals, 54 loops, two of them the k = 2^27 controls). Then, in the B, C and H cases, every argument list of the case's shape runs every probe, each followed by the record value twice, so a probe meets the compiled loop, its exits and the side traces they grow (a K case runs each list three times; `iap` and `ian` run ranges of i); an error ends the call, and the driver records it and calls again where the loop stopped. Four shapes: `v7` (no fixed parameters; 7, 2, 0 and 60 values), `v0`, `p2` (2 fixed parameters, 3, -1, -2 and 0 varargs) and `p2few` (recorded with fewer arguments than parameters). The sources of k: `pl` (a double from a table: a guarded `CONV int.num INDEX`), `st` (a string: `STRTO`, then the same), `ap` and `am` (`x + 1000`, `x - 1000`: narrowing's unchecked ADD and SUB, [3.13](#313-narrowing-and-why-round-3-goes-around-it)), `an` (`x - 1073741804`, a constant beyond int16, which narrowing does not take), `bx` (a 32-bit bit result, through the table-index fold's `CONV int.i64`), `iap` and `ian` (the int loop index plus a constant: `ADDOV`), and a branch (`s`) whose side trace records select too. The probes: 1 to 3, around each list's vararg count m, -1 to -3 and around -m, 0, k whose 8k wraps just past the frame, large k from 2^27 - 1 to 2^31 - 1, k outside int32, fractions, and nine strings for `st` (the header) [S].
- **The reference is `-joff`**, compared whole under `SELOPTS`' 15 option sets ([11.3](#113-option-sets)): results and raises, a raise by its kind (RANGE, NUMBER, NOINT, INEXACT). With PUC, part `puc` (106 cases, integral k below 2^31, where select means the same, [11.2](#112-the-puc-twin)) must equal `-joff` on PUC 5.3.6 [S].
- **The instrument** reads each completed trace's IR through `jit.util.traceir` and prints one `#cov` line: `traced`; `selA`, `selB` and `selC`, the IR of rec_varg's three paths, found through `fr`, the SLOAD with `IRSLOAD_FRAME`: a guard of `fr` against a constant (A, and also `'#'` in a call with fewer arguments than parameters, which path C records as `LT fr, frofs`), B's count, a BSHR by 3 of an ADD of `fr`, and a guard of `fr` against an ADD of a BSHL by 3 (C), which run-bitjit.sh prints as "guards of select's paths"; `kguard`, a ULT against the constant 2^27, which only the fix emits; `convs`, the bx cases' `CONV int.i64` (0x275); `kmaxbad`, the loops recorded only with a k above 2^27 (`v7-K-134217729`, `p2few-K-134217729`, `kx-pl`) in which a select trace completed; and `kmaxctl` of `kctl`, the three controls recorded with k = 2^27 that did [S].
- **The gates.** The default run must say `jit on`, trace `OCLJ_BITJIT_HOTMIN` of the cases, record each path's guard and the fix's guard at least once, and compile all three controls, or the part says PROVED NOTHING (exit 2). `t_select-kmax` fails when any JIT run has `kmaxbad` above 0, and says PROVED NOTHING without a `#cov` line [S]. The controls run first, before any case whose recording aborts: P59 [M].
- **On the implementer's build** (b2, `lj_record.o` `c536c2b9`) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/runs/s-b2-bitjit/`]: 0 differences in all 15 sets, 66 of the 110 cases with a raise somewhere in their output, a trace in 84 cases by default, `selA` 33, `selB` 42, `selC` 77, `kguard` 77, the controls 3 of 3, `kmaxbad` 0 in every set, and PUC equal to `-joff` in all 106 `puc` cases; the bx cases' `CONV int.i64` numbered 5-31 per set where the fold runs (15 by default; 0 under `-O0` and `-O-fold`). In AlmaLinux 8, with the Linux PUC, the part passed the same way: 0 differences in all 15 sets, PUC equal to `-joff`, kmax clean (`sel/impl/alma1/bitjit-all.log`).
- **What it catches** [M: four runs, `sel/impl/runs/s-b2-negctl-1` to `-4`]: SELGUARD (the guard removed) in 11-53 cases in each of the 15 sets (fewest under `-Ohotloop=1 -Omaxside=0`, most under the side-trace sets); SELRECK (the record-time abort removed) only through `t_select-kmax`, 2-3 of the 3 loops in each set, since it changes no value. On `build/native` the part fails in both ways, t_select in every set (13-53 cases on Windows, 17-53 in AlmaLinux 8) and kmax in 2-3 loops a set (`logs/bitjit-sel-base-2.log`, `runs/alma-base-sel-1/`); and a guard of k >= 1 alone fails in 11 of the 15 sets, in 1-3 `am` cases each (`runs/bitjit-sel-gt0-2`).
- **Not covered: traces that start outside the vararg function** [M][S]. Every t_select case's trace starts in its own f's loop. A trace can also reach `rec_varg`'s select path at framedepth 0 when it starts elsewhere and returns into the vararg frame: a stitched trace that starts in a non-vararg callee after a C call (`[TRACE 3 (2/stitch) stitch2.lua:8 -> 2]`), or a side trace from a callee's inner-loop exit. Both diverged on `build/native` and agree with `-joff` on the fixed build [M: the select round's code verifier, `probes/stitch2.lua`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/verify-code/runs/stitch-2/`; its 209-case `probes/vsel.lua`, which has such wraps, read 0 differences in 21 option sets on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8, `runs/vsel-w1-1/`, `runs/alma-vsel-1/`]. A stitch never starts in a vararg frame itself: `recff_nyi` stitches only when `J->framedepth && frame_islua(J->L->base-1)`, and a vararg function's frame link is a `FRAME_VARG` (lj_ffrecord.c ~:162-189, the check ~:168) [S]. By the user's decision of 2026-10-21, such stitched and side-trace cases join t_select in the round that moves the pin to upstream's v2.1 tip, not before (roadmap row 213; [8.2a](#82a-bumping-the-pin)).
- **At the pin move** (roadmap row 270). Upstream's `a4c0645` never compiles path C (P54), so the gates above that want path C's guard and the fix's guard (`selC`, `kguard`) would read PROVED NOTHING, and that round rewrites them [S]; by reading, its abort for a recorded k above `LUAI_MAXSTACK` (65500) also keeps the three k = 2^27 controls from compiling, so t_select's coverage gate on them (`kmaxctl` equal to `kctl`, which lets t_select-kmax's check see a trace) would read PROVED NOTHING too [S][I: not run]. negctl's SELGUARD retires with the guard, and SELRECK is re-anchored on upstream's abort or retired (their anchors are hunk 5's lines, which do not apply there [M: the census, `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/docs-up/negarms-2/`]). Until then the gates hold as written.
- **By hand**: `luajit [options] test/native/bitop/t_select.lua [all|puc]` prints one line per case on stdout and the `#cov` line on stderr (it finds `twin.lua` beside itself); compare stdout with the `-joff` run's, CR stripped, and keep stderr out of the compared file (P57) [S].
- **Lessons** [M]: a value-level differential cannot see a removed record-time bound when the guard behind it still exits, so count the traces it forbids (`t_select-kmax`; P34); a guard's source list must include narrowing's wrapped sums ([8.6](#86-adding-a-guard) step 7); and a control that must compile runs first (P59).

### 11.5c The nc part: t_numconv, ncir

run-bitjit.sh's sixth part (since 2026-10-21, in the default parts; ~:707-801), for FIX-NUMCONV ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)). Two files in `test/native/bitop/`, written by the round's test implementer and run as part `nc` (6-8 s) [S][M]:

- **`t_numconv.lua`**, the value suite (244 lines, md5 `de0d14e9`, no backslash; it loads `twin.lua`) [S]. 25 forms: identities (`x | 0`, `0 | x`, `x & -1`, `-1 & x`, `x ~ 0`, `0 ~ x`, `x << 0`, `x >> 0`, `x ~>> 0`, `x & x`, `x | x`, `(x | 0) & -1`), whose result folds to x's own conversion; cancellations (`~~x`, `(x ~ y) ~ y`, `(x ~ 5) ~ 5`, `~(x ~ -1)`), whose inner operator keeps its guard; and controls whose guard must stay (`x | 1`, `x ~ 1`, `~x`, `x ~ y`, `x << 1`, `x >> 1`, `x ~>> 1`, `x & 0xff`, `(x | 0) | 1`). Each in three modes, 75 cases: `p` on a table's double; `c` on the previous iteration's identity result, carried in a slot as its marked double; `s` on `h & g`, where h and g carry the previous iteration's double, which in the loop body folds to the pre-roll's conversion of x, so the rule meets a PHI-marked `fleft`. Each loop records on 300 small values, then the same compiled loop, with no branch between, meets 132 values, `BIG`'s 22 six times each: at and past 2^53 in magnitude, -2^63 among them; the edges 2^52 + 1, 2^53 - 1 and -0.0, below it; and operands that must raise (NaN, ±inf, 2^63, -2^63 - 2048, 2^64 and fractions). An identity on -0.0 gives 0 and on -2^63 gives -2^63; `~x` of -2^63 raises (2^63 - 1 is inexact) [M: `build/native`'s luajit `-joff`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/fix/runs/edge-1.txt`]. Each result is stored and read after the loop. It runs under `SELOPTS`' 15 sets against `-joff`; its PUC twin, part `puc`, holds every case (with U1-B's rule nothing here means otherwise on PUC) and must equal `-joff` on PUC 5.3.6. Its default run's `#cov` line must say `jit on`, a trace in `OCLJ_BITJIT_HOTMIN` of the cases, and at least one marked `CONV num.i64` of an unmarked conversion of a double (`ncin`, the pair the rule reads), else the part says PROVED NOTHING [S].
- **`ncir.lua`**, the IR read (273 lines, md5 `baaa4490`) [S]. Through `jit.util.traceir` on each completed trace, per pre-roll and loop body: `sig`, the `EQ.i64` guards whose operands are an unguarded, unmarked int64 `CONV i64.num` of a double (op2 & 0x7ff = 0x2ae) and a marked one (0x6ae), exactly the guards the rule removes and the width rule never can, keyed on operands (P56); and `eq`, every `EQ.i64` guard. An identity must read `eq` 0, a cancellation or a control at most 1 and 1 in the first trace's pre-roll (the instrument sees a guard), `x & 0xff` 0, and `sig` must be 0 everywhere. It is also the rule's p4v.lua: every loop must compile (a `stop`, no TYPEINS, JFORL), and its results must equal `-joff`'s. Its loops that carry values across the PHI: `swap1` (`t = h & g; h = x; g = x`, x also converted in the loop), where `phiF`, the PHI-marked pre-roll `CONV num.i64` the rule meets as `fleft`, must be at least 1 and `fb` and `psig`, the body's guard on it, 0, with loop optimization and CSE on; `swap0`, the rule firing in the copy on an unmarked `fleft`; `carry`, `carry2` and `self`; and `ctl`, whose body guard must stay. It runs under `NCIROPTS`, the 13 of `SELOPTS`' sets that fold, and exits 2 under `-O0` and `-O-fold`, where no fold rule runs and the base build reads the same (P61) [S][M]. With the JIT off it reads nothing, prints no summary line and exits 0, so the runner requires that line (`ncir: N of M cases compiled ...`) in every run and otherwise says PROVED NOTHING, rc 2: the code verifier found the part PASSing on a copy whose ncir.lua began with `jit.off()`, and the round's fixer added the check (run-bitjit.sh ~:785-786) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/verify-code/tn-ff-c/`; `numconv/fix/confirm/pre-jitoff.log`, `fix/ff-ncirgate*/`].
- **On the shipped build** (a fresh build of the working tree, `lj_opt_fold.o` `f6d916e2`) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/integrate/run-wb1/bitjit.log`]: t_numconv 0 differences in all 15 sets, 30 of its 75 cases with an inexact result's raise somewhere, a trace in 75 of 75, `ncin` 237, 0 identity result guards left, and PUC equal to `-joff` in all 75; ncir 0 failures in all 13 sets. In AlmaLinux 8, with the Linux PUC, the part passed the same way (`numconv/integrate/ab1/bitjit-all.log`). On `build/native` (209aa95) t_numconv passes, with 126 identity result guards left, and ncir fails 22 cases a set (20 under `-O1` and `-O-loop`, 17 under `-O-cse`), so the default run-bitjit.sh FAILs there, rc 1, at ncir alone [M: `numconv/impl-tests/final-1/full-base.log`].
- **What it catches** [M: five selected negctl runs, every count the same, [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)]: NUMCONVOP1 and NUMCONVW in t_numconv (22-30 and 27 cases per set where a rule runs, 0 under `-O0` and `-O-fold`) and in ncir (9-10 a set); NUMCONVOFF, which changes no value, in ncir alone (17-22 a set). The code verifier's own VNOPHI, the rule refused on a PHI-marked `fleft` (no value changes), is caught by swap1 alone, in the 10 sets with loop optimization and CSE, not under `-O1`, `-O-loop` or `-O-cse`, as designed [M: one run, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/verify-code/sab-vnophi/nc-1.log`]. NUMCONVNOOP (2026-10-22, `oclj_isnumconv`'s opcode test removed) is not the nc part's: t_numconv never sees it, and ncir only under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`, by ref layout, so its catcher is gen ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)) [M: `numconv/verify-code/sab-vnoop/nc-1` to `-3`; the arm listed on `nc:t_numconv`, NOT CAUGHT, `numconv/arm/ff-catch-1/`].
- **Which of ncir's checks fail alone** (its header; every reason is listed, none stops the others) [M: `numconv/impl-tests/runs/ncir-sets-1`, `impl-tests/ff/`]: `sig`, alone on swap0, carry, carry2 and self, on the base build and under NUMCONVOFF; the first trace's pre-roll count of 1, alone on every cancellation and control but `x << 1` under NUMCONVOP1 and NUMCONVW (`x << 1` records as `ADD y, y`, `simplify_shift_ik`, which neither value sabotage reaches); ctl's body guard, under the same two; swap1's `phiF`, on a mutated copy that never finds the `fleft`. Implied, and kept for their messages: an identity's `eq` 0, a part's `eq` at most 1, and swap1's `fb` and `psig`.
- **Under `-O-cse`** `x & x`, `x | x` and `(x ~ y) ~ y` are no identities on any build: their two conversions of x stay distinct, so `comm_dup` and `reassoc_bxor` reach no conversion and their guards stay. ncir holds those three to `sig` 0 alone with CSE off [M: `numconv/impl-tests/runs/explore-2`] (P61).
- **Fail-first** [M: `numconv/impl-tests/ff/`, each on a mutated scratch copy of `test/native`]: t_numconv with `ncin` never counted, and with the JIT switched off inside it, PROVED NOTHING, rc 2 (the coverage gate); an X wrapping two operators, PROVED NOTHING, rc 2 (twin.lua's text check); PUC without U1-B's rule, FAIL in 30 cases of part `puc`; ncir's compiled results altered, FAIL in all 13 sets; ncir told that fold is off, PROVED NOTHING, rc 2; swap1's `phiF` never found, FAIL in the 10 sets with loop optimization and CSE; ncir.lua missing, a setup failure, rc 2. Since the fixer's summary-line check: ncir with `jit.off()` as its first line, and with its summary print removed, PROVED NOTHING, rc 2, where the runner before the check said PASS, rc 0; the unmutated copy PASS, and `build/native` FAIL, rc 1, at ncir [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/fix/confirm/`, `fix/ff-ncirgate*/`].
- **By hand**: `luajit [options] test/native/bitop/t_numconv.lua [all|puc]` prints one line per case on stdout and the `#cov` line on stderr; `luajit [options] test/native/bitop/ncir.lua` with `LUA_PATH` reaching the build's `src/jit/vmdef.lua`; compare stdout with the `-joff` run's, CR stripped, stderr kept apart (P57) [S].
- **Lessons** [M]: a rule that removes a guard that cannot fail changes no value, so its off-switch is caught only by counting what it leaves, keyed on operands and read where its precondition is present (P61); a rule that returns a pre-roll ref across a PHI needs a loop that meets the PHI case and asserts trace events (swap1; S4); and an exactness fact put into a bound shows only in values past the bound, met by the same compiled loop (P60).

### 11.5d The cse part: t_csefwd, cseir, t_cserev

run-bitjit.sh's seventh part (since 2026-10-23, in the default parts; ~:803-951), for the CSE-twin fix (P63, [3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)). Three files in `test/native/bitop/`, written by the round's test implementer and extended by its fixer, run as part `cse` (9-13 s) [S][M]. Every loop is value-only: it reads and writes Lua tables of numbers and strings, nothing through a pointer (FFI is off) [S].

- **`t_csefwd.lua`**, the forward order's values (458 lines, md5 `2c73c0f6`, no backslash) [S]. 32 cases, each its own loop prototype called three times, one pair per loop, the 32-bit producer first in program order. So on a build without the fix our operator's request finds the 32-bit instruction and gets its ref, and no I64 instruction with that op12 is ever created: no 32-bit recorder can be handed an I64 ref, in the pre-roll, in the loop copy (which re-emits in the pre-roll's order) or in a side trace (which records in program order and stops at its root's loop) [S][I]. A load-time check refuses a case list whose texts do not put the producer first, and `copy`'s and `side`'s unless each holds exactly one `bit.` and one `<<`: INSTRUMENT, exit 2. **13 pairs whose served value is wrong**: `lsh3` (`bit.lshift(i, 3)`, then `i << 3`, i from 2^29), `bnot`, `bnotx` (`i ~ -1`, which `simplify_bxor_k` makes a BNOT), `bxorm1`, `lsh35` (a count masked to 3), `u8sh30` (a `string.byte` value, the u8 XLOAD route, which needs no narrowing), `nested` (`(i & 0x1FFFFFFFFFFFFF) << 8`, whose inner BSHL `simplify_shiftk_andk` emits), `reassoc` (`(i << 2) << 1`), `chain` (`(~i) ~>> 40`, a served value fed on), `vlsh` and `vrsh` (variable counts 32-63, which a 32-bit shift masks), `copy` and `side` (below). **7 whose served value is right**, visible only in the IR: `lsh1` (both `ADD i, i`), `band`, `rsh5`, and the in-game families, `select` (G1), `byte2` and `sub2` (G2) and `forin` (G3, in this order only). **12 controls**, run first (P59): each 5.3 operator alone (`ctl-copy` and `ctl-side` among them, `copy`'s and `side`'s shapes with `x * 2^30 % 2^32` for the bit library's call), the bit library alone, two same-type pairs that merge on every build, and `c1`, our operator on the bit library's result, whose operand stays a sign-extending CONV, so no pair forms. Each 5.3 result, and each bit-library result, is checked against a reference computed with plain arithmetic, and the file exits 1 on a mismatch under any VM, `-joff` included; the runner compares each of `SELOPTS`' 15 sets with `-joff`. No PUC twin: the 32-bit halves are LuaJIT's `bit` library, which PUC 5.3.6 lacks. Its default run's `#cov` line must say `jit on`, a trace in `OCLJ_BITJIT_HOTMIN` of the cases, and at least one pair formed, else the part says PROVED NOTHING [S].
- **`copy` and `side`, pairs with no equal operands at record time** (the code verifier's probes, added by the round's fixer) [S][M]. In `copy`, j carries `string.byte(S, i + 2)` into the next iteration, and each iteration does `bit.lshift(j, 30)`, then `string.byte(S, i + 1) << 30`: at record time the operands differ (j's TOBIT, an XLOAD), and in the loop copy both are the pre-roll's one XLOAD, so the pair forms only there (`0070 int BSHL 0063 +30` then `0072 i64 BSHL 0063 +30` in the verifier's dump of the fixed build, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/verify-code/runs/dump-bodyfwd-fix-3/`). In `side`, past i = 300 a branch the root trace never recorded does `b = string.byte(S, i)`, then `bit.lshift(b, 30)` and `b << 30`, so the pair forms only in a side trace. Both keep the forward order.
- **`cseir.lua`**, the IR read (279 lines, md5 `98f5c649`, no backslash) [S]. It loads t_csefwd's case list and reads every trace that completes in a case's loop through `jit.util.traceir` and `tracesnap`, keyed on operands (P56). Our opcodes are BNOT, BAND, BOR, BXOR, BSHL, BSHR, BSAR and ADD; a 32-bit bit instruction is one of them not typed i64, an ADD only when its op2 is an instruction (`ADD idx, k` is a valid stand-in). `served`: a marked `CONV num.i64` (a result's `d`) whose operand is a 32-bit bit instruction, or one of our i64 instructions with one as an operand, the defect's signature; `split`: an i64 and a non-i64 instruction with the same opcode and operands, the fix's; `rserved`: a non-i64 instruction other than a CONV with one of our i64 instructions as an operand, the reverse merge; `direct`: one of our i64 instructions with a non-i64 instruction as a value operand; `i64slot`: snapshot entries that name a non-constant i64 instruction ([9.6](#96-s6-list-every-way-a-value-can-enter-the-trace-and-score-each-one-from-its-type)); and `pre`, direct + served. Every case must read a trace, served 0, rserved 0 and i64slot 0; a pair case, where fold, CSE and (for an index operand) narrowing are on, pre and split at least 1; a control direct at least 1; the bit-library, same-type and `c1` cases split 0. `copy` is judged only with loop optimization on, and `side` and `ctl-side` only where a trace compiled our operator (pre at least 1). It runs under `CSEIROPTS`, the 12 of `SELOPTS`' sets with fold and CSE on, and refuses `-O0`, `-O-fold` and `-O-cse` with INSTRUMENT, exit 2: there no pair forms, or none merges, so split reads at least 1 on every build (P61). Like ncir it reads nothing with the JIT off, so the runner requires its summary line (`cseir: <k> of <n> cases ok (...); pairs judged <j> of <p>`) in every run, and every pair judged in the default one [S].
- **`t_cserev.lua`**, the reverse order, our operator first, only in the two shapes whose 32-bit consumer stores nothing in a Lua slot (313 lines, md5 `2d361505`, no backslash) [S]. The user's decision D2 of 2026-10-23 allows no other: the design's gated `cserev` part, with slot shapes for fixed builds only, was not built, and t_cserev runs ungated in the default `cse` part, so on every build, in negctl's canon and under every arm whose catchers run the part (CSEFWD's), and no arm names it [S]. R5, `r5-sel`: `j << 3`, then `select(j, ...)` with j beyond the varargs; R6, `r6-byte`, `r6-sub` and `r6-mul`: `i << 1`, then `string.byte(S, i + i)`, `string.sub(S, i + i, i + i)` or `string.byte(S, i * 2)`; and four controls. Its header argues from the build copy, line by line, that the merged I64 instruction reaches only an int ADD held in a C local of `rec_varg` or `recff_string_range`, which reaches a guard (and a STRREF), never `J->base`, so no snapshot names it: the select's slot gets nil and the string function's a string or a byte [S: t_cserev.lua's header, written before the file first ran on `build/native`; every cite in it re-read by the round's code verifier]. A load-time check, before any case is loaded, refuses any case text naming `bit` at all, any with other than exactly one `for ` (the outer loop the file writes), and any that does not put our operator first: INSTRUMENT, exit 2.
