The runner compares fields 1-2 (the values) with `-joff` under the 15 sets and PUC 5.3.6's whole output with `-joff`'s (nothing in these shapes means otherwise on PUC), requires its summary line in every JIT run and, in the default run, all four reverse cases judged; its own IR read wants, where fold, CSE and narrowing are on, direct at least 1, split + rserved at least 1, and split at least 1 with rserved 0 [S].
- **On `build/native`** (luajit.exe `f4038cd6`, no fix) the part FAILs, rc 1, on Windows and in AlmaLinux 8 (luajit `cb68e454`) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/runs/cse-base-1` to `-6`, `fix/alma1/base-cse`]: t_csefwd as P63 gives, its default coverage line reading `served 38, split 0`; cseir, in every run with its final judge (`cse-base-2` to `-6` and `alma1/base-cse`), 20 of 32 cases in 7 sets, 19 under `-O-loop` and the two `-Omaxside=0` sets, 2 under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`, every failed pair with served at least 1 (2, the pre-roll's and the copy's, and 1 for `copy` and `side`), and in `cse-base-1`, whose cseir judged `copy` and `side` by its first rules, 21 under the two `-Omaxside=0` sets and 3 under `-O-narrow`; t_cserev its 4 reverse cases in the 10 sets with fold, CSE and narrowing on, each rserved 2, split 0 and i64slot 0, every value equal to `-joff`'s and PUC equal to `-joff` in all 8: the reverse pairs merge and nothing reaches a slot, as read. rserved and i64slot read 0 in every line of t_csefwd's loops. The test implementer's runs of the part's first form, before `copy` and `side`, read t_csefwd 11 of its 28 cases in 10 sets and 1 under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`, cseir 18 and 1 in the same sets, and t_cserev as here [M: `csetwin/round/impl-tests/runs/cse-base-1` to `-3`, `alma-base-1`].
- **On the fixed build** (`lj_opt_fold.o` `a6c5fcc2`) [M: `csetwin/round/fix/runs/cse-fix-2`, `-3`, `bitjit-all-fix-1`; `fix/alma1/cse`; `cse-fix-1` is the first side gate's failure, below]: t_csefwd 0 differences in all 15 sets, a trace in 32 of 32 cases, 20 of 20 pairs formed, served 0, split 38; cseir 32 of 32 ok in all 12 sets, every pair judged in the default set and the six other sets with loop optimization, narrowing and side traces, 19 of 20 under `-O-loop` and the two `-Omaxside=0` sets, 2 of 20 under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`, and in the default set split 2 a pair (`copy` 1, `side` 1; `side` 2 under he1, hx1 and ts1, the sets with `-Ohotexit=1` or `-Otryside=1 -Ohotexit=2`); t_cserev 8 of 8 ok in all 15 sets, its 4 reverse cases split 2, and PUC equal to `-joff`. The same in AlmaLinux 8, with the Linux PUC. The default set judged 20 of 20 in all ten runs of the part on fixed builds, the three negctl canons among them [M: the round's commit facts, `csetwin/round/fix/commit-facts.md` 8].
- **What it catches** [M: three selected negctl runs, [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)]: CSEFWD, the forward merge let back in, in t_csefwd and cseir with `build/native`'s counts (`side` again varying under `-Ohotexit=1`), while t_cserev passes (the reverse stays refused).
- **Which checks fail alone** (cseir's header) [M: `csetwin/round/impl-tests/runs/ff-*`, `csetwin/round/fix/runs/fftests-2`, `fc-fftests-1`]: `split`, on a prototype with split never counted; `i64slot`, with the reader counting NUM refs (every case); the summary line and the default run's "pairs judged", in the runner (PROVED NOTHING); and the `copy` and `side` gates, each removed, FAIL that case on split on the fixed build under `-O-loop` and `-Omaxside=0`. `served` never failed alone on a measured build: with it never counted, `build/native` still FAILed 16 cases on pre and split and 2 (`nested`, `chain`) on split alone; it is kept for its message, which names the defect, and the side gate reads it. `rserved` fails on t_cserev's reverse cases on `build/native`, with split.
- **The side case's value varies on an unfixed build** [M]. Whether a trace compiles `side`'s branch depends on trace formation: on `build/native` it read wrong in every run under the sets other than the two `-Omaxside=0` ones and `-Ohotloop=1 -Ohotexit=1`, in 6 of 7 runs under that one, and in 1 of 14 readings of the `-Omaxside=0` sets, where a side trace is a stub that links to the interpreter (`cse-base-5`, `-Ohotloop=1 -Omaxside=0`, from the first call's 594th result on). So t_csefwd's per-set counts on such a build are ranges: gate on "nonzero", never on a count (P6). cseir judges `side` only where a trace compiled our operator; a first judge that wanted two traces failed on the fixed build under `-Omaxside=0`, where the stub made two traces and no pair [M: `csetwin/round/fix/runs/cse-fix-1`, `fix/probes/onecase.lua`]. The default set must judge every pair, so a run in which `side`'s side trace failed to form there would read PROVED NOTHING, not PASS; in ten runs on fixed builds it formed every time [S][M].
- **Fail-first** ([11.8](#118-fail-first)) [M]: `build/native` FAILs as above; the test implementer's 19 mutations of the three files and the runner (a doctored reference, a doctored `-joff` or PUC output, the JIT off, no pair, no summary line, a broken text check, `-O-cse` given to cseir, split never counted, NUM refs counted as i64slot, a missing file) each read as wanted, FAIL or PROVED NOTHING, and none writes a reverse case; the fixer's order and count checks of `copy` and `side`, its two gates and t_cserev's text check each read INSTRUMENT or FAIL when broken.
- **By hand**: `luajit [options] test/native/bitop/t_csefwd.lua`, `t_cserev.lua` and `cseir.lua`, each with `LUA_PATH` reaching the build's `src/jit/vmdef.lua` (the files load cseir's reader), print one line per case on stdout, and t_csefwd its `#cov` line on stderr; compare stdout with the `-joff` run's (t_cserev's fields 1-2), CR stripped, stderr kept apart (P57) [S].
- **Lessons** [M]: a value that is right when served shows a merge only in the IR, so count the pair kept apart where its precondition is present, keyed on operands (P56, P61); a pair need not have equal operands at record time, so test the loop copy and side traces as routes of their own; a gate on "a side trace exists" is not a gate on "a trace compiled the code" (`-Omaxside=0`'s stub); and a fail-first that would have to write a crash-class shape is replaced by one that proves the same check without writing it (t_cserev's comment mutations, [11.8](#118-fail-first)).

### 11.6 The fuzzer and the matrix

**The fuzzer** (`bitfuzz.lua`, `run-bitfuzz.sh`) draws the same random expression trees on PUC and on ours: the five binary operators and unary `~`, over up to four operands, with 16 operand tuples per case. Its PRNG is integer-exact and literals are printed by exact digit arithmetic, so both VMs read the same text [S]. Ours computes every tuple in five modes [S]:

| mode | how the operands arrive | what it tests |
|---|---|---|
| K | every operand a literal | the parser's constant folding |
| V | as arguments, one call | the interpreter |
| P | as V, with minimal parentheses | the parser's precedence |
| H | as V, in a loop over all tuples, 8 repetitions | the recorder and the assembler |
| U | literals stored to locals in a loop run 120 times (first 3 tuples) | the fold engine (the operands are constants to the recorder) |

- **Reference and coverage** [S]: PUC with `X` at every operator; errors compare by kind. Ours runs twice, JIT on and `-joff`; the JIT-on `#cov` line must say `jit on` and show traces in at least `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_HOTMIN` (0.25) of the H and of the U loops. The build compiles 40-44% of the H loops, and a copy that records no bit operator 5-7% [M] (`impl-C2.json`).
- **Three controls per seed**, with PUC playing the VM [S]: `sim-wrap` (a correct VM) must PASS; `sim-raw` (5.3.6 without the rule) must FAIL in exactly the inexact tuples; `sim-round` (the rejected U1-A) must FAIL in exactly the tuples whose reference is `E:INEXACT`. Probe, seed 1 with `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_CASES=300`: `sim-wrap PASS; sim-raw MISMATCH 1510 of INEXACT 1510; sim-round MISMATCH 1510 of REF E:INEXACT 1510`; at the default 1000 cases the counts read 5159 [M].
- **Crashes**: a nonzero exit from our side is a 1, and an output without `#end` is CRASH (1) before any tuple is compared [S].
- **Campaign**: `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_SEED=11 OCLJ_BITFUZZ_SEEDS=50 OCLJ_BITFUZZ_CASES=20000` ran 16,000,000 tuples, 4,792,268 raising in the reference, 0 mismatches, in 39 min 48 s [M] (round 3 README). It writes about 6.3 GB unless `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_PRUNE=1` [S]. No `~>>` (5.3 has none); t_sar and the generator cover it [S].

**The matrix** (`run-bitop.sh`):

- **Part 1** runs `t_ops` (7467 cases), `t_fold` (63), `t_sar` (437) and `t_bit` (2109, LuaJIT's own `bit` library, unchanged) on the build's luajit.exe, JIT on and `-joff`; each output must equal its `.expect`, then `coerce_agree.lua` runs [M].
- **The `.expect` files** are PUC 5.3.6's output with `X`, reworded to LuaJIT's word order by `reword.lua`, plus the lines `deviations.txt` lists; made mechanically, never by hand [S].
- **Part 2 (provenance)**: PUC with `X`, reworded, must differ from `.expect` in exactly the lines `deviations.txt` lists for the platform (classes LEX, IDIV, RESID-varinfo); and a raw PUC run, printing integers with `%d`, must make `xrule.lua` agree that X raises exactly where an operator's integer has more than 53 significant bits [S] (P23).
- **Part 3** runs `bitop_test.c` through the shim, linked with the objects the DLL links [S].

### 11.7 negctl: one-line sabotages

`negctl-bitop.sh` proves the round's tests have teeth at the fix's own sites: 37 arms since the CSE-twin fix (2026-10-23), round 3's 27, the table-index fold's WIDTHIDX33, PHIIDX and NOLOOPFOLD, the select fix's SELGUARD [sel:t_select] and SELRECK [sel:kmax], FIX-NUMCONV's NUMCONVOP1 and NUMCONVW [nc:t_numconv nc:ncir] and NUMCONVOFF [nc:ncir], its follow-up's NUMCONVNOOP [gen], and the CSE-twin fix's CSEFWD [cse:t_csefwd cse:cseir] [S]:

1. **The copy is the repo's.** The build tree is copied into a fresh work directory; native/luajit's files must `cmp` equal; each patched LuaJIT file must equal what its patch script makes of the pristine file, which is the one the build compiled: `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit-export-<platform>/src` when it exists (the last build was pinned), else `OCLJ_LUAJIT`'s working tree (an unpinned build removes the export), and the run prints which; `eris_lj.o` must carry the serializer hash [S]. negctl-pack.sh's unregistered arm takes its pristine `lib_string.c` the same way [S].
2. **One line, anchored.** An arm replaces one line, or a run of consecutive lines, that must occur exactly once in the canonical file; the result must differ in exactly one line, which must say `SABOTAGE <NAME>:`. Anchors and arm texts write a tab as `@T@` and hold no tab and no backslash, and an anchored file must contain no CR [S]. The rest of the script is ordinary shell: no tab and no CR, but 120 backslashes, 95 of them line continuations and the others in `tr`, `printf` and pattern arguments, none in an arm's text [M: a byte count after the CSE-twin fix, 2026-10-23, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/docs/guide-w/bytecount.py`; 118 and 93 after FIX-NUMCONV's follow-up, `numconv/docs-arm/runs/bytecount-1.txt`, 116 and 91 after FIX-NUMCONV, 110 and 85 at `209aa95`, 106 and 81 at `e7eb613`, 98 and 73 at `eb47daf`; CSEFWD's arm call added two continuations, NUMCONVNOOP's two, FIX-NUMCONV's three six, the select fix's two four].
3. **Only what the file reaches is rebuilt**, from an explicit map, because `Makefile.dep` does not know the included oclj files: the map in [7.4](#74-running-make-by-hand) is this script's [S].
4. **The object fingerprint.** After each build, every `src/*.o` and `src/host/*.o` is compared by md5 with the shipped build's [S]. If no reached object changed, the arm is BROKEN (the sabotage did not reach the code); if any other object changed, BROKEN (something else changed). A `vm_x64.dasc` arm's line must also appear in `host/buildvm_arch.h`, which proves DynASM read it; the object change proves it was assembled (P46). The fingerprint found a gap in the first map: a 32-bit `and` drops REX.W, which moves handler offsets and changes `lj_bc.o` [M] (`impl-C3.json`; P14).
5. **canon and restore.** canon rebuilds every round-3 object from unchanged sources and runs every catcher the arms name (`bitop fuzz gen genrg2 floor fix1 idx sel nc cse security gate stage4` since the CSE-twin fix, 2026-10-23, `nc` since FIX-NUMCONV, 2026-10-21; a part missing from that list is never run by canon, so a broken suite of that part passes canon [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/integrate/ff-negctl/nocanon.log`]): every catcher must pass and every object must come out byte-identical. After the last arm, every file goes back, and the tree must match the shipped build object for object [S][M].
6. **Every listed catcher must fail at its named check** (`suite:check`, e.g. `fuzz:U`), not just any suite; a listed suite that exits 2 makes the arm FAIL as "proved nothing" [S].
7. **The catchers are the real gates.** The bitops gate and the stage-4 word check are extracted from build-native.sh at run time, so weakening a gate there weakens the catcher here [S].
8. **Memory-safe by construction.** Every sabotage computes a different value, raises a different message or none, writes a different version byte or word token, only blacklists loops (FIX1), or changes the IR's shape and, in every suite run, no value (PHIIDX, NOLOOPFOLD, NUMCONVOFF; PHIIDX's values rest on a hint PHI, [8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)); none touches a bound that guards a read or a write [S] (header). Since the table-index fold the JIT arms' argument no longer rests on "no fold removes the int conversion from an int64 source", which the fold makes false for a y of 32 bits or fewer. It rests on the checks after the conversion: a table index is bounds-checked as an int before any load or store uses it (ABC, `rec_idx_abc`, or ULE for an array extension; a hash key is looked up, not addressed); `string.byte`, `sub` and `find` check their int arguments against the string's length, and the other library functions take a wrong int as they take any int argument; a variable shift count passes `ULT 64`. So WIDTHIDX33's truncated y is a different int, checked by the same ABC, length check or ULT: a wrong value, never an address [S] (the header, restated 2026-10-20). SELGUARD removes the guard and keeps the record-time bound, and SELRECK removes the bound and keeps the guard, so each leaves `rec_varg` loading only where upstream's does: for a k within the varargs at record time, behind the unsigned ABC on k-1 (a variable k) or a GE guard of `fr` against a record-time constant (a constant k), and nowhere beyond the varargs, so a wrapping k gives a wrong nil [S] (the header, 2026-10-20; read before any wrapping k was sent to a build without the fix). NUMCONVOP1 reads `IR(fleft->op1)`'s opcode and, only for BNOT to BSAR, whose op1 is always a ref (lj_ir.h ~:57-64), that op1, so every `IR()` it takes is of a ref in the IR array; NUMCONVW reads only a CONV's op2; NUMCONVNOOP reads y's IR type and op2, fields of the same ref in the IR array: per call fewer fields than the shipped predicate reads (it also reads the opcode), though it now reads the op2 of an instruction that is no CONV, which the shipped predicate's `&&` never reaches; it compares op2's bits without following them as a ref, and keeps the `irt_isi64` test, so y keeps its I64 type and no instruction changes type. All three let the fold take an inexact `(i64)(num)y` as y, so a result guard goes and the result is the rounded double where the interpreter raises (U1-B), or a later operator reads y unrounded: a wrong value, never a pointer. NUMCONVW's 54 also lets the table-index fold take `x ~>> k` for a converted double x and a constant k of 22 or more: a different int, checked by the same ABC, length check or ULT as WIDTHIDX33's. NUMCONVOFF only keeps a guard that cannot fail [S] (the header, 2026-10-21; NUMCONVNOOP's sentence, 2026-10-22). CSEFWD, `(irt_isi64(IR(ref)->t) == irt_isi64(fins->t) || irt_isi64(fins->t))` in place of hunk 12's test, only lets one of our int64 instructions take an older instruction's ref on the same operands, as every build before hunk 12 did; its text admits an older instruction of any other type, but without FFI the ones that share opcode and operands with our operators are 32-bit [I: the design's census of producers; M: every candidate the refusal census saw was int, [11.10](#1110-crash-fixes)]: our consumers read the whole register, which holds the 32-bit result zero-extended, so a 5.3 result is a wrong number; a narrower `fins` against an I64 candidate reads false on both sides of the `||`, so the reverse match stays refused and no int64 ref reaches a 32-bit recorder, a slot or a snapshot [S] (the header, 2026-10-23; the code verifier read the same against the source). No arm holds the reverse half: its sabotage would let an int64 ref reach a 32-bit recorder that may store it in a slot, crash-class, which no arm may be (that half is held as [11.10](#1110-crash-fixes) says). New arms must keep this property.
9. **An arm that proves nothing is dropped, with the measurement.** INEXACT-RECABORT changed only the abort reason: the same raises, 26 starts and 26 aborts on both builds [M]. **An arm that changes no value needs a catcher that reads the code's shape or counts exits** (P34): PHIIDX and NOLOOPFOLD compute the right values, and only phiidx.lua's IR read catches them [M]. So does SELRECK: without the record-time bound a loop recorded with a k above 2^27 compiles and still gives the interpreter's values (a variable k exits at path C's guard; a constant k compiles path A's guard, which gives a nil that is right, or, for a constant from about 2^28, wraps into a guard that always exits; `t_select-kmax`'s three loops are `kx-pl`, a variable k, and the constant 2^27 + 1 on two shapes), and only `t_select-kmax`'s count of such traces sees it, in 2-3 of its 3 loops in every set (t_select reads 0 differences under it) [M]. It is kept as a fix site with its own catcher, where INEXACT-RECABORT, which changed only an abort's reason and compiled nothing more, was dropped [S]. So does NUMCONVOFF: the rule removes only a guard that cannot fail, so with it switched off every value is the same, and only ncir's count of the guards the rule removes sees it (17-22 cases a set; t_numconv reads 0); its `lj_opt_fold.o` is HEAD `209aa95`'s byte for byte (`1d018acc`: gcc drops the dead call and the unused static function, with no warning), which is not the shipped one (`f6d916e2`), so the fingerprint takes it, and the arm is exactly the build without the rule [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/integrate/runs/negctl-nc-1` to `-5`, each arm's `obj.md5`]. Not arms: `oclj_isnumconv`'s three type tests, which no test can show matter on this build (the user's decision, 2026-10-21; [3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)); and hunk 12's test dropped, which is the unfixed build: caught by the same checks as CSEFWD, it would prove nothing more, and it opens the reverse order too (P34; the fix round's design 5). Its fourth test, the opcode test, has had an arm since 2026-10-22, NUMCONVNOOP [gen]: that decision rested on the main session's claim that no test could show one of the four matters, which was false for the opcode test (dropped, it is value-visible), and the main session added the arm, correcting its own proposal's premise; the user may drop it [S][M]. A selection made with `OCLJ_NEGCTL_ARMS` proves only the arms it ran, and says so [S].
10. **Margins, not verdicts.** The JIT catchers vary from run to run (P6): measure each arm's margin over three runs, and gate on "nonzero", never on a count [M]. The table-index fold's three arms were measured in three runs (the implementer's, the code verifier's and the fixer's), and genrg2's catches in two runs for the five arms that list it and in one for the rest (2026-10-20, the header's THE MARGINS) [M]; genrg2's three-run margins are still to be read (open question 18). The select fix's two arms were measured in four runs, canon and restore passing in each: SELGUARD 11-53 cases in each of t_select's 15 sets (11-15 under `-Ohotloop=1 -Omaxside=0`, 51-53 under the side-trace sets), SELRECK 2-3 of 3 loops in each set (the header's THE MARGINS) [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/runs/s-b2-negctl-1` to `-4`]. FIX-NUMCONV's three arms were measured in five selected runs on the integrator's build of the working tree (three on an earlier header text of the script, one on a second, one on the final text; only comment lines differ), canon and restore passing in each, every count the same in every run and equal to the test implementer's three runs on the prototype's sabotage builds: NUMCONVOP1 t_numconv 30 cases in 10 of its 15 sets, 22 under `-O1`, `-O-loop` and `-O-cse`, 0 under `-O0` and `-O-fold`, and ncir 10 in each of its 13 (9 under `-O1`, `-O-loop` and `-O-cse`); NUMCONVW t_numconv 27 in each of the 13 sets that fold, ncir as NUMCONVOP1's; NUMCONVOFF ncir 22 a set (20 under `-O1` and `-O-loop`, 17 under `-O-cse`) and t_numconv 0 [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/integrate/runs/negctl-nc-1` to `-5`, `integrate/logs/margins-nc-12345.txt`; `numconv/impl-tests/final-1/`]. On the same build the twelve fold-engine arms were caught again in one run each, canon and restore passing; SELFGUARD's gen read 514 and WIDTH55's genrg2 153, just below the minima the header records (516; 154) [M: `integrate/runs/negctl-old-A.log`, `negctl-old-B.log`]. NUMCONVNOOP was measured in three selected runs on 2026-10-22, on the arm agent's fresh build of the working tree (`lj_opt_fold.o` `f6d916e2`, the shipped object; the sabotage's `e4fd19d9` in each run, the same object as the code implementer's and the code verifier's sabotage builds), canon (101-103 s) and restore passing in each: gen in every seed, 18-29, 18-32 and 19-31 cases a seed, so its fewest is 18, against WBAND's and WADD0's 11 [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/arm/negctl-1` to `-3`]. A fourth selected run, the arm verifier's on its own fresh build of the same tree (the same sabotage object), read 17-27 with canon and restore passing, so the fewest of four runs is 17 [M: `numconv/verify-arm/negctl-1/`]. The final check's full sweep, a fifth run, read 20-26 a seed, the sabotage object `e4fd19d9` again, so 17 stays the fewest of five [M: `numconv/main/final-1`, the archive's `logs/final-main/negctl-work/NUMCONVNOOP/`]. The arm agent's three ran a text of the script whose margin and cost comments still held placeholders (`b7a31dd3`), and the arm verifier's ran the arm agent's final text (`d3e7e4d3`); each differs from the final text (`0e453ecb`, the fixer's rewording) in comment lines only [M: `numconv/arm/recon-1/`, `numconv/fix2/cdiff-1/`]. negctl names its work directory by a UTC stamp, so the arm agent's runs of 2026-10-22, local time, sit in `20261021T21*Z-*` directories (the arm verifier's in `verify-arm/b1/negctl-bitop/20261021T223133Z-2025`). genrg2 caught it too, 24-28 cases a seed in one run on the code implementer's scratch build of the same sabotage (2026-10-21), and is not listed: it would add about 35 s (negctl's header, one run of each, 2026-10-20) to a catch gen already makes [M: catches, `numconv/impl-code/sab-noop/r1/`]. CSEFWD was measured in three selected runs on 2026-10-23, canon and restore passing in each, its sabotage object `ccf83851` in each (the same object as the test implementer's and the code verifier's one-file prototypes of it): the integrator's, on its build of the round's tree before t_csefwd gained `copy` and `side`, read t_csefwd 11 cases in 10 of its 15 sets, 1 under `-O1` and `-O-narrow` and 0 under `-O0`, `-O-fold` and `-O-cse` (no pair forms there, or none merges), cseir 18 in 10 of its 12 sets and 1 under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`, every count equal to the test implementer's on the prototype [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/integrate/runs/negctl-cse-1.log`; `csetwin/round/impl-tests/runs/cse-fwdsab-1`]; the fixer's two, with the 32-case t_csefwd, read t_csefwd 13 cases in 7 sets (6 in the first, where `side` read right under `-Ohotexit=1`, as it did once on `build/native` under `-Ohotloop=1 -Ohotexit=1`), 12 under `-O-loop` and the two `-Omaxside=0` sets and 2 under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`, and cseir 20 in 7 sets, 19 in those three and 2 under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`; t_cserev passed in all three [M: `csetwin/round/fix/runs/negctl-cse-1.log`, `-2.log`, their work directories `csetwin/round/fix/b1/negctl-bitop/20261023T124628Z-1438` and `20261023T125018Z-1335`]. A fourth reading is the final check's full sweep (Cost, below). Under `-Ohotexit=1` the code verifier's prototype of the sabotage read `side` wrong in 5 of 5 standalone runs, and `build/native` in 3 of 3 [M: `csetwin/round/fix/runs/pfwd-hx1-1`], so the fixer's first run is the rare reading: `side`'s count under that set is a range, as P6 says of every JIT catcher.

**Adding an arm, a catcher, a suite or a file.** Every list is hardcoded [S]:

- **An arm** is one call, `arm <NAME> <file> '<suite:check ...>' '<old line(s)>' '<new line(s)>'` (the comment above `arm()`, ~:713; the calls, in `armlist`, ~:785-900). Its new line must contain `SABOTAGE <NAME>:` (~:734). Add a row to the header's arm list, and its measured margin. Count each anchor in every copy of its file before trusting it, the build copy, the patch script's output on the pristine file and the repo's own file: FIX-NUMCONV's integrator counted all 35, each once, and the counter fails on an altered anchor, an anchor set to `  }` (70 matches), a dropped marker and a two-line new text [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/integrate/logs/anchors-1.log`, `integrate/ff-anchors/`]; the follow-up's arm agent counted NUMCONVNOOP's, once, in the repo's `oclj_bitop_fold.c` (`oclj_isnumconv`'s return line), and ran negctl's own `sabotage()` text on it, which refused an altered and a doubled anchor, a two-line new text and a dropped marker [M: `numconv/arm/ff-sab-1.txt`]; the CSE-twin fix's integrator counted all 37, each once in each copy, CSEFWD's anchor, hunk 12's type-test line, among them, and the counter, run on mutated copies of negctl, read x0, x70, `mark 0` and `DIFF 1/2`, each rc 1, with only CSEFWD's rows failing [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/integrate/logs/anchors-1.log`, `integrate/ff-anchors/results.txt`; again on the final text, `csetwin/round/fix/smoke/s1`, NEGCTL-ANCHORS].
- **A new catcher** needs a `run_*` function and a case in `runcatchers`, `rcof`, `check` and `info` (~:514-701), and, as the CRC32 follow-up found, a place in canon's catcher list (the `arm canon` line, ~:786), in `info`'s failure summary (its awk names each suite's failing cases), and in the header's catcher and arm lists [S]. `run_bitjit` pins `OCLJ_BITJIT_GENMODE` empty, so a generator mode as a catcher needs its own run function and `R_` status, as `run_genrg2` and `R_genrg2` are (~:529) [S]. An unknown catcher fails setup with "unknown catcher". Grep for a line only the suite's own comparison prints: the nc checks grep `^FAIL t_numconv (` and `^FAIL ncir (`, and the cse checks `^FAIL t_csefwd (` and `^FAIL cseir (`, so a failed `-joff` run (`FAIL ncir: -joff exited`) is no catch [S].
- **A new run-bitjit.sh part**, as the select fix's `sel`, FIX-NUMCONV's `nc` and the CSE-twin fix's `cse` were, touches both scripts [S: the select round's implementer; the two diffs of each round]. In run-bitjit.sh: the header's part list and its catches, the `OCLJ_BITJIT_PARTS` default and the `case` that validates it (~:254, ~:283), the file check and `inputs.md5` (~:279, ~:315), the part itself, and the full-set string of the final "parts run" line (~:954), which otherwise reports every default run as a subset. In negctl-bitop.sh: the part's name in `rcof` and `runcatchers` (it rides on `run_bitjit`, which runs the listed parts), a case per check in `check` (`grep` for the part's `FAIL` line), its failing lines in `info`'s awk, canon's catcher list, and the header's catcher list.
- **To measure what a candidate catcher catches**, run a scratch copy of `test/native` whose arms name only the candidate (the implementer's `scripts/mkprobe.py` rewrites the catcher strings of the arm lines, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/impl/scripts/`), with `OCLJ_REPO` set to the real repository [M: `crc2/impl/logs/negctl-probe-genrg2-A.log`, `-B.log`]. negctl finds its runners beside itself and the repo's files through `OCLJ_REPO`, so the copy changes nothing else. The same copy, with one arm's catchers changed to a check that must not see the arm, is that catcher choice's fail-first: NUMCONVNOOP listed on `nc:t_numconv` read NOT CAUGHT, rc 1, canon and restore passing [M: `numconv/arm/ff-catch-1/`, its tree `numconv/arm/ff-catch-tree/`], and so did CSEFWD listed on `nc:t_numconv`, in a call whose tree also had cseir's compiled results doctored, so canon FAILed naming cseir alone [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/integrate/runs/negctl-ff-1.log`].
- **A new floor suite** (round 3's t_rec, t_jit2 and p4v; t_rg2; the idx part's t_idx, t_cnt33, t_idx2 and phiidx; the nc part's t_numconv and ncir; the cse part's t_csefwd, cseir and t_cserev) goes into `test/native/bitop/` and into run-bitjit.sh's file check and `inputs.md5` (~:279, ~:315). Give it option sets (the runner's own are fixed, ~:356-359, the idx part's `IDXOPTS`, ~:554, the sel part's `SELOPTS`, ~:638, the nc part's `NCIROPTS`, ~:712, and the cse part's `CSEIROPTS`, ~:809), `-joff` as its reference, a PUC twin or a stated reason for none, and a coverage check that counts the rule's own IR signature where a rule is under test (P56; for a rule that removes, P61).
- **A new native/luajit file** goes into build-native.sh's `BITOP_FILES`, its include assertions and its freshness map (~:1422-1455, ~:1519-1524), and into negctl's `NATIVE_FILES`, `ALLFILES` and `objs_of` (~:382-385, ~:440-453). A new static function in an existing one goes into `bitop_wgate.c` ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)), as `oclj_isnumconv` did.
- **A newly patched upstream file** goes into `BITOP_PATCHES` (build-native.sh ~:1423) and into negctl's `PATCHED`, `ALLFILES` and `objs_of`.

- **Cost**: round 3's full run, 27 arms, took about 8 minutes (7 min 57 s on Windows, 6 min 20 s on Linux), left about 445 MB in its work directory and deleted nothing [M]. With 30 arms, and genrg2 listed on five of them, the full sweep in one call took 14 min 3 s (843 s) on Windows, canon 109 s, and left 878 MB in its work directory: the three idx arms took 15 s each, and genrg2's five arms 42-76 s each [M: the CRC32 round's final check, `bench/runs/2026-10-20-crc32/logs/final-main/logs/negctl-bitop.log`]. With the sel part canon took 107-108 s and each select arm 7-9 s, in the select round's four selected runs [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/runs/s-b2-negctl-1` to `-4`]; the full 32-arm sweep in one call, in that round's final check, took 14 min 30 s (870 s), canon 111 s and each select arm 9 s, and left 887 MB in its work directory [M: `bench/runs/2026-10-21-select/logs/final-main/logs/negctl-bitop.log`]. With the nc part canon took 95-100 s and each nc arm 8-9 s, and each selected call of canon and the three nc arms left 179 MB [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/integrate/runs/negctl-nc-1` to `-5`]. NUMCONVNOOP, the 36th arm, a gen run, took 37 s in each of its three selected calls, canon 101-103 s, and each call left 194 MB [M: du of the work directories, `numconv/arm/b1/negctl-bitop/20261021T21*Z-*`; logs `numconv/arm/negctl-1` to `-3`]. The full 36-arm sweep in one call, run by the main session in the round's final check, took 768 s (12 min 48 s; canon 99 s, each nc arm 9 s, NUMCONVNOOP 36 s), and its work directory held 955 MB [M: `numconv/main/final-1`, 2026-10-21 23:11-23:24Z; the archive's `logs/final-main/logs/timing.txt`, `logs/negctl-bitop.log`] ([11.11](#1111-long-runs-and-results-directories)). With the cse part canon took 108 s in the CSE-twin integrator's selected call, which left 157 MB, and 110 and 112 s in its fixer's two, and CSEFWD, the 37th arm, 12 s in each [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/integrate/runs/negctl-cse-1.log`, `csetwin/round/fix/runs/negctl-cse-1.log`, `-2.log`]. The full 37-arm sweep in one call, in that round's final check: PASS, every arm caught by its catchers (CSEFWD by `cse:t_csefwd` and `cse:cseir`), canon 122 s and CSEFWD 13 s, 865 s in all, its work directory 947 MiB [M: `bench/runs/2026-10-23-csetwin/`].
- **Splitting a sweep** [M: crc/combine, design.md gap 19; the implementer's report]. `OCLJ_NEGCTL_ARMS` runs canon and the named arms only, and proves only those. Canon repeats in every call (100-110 s), so the combined prototype's 37 arms took 21 minutes in five calls. The work directory is always `$OCLJ_BUILD/negctl-bitop/<stamp>-<pid>`, and those five calls left about 1.1 GB there, so an agent checking a build it did not make copies the build first. Two calls on one `OCLJ_BUILD` at once work, each in its own `<stamp>-<pid>`, and slow each other (canon 98-113 s).
- **The machinery was fail-firsted too** [M] (`impl-C3.json`, FF1-FF8): setup checks that exit 2 (a copy of the round-2 build, an extra line in `lj_parse.c`, a bogus arm name, a renamed stage-4 block, the wrong `eris_lj.o`); BROKEN arms (a no-op `1 &&`, a missing anchor, a missing marker, a two-line change); an arm held to the wrong catcher (NOT CAUGHT); a restore that skipped one file (FAIL).

How the round did it [S]: run the fail-first on the build before the change and record which build it was (`failfirst/build-under-test.md5`); use the check's own text, extracting a gate from build-native.sh instead of retyping it (`scripts/run-gate.sh`); include a positive control that must pass in the same run (t_bit; canon in negctl); keep `.exit`, `.start` and `.end` files beside each log.

**A check that the others imply proves nothing alone: make each one fail alone, drop it, or keep it for its message and say in the script that it cannot fail alone** [M: design.md gap 21]. rg2's prototype added an order check to `verify_content` that no input could fail while every hunk's text matched, because the order it checked lay inside one hunk (verify-rg2). The shipped script's comment above `verify_content` names which of its checks fail alone and which are implied: with every hunk's text matched, the `#if 1` count, the `oclj_bitwidth(` count and one order link (the pristine file's hunk order); on an edit of n11 or n7, the order links inside that hunk; implied, the include count and the two calls' counts, because an extra matching line also breaks an order link and a missing one the missing-line check. The first version of that comment said each check it named had been made to fail alone, which the code verifier's evaluation of every check, none exiting early, showed false for those three counts (the table above) ([8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) step 0). Test a claim of the form "this check fails alone" by evaluating every check on each mutation, not by the script's first refusal [M]. **And a check of the fix's own lines says nothing of the lines beside them**: hunk 12's first checks held the type test's text, place and neighbours exactly, and still passed an n12 that added a second, reverse-allowing match next to it at the same line count; only a count over the whole function (one match, two returns) refuses that ([8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) step 0) [M: the CSE-twin round's code verifier, m1-m4].

### 11.9 Instrument-first

When a reading surprises you, suspect the instrument first [S]. In round 3, each of these looked like a fact about the VM and was a fact about the instrument:

| the reading | what it was |
|---|---|
| bit-operator loops "do not compile" under `luajit -jdump` | `jit/bc.lua` raised inside dump's record handler on a signed bytecode word and aborted the trace: the tool changed what the JIT did (P9) [M] |
| 25 differences in an ad-hoc diff | CR from Windows stdout (P18) [M] |
| the fuzzer's JIT-on run passed | `LUA_INIT` had made it a second interpreter run (P22) [M] |
| PUC and ours agreed on `9007199254740993` | `%.17g` printed two different values alike (P23) [M] |
| the bitops gate passed a JIT that computed wrong values | the gate's loop discarded its running sum at the raise (P25) [M] |
| the guard-exit loop looked fine under NOINT-JIT | it never compiled: an `FNEW` in the loop (P28) [M] |
| `-jdump` showed `BAND 0011 +1` for a mask of 0x3FFFC | `tracek` returns the trace number for a KINT64 (P3) [M] |
| `-jv` printed nothing | the probe's own `jit.attach` replaced `-jv`'s handler (P11) [M] |
| `build/native/luajit_build.log` had no `-D WIN` | it was the last platform's build log (P19) [M] |
| an exit probe that "should" raise did not | `x << 20` with x near 2^33 has only 34 significant bits: significant bits run from the top set bit to the lowest [M] |
| the CRC32 follow-up's idx suites saw the table-index fold fire on a build that had only rg2 | rg2's range guard emitted the fold's own IR signature, `CONV int.i64` mode 0x275 (P56) [M] |
| a run-bitjit.sh run from a scratch repository passed | it had no PUC and skipped every twin; it now says PROVED NOTHING ([11.2](#112-the-puc-twin)) [M] |
| a probe's log read rc 0 for every option set | `rc=$?` after `cmd \| tr -d ...` is `tr`'s status; take a command's exit code through a file, as run-bitjit.sh's `run()` does [M: the code verifier's `optsets.sh`] |
| the select fix's coverage gate said PROVED NOTHING on a correct build, once | a control loop never got hot: an earlier case's abort penalty sat in the hot counter its pc shares (P59) [M][I] |
| FIX-NUMCONV's IR count read no identity guard under `-O0` and `-O-fold` on the base build too | no fold rule runs there, and a removal's signature is an absence, so ncir refuses those sets (P61) [M] |
| the nc part passed with ncir's JIT switched off | ncir exits 0 with the JIT off, and the runner read only its exit code and results; it now requires the summary line only a JIT run prints ([11.5c](#115c-the-nc-part-t_numconv-ncir)) [M: the code verifier's `tn-ff-c`, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/verify-code/`] |
| a value differential hung on an identity result | a float loop started at 2^53 with step 1 never advances (2^53 + 1 rounds to 2^53); bound such loops, and know that Git Bash's `timeout` does not kill a Windows luajit.exe child [M: the code verifier's first `vdiff.lua`] |
| a guard against writing in the repo let a path through | Git Bash's `pwd -P` keeps the case it was given and Windows ignores case, so `/c/users/.../oc-luajit/x` is the repo spelled otherwise; compare directories by identity (`[ "$d" -ef "$R" ]` on every ancestor), not by spelling ([11.11](#1111-long-runs-and-results-directories)) [M: the code verifier's F1] |
| cseir failed the `side` case on the fixed build under `-Omaxside=0` | its gate asked for two traces, and under `-Omaxside=0` the second is a stub side trace that links to the interpreter, with no pair in it; it now judges `side` only where a trace compiled our operator ([11.5d](#115d-the-cse-part-t_csefwd-cseir-t_cserev)) [M: the CSE-twin round's fixer, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/runs/cse-fix-1`] |
| the refusal census read one refused CSE match outside the cse part | the instrument's own format string, inside `bitop_test.exe`, linked with the instrumented `libluajit.a`; a recount over text files only (`grep -I`) read 0 [M: the CSE-twin round's integrator, `csetwin/round/integrate/t4-census-1.recount.txt`] |

What to do [S]: ask what else would produce this exact reading; check that the instrument points at the build you mean (run-bitjit.sh and run-bitfuzz.sh write `inputs.md5`, every `#cov` line says `jit on`); build the fingerprint into the check (controls, coverage gates, the object fingerprint); make absence explicit (a check that could not see anything says PROVED NOTHING and exits 2).

### 11.10 Crash fixes

A fix for a native crash is proved by two things: a black-box census A/B (the same unmodified workload, run against the library before and after the fix, abnormal exits counted per process) and the build's content assertions that the fix is present, each seen to fire on a sabotaged copy. It is never proved by a test that forces the crash. The evidence states that it is a census, not a unit test. Example: `bench/runs/2026-10-05-errmem/` (change 2) [S].

**A crash-class half that no workload reaches: the CSE-twin fix (2026-10-23).** Its forward half is a value fix, proved by values and IR (t_csefwd, cseir and CSEFWD, [11.5d](#115d-the-cse-part-t_csefwd-cseir-t_cserev), [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)). Its reverse half, a 32-bit recorder handed our I64 instruction that stores it in a slot a snapshot restores with no I64 case (P63), was never observed and was never run on any build: no reverse slot case exists anywhere, by the user's decision D2 of 2026-10-23, and the design's gated slot cases were not built [S]. A census A/B of the usual kind (`census-os.sh`, base against fixed) could not see it, since no known workload reaches the reverse order, and would read 0 against 0 [I: the round's design 4.8]. So the round held it by: **build assertions**, `verify_content`'s checks of hunk 12, which every build runs through the patch script (the type test's exact text once, inside `lj_opt_cse`, between its `if` and its return, and no other match or return there, [8.2](#82-writing-a-patch-script) step 0), each seen to refuse its mutation, the reverse-allowing form and the extra reverse matches m2, m4 and m5 among them [M]; **the test's symmetry**, an equality of the two instructions' I64-ness, which refuses an I64 candidate for a narrower `fins` exactly as it refuses a narrower candidate for an I64 `fins` [S]; **t_cserev's two no-slot shapes**, which show the reverse merge on `build/native` (rserved 2, split 0, every value right, i64slot 0) and its refusal on the fixed build (split 2, rserved 0) [M]; and, in place of the A/B, **a census of what the fix refuses**: a never-shipped copy of the fixed build (the one-file method of [7.4](#74-running-make-by-hand), two added lines in `lj_opt_fold.c` printing each refused match to stderr) read 830 refusals in the cse part as it stood before `copy` and `side` (754 forward, 76 reverse, the reverse all t_cserev's) and 0 in every other suite's output: run-bitjit.sh's gen (four seeds), floor, fix1, idx, sel and nc parts with the side-trace sets he1 and ts1 added, run-bitop.sh, the matrix files, p4v and phiidx under three sets, and the four timing benches [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/integrate/runs/t4-cse-1`, `integrate/t4-census-1/`, `integrate/timing/t4-benches/`]; the code verifier's own instrument read the same 830, one more from its own probe, and 0 in run-bitjit.sh's parts with gen at seeds 21-24, run-bitop.sh and run-bitfuzz.sh at seeds 11-15 [M: `csetwin/round/verify-code/runs/t4c-ff`, `t4c-bitjit`, `t4c-bitop`, `t4c-fuzz`]. So outside collision sites the fix changed no CSE decision in any suite the two censuses ran (they did not run the nine native runners, the generator's idx, rg2 and phi modes or the JVM harness), and at a collision site a refused match emits what `-O-cse` would [M][S].

### 11.11 Long runs and results directories

- **Anything longer than a minute runs from the main session**: `nohup sh chain.sh > log 2>&1 &`, plus a monitor that waits for a done file [S]. That covers negctl (about 8 minutes with round 3's 27 arms, 14 min 3 s measured with 30 and 14 min 30 s with the select fix's 32, 12 min 48 s with FIX-NUMCONV's 36, and the CSE-twin fix's 37 as [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)'s Cost gives it [M]), a round's final check (the CSE-twin fix's, [11.8](#118-fail-first)), timing chains (the CRC32 follow-up's took 7.5 minutes) and campaigns (4-40 minutes). When an agent's turn ends, whatever it launched dies; agents write the chain and prove its checks on something short.
- **Never edit a script that a running chain executes** (P17).
- **A chain that must not write in the repo refuses by identity, before it creates anything**: an outdir, and any build directory its runners will write into, is refused when any ancestor `-ef` the repo; a spelling comparison misses `/c/users/...` and `/C/...` on Windows, and WSL mounts only the lower-case `/mnt/c`. The CRC32 follow-up's final check does both since its fixer's version [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/fix/ff/real-final/results.txt`, 13 refusals; the first version passed 7 of those paths, `crc2/fix/ff/guardonly-old/`].
- **Results go to a fresh directory.** Every runner refuses an existing `*_OUT` with "exists; results are never overwritten" and exits 2 [M]. Never `rm -rf` a directory and reuse it; if a path must be reused, move the old one aside. Archive the md5s of binaries, not the binaries (P20).
- **As an agent, point `OCLJ_BITOP_OUT`, `OCLJ_BITJIT_OUT` and `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_OUT` at a fresh scratch directory**: their defaults are under `$OCLJ_BUILD` [S].
- **Wait on a file, not on a process list**: a sentinel the job writes (`done`), or its output; `pgrep` does not exist in Git Bash (P58) [M].
- **Hygiene of new files.** `git diff --check` ignores untracked files; `git diff --no-index --check /dev/null <file>` checks one, rc 1 when it is clean and rc 3 for a whitespace error [M: the CRC32 follow-up's implementer; again 2026-10-20 on a clean and a dirty scratch file, `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/docs/ws/`]. A census of `build/` (the md5 of its 38,985 files) took 210-217 s cold and 20-31 s warm [M: the implementer's final-check smoke runs].

### 11.12 Measuring speed

- **The tools in the repo** [S]. `bench/runs/2026-10-17-bitop/scripts/bench-r3.sh <fresh outdir>` runs `bench_bit.lua` and `bench_bit2.lua` (its `crc-table` field is the CRC32 row) three times. It alternates the round-2 baseline b0 (`C:/Users/astro/claude-tmp/oclj/r3/synth/b0/build/native/luajit-windows-x86_64/src/luajit.exe`, 32-bit operators) with the build under test, so the two share each run's machine state, and records `inputs.md5` and the running JVMs (`jvms.txt`). For a scratch build, copy the script and change `SH`. Round 3's reading is `logs/bench/bench.txt`: shipped `crc-table` 7.20 ns/byte, b0 2.80-2.90 [M]. review1's `bench_tf.lua` adds crc masked each byte (`crctm`) ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)).
- **The CRC32 follow-up's chain** (`bench/runs/2026-10-20-crc32/scripts/`) [S][M]: `timing-chain.sh <fresh outdir>` times up to five VMs (four builds that differ only in `lj_opt_fold.o`, and PUC 5.3.6) over nine rounds, each round in an order rotated by one, with six benches per VM (`bench_bit.lua` and `bench_spec.lua` at 2e7, `bench_bit2.lua`, `bench_crcx.lua`, `bench_idx.lua` and `ident.lua` at 1e7), each the best of 3 inside, then one `-Omaxside=0` pass of `bench_spec.lua` at 2e6. It records `inputs.md5`, the CPU load before and after, and the process list; it refuses an existing outdir (rc 99) and a running `java` or `javaw` (rc 97), and stops between rounds if one appears (rc 96). `bench_crcx.lua` refuses an instrument whose crcprobe(20000) is not 3012454781. `summarize.py` gives every VM's per-round ns and median and each build's ratio to base in the same round, and says RESULTS DIFFER, exit 1, when any result differs between the builds; `pairratio.py` gives one build's ratio to another per round; `b0-companion.sh` and `b0sum.py` time round 2's b0 beside three builds the same way. The outdir and `java` refusals, the crcprobe check (on round 2's b0, exit 2) and the RESULTS DIFFER exit were seen to fire; the stop between rounds was not [M: crc/combine `ff-chain/`, design.md]. Nine rounds took 7.5 minutes (`logs/design-chain/`).
- **Method.** Alternate builds within each run, take the best of 3, and read ratios within the run, not absolute ns: the bit and RGB rows print a ratio to a same-run arithmetic loop, and the `crc-table` row has none, so compare it with b0 in the same run [S][M]. Use a quiet machine, with no game, harness or campaign running (the script's header). Keep N at 2e7 or below: review1 saw 13-15% mode jumps in some runs at N=5e7 and 1e8 and never at 2e7 (eight processes per build) on this hybrid CPU [M review1]; `bench_bit2.lua` defaults to 1e7. **And keep it at 1e7 or above for rows of a few ns**: `os.clock` steps 1 ms, so a reading moves in steps of 1 ms / N, 0.1 ns at 1e7, 0.05 at 2e7 and 0.5 at 2e6, and a difference of a step or two is noise [S][M: the chain's summary prints the quantum]. A timing session is a chain and belongs to the main session ([11.11](#1111-long-runs-and-results-directories)).
- **Measure where a speculation breaks**, not only the target, with loops that are legal under U1-B. review1's xorshift64 and `h ~ (i<<40) ~ (h>>3)` raise under U1-B, so they measure nothing there; `bench_spec.lua`'s hi60, flag63, sh40, top8 and half are B-legal loops whose exact results leave [-2^53, 2^53) (the range is that, not "2^53 or more": -2^53 is inside it, 2^53 is not), and `ident.lua` puts identity operations on doubles at and past 2^53 [S][M: design.md gap 26]. Round 3's other rows (shl/xor/and, RGB, bit-serial CRC) must not slow down ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)) [S].
- **A result check against PUC must expect the rows past 2^53.** Where plain arithmetic passes 2^53, PUC's integers and our doubles differ by design: `bench_bit2.lua`'s rgb and ref, whose `i * 2654435761` reaches 2.7e16, and `ident.lua`'s big rows. The chain's summary lists them and calls any other difference a finding [M: `logs/design-chain/summary.txt`].

## 12. Tools

Run `luajit.exe` from the build copy's `src/` with `LUA_PATH='./?.lua'`, so `require("jit.dump")` and the other `jit.*` modules find `src/jit/*.lua` [S] (build-native.sh, jit tools gate). The sandbox has no `jit.*` modules [S]. These modules use the pin's own syntax extensions, `!=`, `..=` and `c ? a : b` (doc/extensions.html), which our luajit accepts [M]; keep that in mind if you patch them.

### 12.1 luajit -jv

One line per trace event [M]:

```
[TRACE   1 p_typeins2.lua:7 loop]                      root trace, links to itself
[TRACE   2 (1/1) (command line):1 -> 1]                side trace of trace 1, exit 1, links to trace 1
[TRACE --- (command line):1 -- NYI: bytecode FNEW  ]   recording aborted, with the reason
```

`LUAJIT_VERBOSEFILE` redirects the output, overwritten at each start (`jit/v.lua`) [S]. `-jv` attaches to `"trace"` (`jit/v.lua` ~:164), so a probe that attaches to `"trace"` silences it (P11). It prints no line for the blacklisting itself; a later `blacklisted at` line is another recording's abort (P39).

### 12.2 luajit -jdump

| char | default | what it dumps |
|---|---|---|
| `t` | yes | a line for each trace start, stop and abort |
| `b` | yes | the recorded bytecode (through `jit/bc.lua`: P9) |
| `i` | yes | the IR |
| `r` | | the IR with register and stack slots |
| `s` | | the snapshot map |
| `m` | yes | the machine code |
| `x`, `X` | | each taken trace exit; `X` adds every register |
| `a` | | the IR of aborted traces too |
| `T`, `A`, `H` | | plain text, ANSI colour, HTML |

[S] `jit/dump.lua` header.

- **The mode string**: `-jdump=is` replaces the default set, `+s` adds to it and `-b` removes from it. A second argument after a comma, or `LUAJIT_DUMPFILE`, names the output file, overwritten at each start [S]. Inside `-j` arguments, use Windows paths (P48).
- **Colour** comes from `TERM`, regardless of redirection; add `T` for logs and greps. `-jdump=T` alone is the default set in plain text (P12).
- **One handler per event**: `-jdump` attaches to `"trace"`, to `"record"` (for `b`) and to `"texit"` (for `x`); the last `-j` module on the command line wins `"trace"` (P11) [S][M].
- **Reading it**: [3.5](#35-reading-a-dump) and the annotated trace in [8.7](#87-reading-a--jdump). Every trace contains the CHECKHOOK lines near its head, and again after LOOP (P31); KINT64 constants print as the trace number (P3); the exact mark is never shown (P30).

### 12.3 luajit -b

- `-bl file.lua` lists a file's bytecode, `-bl -e "chunk"` a string's; the parser's folding shows there (`1 << 40` lists as `KNUM ... ; 1099511627776`) [M].
- `-b in out` writes a raw dump, whose header is `0x1b 'L' 'J'` followed by `BCDUMP_VERSION` 0x80 [M].
- `-b -t obj` (or `-t o`) needs FFI: "FFI library required to write this file type", exit 1 [M]. So does a raw dump to stdout on Windows (`-b in.lua -`: "FFI library required to write binary file to stdout", exit 1) [M]. `-t c` and `-t h` (a C source and a header array) work without FFI, exit 0 [M]. The checks are in jit/bcsave.lua's `bcsave_obj` (~:531) and `bcsave_raw` (~:153) [S].
- **Exit status**: an error raised while listing is reported and luajit exits 0 (P10). Gate on stderr, not on the exit code.

### 12.4 Counting traces and aborts per function

**Events** [S]:

| event | callback arguments | sent by |
|---|---|---|
| `"trace"` | `("start", tr, func, pc[, parent, exitno])`, `("stop", tr, func)`, `("abort", tr, func, pc, code, info)`, `("flush")` | lj_trace.c `trace_start` ~:470, `trace_stop` ~:562, `trace_abort` ~:622, the flush ~:299 |
| `"record"` | `(tr, func, pc, depth)`, once per recorded bytecode | lj_trace.c ~:701 |
| `"texit"` | `(tr, exitno, ngpr, nfpr, gpr values..., fpr values...)`, 16 and 16 on x64 (jit/dump.lua `dump_texit`) | `lj_trace_exit`, lj_trace.c ~:928 |
| `"bc"` | a new prototype from the parser | lj_parse.c ~:1736 |

- **One handler per event** (`jit_attach`, lib_jit.c ~:123): a second attach to the same event replaces the first; `jit.attach(f)` without an event detaches `f` [S][M] (P11). To count beside a script that attaches its own handler, wrap `jit.attach` before loading the script (P11's recipe); a probe's own handler silences `-jdump`.
- **Trace numbers are reused** (P29): key per-trace state on the `start` event.
- **`"texit"` counts exits that reach `lj_trace_exit`, not guard failures**: once an exit is patched to a side trace or to a "fallback to interpreter" stub, it sends no event, so the count stops growing while the guard keeps failing ([11.3](#113-option-sets)) [M: proto-rg2, verify-rg2].
- **Abort codes**: `require("jit.vmdef").traceerr[code]` gives the reason, a format string that takes `info`; `p4v.lua` finds TYPEINS by its message, "persistent type instability" [S].

The bitops gate's pattern (build-native.sh) [S]:

```lua
local jutil = require("jit.util")
local st, mine = { root = 0, loop = 0, stitch = 0, abort = 0 }, {}
local function cb(what, tr, func)
  if what == "start" then mine[tr] = (func == target)          -- numbers are reused: key on start
  elseif what == "stop" and mine[tr] then
    local lt = jutil.traceinfo(tr).linktype
    st[lt] = (st[lt] or 0) + 1
  elseif what == "abort" and mine[tr] then st.abort = st.abort + 1 end
end
jit.flush()               -- no traces left over from earlier code
jit.attach(cb, "trace")   -- this replaces -jv's or -jdump's handler
target()
jit.attach(cb)            -- detach
```

- **Blacklisting** shows in the loop's opcode: `JFORL` means a trace is attached, `IFORL` blacklisted. Read it with `jutil.funcbc(f, pc) % 256` and `vmdef.bcnames:sub(op * 6 + 1, op * 6 + 6)` (`p4v.lua` `loopop`) [S].
- **A JIT-off twin** must be a separate function with the same text, not a second closure of one prototype (P36) [S].

### 12.5 jit.util

| function | returns | on this build |
|---|---|---|
| `funcinfo(f[, pc])` | a table: `linedefined`, `bytecodes`, `params`, `children`, `currentline`, ... [S] | `bytecodes` includes the header instruction (the jit tools gate subtracts 1) [S] |
| `funcbc(f, pc)` | `ins, mode` [S] | `ins` is a non-negative number since change 12 (upstream returned it signed) [M]; decode with `% 256` or masks |
| `funck(f, k)` | a number constant for k ≥ 0, a GC constant for k < 0 [S] | |
| `funcuvname(f, k)` | an upvalue's name [S] | |
| `traceinfo(tr)` | `{nins, nk, link, nexit, linktype}`, or nil [S] | `linktype` is one of none, root, loop, tail-recursion, up-recursion, down-recursion, interpreter, return, stitch (lib_jit.c ~:275-292) [S] |
| `traceir(tr, ref)` | `m, ot, op1, op2, ridsp` [S] | the opcode is `ot >> 8` (its name is 6 characters at that index in `jit.vmdef.irnames`), the type `ot & 31`, a guard `ot & 128`, a PHI operand `ot & 64` [S][M] |
| `tracek(tr, k)` | `value, irtype[, slot]` [S] | wrong for KINT64 (P3) |
| `tracesnap(tr, sn)` | a table of snapshot entries [S] | entries and the end marker are unsigned (the end marker is 4278190080) [M]; `tracesnap(tr, sn, true)` gives the resume pc ([4.3](#43-snapshots)) |
| `tracemc(tr)` | `mcode, address, loop offset` [S] | |
| `traceexitstub(exitno)`, `ircalladdr(idx)` | addresses [S] | x64 has shared exit stubs (`EXITSTUBS_PER_GROUP`), so `traceexitstub` reads argument 1 as the exit number; the `(tr, exitno)` form is for per-trace targets (lib_jit.c `jit_util_traceexitstub`) [S] |

`jit.vmdef` (generated by `buildvm -m vmdef`) provides `bcnames`, `irnames` and `traceerr`, among others [S].

**Reading a trace's shape** (the CRC32 follow-up's recipe; phiidx.lua, stale.lua and stalegen.lua use it, and since FIX-NUMCONV ncir.lua, [11.5c](#115c-the-nc-part-t_numconv-ncir)) [S][M]. A fold that crosses a PHI and still computes the right values, or one that never fires in a loop body, is invisible to differential suites and to trace events; only the IR shows it (S4, P34). On a `"stop"` event:
- walk `ref = 1, jutil.traceinfo(tr).nins`; `local m, ot, op1, op2 = jutil.traceir(tr, ref)`;
- the opcode's name is `vmdef.irnames:sub((ot >> 8) * 6 + 1, (ot >> 8) * 6 + 6)` (padded to six characters); `ot & 0x40` marks a PHI operand and `ot & 0x80` a guard; the ref at which the name is `LOOP` divides the pre-roll from the body;
- a ref operand comes back bias-relative (`ir->op1 - REF_BIAS`, lib_jit.c ~:310-311): an instruction is positive and comparable with the `LOOP` ref, a constant is negative and is read with `jutil.tracek(tr, k)` (not a KINT64, P3); a literal operand, such as a CONV's mode, comes back raw, so `op2 & 0x3ff` is a CONV's type pair (0x275 `int.i64`, 0x26e `int.num`), with the exact mark (0x400) and the check mode (0xf000) masked off;
- follow operands to match a shape: phiidx.lua classifies each `CONV int.i64` by where its operand and the operand's own conversion sit, and stale.lua follows `ULE` → `ADD` → `CONV` → `BSAR` to find a body guard built on a pre-roll instruction.
Key such a count on operands, not only on opcode and mode (P56).

### 12.6 Reading the exact mark

```lua
-- run from the build's src/; not together with -jdump (one handler per event)
local jutil, vmdef = require("jit.util"), require("jit.vmdef")
jit.attach(function(what, tr)
  if what ~= "stop" then return end
  for ref = 1, jutil.traceinfo(tr).nins do
    local m, ot, op1, op2 = jutil.traceir(tr, ref)
    if vmdef.irnames:sub((ot >> 8) * 6 + 1, (ot >> 8) * 6 + 6) == "CONV  " then
      print(tr, ref, op1, string.format("0x%04x", op2), (op2 & 0x400) ~= 0 and "EXACT" or "")
    end
  end
end, "trace")
```

On a bit-operator loop this read `0x05d5` (num.i64, marked) for each result conversion, `0x16ae` (i64.num, ANY, marked) for the guard's own conversion back, and `0x12ae` (unmarked) for an operand's conversion of a slot [M], as `oclj_bitop_rec.c` describes [S]. The table-index fold's conversion reads `0x0275` (`int.i64`, no check bits, unmarked) [S][M: phiidx.lua's count]. For the rest of a trace's shape, see [12.5](#125-jitutil).

### 12.7 jit.flush, jit.off, jit.on

- `jit.flush()` flushes every trace, `jit.flush(n)` trace n, and `jit.flush(f)` the traces of f's prototype [S] (lib_jit.c `jit_flush` ~:78; lj_dispatch.c `setptmode` ~:225).
- `jit.off(f)` sets `PROTO_NOJIT` on f's prototype, covering every closure of it, and flushes its traces; `jit.off(f, true)` applies to f's sub-functions too; `jit.on(f)` re-enables it and unpatches the loop bytecodes. `-joff` and `jit.off()` turn the whole engine off [S] (P36).
- A JIT off-to-on transition re-initializes the hot counters (lj_dispatch.c ~:209-211) [S].

### 12.8 jit.opt.start and -O

- **How `-O` reaches the JIT**: each `-O` argument goes to `jit.opt.start`, and commas split it, so `-Ohotloop=1,-loop` works [S][M] (luajit.c `dojitopt`).
- **Levels**: `-O0` to `-O3` replace the whole flag set (`jitopt_level`, lib_jit.c ~:438), so order matters (P35) [S][M].
- **Flags**: `fold cse dce fwd dse narrow loop abc sink fuse fma`, prefixed `+`, `-` or `no` (lj_jit.h ~:80-90); the default is `-O3`. `jit.status()` here reads `fold cse dce fwd dse narrow loop abc sink fuse` [S][M].
- **Parameters** (lj_jit.h `JIT_PARAMDEF`, ~:108-128) [S]:

| param | default | param | default |
|---|---|---|---|
| maxtrace | 1000 | hotexit | 10 |
| maxrecord | 4000 | tryside | 4 |
| maxirconst | 500 | instunroll | 4 |
| maxside | 100 | loopunroll | 15 |
| maxsnap | 500 | callunroll | 3 |
| minstitch | 0 | recunroll | 2 |
| hotloop | 56 | sizemcode, maxmcode | 64, 2048 |

- **Side effects**: setting `hotloop` re-initializes every hot counter (`jitopt_param`, lib_jit.c ~:503-504); with no arguments `jit.opt.start` resets the flags only; settings last for the whole process (P35); an unknown name raises "unknown or malformed optimization flag 'bogus'" [S][M].

### 12.9 -jp

The profiler is in this build and runs on Windows: `luajit -jp=f -e "<loop>"` prints a per-function profile, and `jit.profile` loads [M].

### 12.10 Our runners and their knobs

What each does [S]:

- **run-bitjit.sh and run-bitfuzz.sh** unset `LUA_INIT` and `LUA_INIT_5_3`, strip CR before comparing, refuse a zero seed, case or tuple count, refuse an existing output directory, write `inputs.md5`, and exit **0** for pass, **1** for fail (or a crash), **2** for proved nothing.
- **run-bitop.sh** does the same, except that it has no counts to refuse and writes no `inputs.md5`.
- **negctl-bitop.sh** refuses an existing work directory and exits 2 on a setup failure, but does not unset `LUA_INIT` itself. Its catchers that are runners do; its `gate` and `stage4` catchers run luajit.exe directly (~:538-558), so unset `LUA_INIT` and `LUA_INIT_5_3` before running it (P22).

| runner | knob | default | meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| all | `OCLJ_BUILD` | `build/native` | where the luajit.exe, the archive and the objects are |
| all | `OCLJ_LUA53` | `bench/vendor/lua-5.3.6/src/lua.exe` (relative to `OCLJ_REPO`) | PUC 5.3.6; refused unless `-v` says "Lua 5.3.6". On Linux, pass one built on that distribution ([7.7](#77-the-linux-path), P52). Name it in every call: a scratch repository has no `bench/vendor` |
| `run-bitop.sh` | `OCLJ_BITOP_OUT`, `OCLJ_JNI`, `OCLJ_SHIM`, `CC` | fresh `$OCLJ_BUILD/bitop-<stamp>` | part 3 needs `jni.h`; without PUC, part 2 says SKIPPED |
| `run-bitjit.sh` | `OCLJ_LUA53` | as above | since 2026-10-20 a missing PUC makes the run PROVED NOTHING (exit 2); `none` declines PUC on purpose, and the run can pass ([11.2](#112-the-puc-twin)) |
| | `OCLJ_BITJIT_PARTS` | `gen floor fix1 idx sel nc cse` (idx and sel since 2026-10-20, nc since 2026-10-21, cse since 2026-10-23) | a subset proves only its parts, and says so |
| | `OCLJ_BITJIT_SEED`, `_SEEDS`, `_CASES` | 1, 4, 2000 | the generator's size; a default run of all four parts with PUC took 64 s on the CRC32 round's implementer's build (about 42 MB for the generator), of all five 63 s on the select round's [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/sel/impl/runs/s-b2-bitjit/`], of all six 61 s on FIX-NUMCONV's prototype build [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/impl-tests/runs/full-proto-1.log`], and of all seven 74 s on the build of the CSE-twin round's fixer [M: `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/round/fix/runs/bitjit-all-fix-1`] |
| | `OCLJ_BITJIT_GENMODE` | empty | `phi`: PHI-heavy statements; `idx`: index expressions at the table-index fold's bound; `rg2`: results at and past 2^53 ([11.4](#114-the-generator)); each is a campaign, not part of the default run |
| | `OCLJ_BITJIT_HOTMIN` | 0.25 | the coverage gate, for the generator and for the idx, sel, nc and cse parts |
| | `OCLJ_BITJIT_OUT` | fresh `$OCLJ_BUILD/bitjit-<stamp>` | |
| `run-bitfuzz.sh` | `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_SEED`, `_SEEDS`, `_CASES`, `_TUPLES` | 1, 1, 1000, 16 | PUC is required (otherwise exit 2) |
| | `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_HOTMIN` | 0.25 | the coverage gate, for H and for U |
| | `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_STRICT` | 0 | 1: also compare the type error's verb |
| | `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_PRUNE` | 0 | 1: delete a passing seed's tuple files |
| | `OCLJ_BITFUZZ_OUT` | fresh `$OCLJ_BUILD/bitfuzz-<stamp>` | |
| `negctl-bitop.sh` | `OCLJ_NEGCTL_ARMS` | every arm (37) | canon always runs |
| | `NEGCTL_GEN_SEEDS`, `NEGCTL_FUZZ_CASES` | 4, 1000 | every other runner knob is pinned; `NEGCTL_GEN_SEEDS` sizes both gen and genrg2 (the generator's `rg2` mode) |
| | `OCLJ_LUAJIT`, `OCLJ_SER`, `OCLJ_JNI`, `JOBS` | pristine tree, `serializer/`, auto, 4 | its work directory is `$OCLJ_BUILD/negctl-bitop/<stamp>-<pid>`; the pristine files it re-patches come from `$OCLJ_BUILD/luajit-export-<platform>/src` when that exists, else from `OCLJ_LUAJIT` ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)) |
