## Appendix B. Glossary

- **arm (negctl)**: a one-line sabotage of a fix site, with the checks that must catch it. **BROKEN**: the arm changed no reached object, or changed an unreached one. **canon / restore**: rebuilding from unchanged sources must reproduce every object, before and after the arms. ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages))
- **B-legal**: a loop or value under which no operator raises under U1-B; the loops that measure a speculation where it fails must be B-legal ([11.12](#1112-measuring-speed)).
- **blacklist**: `blacklist_pc` turns `FORL` into `IFORL` after 11 aborts; the loop is never recorded again ([2.5](#25-hot-counting-and-trace-start)).
- **build copy**: `build/native/luajit-<platform>`, made afresh on every run from the pin's tree as committed (its export, `luajit-export-<platform>`, checked against the pin's 40 digits), plus our twelve changes and seven files ([1.2](#12-our-twelve-changes-to-luajit), [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)).
- **census A/B**: the same unmodified workload run before and after a fix, abnormal exits counted per process: how crash fixes are proved ([11.10](#1110-crash-fixes)).
- **CHECKHOOK**: the hook-mask check near the head of every trace and again after its `LOOP` (P31; [docs/watchdog.md](watchdog.md)).
- **coverage gate**: a check that the JIT actually compiled a quarter of the loops; failing it makes a run PROVED NOTHING (exit 2) (P27).
- **CSE**: common-subexpression elimination, `lj_opt_cse` and `cse_conv` ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)).
- **CSE twins**: one of our int64 instructions and a 32-bit one with the same opcode and operands, which upstream's `lj_opt_cse` merged once `simplify_conv_sext` had handed ours an int ref; kept apart since 2026-10-23 by hunk 12's type test. **Forward** and **reverse order**: the 32-bit instruction recorded first (our operator got its value), or ours first (a 32-bit recorder got our I64 ref) (P63, [3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400), [11.5d](#115d-the-cse-part-t_csefwd-cseir-t_cserev)).
- **exact mark**: bit 0x400 (`OCLJ_IRCONV_EXACT`) of a CONV's op2; on `num.i64` it says the double equals its int64 wherever used; on `i64.num` it marks a guard's own check ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)).
- **exit / exit stub**: the code a failed guard jumps to; it restores the interpreter state from a snapshot ([4.7](#47-exits)).
- **fail-first**: running a new check against a case that must fail it before trusting a pass ([11.8](#118-fail-first)).
- **fix1**: the type-stability condition on `simplify_conv_i64_num`: across a PHI, fold only to an int64-typed ref ([4.5](#45-fix1-the-worked-case)).
- **FIX-NUMCONV**: `simplify_conv_i64_num`'s third rule (2026-10-21; hunk 7): `(i64)(num)y` ==> `y` when y is an unguarded `CONV i64.num` of a double (`oclj_isnumconv`), whose value is exactly a double whatever its range on x64, so an identity operation's result guard goes; a fact of exactness, never scored by `oclj_bitwidth` ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound), P60).
- **fold key**: `o << 17 | left->o << 10 | right->o`, with a literal op2 contributing `op2 & 0x3ff` ([3.6](#36-how-an-instruction-is-folded)).
- **guard**: an IR instruction with `IRT_GUARD`; if it fails, the trace exits ([8.6](#86-adding-a-guard)).
- **hunk engine**: the shared awk core of round 3's patch scripts ([7.5](#75-the-patch-script-engine)).
- **KINT / KINT64 / KNUM**: interned int32, int64 and double constants ([3.3](#33-references-constants-literals-trefs)).
- **marker**: the string a patch script leaves in its output, counted by the script and by build-native.sh.
- **option set**: a set of `-O` flags under which a suite runs, each exercising one path ([11.3](#113-option-sets)).
- **PHI / PHIBARRIER**: a loop-carried value at the loop branch; `PHIBARRIER` stops a rule from building a new instruction on a PHI operand's operand ([3.8](#38-what-a-rule-returns-and-upstreams-requirements), [4.2](#42-phis)).
- **PHIIDX, PHIREUSE**: the table-index fold across a PHI (a negctl arm, value-correct in every suite through `loop_unroll`'s hint PHI, caught only by phiidx.lua's IR read), and the alternative that returns the pre-roll's own conversion instead ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)).
- **pre-roll**: the recorded iteration, which runs once before the `LOOP` copy ([4.1](#41-copy-substitution)).
- **pristine tree**: `prototype/watchdog/luajit` at `1ee778a4`, tracked sources only ([1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is)); a pinned build compiles the pin's tree from its `.git`, checked against the pin, and reads its working tree only to name, in a WARNING, the tracked files that differ.
- **rg2**: the CRC32 follow-up's speculative range guard, `(y ~>> 53) + 1 <= 1` unsigned in place of a result guard's round trip, in loop bodies; prototyped, verified, measured and not shipped ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)).
- **select fix**: `patch-bitop-record.sh`'s hunks 4 and 5 (2026-10-20): `rec_varg` aborts the recording for a select index above 2^27 and guards `1 <= k <= 2^27` before path C's 32-bit shift, so compiled `select(k, ...)` gives the interpreter's results and errors (P54, [11.5b](#115b-the-sel-part-t_select)). **Paths A, B, C**: `rec_varg`'s three ways to record `select(k, ...)`, a constant k, a k within the varargs, a k beyond them ([2.6](#26-the-recorder)).
- **side trace**: a trace recorded from a hot exit of its parent ([4.8](#48-side-traces)).
- **snapshot**: the slot→ref map and resume pc an exit restores ([4.3](#43-snapshots)).
- **stitch**: a trace that ends at a call the recorder cannot follow and links to the next trace ([4.6](#46-closing-a-trace-and-linking-it)).
- **table-index fold** ("fold a"): `CONV int.num (CONV num.i64 y)` ==> `CONV int.i64 y` when `oclj_bitwidth(y)` ≤ 32, never across a PHI; `simplify_conv_int_num`, hunk 11 ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)).
- **TRef**: a ref plus its IR type and flags, what the recorder passes around ([3.3](#33-references-constants-literals-trefs)).
- **twin**: the same test program on PUC 5.3.6, generated from the same source, with the rule applied per operator ([11.2](#112-the-puc-twin)).
- **TYPEINS**: `LJ_TRERR_TYPEINS`, a loop-carried value whose IR type changes between the pre-roll and the copy ([4.4](#44-type-instability-typeins-retry-blacklist)).
- **U1-B**: the user's 2026-10-17 decision: a result with more than 53 significant bits raises, every other result is 5.3.6's.
- **width rule**: fold `(i64)(num)y` to `y` when `oclj_bitwidth(y)` ≤ 54 ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound)).

## Open questions

Claims this guide could not settle, and gaps it found. Each unsettled claim is marked [I] where it appears.

1. **The `num PHI` that stays alive** (0048 in [8.7](#87-reading-a--jdump)): attributed to SNAP #3 naming it; `loop_emit_phi`'s redundancy passes were not traced.
2. **`exit 5` once, then `exit 1` ten times** in `side1` ([4.8](#48-side-traces)): explained by later iterations entering through `JFORL` at the trace head; consistent with the dump, not traced in code.
3. **Re-executability between a snapshot and a guard** ([4.3](#43-snapshots)): read from the `needsnap` sites and stated as LuaJIT's design; not audited exhaustively. One instance is measured: a loop-body guard after `math.random` exits through the snapshot that follows the call, so the exit does not draw again (verify-rg2's `reexec_random.lua`, `runs/dump-reexec-random-2.txt`, 2026-10-20).
4. **The `jp` after `ucomisd`** in the interpreter ([6.7](#67-round-3s-checks-instruction-by-instruction)): necessary by the ISA; no negctl arm removes it.
5. **Int-typed operands under spill pressure** ([5.1](#51-our-ir-in-x64)): no test forces register pressure and spilling of a zero-extended index into an int64 op. The table-index fold adds the converse, an int taken from an int64's low half: verify-folda's `vf_press.lua` read 0 differences, but the fused-load path that would read a 64-bit spill of y's register is not shown exercised (verify-folda F12). The code verifier's `regpress3.lua` (2026-10-20) put y in a spill slot at all 54 `CONV int.i64` of a `-jdump=irm` dump, and the backend reloaded the full 64-bit value (`mov rdx, [rsp+0x100]`; dump.lua prints a spill as `[%x]` of a byte offset from rsp) rather than fusing a 32-bit load; values equal `-joff` under 10 option sets, and a bound-33 sabotage differed in all 10 [M: its report, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/crc2/verify-code/runs/regpress-4`, `-5`]. The fused path is still not observed.
6. **The hunk engine's `ncr()` check cannot fail on Git Bash** ([7.3](#73-line-endings-and-the-git-bash-instrument)): a prove-a-check-can-fail gap. A fix (`awk -v BINMODE=1`, or a byte count) changes all seven scripts identically and needs its own fail-first.
7. **GC64 pointer arithmetic that reassociates KINT64 constants now folds** ([3.10](#310-the-two-rules)): from the implementation review's reading; not measured.
8. **`oclj_bitop.h`'s list of `IRCONV_NUM_INT` equality tests omits lj_asm_x86.h ~:1993** (`asm_x87load`, 32-bit only, harmless): a comment fix for the next round that touches the header.
9. **Compound bitwise assignments have no suite** ([2.2](#22-lexer-and-parser)): `&= |= ~= <<= >>= ~>>=` reach the same emitter and were probed by hand for this guide, but PUC 5.3 has no such forms, so the twin, the generator and the fuzzer cannot produce them, and no file in `test/native/bitop/` uses one.
10. ~~**review1's CRC32 material lives outside the repo.**~~ **Closed 2026-10-20** ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)): `t_idx.lua` and `t_cnt33.lua` are in the repo, rebuilt for U1-B with a PUC twin (`twin.lua`), which replaced `refidx.lua`; both prototype diffs stay in review1's work directory, and the round's own prototypes in `claude-tmp/oclj/crc/`.
11. ~~**The rg2 speculation's cost under U1-B is unmeasured.**~~ **Closed 2026-10-20** ([8.8](#88-the-crc32-follow-up-the-table-index-fold-and-what-remains)): measured by the main session's chain, 0-5% on the CRC32 rows on top of the fold against 3-7x on B-legal loops past 2^53; rg2 was not shipped.
12. **Some [M] probes are described, not printed**: 2.2's upvalue loop, 2.5's hot-count and FNEW loops, 12.1's `p_typeins2.lua` and P39's blacklist probe live only in this guide's scratch directories. The four the pipeline sections lean on most (`side1`, `side2`, `exit1`, `abort2`), `guide1` and `crcprobe` are printed in full; a review re-creates the others from their descriptions.
13. **The pin's export no longer depends on the host's git** ([7.3](#73-line-endings-and-the-git-bash-instrument), [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)): since the export-check round it is archived with no line-ending conversion and every file must hash to its blob id, so any conversion a host's git applies (an attribute in the tree, `.git/info/attributes`, a user or system attributes file, `core.eol`, a filter, configuration from the environment) is a BUILD FAIL naming the file, not a silent change, and the Windows and AlmaLinux 8 exports are byte-identical [M: `pin3/impl/fc4e-w1/export-win-vs-alma.txt`, `fc4e-w2/`]. Archiving the tree rather than the commit is still what keeps `.relver` unsubstituted; that a tree archive never substitutes is documented (gitattributes(5), export-subst: "if git-archive has been given a tree instead of a commit or a tag then no replacement will be done", in both hosts' git documentation) [S], and measured on git 2.55.0.windows.5 and 2.43.7 only [M]. Open: a git that applied export-subst to a tree would change `.relver`'s bytes, and link 4 would refuse the export, so the build would stop rather than ship it [I]. Also not compared: the exported files' modes, and a symlink in a future pin's tree, which `hash-object` would hash through and link 4 would then refuse [I].
14. **Two runs in one `OCLJ_BUILD` at once have no lock** (pre-existing): a pinned and an unpinned run started together in the same `OCLJ_BUILD` remove and remake the same `luajit-<platform>`, and the pinned run's link could take the other run's `libluajit.a` under the pinned `eris_lj.o` stamp. A `mkdir`-based lock on `$OCLJ_BUILD` would close it; not measured [I] (the build-copy round's review).
15. **What the environment and directory guards leave** (rounds 4 and 5, [7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage)), none measured unless marked [I]:
    - **Names make reads that the list does not drop.** The list is what was found: round 4's missed `MAKEOVERRIDES`, which its review then measured reaching every compile line under mingw32-make 4.4.1 (dropped since round 5) [M]. An environment entry whose name is not a shell identifier passes to make and gcc and cannot be unset by sh; make takes such names too, its own `-*-command-variables-*-` among them, which may act as `MAKEOVERRIDES` did. None of the dropped names is one, and Windows' case-insensitive `getenv` is not known to match another Unicode spelling of these ASCII names. A refusal of such names would have to spare the ones Windows itself sets (`ProgramFiles(x86)` and the like).
    - `LDEMULATION` and the other binutils variables were measured only on a two-line shared object, and on Linux.
    - **Spellings Windows' own programs read otherwise.** Since round 5 every directory input is the physical path this shell resolves, and a name ending in a dot or a space is refused, which closes the two readings round 4's review measured (`..` after a junction, the trailing dot) [M]. Other Win32 readings were not tried: a reserved device name (`NUL`, `CON`, `COM1`) as a path component, a `:` (an NTFS stream), or another character MSYS stores under a private-use code point.
    - **The spelling comparison of directories that do not exist yet** refuses more than it must: on a case-sensitive Linux file system it can refuse two distinct paths that differ only in A-Z case, and take `<repo>/Build` for `<repo>/build` while `build/` does not exist; and since round 5 two names that differ only in bytes outside printable ASCII, as many of them, compare equal ([7.6](#76-build-nativesh-stage-by-stage); the "documented cost" case of `pin5/scripts/fn-test5.sh` [M]). Each is a false refusal, never a pass.
    - **The script's own repository** is guarded as `OCLJ_REPO` is since round 5, as the directory above the script's: a copy of the script kept directly in a directory of scratch makes that directory's parent a guarded "repository", so give a copy a directory of its own, `<copy>/native/`, as final-check2.sh does [S].
    - **Files of the build's names that are not the build's.** The guards keep `$OCLJ_BUILD` out of the checkout and out of the repositories; anywhere else, a file named like one the build writes there (`strpack.lua`, `luajit_build.log` and the rest) is overwritten. `OCLJ_BUILD` is documented as a scratch directory [S].
    - **Writes through planted symlinks.** The write-through cases the build-copy round's implementer listed (`mkdir -p` of a symlinked `obj-<platform>` or `OCLJ_OUT`, `>` into a symlink planted in `OCLJ_BUILD`, `cp` into a symlinked `dist` entry) are deliberate plantings, outside the threat model of [1.1](#11-what-the-fork-is), and left as they are.
16. **A latent CSE hazard, FFI and string.buffer** ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400)): `cse_conv` compares `MODEMASK`, which leaves out `IRCONV_NONE`, so a NONE-mode `CONV int.i64` (emitted by FFI code and by string.buffer's `recff_sbufx_len`, always of a pointer difference) would common with the table-index fold's zero-extending one of the same operand. Unreachable while `LJ_HASFFI` is 0 and `oclj_bitwidth` scores a SUB 64; read from source by proto-folda, verify-folda and the docs verifier, not measured.
17. **Side instructions on a PHI-marked `fleft` whose own operand is PHI-marked** ([8.4a](#84a-emitting-instructions-or-a-guard-from-a-fold-rule)): whether a rule may build new instructions there is not settled. The shipped rules build none on any PHI-marked `fleft`.
18. **The CRC32 follow-up's thin margins** ([11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages) item 10): WIDTHIDX33, PHIIDX and NOLOOPFOLD were measured in three runs (2026-10-20), but genrg2's catches in two runs for the five arms that list it and in one for the six it catches too thinly to list. `t_rg2.lua` failed under KFOLD64 [M], and no arm names `floor:t_rg2` as a catcher; whether one should is the implementer's open question.
19. **Why t_select's control once compiled nothing** (P59): the penalty written into a shared hot counter is read from `penalty_pc` [S], and that it is what kept `p2few-K-134217728` from compiling in one of eight runs is inferred [I]; no run traced the counter. Running the controls first made them compile in every later run read (285 of 285) [M], which fits the account without proving it.
20. ~~**`oclj_isnumconv`'s opcode test has no arm.**~~ **Closed 2026-10-22** ([3.11](#311-oclj_bitwidth-the-bound), [11.7](#117-negctl-one-line-sabotages)): it has one, NUMCONVNOOP, caught by gen in every seed of four selected runs, 17-32 cases a seed [M: scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/arm/negctl-1` to `-3`, `numconv/verify-arm/negctl-1/`]. The user's decision of 2026-10-21, made on the main session's proposal, kept all four of the predicate's tests without arms on the main session's claim that no test could show one matters. For the opcode test that claim was false, and the mistake was the main session's: a scratch build without the test made gen, t_rec and genrg2 differ from `-joff` (`numconv/impl-code/sab-noop/r1/`), because an unguarded int64 instruction whose op2 reads `IRT_NUM` in its low five bits passes as a NUM-source CONV [S]: in the traced case a bit operator's op2, the constant ref 0x7fee in ncir's `x | 1` loop under `-O1` [M: the code verifier, `numconv/verify-code/noop-probe-1/`], and in the generator's traces instructions' refs as well [M: the arm verifier, `numconv/verify-arm/irscan-1/`]; that these are what made gen, t_rec and genrg2 differ is [I]. On 2026-10-22 the main session added the arm, correcting its own proposal's premise. Its catcher is gen: the nc part sees that build only through ncir under `-O1` and `-O-narrow`, by ref layout, and t_numconv never [M: `numconv/verify-code/sab-vnoop/nc-1` to `-3`]. The three type tests stay without arms, as decided. The user is told and may drop the arm.
21. ~~**The nc arms' other catchers.**~~ **Closed 2026-10-22** by the main session's decision ([11.4](#114-the-generator)): NUMCONVOP1 and NUMCONVW keep only the nc part's catchers, as the idx and sel arms list only their own part's. Their one-run catches by gen, the floor (t_rec, t_jit2, t_rg2) and genrg2, on the prototype's sabotage builds, whose `lj_opt_fold.o` came from an `LJ_AINLINE` helper rather than the shipped `static int`, stay recorded in 11.4 (`claude-tmp/oclj/numconv/impl-tests/runs/genfloor-*-1.log`, `genrg2-*-1.log`); listing genrg2 would add about 35 s an arm to the sweep (negctl's header, one run of each, 2026-10-20).
22. **Hunk 12 compares I64 against the rest, which is enough only without FFI** ([3.9](#39-cse-and-why-the-exact-mark-is-bit-0x400), P63): with FFI the recorders also emit U64 instructions (lj_ffrecord.c ~:1105-1106's `CONV u64.int`, under `LJ_HASFFI`, and `lj_crecord.c`), and a U64 instruction and a narrower one on the same stand-in operands would still merge. A round that turns FFI on must widen the test to every 64-bit integer type against 8-32-bit ones (the design's B-width) or to `irt_sametype` (B-all), and look at open question 23 [S][I: the fix round's design 3, 11 point 4].
23. **`lj_opt_mem.c`'s `reassoc_trycse` is a second type-blind CSE** (~:786-798), over ADD and BSHL, which hunk 12 does not touch. It is reached only for an XLOAD whose address is an ADD, which needs FFI: without it XLOADs address a STRREF or a KPTR [S: the fix round's census, scratch `claude-tmp/oclj/csetwin/census/census.md` 7 point 4][I]. Note it if FFI is ever turned on.
24. **Upstream's CSE twin is not verified or reported** (roadmap row 269): stock LuaJIT 2.1 with FFI has the same class at the pin and at `a4c0645`, by reading, through FFI's 64-bit arithmetic and narrowing, routes our fork does not have [S][I: the fix round's upstream part, `csetwin/upstream/upstream.md`]. Its reproducer, forward order and values only, was written and not run; by the user's decision D3 of 2026-10-23 nothing is verified or filed for now. If upstream fixes the class, the pin-move round re-decides hunk 12 and CSEFWD ([8.2a](#82a-bumping-the-pin) step 4; roadmap row 270).
